# Overnight — 2026-08-21

Unattended work against the frozen loop. Each unit builds up to the loop boundary and never
across it: no new Events, Effects, redaction-schema rows or `reduce()` changes. Anything that
touched what a client receives, what an ability reveals, the lamp, or a schema entry was written
into **Escalations** and skipped rather than guessed at.

Every guard below was proven by injecting the bug it exists for and watching it fail, with the
failure text quoted from the test-result XML. Counts are read from `tests=` / `failures=`, never
from an exit code.

---

## Unit 1 — the flow spine: a walkable screen graph in `:ui`

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `4fdaaaa` — *"The screen list learns what may follow
what"*. Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=364,
failures=0** across every module and both targets (desktop and `iosSimulatorArm64`), up from 344
— 20 new. `DeviceLayoutTest` stayed green throughout (56 screens × 2 roles against a faked
notch), and `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean` — the flow layer sees no `:core`.

### What it is

`ScreenId` has always been *a screen list, not a navigation graph* — it says what exists and
nothing about what may follow what. `ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/home/someoneshome/ui/Flow.kt` is
the other half:

- **`ScreenGraph.exitsOf`** — what each screen's own controls reach, as an exhaustive `when` over
  `ScreenId`, so adding a screen without deciding what it reaches is a compile error rather than a
  screen with no way out. Same discipline as `Screen`'s own `when`.
- **`Flow.autoAdvance`** — the transitions the house makes with no tap, as a map. Boot's
  fall-through, the scan's ten-second window, the meeting from the ring to the lights going out,
  and the same meeting walked from outside the system.
- **`Flow.houseDriving`** — where stepping backwards is refused.
- **`Flow.housePushed` / `Flow.unrouted` / `Flow.viaActions`** — the three honest footnotes: the
  screens only the authority can reach, the two the port cannot reach at all, and the one edge the
  actions layer owns rather than a screen.
- **`FlowModel` + `FlowHost`** — the thing that walks it. `PanelState` stayed flat and inert; the
  trail sits beside it, is never read by a screen, and decides no game question.
- The playtest and debug roots now open on `Boot` and walk themselves. `CheatRoot`'s private
  local-state navigation is gone; the picker is now for the screens no walk reaches plus the role.

### The timings are placeholders, and that is the point

**Every number in `Flow.autoAdvance` is presentation timing.** In play the house owns the clock —
it decides when a ring is answered, when the talk starts, when the lights go out — and pushes the
screen. The table is what a phone with no house attached does instead, and it goes away when the
transport carries a round. Where the design already carried a number the table borrowed it rather
than inventing one; every row carries a `why` naming the cue on the screen that asked for it, and
a test fails a row that has none.

The one number that is *not* arbitrary is the scan window: `SCAN_SEGMENTS * 500` = ten seconds,
derived from the bar that draws it rather than restated beside it, so the countdown on screen and
the moment the light goes dark cannot drift apart.

### The transcription is read off the screens, not reviewed against them

`ScreenGraphTest` renders all 56 screens across both roles, both room types and markers on and
off, fires every tap target each one publishes via its click semantics action, and compares what
the screens asked for with what the graph claims. A retargeted button fails the build and names
both sides. It uses the semantics action rather than `performClick` deliberately: a pointer event
at a node's centre never reaches a control drawn underneath another one, so the graph would come
out *smaller* than the screen and agree with itself.

It passed on its first run against a graph transcribed from a throwaway probe of the same shape —
which is the result you want from a transcription, and worth nothing until it has been made to
fail (below).

### The no-back set is derived, not invented

D-102 ratified the haptic doctrine: *a screen that arrives unasked buzzes; a screen you tapped
into does not.* Read the other way round, a screen that buzzed is one the house put you on, and
nothing behind it is still true. So `houseDriving` is built from `PanelVals.BUZZING` minus the one
exception the doctrine itself names — the host's registration scan, which buzzes because the phone
is against a card with the display angled away — plus seven screens that buzz on nobody's phone
and are still the house's, each named with its reason at the declaration.

`PanelVals.buzzes` was a `setOf(...)` literal rebuilt on every construction of a `PanelVals`, i.e.
on every recomposition of every screen, to answer one membership question. It is now a
`companion object` constant. Against a whole-app budget of ~0.5 MB/s that is a small win on its
own; the reason it is here is that it makes the buzzing set a single source the flow layer can be
*derived* from rather than kept in step with by hand.

### What the tests actually hold

`FlowTest` (20, common — runs on desktop and on `iosSimulatorArm64`) proves the properties that
hold over the whole graph at once, which is exactly the kind nobody checks by walking a phone:

- every screen either offers a way onward or is one of six that are **waiting for the house**
  (revoked, restrained, outside the system, disconnected, and the two endings);
- starting from `Boot` plus the house-pushed screens, **every screen in the game is reached** — no
  orphans, nothing drawn and reachable by nothing;
- and its converse, that **nothing walks to a screen only the house can push**. That one bites: a
  tap wired straight to `Revoked` would let a client put itself out of the round, and the list
  would still say the house did it;
- back is refused on exactly the house's screens and works everywhere else, in both directions;
- the meeting is a line from any of the three ways it starts through to the lights going out;
- the two ways out are still not synonyms — walking onto an out screen carries the cause, so the
  carrier reads REVOKED or RESTRAINED rather than the UNREGISTERED fallback.

`FlowHostTest` (3, desktop) proves the *host* drives rather than the table being well-formed, on
the test clock so a ninety-second discussion is checked in microseconds.

### Injection log — five bugs written, watched to fail, reverse-replaced

Each was reverted with a targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`.

| injected | caught by | XML |
|---|---|---|
| `ScanBad`/`ScanUnknown`'s SCAN ANOTHER retargeted to `Home` | `ScreenGraphTest` | `tests="1" failures="1"` — *"ScanBad — the screen reaches [Home, Work], the graph says [Scan, Work]"* |
| `Tally`'s auto-advance row deleted, and `Tally` dropped from `houseDriving` | `FlowTest`, four independent tests | `tests="19" failures="4"` — *"unexpectedly stuck [Tally]"*, *"Tally does not move on; the meeting stalls there"*, *"Vote advances to Tally, which is a blind alley"*, *"these buzz, so they arrived unasked, so back must be refused on them"* |
| `Tally` advanced into `Revoked`, and `Files` added to `houseDriving` | `FlowTest` | `tests="20" failures="5"` — *"Revoked is listed as unreachable by tapping, but something reaches it: [Tally]"*, *"the no-back set changed. Widening it strands a player on a screen they opened"* |
| `LaunchedEffect(Unit)` — the classic wrong key | `FlowHostTest` | `tests="3" failures="1"` — *"expected `[Call, Assemble, Notice, Discussion, Vote, Tally, Home]` but was `[Call, Assemble, Assemble, Assemble, Assemble, Assemble, Assemble]`"* |
| the same, plus the stale-screen re-check removed | `FlowHostTest` | `tests="3" failures="2"` — *"a spent scan window moved the phone anyway"* |

**The third injection changed a test.** `Files` in `houseDriving` was caught only by two tests
that happen to use `Files`, and not by the test whose subject is the no-back set — that one
derived its expectation from the very set it was checking, so a wrongly-*widened* set passed it.
`theHousesScreensAreExactlyTheOnesNamed` was added to close that, and the injection was left in
place until it failed by name. A guard that can only catch the set getting smaller is half a
guard.

### Decisions taken (minor, inside the established design language) — flagged, not silent

- **`RoomEdit`'s STAIRS chip now routes to `StairsWarn`, and the type does not change until the
  host answers.** Without it that screen is an orphan: nothing on any screen reaches it, because
  the chip calls `pickRoomType` rather than naming a target — the one place the port hands a
  navigation decision to the actions layer. The design's reason is D-099: stairs hold nothing, so
  turning an occupied room into one unregisters every card in it, and the host is told what that
  costs *before* it happens. Setting the type first and asking afterwards would be the destructive
  edit happening before the question about it. `Flow.viaActions` records it as an actions-layer
  edge rather than smuggling it into `ScreenGraph`, where it would be a claim about a control that
  does not exist. Confirming (UNREGISTER AND CONTINUE) sets the type and returns to the editor;
  the port's `confirmStairs` previously navigated without changing anything.
- **The port's held room always holds markers, so the warning is unconditional.** Whether a
  particular room holds anything is host-setup data that arrives with the state; an empty room
  should one day change type in place with nothing to warn about.
- **The trail is capped at 32.** A round is twenty-five minutes of tapping around a springboard,
  and a list that only ever grows is an allocation the whole-app budget pays for.
- **`arrivingAt` moved out of the cheats into `:ui`** and both callers now share it. A picker that
  landed on the revoked screen with no cause rendered UNREGISTERED and read as a bug in the chrome.

### Flagged for a person — not escalations, but somebody should look

- **`Ghost2` does not buzz.** A revoked player sitting in the dark is told to STAND AND WALK IN,
  on a near-black screen facing away from them, with no haptic. D-102's canonical set is twelve
  screens and `PanelVals.BUZZING` holds seventeen; `Ghost2` is in neither. A buzz is identical for
  every player so adding one is not a tell, but it is D-102's set and I did not touch it.
- **`TermTaken` and `NoTerminal` are unreachable — recorded in `Flow.unrouted`.** Both are
  host-setup refusals the host is meant to hit during the setup walk (a terminal card offered to a
  second room; a save attempted with no terminal anywhere), and both are stranded because the
  screen that ought to route to them names an unconditional target instead: `Editor`'s REVIEW HOME
  always goes to `SaveName` even when `saveLabel` reads NEEDS A TERMINAL, and `ScanMarker`'s DONE
  SCANNING always goes to `MarkerSheet`. Closing it is a change to those two screens, not to the
  flow layer, so it was left alone and written down.
- **`IntegrityWidget`'s KDoc is stale after D-103.** It still says *"28 of 32, thirty-two segments
  rather than a percentage"* while the widget it documents renders `88%`. The code is right and
  the comment describes the design that revision 21 superseded. Not touched — it is a one-line doc
  fix somebody should make deliberately rather than have an overnight unit make silently.
- **`PanelVals` builds its fixtures eagerly on every construction** — `inbox`, `ballots`,
  `roomMarkers`, `current` — which means once per recomposition of every screen. `buzzes` was
  fixed because the flow layer needed the set; the rest was left alone as out of unit. Worth a
  pass against the ~0.5 MB/s budget before there is a lamp on the same device.

### Simulator (layout and self-advance only — no BLE, torch, camera or haptics)

Playtest variant built and launched on the simulator. The app opened on `Boot` and **fell through
to `Perms` on its own**, then held there — the screenshot taken seconds later and the one taken
after are byte-identical, so nothing runs on past a screen that owes no advance. Whole screen
checked: status row level with the Island, nothing under the cutout or the home indicator, the
PLAYTEST chip where it has always been. No errors in the device log. Nothing here certifies
anything about the game.

## Unit 2 — the plan model: what the host drew, in `:model`

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `34a7001` — *"The house learns its own shape"*. Full
`./gradlew check`: **tests=464, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and both targets, up
from 364 — 100 new (46 model tests running on both `jvm` and `iosSimulatorArm64`, 8 platform tests
on `iosSimulatorArm64`). Both lints and `:ui:boundaryCheck` still green; nothing in this unit goes
near `:ui`.

### What it is

The setup walk has two halves. `HouseMap` was the half that binds cards to rooms; `HousePlan` is
the half that says what the rooms **are**.

- `model/.../HousePlan.kt` — `Cell`, `CellRect`, `PlanRoom`, `Floor`, `HousePlan`, `PaintResult`.
  One grid plan per storey, rooms painted as unions of rectangular drags, named and tagged room or
  stairs. `paint` refuses politely so an editor can say why with the finger still on the screen;
  `HousePlan.of` `require`s the same things as the last-ditch guarantee for storage and every other
  path. Same shape as `HouseMap.register` and `Registration`'s `init` (D-099).
- `model/.../HousePlanText.kt` — format version 1, `someone-is-home/house-plan/1`. HouseMapText's
  doctrine applied line for line: versioned header checked rather than tolerated, every failure
  throwing `MalformedHousePlan` naming the line, nothing skipped, names escaped so a room called
  `KITCHEN|room|0,0,1,1` cannot forge a row.
- `platform/.../HousePlanStore.kt` + the iOS actual, beside `HouseMapStore`.
- `platform/.../DocumentStore.ios.kt` — the atomic write, the Documents path and the
  create-if-absent step, now written once instead of twice. `HouseMapStore.ios.kt` is rewired onto
  it and keeps the bug story that produced it in the shared file's KDoc.

### A room is a LIST of rects, not one rect

**This is a deliberate departure from the unit brief's wording, and it is the whole reason the
grid exists.** The GDD argues the grid over free rectangles on three counts, and the second is
that *L-shaped rooms just work, because real houses have them and rectangles cannot express them*.
A room modelled as a single rect deletes that quietly: an L-shaped kitchen becomes two rooms with
two names, and a player told to go to the kitchen has two places to stand — which is exactly the
`ShapeAlreadyRegistered` hazard D-086 refused, arriving through geometry instead. The **stroke**
is rectangular, because a drag is; the **room** is the union. E4's acceptance criterion —
*an L-shaped room is expressible* — has a test on it that reads the eight cells back.

The other two counts are paid in the same file. `Floor.neighboursOf` derives adjacency from cell
neighbours with no geometry (E4.9's *adjacency is queryable without geometry*), and there are no
resize handles, overlap rules or snapping anywhere in it.

### Decided here, and flagged rather than buried

1. **Adjacency is four-neighbourhood, not eight.** Two rooms meeting at one corner share no
   doorway; a diagonal neighbour would report a route through a wall, and error injection would
   place a player somewhere they could not have walked to. Injection-verified — adding the two
   diagonals fails `rooms touching only at a corner are not neighbours`.
2. **Adjacency never crosses a floor, stairs included.** *No vertical-connection logic* — the app
   renders what was drawn. A property test holds it.
3. **`Floor` carries no number.** Floors are purely additive, and an index is an ordering, which
   is one short step from "the stairs on floor 0 lead to floor 1" — the logic the design refused.
   Position in `floors` is the only order there is and means only the order they were added in.
4. **Room names are unique across the whole plan, not per floor.** A card is registered to a
   `Room`, which is a name and a kind — so two rooms called BATHROOM on different storeys give a
   registration two places it could mean, and the host who painted them is the only person who
   would ever know. Costs a host one rename during setup, in the light. **This is a judgement
   call and is the one most worth a second opinion.**
5. **`HousePlan.LIMIT = 256` cells.** A format sanity bound, **not a balance value** — a file
   claiming a stroke two million cells wide is corrupted, and expanding it to cells before finding
   that out is how a host's phone fails mid-setup. Roughly six times the widest plan anyone would
   paint by hand.
6. **Negative coordinates are ordinary.** A host who starts painting in the middle of the hall and
   works outwards must not hit an origin.
7. **A separate file, not a section of the map file.** The plan and the map are lost differently —
   a host repaints a wall without re-registering forty cards. One file means every save rewrites
   both, so a plan could be destroyed by a save that was only ever about a card. A test holds it.
8. **No `housePlanStorageDirectory()`.** There is one setup directory and `houseMapStorageDirectory`
   already names it.

### The bug the injection pass found

The reader tracked the open storey's name in a `var` starting at `""`. That sentinel is **also**
what a nameless floor row parses to, so `F ` was refused as *"two floors called ''"* by the
duplicate check before the empty-name check could speak. The right answer for the wrong reason,
and the reason is the half a host reads. It surfaced only because the empty-name guard came back
ASLEEP under injection — no test failed when it was disabled, because another guard was quietly
answering for it. The name and its rooms are now one nullable pair, and a test asserts the
message rather than only the refusal.

### 31 guards, each proven by breaking it

Every refusal was injection-verified: break the guard, watch the named test fail, read the failure
from `model/build/test-results/jvmTest/TEST-*.xml`, put it back by **targeted reverse-replace**
with a byte-identical assertion on the restored file. Never `git checkout` — the files were
uncommitted the entire time. Sixteen reader refusals (header, unknown row, room-before-floor,
field count, kind token, four stroke-parse cases, the grid limit, empty names, duplicate floor,
duplicate room, overlap, and the two escape cases), eleven construction `require`s, and four
editor refusals plus the adjacency shape.

A sample, quoted from the XML:

- `two floors with one name are refused on the second one's line` — *Expected an exception of class
  MalformedHousePlan to be thrown, but was java.lang.IllegalArgumentException: two floors called
  'GROUND'* (the reader stopped naming a line and let `HousePlan.of` answer instead)
- `a stroke past the grid limit is refused before it becomes cells` — *…but was
  java.lang.IllegalArgumentException: a painted stroke of 2000000x1 is past the 256-cell limit*
- `rooms touching only at a corner are not neighbours` — *expected:`[]` but was:`[PlanRoom(room=Room(name=PORCH, kind=Room), …)]`*
- `an unknown row is refused rather than skipped` — *…but was completed successfully with the
  result: `<HousePlan@117…>`* (a plan one room short, and nobody would know)

One test in the file is **not** a guard and is labelled as such: `adjacency never crosses a floor`
is a property of the structure — there is no cross-floor path to disable — so it was not injected.

### Not in this unit

Markers into cells (4.4), the Terminal and Array Wipe designations (4.5), the staircase
acknowledgment screen (4.8), the couch blind zone (4.10), the QR PDF (4.11), and the painter UI
itself. The loop is untouched: no Events, Effects, redaction-schema rows or `reduce()` changes.
Nothing here is read during a round.

**One follow-on worth someone's time, not raised as an escalation because it is a balance number
and those are out of scope:** decision-log:219 wants the Egress timer's *default* proposed from
the painted grid rather than fixed at 120s. The geometry that makes that computable now exists.

## Unit 3 — the editor, real: `HomeEditorModel` in `:ui` over U2's plan

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `909f4cf` — *"Two corners and a name"*. Full
`./gradlew check`: **tests=525, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and both targets, up
from 464 — 61 new (26 editor tests running on both desktop and `iosSimulatorArm64`, 5 gesture
tests on desktop, 2 flow tests on both). Both lints green, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports
`ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green throughout.

### What it is

The host-setup screens were a picture of one bungalow: six rooms in a hardcoded rect list, six
label placements nudged by hand, "6 ROOMS . 5 MARKERS" written down, and a plan you could tap
anywhere on to reach a room panel that always said GARAGE. They are now an editor.

- `ui/HomeEditor.kt` — `HomeEditorModel`, the editing state, sitting **beside** `PanelState` the
  way `FlowModel`'s trail does. `PanelState` stayed flat and inert; a plan under a finger is
  neither flat nor decided. Nothing in it answers a game question and nothing can — it is
  host-side setup drawn in the light, saved, and never read during a round, which is what makes
  real mutable state lawful in a module that cannot see `:core`.
- `EditorSurface` — the grid under the finger. Drag two corners on bare grid to paint a room;
  **drag starting inside a room to add a stroke to it**, which is how an L-shaped room gets
  drawn. `FloorTab`s switch storeys, the labels and chips are read off the painted rooms, and
  the drag draws a live preview that goes to the caution colour when it would be refused.
- Every refusal `HousePlan.paint` can return is translated in one place, so a crossing drag says
  THAT CROSSES KITCHEN under the host's finger rather than doing nothing — which is
  indistinguishable from a missed touch.
- The room panel's five preset chips and the name field are the same call. A rename carries the
  room's cards and the T card with it.
- Floors are real: tabs switch, ADD A FLOOR adds one and opens it empty, and every count on
  Floors and Save Home is counted off the plan.
- REVIEW HOME asks the plan whether any room holds the T card, and goes to `NoTerminal` when
  none does.

### D-098 and D-099 surfaced rather than re-derived

D-098 already held structurally — `RoomKind` has carried exactly `Room` and `Stairs` since
revision 20 — so the type chips read that enum directly and `ui.RoomType`, which was a second
copy of it, is deleted. D-099 is enforced *in the change*: `setKind(Stairs)` unregisters the
room's cards as part of becoming stairs, not in a step that follows it. `PanelState.roomType`
and `PanelState.hasTerminal` went with it: the plan is where a room's type and the terminal are
written down, and a second copy on `PanelState` is a second copy that would one day disagree
with the plan it describes.

### The stairs warning is conditional now, which closes a unit-1 flag

Unit 1 recorded: *"The port's held room always holds markers, so the warning is unconditional…
an empty room should one day change type in place with nothing to warn about."* The plan knows
what a room holds, so it does. A host tagging a bare stairwell is no longer interrogated about
cards that were never there.

### `NoTerminal` is routed, which closes another

`Flow.unrouted` is down to `TermTaken` alone. `TermTaken` stays stranded for the same reason it
always was, now written down properly: it is the refusal for *scanning a second T card*, which
is story 4.5 and has no camera behind it yet.

### Two bugs the gesture test found, and nothing else could have

`EditorSurfaceTest` drives real touch input. It exists because `ScreenGraphTest` fires click
**semantics actions** and a `pointerInput` publishes none, and `DeviceLayoutTest` measures
pixels — so between them the entire painting gesture could have been wired to nothing with every
test green. Both of these were live in code that had already passed a full green `check`:

1. **`detectDragGestures` reports the position at which a gesture *became* a drag**, after touch
   slop. The anchor arrived a cell late: a four-cell room came out three cells, and a drag
   started inside a room made a *new* room beside it instead of growing it —
   `expected:<[Cell(x=4, y=0), Cell(x=4, y=1), Cell(x=4, y=2), Cell(x=4, y=3)]> but
   was:<[Cell(x=4, y=1), Cell(x=4, y=2), Cell(x=4, y=3)]>`. Rewritten with `awaitFirstDown`.
2. **`awaitFirstDown` defaults to `requireUnconsumed`**, and the tap detector is the inner node
   and consumes the down first. The drag then waits forever and paints nothing, silently — *"the
   drag painted nothing: null"*.

The model's own tests could not have caught either: they are *told* which cells the finger
crossed, so they were being told the truth about a lie.

### Injection log — 22 guards, each broken, watched to fail by name, reverse-replaced

Never `git checkout`; the files were uncommitted the whole time. Read from
`ui/build/test-results/desktopTest/TEST-*.xml`, never an exit code.

| injected | caught by | failure text |
|---|---|---|
| `spanning` takes the corners in order rather than their extents | `HomeEditorTest` ×4 | *a painted stroke covers no cells (-1x1) — a drag that selected nothing* |
| `dragBlocked` does not exempt the room being grown; `dropDrag` always makes a new room | `HomeEditorTest` ×2 | *the kitchen was shown as blocking itself*; *the second drag made a new room instead of growing the first* |
| the overlap refusal is swallowed; the rename drops markers and the terminal; the duplicate-name check removed | `HomeEditorTest` ×5 | *expected:`<THAT CROSSES KITCHEN>` but was:`<null>`*; *expected:`<A ROOM IS ALREADY CALLED KITCHEN>` but was:`<null>`* — the rename **succeeded**, replacing the kitchen's cells with the garage's |
| stairs no longer unregister; `unregisterAll` leaves the terminal; `deleteHeld` holds nothing | `HomeEditorTest` ×5, `FlowTest` ×1 | *the terminal survived its room becoming a walk-through*; *the panel is left holding nothing while rooms remain*; *stairs hold nothing* |
| `addFloor` does not open; `openFloor` does not re-hold; the sample's rect height off by one; a tap on bare grid opens the panel | `HomeEditorTest` ×3, `FlowTest` ×1 | *expected:`<FLOOR 3>` but was:`<GROUND>`*; *the panel is still holding a room on the storey the host left*; *an empty cell opened the room panel* |
| REVIEW HOME goes to `SaveName` unconditionally | `ScreenGraphTest` | *Editor — the screen reaches `[Maps, Floors, SaveName]`, the graph says `[Maps, Floors, SaveName, NoTerminal]`* |
| the stairs warning made unconditional, then removed entirely | `FlowTest` ×1, then ×2 | *nothing was at stake and it asked anyway expected:`<RoomEdit>` but was:`<StairsWarn>`*; then *expected:`<StairsWarn>` but was:`<RoomEdit>`* |
| provisional room/floor names not checked for freedom; `markerCount` counts rooms not cards; a drag paints onto a fixed storey; the empty-name check disabled | `HomeEditorTest` ×6 | *expected:`<ROOM 1>` but was:`<ROOM 7>`*; *a floor called 'FLOOR 3' is already in this house*; *the ground floor grew a room expected:`<6>` but was:`<7>`*; *a room with no name* |

### Decisions taken (minor, inside the established design language) — flagged, not silent

- **A drag that starts inside a room grows that room.** The design says only *drag two corners to
  add a room*, but a room is a list of strokes and the whole point of the grid is that L-shaped
  rooms work — an editor that could only ever make single-rect rooms deletes E4's acceptance
  criterion quietly. Starting inside a room is unambiguous: a drag from bare grid can only be a
  new room, and a second room where one already is would overlap. The editor's hint gained a
  third line, DRAG FROM INSIDE ONE TO GROW IT, or it is undiscoverable.
- **A tap on the plan is an actions-layer edge** (`Flow.viaActions[Editor] = {RoomEdit}`), and
  `ScreenGraph.exitsOf(Editor)` no longer claims `RoomEdit`. Where a tap lands depends on which
  room is under it, and on most of a grid there is no room and it lands nowhere — a screen cannot
  name a target that is a position. Walked by a test, like the other two.
- **`NoTerminal`'s OPEN A ROOM goes to `Editor`, not `RoomEdit`.** The host has to choose *which*
  room the terminal goes in — *put it somewhere awkward* is the load-bearing line — and only the
  plan can ask that.
- **The editor draws no held room; the room panel does.** The panel always holds a room so it
  never opens on nothing, but an editor that lit the held one would open on a house with a room
  already picked out, reading as a selection the host did not make. Confirmed by screenshot: the
  first render had KITCHEN dark for no reason a host could explain.
- **New rooms are named `ROOM 1`, `ROOM 2`…, new storeys `FLOOR 3`, `FLOOR 4`…** A painted room
  must have a name, unique across the plan, because that is what a card is registered to.
  Obviously-provisional is what sends the host to the panel to say what it really is.
- **Room labels sit at each room's top-left cell.** The port's hand-tuned placements described one
  bungalow; a host who painted their own house got six labels in the wrong places.
- **The T chip is drawn as well as the count, not instead of it.** A room can hold cards *and* the
  terminal, and a chip that showed one would hide the other from the host about to go and find it.
- **DELETE ROOM names what it takes**, pluralised, and says nothing about cards when there are
  none. **The editor's viewport is a fixed ten-by-twelve window** — the design's own grid — over
  an unbounded coordinate space; panning is a later story.
- **Markers and the terminal are fixtures held beside the plan** (`markers: Map<String, List<
  MarkerShape>>`, `terminal: String?`) — 4.4 and 4.5 build the real thing. What is real now is
  the *shape* of that data, which is what lets D-099 and the REVIEW HOME gate be real rules
  rather than flags.
- **`ui.PlanRoom` renamed `PlanRect`.** It is a rectangle on the live-map fixture;
  `model.PlanRoom` is the painted room. Two types under one name in one package, introduced by
  this unit, was going to cost somebody an hour.

### Flagged for a person — not escalations, but somebody should look

- **All five preset chips are already taken in the sample home.** KITCHEN, LIVING, GARAGE, BATH 1
  and BED 1 are all painted in the bungalow, so in that plan every chip refuses. Correct
  behaviour and useless demonstration; a host starting from an empty plan sees them work. Worth
  deciding whether the chips should grey out when the name is taken.
- **Renaming and deleting a *storey* are still inert.** Both need something this unit does not
  build — a text field for one, a two-second hold for the other. They name the open storey
  truthfully and do nothing, which is better than naming a storey that is not open and doing
  nothing, but they are two defunct controls.
- **The floor tabs will crowd past four storeys.** They are weighted in a fixed-height row, as
  the design drew them for two. `FLOOR 3` at chip size in a quarter of 300 units will clip.
- **The home's own name is still the fixture's THE BUNGALOW** on the editor heading, and Saved
  Homes / Home Detail / Delete are still fixtures. Naming a home is the Save Home screen's field
  and wiring saved homes is the persistence story, both out of this unit.
- **`PanelState.markersOn` and the whole `marker*` block of `PanelVals` are defunct** — no screen
  reads any of them. They belong to the design's marker-placement mode (4.4). Left alone as out
  of unit, but they are currently an axis `ScreenGraphTest` spends renders on for nothing.
- **`IntegrityWidget`'s KDoc is still stale after D-103** — unit 1 flagged it, still true.

### Simulator (layout licence only — no BLE, torch, camera or haptics)

Playtest variant built and launched twice: once as it ships, where the app opened on `Boot` and
fell through to `Perms` on its own, and once with the playtest root temporarily started on
`Editor` so the grid could be looked at on a phone. Reverted by targeted reverse-replace. Whole
screen checked on both: status row level with the Island, nothing under the cutout or the home
indicator, the PLAYTEST chip where it has always been, room labels and marker chips landing on
their rooms, the stairs hatch reading as transit. Nothing here certifies anything about the game.

## Unit 4 — saved homes, wired to the phone's own filesystem

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `eb3b958` — *"The house survives the phone being stopped"*.
Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=639, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and both targets.

**On the count.** 639 is the number this tree produces from `tests=`/`failures=` across every
`*/build/test-results/**/TEST-*.xml` after deleting the results directory and re-running. The same
count run against `HEAD` in a throwaway `git worktree` gives **509**, so this unit adds 130. Unit
3's section above reports 525 for the same commit, which means it counted something this method
does not (probably the nested variant sweeps, whose results overwrite `app`'s directory in place).
Recorded rather than reconciled: two different numbers for one commit is worth somebody knowing
about before it is quoted again.

### What it is

Saved homes were three hardcoded rows going to a detail screen about a fixture. They are now the
host's own, on the phone, surviving the app being stopped.

- `model/.../SavedHome.kt` — a named home: the plan, what is registered in which room, and the one
  room holding the terminal. Refuses a home with no name, cards in a room the plan does not have,
  and **cards or a terminal in stairs** — D-099's last-ditch guarantee at the storage boundary,
  the same shape `Registration`'s `init` already is.
- `model/.../SavedHomesText.kt` — format version 1, `someone-is-home/saved-homes/1`. Same doctrine
  as `HouseMapText` and `HousePlanText`: versioned header checked rather than tolerated, every
  failure throwing `MalformedSavedHomes` naming the line, nothing skipped.
- `platform/.../SavedHomesStore.kt` + the iOS actual — `saved-homes.txt` beside the map and the
  plan, atomic, and a failed save throws (D-087).
- `ui/.../SavedHomes.kt` — `SavedHomesModel` beside `PanelState` the way the editor is, over a
  `HomeStore` port of two functions, because `ui` cannot see `platform`.
- `app/.../SavedHomesDocument.kt` — the port's real implementation. This is what `app` is for.
- `HomeEditorModel` now holds a whole home rather than only its geometry: `name`, `load`,
  `startNewHome`, `asSavedHome`.

### The plan's rows are the plan's, written by the plan's own writer

A home's plan sits in the middle of this file as `HousePlanText` wrote it, header and all, and is
read back by `HousePlanText`. There is no second copy of the plan grammar here, and the two
version numbers keep answering their own questions.

**A refusal from inside a plan is re-thrown against the line it really occupies in this file.**
`HousePlanText` counts from the top of what it is handed, which here is the middle of a longer
file; a host sent to line 2 for a row that is on line 5 goes looking at the wrong house. The row's
real line number is carried alongside it and swapped in.

Escaping is backslash and newline only, and that is a decision rather than an omission: **no row
here ends in a field that could be confused for another.** `H` and `T` take the rest of the line
and `M` puts the shapes first — lowercase words by construction — and splits at the first pipe. A
room called `KITCHEN|ring|LIVING` survives intact, and there is a test on exactly that. It also
means this file is not a third copy of the pipe-escaping logic the other two formats share.

### The relaunch proof, on the simulator, in two builds

The write and the read were deliberately done by **different builds**, because a process that
wrote a file and then read its own memory back has proved nothing.

1. **Build A** carried a temporary launch-time effect calling the real `FlowModel.saveHome()`.
   Screenshot: `HomeDetail` showing `THE ANNEXE / 2 FLOORS . 11 ROOMS . 9 MARKERS / ABOUT FIFTEEN
   MINUTES OF WALKING`.
2. **The container was read, not the code** — the practice D-096 named. `xcrun simctl
   get_app_container … data` → `Documents/saved-homes.txt`, 502 bytes, holding `H THE ANNEXE`, the
   eleven `R` rows across two `F` rows, eight `M` rows and `T HALL`.
3. **Build B had the writer removed** and the playtest root temporarily started on `Maps`.
   Screenshot: `SAVED HOMES` listing `THE ANNEXE — 2FL . 11RM`, read off the phone by a build with
   no code in it that could have written it.
4. **Stopped and relaunched** — `simctl terminate`, confirmed gone from `launchctl list`, launched
   again: the screenshot is **byte-identical** to the one before it.

Both temporary edits were reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, and the
simulator's `saved-homes.txt` was removed afterwards so the next unit's screenshots do not inherit
a house from this one.

The unit test `a second model over the same store sees the same homes` is the same proof in
miniature and is the one that fails on the build.

### Injection log — 15 guards, each broken, watched to fail by name, reverse-replaced

Read from the test XML, never an exit code. Never `git checkout`; the files were uncommitted
throughout.

| injected | caught by | failure text |
|---|---|---|
| the stairs check inverted in `SavedHome` | `SavedHomesTest` ×2 (+6 collateral) | *Expected an exception … but was completed successfully*; and *'THE BUNGALOW' says cards are registered in 'KITCHEN', which is stairs* |
| an unknown row skipped rather than refused | `SavedHomesTest` | *Expected an exception of class MalformedSavedHomes … but was completed successfully* |
| the duplicate-home-name check disabled | `SavedHomesTest` | *'X' has no plan* — the right refusal for the wrong reason, caught because the test asserts the line **and** the detail |
| the plan's refusal reported at its own line | `SavedHomesTest` | *expected:`<5>` but was:`<3>`* |
| an unknown shape id falling back to a default | `SavedHomesTest` | *Expected an exception … but was completed successfully* |
| newline not escaped in a name | `SavedHomesTest` | *unknown row 'UPSTAIRS 🕯'* — the forged row, exactly |
| the duplicate-card-row and two-terminal checks removed | `SavedHomesTest` ×2 | *…but was completed successfully* |
| the store's filename changed to the plan's | `SavedHomesStoreTest` (simulator) | *keeping a home destroyed the painted plan* |
| the list updated before the write instead of after | `SavedHomesModelTest` | *the list showed a home the phone does not have* |
| `unreadable` no longer blocking writes; the name-clash check removed; the open home no longer replaced | `SavedHomesModelTest` ×4, `FlowTest` ×2, `HostSetupInputTest` ×1 | *expected:`<[MUM & DAD'S]>` but was:`<[MUM & DAD'S, THE BUNGALOW]>`*; *a rename left a second copy behind*; *a refused save walked away anyway* |
| the hold confirming on touch rather than after two seconds | `HostSetupInputTest` ×2 | *the home went the moment a finger landed on it expected:`<3>` but was:`<2>`* |
| the hold carrying its previous attempt over | `HostSetupInputTest` | *hold 2 finished off the one before it expected:`<3>` but was:`<2>`* |
| the field ignoring what was typed | `HostSetupInputTest` ×3 | *the field did not shout it expected:`<THE [ANNEXE]>` but was:`<THE [BUNGALOW]>`* |
| `mapNewHome` not emptying the editor; `openSavedHome` loading it | `FlowTest` ×2 | *expected:`<0>` but was:`<11>`*; *the half-painted house was replaced by a tap* |
| HOLD TO DELETE wired as an ordinary tap | `ScreenGraphTest` | *Delete — the screen reaches `[HomeDetail, Maps]`, the graph says `[HomeDetail]`* |

**Two injections changed a test rather than only proving one.** The hold tests originally failed
with *"Failed to inject touch input … could not find any node"* — true, but it names the lift
rather than the fault, and it would send the next person looking at the wrong control. Both now
assert **with the finger still down**, and fail by name.

### Decisions taken (minor, inside the established design language) — flagged, not silent

- **PLAYED 3 TIMES and LAST PLAYED 12 AUGUST are gone from Home Detail**, replaced by `ABOUT
  FIFTEEN MINUTES OF WALKING`. Nothing in this app has ever hosted a round, so a play count is a
  number with no source that a host would nonetheless believe. The line that replaced it is one
  the plan really knows.
- **The minutes are derived: a room is a minute, a card is half of one, rounded to the nearest
  five, never below five.** Against the design's own home — eleven rooms, nine markers — that is
  *fifteen*, which is the number the design wrote. It is presentation, not a balance value:
  nothing locks at arming and nothing reads it.
- **DUPLICATE was inert and now works** — a copy under a free name, opened, staying on the screen.
  The unit brief lists three rows for this screen and this is a fourth; it was already drawn, and
  a working control beats both a defunct one and a deleted one. **Say if it should go.** No export
  was added anywhere.
- **The name field is the first real text input in this interface.** A `BasicTextField` in the
  readout face, with the field's own caret instead of the drawn one, so there is exactly one caret
  and it sits where the next character goes. Typing is uppercased on the way in, like a room name.
- **MAP A NEW HOME empties the editor**; opening a home from the list does not. Opening is
  looking; EDIT THE PLAN and RENAME are the two controls that say they will replace what you were
  painting, and they are the two that do.
- **A new home is offered `HOME 1`**, obviously provisional, for the editor's `ROOM 2` reasons.
  A copy of `THE BUNGALOW 2` is `THE BUNGALOW 3`, not `THE BUNGALOW 2 2`.
- **The list is most-recently-saved first**, and the top row is the brighter of the design's two
  intensities. That is the only ordering claim the screen makes.
- **A file this build cannot read is never written over.** The model comes up empty and
  `unreadable`, every write is refused while that holds, and Maps says so where the homes should
  have been. Coming up empty and saving on top would delete every house on the phone to make room
  for one, silently.
- **A failed write is a refusal on screen, not a crash.** D-087 says a failed save throws so that
  success is never reported; the throw still happens in `platform`, and `SavedHomesModel` catches
  it, leaves the list exactly as it was, and says THIS PHONE DID NOT SAVE IT. Louder to the host
  than a crash, and it does not take the plan with it.
- **Empty state**: NO HOMES YET. MAP ONE AND IT STAYS ON THIS PHONE. The design never drew one.
- **The lobby now names the home the host chose**, because HOST WITH THIS HOME otherwise walked
  them to a screen naming a different house. Everything else on that screen is still a fixture.
- **`Screen` gained a `homes` parameter** defaulting to the design's three with the bungalow open —
  the state every host-setup screen was drawn in, and what keeps `ScreenGraphTest` and
  `DeviceLayoutTest` rendering the screens the design intended.

### Flagged for a person — not escalations, but somebody should look

- **`HousePlanStore` (unit 2) now has no caller.** A home's plan is stored inside `saved-homes.txt`
  and nothing reads or writes `house-plan.txt`. It was left alone rather than deleted — removing
  another unit's shipped API overnight is not this unit's call — but two stores for the same data
  is the drift hazard this project keeps writing down. Delete it, or say what it is for.
- **A newly painted home still cannot be saved**, because REVIEW HOME is gated on a terminal and
  nothing places one without a camera (story 4.5). That is why the relaunch proof saved the
  editor's fixture rather than a hand-painted house. It is correct behaviour and it means the
  MAP A NEW HOME path currently blind alleys at NEEDS A TERMINAL on a phone.
- **Deleting a home cannot be proved on the simulator**, because there is no way to hold a finger
  down through `simctl`. It is proved at the store level instead (`deleting the open home takes it
  off the phone` re-reads the store), and by the gesture test against the real touch stack.
- **`Delete` and `SaveName` moved from `ScreenGraph` into `Flow.viaActions`.** Both are honest —
  a hold publishes no click action, and a refused save stays put — but it is two more edges the
  rendering test cannot read off a screen, and `viaActions` is now five entries rather than three.
- **`IntegrityWidget`'s KDoc is still stale after D-103** — units 1 and 3 flagged it; still true.
- **`PanelState.markersOn` and the `marker*` block of `PanelVals` are still defunct** — unit 3
  flagged it; still true.

### Simulator (layout licence only — no BLE, torch, camera or haptics)

Whole screen checked on both screenshots: status row level with the Island, nothing under the
cutout or the home indicator, the PLAYTEST chip in its usual place over the bottom control. The
detail screen's middle is empty by design — the delete button is pushed to the bottom — which is
worth a look by somebody with an opinion about it. Nothing here certifies anything about the game.
