## Unit 5 — marker registration: a card, a room, and every answer the map can give

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `083f0f6` — *"The cards stop being a picture of
cards"*. Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=724, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and both
targets. The same count run against `HEAD` (`eb3b958`) in a throwaway `git worktree` gives **639**,
so this unit adds **85**. Both lints green, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`,
`DeviceLayoutTest` green throughout.

### What it is

The setup walk's second half. Unit 2 gave the host a plan, unit 3 an editor, unit 4 somewhere to
keep it — and the cards in it were `Map<String, List<MarkerShape>>`, a fixture with every rule
already built on top of it. The rules were real; the data underneath them was not.

- `platform/.../CardScanner.kt` — the camera contract and the deck that stands in for it. It hands
  over **the payload**, nine characters as printed, not a decoded card: decoding is `CardPayload`'s
  in `model`, and a scanner that decided what a card *is* would be a second opinion about a format
  that describes paper. `SeededCardScanner` encodes real `MarkerCard`s with `CardPayload.encode`,
  so what the flow receives is what a camera would have produced and the decoder is exercised
  rather than bypassed.
- `HouseMap` learned the terminal. `register` routes the card marked T to `terminal` and never into
  `registrations`; `moveTerminal`, `forgetTerminal`, `forgetIn` and `renamedRoom` are the rest of
  what an editor needs from it. One new result, `TerminalTaken`.
- `HouseMapText` is **version 2** — it carries a `T` row. `SavedHomesText` is **version 2** and now
  embeds `HouseMapText.write(map)` beside `HousePlanText.write(plan)`, headers and all, the way
  unit 4 embedded the plan. Its own `M`/`T` rows are gone; there is no second copy of either
  grammar in it.
- `SavedHome` is `(name, plan, map)`. `HomeEditorModel` holds a real `HouseMap` and a `ScanOutcome`.
- `FlowModel.cardScanned(payload)` is where a card enters. `ScanMarker` shows what happened;
  `TermTaken`, `TermRemove` and the marker sheet's chips do what they say.
- The cheat surfaces gained a **shutter**: a chip that appears only on `ScanMarker`, names the card
  it will present, and calls the fake. Compiled out of release with the rest of that file.

### Both embedded formats spell a row `R`, and they do not mean the same thing

A painted room in one, a registered card in the other. The reader assigns a row to whichever
section is open and a section opens at its own header, which is why the headers are written out
rather than stripped. **A row arriving before either header is refused rather than guessed at** —
guessing files a painted room as a registered card or the other way round, and the file would read
back clean.

### Version 1 saved homes are refused by name, not migrated

A v1 file holds shapes with no printed ids. There is nothing honest to turn one into: an invented
id is a card the host does not have, and the card it collides with is a real piece of paper. The
refusal says what changed rather than reporting an unexpected string. **This deletes any home
saved by an earlier build** — which today is nothing outside a simulator container, but it is the
kind of thing worth knowing was decided rather than overlooked.

### Injection log — 23 guards broken, watched to fail by name, reverse-replaced

Never `git checkout`; the files were uncommitted throughout. Read from the test XML, never an exit
code. A sample:

| injected | caught by | failure text |
|---|---|---|
| `register` no longer routes the T card | `TerminalTest` ×6 | *expected:`<Room(name=HALL, kind=Room)>` but was:`<null>`*; and the reader's own *'1+TTTTTTT' is the card marked T, registered as an ordinary marker — it never is* |
| the terminal moves silently instead of asking | `TerminalTest`, `FlowTest`, `RegistrationTest` ×3, `HostSetupInputTest` | *expected:`<TermTaken>` but was:`<ScanMarker>`*; *the host was not told which room lost it expected:`<HALL>` but was:`<KITCHEN>`* |
| `forgetIn` leaves the terminal behind | `TerminalTest`, `HomeEditorTest` ×2, `ScreenGraphTest` | *the terminal survived its room becoming a walk-through* |
| `renamedRoom` drops the terminal | `TerminalTest`, `HomeEditorTest` | *expected:`<LANDING 2>` but was:`<HALL>`* |
| `HouseMap.of`'s T-card `require` removed | `SavedHomesTest` | *Expected an exception … but was completed successfully with the result: `<HouseMap@41f69e84>`* |
| the `T` row is not written | `SavedHomesTest` ×5, `TerminalTest`, `FlowTest`, `RegistrationTest`, `SavedHomesModelTest` ×3 | *the terminal stayed behind expected:`<HALL>` but was:`<null>`* |
| the map section header is ignored, so card rows land in the plan | `SavedHomesTest` ×17, `FlowTest` ×6, `SavedHomesModelTest` ×5, `ScreenGraphTest`, … | *a room row is kind\|cells\|name, and this one has 2 field(s)* |
| a map refusal reported at its own line rather than the file's | `SavedHomesTest` ×5 | *expected:`<7>` but was:`<2>`* |
| the plan/map agreement check dropped | `SavedHomesTest` ×3 | *Expected `MalformedSavedHomes` … but was `IllegalArgumentException: 'X' says cards are registered in 'CELLAR'* — the right refusal for the wrong reason, so the host is sent to no line at all |
| `ShapeAlreadyRegistered` produces no readout | `RegistrationTest` | *Expected `ScanOutcome$Refused`, actual `<null>`* |
| an unreadable payload produces no readout | `RegistrationTest` | *Expected `ScanOutcome$Unreadable`, actual `<null>`* |
| the marker chip's tap removed | `HostSetupInputTest` | *the wrong card came off the sheet expected:`<[HOME-05]>` but was:`<[HOME-04, HOME-05]>`* |
| the deck's shapes put back to ones the sample home holds | `SeededWalkTest` ×3 | *these cards are refused before they can demonstrate anything, against [triangle_up, square, diamond, ring, star, crescent, wide_rect, circle, triangle_down]. Expected `<[]>`, actual `<[SEED001, SEED002]>`* |

### Three injections came back ASLEEP, and two of them became tests

**1. The duplicate-card-id check ignored the terminal.** A file naming one printed id as both a
marker and the terminal read back clean, which is D-069's hazard arriving through the file instead
of through a reprint. `a card id used by a marker and by the terminal is refused` was written, the
injection repeated, and it now fails by name.

**2. The whole scan readout could be deleted from the screen and nothing failed.** The unit is a
card being offered to a map, and the visible half of it is one line in the middle of a black
rectangle — a scan that changed the map and said nothing is a host walking out of a room with the
wrong idea about what is in it. `everyScanOutcomeIsSaidOnTheViewfinder` renders the real screen and
reads all three branches back off it; deleting the readout now fails with *could not find any node
that satisfies: (Text … contains 'KITCHEN . ADDED')*.

**3. `HouseMap.inRoomNamed` is not a guard, and its KDoc said it was.** Replacing it with
`inRoom(Room(name))` breaks nothing, because `RoomKind` has two values (D-098) and a registration
into stairs cannot be constructed (D-099), so every room in a map is ordinary and matches
`Room(name)` exactly. No test was added — there is nothing to catch. The KDoc now says so instead
of claiming a hazard it does not hold off.

### Decisions taken — flagged, not silent

- **The playtest deck's shapes were chosen to miss the sample home's.** They collided at first and
  every ordinary card was refused by D-086 before it could demonstrate anything, on a build whose
  purpose is to walk the flow on a phone. Neither module could have noticed alone, which is why
  the test lives in `app`: `SeededWalkTest` is the only place that can see the deck and the home at
  once.
- **Five of the six scan outcomes are a line on the viewfinder and no movement**; only the terminal
  already being elsewhere is a screen, because that one is a decision. The design's own KEEP
  SCANNING TO ADD MORE is what this is derived from — a host with a stack of paper is not put
  through a screen per card.
- **A refusal always names a shape as well as a room.** The host is holding three cards; THAT SHAPE
  IS ALREADY IN GARAGE with no shape on it names none of them.
- **The card's printed id is shown, quietly, under the readout — the only place in the app that
  ever shows one.** Everywhere else navigates by shape (D-069). A host hunting for one specific
  card among nine has nothing else to go on, and this is host setup in the light.
- **A payload this build cannot read says so plainly** (D-071 permits it) with four messages off
  `CardRejection`. Registration had no screen for a bad card and this is not one — it is a line.
- **The scan readout is cleared when the host opens a different room**, not when they leave the
  scan screen. A readout is about a card *in a room*; stepping out to the marker sheet and back is
  looking, and dropping it there erases the confirmation they went to look at.
- **TAP A MARKER TO REMOVE IT works now**, keyed on the card rather than the shape it draws.
- **The bungalow fixture's cards have readable ids** — `HOME-00` through `HOME-09`, hyphens rather
  than spaces because `MarkerShapes.ALPHABET` is QR alphanumeric **minus SPACE**.
- **`PanelVals.lastRegistered` is deleted.** It was a hardcoded ring; the readout is the editor's.

### Flagged for a person

- **Story 4.5's Array Wipe designations (Spares / Rack / Disposal) are not built.** The unit brief
  named the terminal and not those, and there is no screen for them anywhere in the port. They are
  the other half of 4.5 and they need one.
- **The registration haptic is not stubbed.** D-102's doctrine is that `ScanMarker` buzzes because
  the phone is against a card with the display angled away; that is hardware and this build has
  none, so nothing was faked. A stubbed buzz would be noise nobody could tell from the real thing
  once there is one.
- **`HouseMapStore` now has no caller either.** Unit 4 flagged `HousePlanStore`; the map is stored
  inside `saved-homes.txt` for the same reason the plan is, so `house-map.txt` is a third file
  nothing reads. Three stores for two pieces of data.
- **`Registration` is used for the terminal as well as for markers**, which reads well and means
  the type's name is slightly wider than its KDoc. Worth a look by somebody with an opinion.
- **`PanelState.markersOn` and the `marker*` block of `PanelVals` are still defunct** — units 3 and 4
  flagged it; still true, and now that registration is real somebody could decide what 4.4's
  marker-placement mode wants from them.
- **`IntegrityWidget`'s KDoc is still stale after D-103** — units 1, 3 and 4 flagged it.

### Simulator (layout licence only — no BLE, torch, camera or haptics)

Two playtest builds, each with a temporary launch-time effect opening on `ScanMarker` with KITCHEN
held and dealing from the deck, reverted afterwards by targeted reverse-replace.

1. **Three cards.** Two land; the third carries the first's shape and is turned away. Screenshot:
   the bowtie drawn large in the caution ink, THAT SHAPE IS ALREADY IN KITCHEN, `SEED003` under it,
   and THIS ROOM showing the fixture's triangle beside the two that landed. The shutter chip reads
   SCAN SEEDT01 — the next card, named before it is dealt.
2. **Four cards.** The fourth is the card marked T, and the phone is on `TermTaken`: ALREADY
   REGISTERED, *this home has one terminal and it is in* HALL, KEEP IT IN HALL / MOVE THE TERMINAL
   TO KITCHEN, and the footnote about what moving it costs. Every name on it is read off the map.

Whole screen checked on both: status row level with the Island, nothing under the cutout or the
home indicator. The shutter chip overlaps the bottom-left corner of DONE SCANNING, which is the
same cost the PLAYTEST marker has always paid and only cheat builds pay it. Nothing here certifies
anything about the game.


## Unit 6 — the lobby, over the real transport

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `8a7634c` — *"Six names become six marks, and the line
goes nowhere but the house"*. Full
`./gradlew check`: **tests=797, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and both targets, up
from **724** at `083f0f6` — this unit adds **73**. Both lints clean (`vocabularyLint clean` on
`model`/`core`/`ui`/`app`, `redactionLint clean — 33 file(s)`), `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports
`ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green throughout, and both variant sweeps (`variantSweepRelease`,
`variantSweepPlaytest`) ran.

### What it is

The three cold-start screens were a fixture: a drawn caret where a name should be typed, three
hardcoded networks, a sentence somebody else wrote in the one-line field, and six named seat chips
in a lobby that had never spoken to anything. They are now the real thing — mDNS finds the homes,
a websocket carries a line up and counts down, and **the lobby names nobody**.

- `model/.../InsiderBand.kt` — D-103's balance envelope, written down once. `of(seats)` is the
  band, `clamp(seats, chosen)` is the amendment: **both edges clamp the setting itself**, not just
  the draw, so a host cannot hand-pick a count outside it any more than UNKNOWN can land on one.
  The placeholder edges are the decision's own (`max(1, ⌊seats/6⌋)`..`⌈seats/4⌉`, integer-only —
  a band edge that depends on rounding mode differs between two phones). **Playtest owns the
  numbers, as it owns the 7**; nothing here chose one.
- `model/.../protocol/LobbyWire.kt` — the two bodies that ride inside a `TransportFrame.Carry`,
  and the guard that is the point of the file. `Handover(line)` goes **client → host** only;
  `Standing(joined, linesIn, insiders)` goes **host → client** and has no `String` in it at all.
  One body with a nullable `line` was the obvious shape and is the shape rule 3 forbids — a nulled
  field still exists and somebody makes it non-null later. As two types, *the lobby leaked a name*
  is not a bug that can be introduced by filling in a field.
- `platform/.../transport/LobbyDesk.kt` — the host's table, beside `SeatLedger` and for the same
  reason: pre-arm transport bookkeeping, no `Event`, no `Effect`, nothing that survives arming.
  It holds the lines **in memory with no store reachable from it**, drops a leaver's line with
  them (so `linesIn` can never exceed `joined`, which the gate depends on), clamps the Insider
  setting on every change *and* every time the seat count moves under it, and wipes every line on
  `roundEnded()`.
- `ui/.../Lobby.kt` — `HomeFinder` and `LobbyLink`, which are all `ui` is allowed to know about
  the radio (the same shape `HomeStore` gives it of the filesystem), the `MemoryHomeFinder` /
  `MemoryLobbyLink` fixtures behind every render, `OneLine`, and `LobbyModel` beside `PanelState`
  with the editor and the homes.
- `app/.../LobbyOverTransport.kt` — the one place mDNS and the websocket meet the screens.
  `NearbyHomes` over `HostBrowser`; `LobbyHouse` (ledger + desk + `TransportHost` + advertiser,
  bound on port 0 so the advertisement carries whichever port came up); `LobbyOverTransport`
  joining it as a client. Wired into `CheatRoot`, with discovery events landing in the 0.8 rig's
  log — mDNS failing is otherwise completely silent, which was the entire symptom on the first
  device run.

### The host joins its own house

HOST WITH THIS HOME brings the server up on that phone and then **joins it over loopback like
everybody else**. That is not ceremony: a host reading its own lobby straight out of its own desk
would have counts arriving by a route no other phone uses, and the route no other phone uses is
the one nobody tests. The host's Insider setting goes to the desk sitting next to it and the
answer comes back around the socket — the number the host reads arrived exactly the way the number
everybody else reads did.

### NO NAMES — what was removed, and what replaced it

The lobby drew six seat chips with names in them. They were a fixture, and reinstating them would
be a decision about what a client receives, which is not a call a lobby screen makes. What is
there instead is the same two integers drawn rather than written: one mark per seat, `linesIn` of
them filled. **The fill order is arithmetic, not identity** — this phone was told two integers and
could not order the marks by seat if it wanted to.

The guard is stronger than "the screen happens to have no names to draw", because the phone holds
two pieces of text that would be natural to put there: the resident name its owner typed, and its
owner's one line. `LobbyInputTest` renders the lobby with both set and asserts neither appears.

### The resident name does not leave the phone — a boundary, not an omission

Name entry is real typing and the name stays local. Nothing on the far side reads it: who is in a
round and what the house calls them is the loop's, and the loop is frozen. Handing player-
identifying text up to a host that has no lifecycle for it is a thing to be asked about rather
than started quietly, so it was not started. Wiring it is loop work.

### LIGHTS OUT — the boundary, stated explicitly

**The transition is presentation only.** The button is gated on `everyLineIn` — every seat here,
every line in, read off the house's counts and not off anything this phone decided — and when it
fires it walks *this* phone to the ARMED screen and does nothing else. It does not lock the seat
ledger, draw the Insider count, stamp the balance values into a recording, start a clock, or tell
another phone anything. Every one of those is arming; arming is the loop's; the loop is frozen. In
play this button becomes an intent the house answers by pushing a screen to everybody at once,
which is exactly why none of it may be decided on a device. `FlowModel.lightsOut`'s KDoc says the
same thing at the call site, where somebody will actually read it.

The gate is also the host's alone. A client is told *WAITING FOR THE HOST* rather than given a
button that would do nothing — the host turns the lights off, and everything after that is the
house answering rather than the host announcing.

### Guards, and the bug injected into each

Every one was proved by injecting the fault it exists for, watching it fail, and reverting with a
targeted reverse-replace.

| Injected | Caught by | Failure, from the XML |
|---|---|---|
| `LobbyDesk.handedOver` also calls `saveSavedHomes(line)` | `OneLineNeverPersistedTest` (iosTest, real Documents directory) | `tests="1" failures="1"` — *"a one line was written to saved-homes.txt"* |
| `LobbyDesk.setInsiders` stores `chosen` instead of `InsiderBand.clamp(...)` | `LobbyDeskTest` | `tests="11" failures="1"` — *"the maximum edge did not clamp the setting. Expected <2>, actual <5>"* |
| The lobby draws `lobby.residentName` above the presence strip | `LobbyInputTest` | `tests="10" failures="1"` — *"the lobby printed the name typed on this phone expected:<0> but was:<1>"* |
| `OneLine.toString()` interpolates the text | `LobbyModelTest` | `tests="13" failures="1"` — *"the one line printed itself: OneLine(i still have priya's spare key, handedOver=false)"* |

**The never-persisted sweep is self-verifying.** `OneLineNeverPersistedTest` runs on the simulator
against the *real* Documents directory — where every durable store this app has writes, so it is a
sweep of everything the phone can keep — and it first writes a canary through `saveSavedHomes` and
asserts the sweep **finds** it. A test that reads no files passes for the same reason a test that
reads all of them does; the positive control is what makes the negative result mean anything. It
also checks fragments, not just whole lines: the first four words of somebody's line identify it
completely in a room of six people who all know each other.

`LobbyLoopbackTest` runs the whole exchange over an actual socket — one host, two clients on
loopback, lines up, standings down, a phone leaving and taking its line with it — which is the
code the two-phone evening will run. `HostDiscoveryTest` (already green) covers advertise-and-
resolve on the simulator. **No phone was required for any of it.**

### Two shared controls stopped lying

Both were found by a test that needed the truth and are worth knowing about beyond this unit:

- **`RowButton`'s `onClick` defaulted to an empty lambda**, so every settings row in the game
  published a click action that did nothing. It is nullable now, and a row with no handler is not
  a tap target. This matters most on the lobby, where the same settings are the host's to change
  and everybody else's to read, and where the difference has to be visible in what the screen does
  when you press it.
- **`PanelButton` has the same problem** and was *not* changed — twenty-five call sites, and none
  of them are load-bearing on it today. The gated LIGHTS OUT therefore draws its own inert block
  rather than reusing `PanelButton`, with the reason at the call site. Flagged below.

### Decisions taken inside the design language, and flagged

1. **Signal bars are gone from the Join rows; the address is in their place.** mDNS resolves a
   name, an address and a port and reports no signal strength at all, so `///` would be an
   invention on the screen a player uses to pick which house they are walking into. The address is
   also the only true thing that separates two homes when both households have called theirs THE
   BUNGALOW — which is why the nearby list dedupes by address and port and never by name.
2. **`ReadoutField` grew a `hint`.** The design drew a blinking `Caret` in every field to promise
   the device was listening; a real field shows its caret only once focused, so an empty one reads
   as a box rather than as somewhere to type. YOUR NAME and `one line` are the two places it is
   used — both screens where somebody is being asked for something.
3. **The one-line field does not shout.** `transform` is identity there, against the field's usual
   uppercase: a house name is a label and a confession is a sentence.
4. **A blank line is refused and the screen stays put**, with the reason under the button — the
   same shape as a refused SAVE HOME, and for the same reason: walking away would leave the player
   believing the house holds something it does not, with the lobby's count agreeing with them.
5. **`0 OF 0 HANDED THEIRS OVER` is not drawn** while nobody has joined; the strip above already
   says NOBODY HAS JOINED YET in words.

### The screen graph moved, and the two new edges are the actions layer's

`ScreenGraph.exitsOf(Secret)` is now empty and `exitsOf(Lobby)` is `{Secret}`; `Flow.viaActions`
gains `Secret → {Lobby}` and `Lobby → {Armed}`. Both belong there under the rule already written
in that file — where a tap lands depends on an answer the screen does not have. HAND IT OVER can
be refused; LIGHTS OUT is a control only while the house's counts say the gate is closed, so
whether the screen publishes it at all is not a fact about the screen. `Flow.unrouted` is still
empty and `FlowTest` walks both new edges.

### Looked at

`ScreenshotTest` gained a second viewer for the lobby's three states the id-driven sweep
structurally cannot reach — **empty** (what a host sees for the first thirty seconds of every
evening), **client** (no settings control, no LIGHTS OUT), and **ready** (the gate open, the slate
button live). All three were rendered and read whole, along with Join and Secret. `DeviceLayoutTest`
renders all 56 screens × 2 roles against a faked notch and stayed green.


## Unit 7 — notifications: one gesture, and what survives it

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `d86b84c` — *"Everything that pops, and the one gesture
that takes it away"*. Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=814, failures=0, errors=0** across every
module and both targets, up from 797 — 17 new (`NotificationInputTest` 8, `NotificationsTest` 4 and
one `ScreenshotTest` viewer on desktop, `FlowTest` +2 on both desktop and `iosSimulatorArm64`).
Both lints clean,
`:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`, and `DeviceLayoutTest` stayed green throughout.

### What it is

D-105 deletes the read concept and replaces the whole surface with one idea: *everything that pops
is a notification, swipe up dismisses it, and that is the whole gesture vocabulary.* The port had
banners; it had no gesture, and the interesting half of D-105 — what is still there afterwards —
was not expressed anywhere.

- **`ui/.../Notifications.kt`** — `NotificationKind`, `Notification`, and the three the port draws.
  The load-bearing field is `NotificationKind.heldBy`: **where the thing survives once the banner
  is gone, or `null` for nowhere.** Messages holds every text, the Egress widget holds the
  countdown, and a house notice is held nowhere at all. It is a field rather than a paragraph
  because a paragraph cannot be tested and this is.
- **`NotificationBanner`** replaces `HouseBanner` and `EgressBanner` — one composable taking one
  piece of data, so there is no per-kind banner to write differently by accident. It tracks the
  finger the whole way up, springs back short of `SWIPE_DISMISS`, and only ever moves **up**: down
  is not a shorter swipe up, it is a second gesture, and this app has one.
- **`PanelFrame`'s overlay box now clips.** The banner moves, and the status row is the one thing
  on screen that must not — it is how a player confirms the perimeter is still armed while
  something is arriving. The banner slides *under* the boundary rather than over the clock.
- **`FlowModel.dismissNotification`** — the actions-layer edge, declared in `Flow.viaActions`
  beside HOLD TO DELETE and the other gestures no synthetic click can reach, and walked by
  `FlowTest`. **Nothing is written down**: not which notification it was, not that it was
  dismissed, not when.

### Three sentences that were each written twice are now written once

Every notification appears on two surfaces — the banner, and the thing that holds it afterwards —
and two copies of a sentence agree on the day they are written and never again.

| said twice | now |
|---|---|
| the house's text, on the banner and as the Messages preview | `Notifications.text.body`, and `NotificationKind.Text.heldBy` says why |
| UTILITY and LANDING, on the Egress alert and on the widget | `Notifications.EGRESS_NODES`, joined two ways |
| the house notice, on the meeting screen | `Notifications.notice.body`, drawn in exactly one place |

The Egress pair is the one that would have hurt. Containment needs two people at two separate
markers and **nobody may speak**, so the pair of room names *is* the coordination; a widget naming
a different pair from the alert would send two silent people to opposite ends of a dark house, and
it would look like a typo in the diff.

### "Stored nowhere" is proved by looking everywhere else

There is no notifications model, and that is the strongest form of the property — but it is also
unprovable by inspection, because the way this feature grows a memory is that somebody adds an
inbox, a history, a list-of-what-arrived, and the notice is caught on its way past. So
`NotificationsTest` renders **all 56 screens in both roles** and asks which ones have the notice's
words on them. The answer must be exactly `{Notice}`. A leak into Messages, Notes, a thread or a
tile fails there and is named.

Its converse is in the same file, because a rule that only ever says *fewer screens* is satisfied
by an app with no screens on it: the text banner **must** still be readable in Messages, and the
Egress nodes **must** be named on both the alert and the widget.

### D-105's other half: no read state, held by rendering rather than by lint

`noSurfaceCountsWhatHasBeenLookedAt` renders every screen in both roles and fails on UNREAD,
UNOPENED, UNSEEN, or NEW as a tag. The one count the design keeps is the backlog header — *N
MESSAGES* — which counts what is there rather than what has not been attended to.

**A vocabulary-lint stem was considered and rejected.** `VocabularyRules` matches stems by
containment, and `unread` is a substring of `unreadable`, which is `SavedHomesModel`'s own word for
a store it could not read. A stem there would have fired on four existing call sites and been
switched off within a week; and the words that would actually need catching (`isRead`, `readAt`)
cannot be matched by containment without also catching `thread`, `already`, `Reader` and
`spread`. Rendering catches the thing that matters — a mark a player can see — which the lint
never could.

### Injection log — eight bugs written, watched to fail, reverse-replaced

Each was reverted with a targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`.

| injected | caught by | XML |
|---|---|---|
| the swipe threshold deleted — any drag dismisses | `NotificationInputTest`, two tests | `tests="7" failures="2"` — *"a short drag dismissed it anyway expected:<Notify> but was:<Home>"*, *"dragging the banner DOWN dismissed it"* |
| the notice added to the Messages backlog | `NotificationsTest` | `tests="4" failures="1"` — *"a house notice is shown once and stored nowhere — these screens still have it expected:<[Notice]> but was:<[Reveal, Notice]>"* |
| a NEW tag on every inbox row | `NotificationsTest` | `tests="4" failures="2"` — *"Did not expect any node but found '7' nodes that satisfy: Text contains 'NEW'"* |
| the Egress widget naming STUDY instead of LANDING | `NotificationsTest` | (same run) *"LANDING is named on [Banner] — the alert and the widget it survives into must agree"* |
| `dismissNotification` navigating with no banner up | `FlowTest` | `tests="30" failures="1"` — *"Boot has no banner and a swipe moved the phone expected:<Boot> but was:<Home>"* |
| `clipToBounds` removed from the overlay box | `NotificationInputTest` | `tests="7" failures="1"` — *"the banner reached into the status band while being swiped away — first at (12,46)… The bar is the one thing that stays put."* |
| the panel dimmed unconditionally, not with the banner | `NotificationInputTest` | `tests="7" failures="1"` — *"the panel behind the banner was at 2.874 and came back to 2.874 — the dim did not go with the banner"* |
| the banner's tap retargeted from Messages to Notes | `NotificationInputTest` **and** `FlowTest` | `tests="8" failures="1"` — *"a tap on the house's text did not open Messages expected:<Reveal> but was:<Notes>"*; `tests="30" failures="1"` — *"the Text notification opens Notes and survives into Reveal"* |

**Three of these changed a test, and two of them were my own guards failing.**

1. **The clip test passed with the clip deleted.** It swiped the banner so far that it had left the
   status band entirely by the time the frame was captured — a check that measured a banner which
   was not there. It now swipes a bounded distance *and asserts, from `positionInRoot`, that the
   banner really is over the band before measuring anything*, so a future version that overshoots
   fails loudly instead of passing quietly.
2. **The tap test asserted a value equalled itself.** It read the expected screen back off
   `Notification.opens` — the very field being injected — so the retarget went through green. It
   names `ScreenId.Reveal` outright now, and `FlowTest` gained the invariant behind it: **where a
   notification opens is where it survives** (`opens == kind.heldBy`), which is why two independent
   tests catch it on the second run. Worth noting that `ScreenGraphTest` structurally cannot: every
   springboard screen is already an exit of `Notify`, because `Notify` *is* the springboard with
   something on top.
3. **A claim in a KDoc turned out to be false.** The banner's doc said the drag detector sits
   outside the tap "which is why" both gestures work. Swapping the two modifiers and re-running all
   seven gesture tests showed it makes no difference — a drag consumes nothing until touch slop,
   and a tap past slop is no longer a tap. The comment now says that, and says it was checked.

### Decisions taken (minor, inside the established design language) — flagged, not silent

- **A swipe on a screen with no banner does nothing**, rather than navigating anyway. Both banner
  screens draw the *same* springboard underneath, so a dismissal that fired regardless would send
  a player who flicked at a tile back to page 1 from wherever they were — and it would be blamed
  on the touchscreen. `FlowTest` walks every screen in the game to hold it.
- **`SWIPE_DISMISS` is 14 design units.** Longer than a brush, shorter than a throw. Both failure
  directions are real in a dark room with one thumb: a dismiss that fires on contact throws away
  the one thing the house said, and a dismiss that needs a full swipe is a player fighting their
  phone. It is a presentation number, not a balance value.
- **The gesture tests read touch slop and the threshold out of the running composition** rather
  than hardcoding pixels. A hardcoded distance is a test that keeps passing while silently no
  longer exercising the branch it names.
- **`ScreenshotTest` gained a third viewer** — a banner at rest, mid-swipe and going, against a
  faked notch. The travel is a position, and a position is not a `ScreenId`, so the id-driven
  sweep structurally cannot reach it.

### Flagged for a person — not escalations, but somebody should look

- **The house notice still has a DISMISS button, and D-105 says the vocabulary is one gesture.**
  The port draws house notices as a full meeting screen (`ScreenId.Notice`, in the
  Assemble→Notice→Discussion line) with a button on it. D-105 calls house notices *"the popup from
  a phantom app"* and says swipe-up is the whole gesture vocabulary — which reads as though the
  notice should be a banner like the other two. Turning it into one restructures the meeting line,
  which is not a notifications change, so **the kind exists and its words are single-sourced, and
  the surface was left exactly as drawn.** Somebody should decide whether the notice is a screen
  the meeting stops at or a banner over the meeting.
- **The Perms screen lists a NOTIFICATIONS permission as PENDING.** That is the *operating
  system's* notification permission, and nothing in this unit is an OS notification — the phantom
  OS draws its own. Whether the real app ever asks for the system one (for a banner while
  backgrounded, say) is a question nobody has answered, and it is sitting on a screen telling every
  player it will be asked for.
- **`Notification.bodySize` is presentation on a data class.** It earns its place today because a
  headline and a sentence are set differently, but a data type carrying a font size is the sort of
  thing that grows a second one. Worth a look if a fourth kind appears.

### Looked at

Three banner frames rendered to PNG and read whole (`ui/build/screens/banner-*.png`): at rest the
panel behind is at `NOTIFIED_DIM` with the status row bright and clear above it; mid-swipe the
HOUSE/NOW header has gone under the boundary with only the body line showing and **the status row
untouched**; the amber block ends cleanly on the band edge rather than riding over it.

Playtest variant built, installed and launched on the simulator. It opened on `Boot` and fell
through to `Perms` on its own, as before. Whole screen checked: status row level with the Island,
nothing under the cutout or the home indicator, the PLAYTEST chip in place, no errors in the device
log. **It says nothing about this unit's work** — reaching a banner needs taps the simulator path
cannot make — so the visual instrument here was the render harness above plus `DeviceLayoutTest`,
which renders all 56 screens × 2 roles against a faked notch and stayed green.

## Unit 8 — in-screen interactivity: echo, and the four buttons that stopped predicting

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `4a5fa20` — *"One phone says it is done, and the room
decides when"*. Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=863, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and
both targets, up from 814 — 49 new (`CountdownTest` 7 and `MeetingTest` 9 in common, so 32 across
desktop and `iosSimulatorArm64`; `MeetingInputTest` 12 and `MeetingDisclosureTest` 4 on desktop;
one `ScreenshotTest` viewer). Both lints clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`, and
`DeviceLayoutTest` stayed green throughout.

### The finding the unit turned on

The brief asked for vote-selection echo and check-in echo. Reading the four meeting screens
together turned up something bigger: **I AM HERE, READY TO VOTE and LOCK IN look like three
different buttons and are the same button** — *one player says they are done* — and all three
navigated.

What follows each of them depends on **every** phone in the house:

| control | what it waits for | who can know that |
|---|---|---|
| I AM HERE | every living player *and* every out player standing at the meeting area (D-104) | the house |
| READY TO VOTE | a **unanimous** ready | the house |
| LOCK IN | the vote window closing and the ballot being read | the house |

A phone cannot count phones. So a press on any of them was the device predicting an outcome it
cannot see — the leak surface `ui ↛ core` exists to delete, arriving through the navigation layer
rather than through the rules. `Ghost2`'s I AM HERE was the same button a fourth time.

All four now echo and go nowhere. `ScreenGraph.exitsOf` is `emptySet()` for `Assemble`,
`Discussion`, `Vote` and `Ghost2`, and `Flow.autoAdvance` — which already carried every one of
those transitions — is the only way off them. Answering a ring stays an edge: a call is your own
phone's business.

### What is in the tree

- **`ui/.../Meeting.kt`** — `MeetingModel` beside `PanelState` with the editor, the homes and the
  lobby. It holds a check-in, a hand up, a selection and what was last handed over, and it can say
  nothing else. `locked` **compares** the selection with the handed-over vote rather than being a
  flag of its own: the design says the vote is *changeable until the clock ends*, so a flag would
  keep reading LOCKED IN over a row the house has never heard of.
- **`MeetingModel.meetingBegan()`**, called from `FlowModel.arrive` on any of the four ways into a
  meeting. A round holds several meetings and the model outlives all of them; a check-in from the
  first still lit at the second is a phone telling a player they are already standing where they
  have this second been told to walk.
- **`OutsideView` is a separate type, and that is the load-bearing part.** The living see a count;
  only a player outside the system sees who cast what (D-075). A living phone's meeting state is
  now *physically incapable* of carrying attribution — the same discipline as `Observation` vs
  `ObservationView`, applied to the ballot.
- **`ui/.../Countdown.kt`** — every clock is one value. The **window** is read off the auto-advance
  that ends it rather than restated, so a screen cannot say nine seconds and move after fifteen;
  the **position** arrives on `PanelState.secondsLeft`, with the design's drawn moment standing in
  until the house sends one, exactly as `arrivingAt` stands in for a missing cause. It fails
  closed: a screen not in the table has no clock even when the house sends a number for it.
- **Real tap targets.** `TAP_TARGET` is 36 design units — 44pt on the narrowest phone in range
  (375pt wide, scale 1.25, 35.2u, rounded up). The vote rows were 22u, about 27 points.

### Four numbers that were written twice are written once

Same class as Unit 7's three sentences, and one of them had already drifted in the design.

| said twice | now |
|---|---|
| the scan's `6S LEFT` beside `PanelVals.SCAN_LIT = 12` | both off `vals.countdown`; `SCAN_LIT` is gone |
| the ghost meeting's `0:24` beside `meetingLit = 12` | both off `vals.countdown`; `meetingLit` is gone |
| the ghost meeting's `VOTES CAST 3 OF 5` beside the five rows it counts | `OutsideView.cast`, counted off the rows |
| `4 OF 6 CHECKED IN`, on the living's screen and the out player's | one `CheckIn()` composable, because D-104 makes them one gate |

`CountdownTest.theDerivedBarsAreTheOnesTheDesignDrew` is what makes that safe to do: the derived
values have to land on the same twelve-of-twenty and twelve-of-thirty the design drew, or the
derivation is a redesign wearing a refactor's clothes. They do, exactly.

**The discussion screen's six segments are NOT its clock**, and nearly got converted into one. They
are the readiness count the line directly under them spells out — *3 OF 6 READY* — which is why
they read from `counts.ready` and not from the time. Deriving them from the clock would have been
a plausible-looking change that quietly made the bar mean something else.

### The tap-target audit found a fault in its own fix

`everyControlAtAMeetingIsBigEnoughToHitInTheDark` measures real pixels off the rendered tree and
converts back to design units, so it cannot pass by agreeing with a constant. It **asserts** on the
ten meeting screens and **reports without asserting** on the rest of the app, because the
springboard and the host-setup screens were drawn to a different brief and bringing them up is a
design pass rather than an overnight edit.

The reported list is worth a morning: `‹` back chevrons at 24u across six host-setup screens, the
saved-home and nearby-home rows at 26u, `HOST WITH THIS HOME` / `EDIT THE PLAN` / `RENAME` /
`DUPLICATE` at 26u, the storey chips at 19u, the lobby's `INSIDERS` and `YOUR ONE LINE` rows at
19u and 26u, `SLIDE TO OPEN` at 30u, and the springboard tiles at 5u — that last one is not a real
5u control but a zero-height overlay node, and somebody should look at what it is.

Every one of those is a **light-field, host-setup** control pressed in a lit room, which is the
argument for leaving them; the meeting screens are pressed in an unlit one by someone who may not
speak, which is the argument for the ten that are asserted.

### Injection log — eight bugs written, watched to fail, reverse-replaced

Each was reverted with a targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`.

| injected | caught by | XML |
|---|---|---|
| `tapTarget` stops enforcing a minimum | `MeetingInputTest` | `tests="12" failures="1"` — *"Vote/Resident — \"MARCUS YOUR VOTE\" is 22u, under 36u"*, and nine more |
| LOCK IN walks to the result | `MeetingInputTest`, `ScreenGraphTest` | `tests="12" failures="3"` — *"one phone's press walked to the result expected:<Vote> but was:<Tally>"*; `tests="1" failures="1"` — *"Vote — the screen reaches [Tally], the graph says []"* |
| …and the graph updated to agree with it | `MeetingTest` | `tests="9" failures="1"` — *"Vote publishes a way onward. LOCK IN — the ballot is read when the window closes expected:<[]> but was:<[Tally]>"* |
| the check-in advances the house's gate | `MeetingInputTest`, `MeetingTest` | `tests="12" failures="1"` — *"Assemble: the phone advanced the house's gate"*; `tests="9" failures="1"` — *"one phone's press moved the house's counts … present=5"* |
| a `LaunchedEffect` ticking the vote clock | `MeetingInputTest` | `tests="12" failures="1"` — *"Vote ran its own clock: 0:38 was redrawn as something else"* |
| the result screen publishes attribution | `MeetingDisclosureTest`, two tests | `tests="4" failures="2"` — *"attribution reached a screen a living player can be on … expected:<[GhostMeeting]> but was:<[Tally, GhostMeeting]>"* |
| the clock's window written down separately from the thing that ends it | `CountdownTest` | `tests="7" failures="4"` — *"Scan counts down 45s and moves after 10000ms expected:<10> but was:<45>"*, *"the scan bar moved off the design expected:<12> but was:<3>"* |
| the new-meeting reset removed | `MeetingTest` | `tests="9" failures="1"` — *"Calling kept the last meeting's check-in"* |

**Two of these taught something.**

1. **The LOCK IN injection was run in both directions on purpose.** Retargeting the button alone is
   caught by `ScreenGraphTest` (it reads the graph off the screens) and *not* by `MeetingTest` (it
   reads the declared graph). Updating `ScreenGraph` to agree with the retarget silences
   `ScreenGraphTest` and is caught by `MeetingTest` instead. Unit 1's lesson — a guard that can
   only catch a set getting smaller is half a guard — so both halves were made to fail by name
   before either was trusted.
2. **One assertion was reordered because its message was uninformative.** The check-in injection
   fired `onNodeWithText("4 OF 6 CHECKED IN").assertExists()` first, so the failure read as a
   missing node rather than as *the phone advanced the house's gate*. The negative is asserted
   first now. A guard that fires on the right bug with the wrong sentence sends the next person
   looking at the layout.

### Decisions taken (minor, inside the established design language) — flagged, not silent

- **`VoteScreen`'s KDoc claimed the opposite of D-075 and of its own screen copy.** It said *"not
  voting is an abstention rather than a Skip — which matters, because ties resolve to Skip"*, over
  a body reading `NOT VOTING COUNTS AS A SKIP`. D-075 ratified the Skip reading and reversed the
  GDD paragraph the comment came from. Corrected, with a note saying which way it was wrong —
  leaving a comment that contradicts the button underneath it while rewriting that button would
  have been worse than either.
- **`PanelButton.onClick` is nullable now**, the fault Unit 6 flagged on `RowButton` and left at
  twenty-five call sites. A button with no handler no longer publishes a click action. It is what
  lets a spent control keep its place and lose its press, which the four echo controls all need,
  and it changed no graph edge — the four handler-less call sites went nowhere already.
- **`PanelButton` also gained the 36u minimum**, so every primary control in the game clears 44pt.
  `DeviceLayoutTest` and the simulator both confirm nothing collided.
- **A spent control is dimmed, not removed** — YOU ARE HERE, YOU ARE READY, LOCKED IN, all at
  border `Faint` / ink `Dim`. The lobby's gated LIGHTS OUT set the precedent: a control that
  vanishes on being pressed takes the thing the player just aimed at out from under their thumb.
- **`MeetingModel.sample()` arrives with MARCUS chosen and not locked in**, the same shape as
  `LobbyModel.sample()`'s line — typed and not handed over. It is what the design drew, and it puts
  both halves of the control on screen at once.
- **SKIP is a row like the others**, tappable, dashed, and lighting when held. It was drawn as an
  inert dashed row; restraining nobody is a vote and the design says so in the words beside it.

### Flagged for a person — not escalations, but somebody should look

- **The vote window is 60s in the flow table and 45s in the GDD.** `gdd.md:412` and `:1006` both
  say *Vote — 45s (lobby setting)*; `Flow.autoAdvance` uses `60_000`, which Unit 1 wrote as a
  presentation placeholder. Balance numbers are out of this unit's scope so **neither was touched**
  — but the countdown now *derives* its window from the table, so the vote screen currently counts
  down out of 60. Whoever settles the number changes one line and both the clock and the bar
  follow. The discussion's 90s matches the GDD already.
- **A playtest walk now waits out the meeting's real clocks.** With the four controls no longer
  navigating, the line takes 8s + 9s + 90s + 60s + 15s to walk unattended. That is what a round
  actually does, and the cheat picker still jumps anywhere — but somebody walking the port by hand
  will notice the discussion.
- **`Notice` still has a DISMISS button that navigates to the discussion**, which is the same shape
  as the three this unit changed. It was left alone because Unit 7 already flagged the bigger
  question — whether a house notice is a screen the meeting stops at or a banner over it — and
  changing what its button does before that is answered would be guessing twice.
- **The springboard reports a 5u clickable node** in the tap audit, on `Home`, `Banner` and
  `EgressWidget` for both roles, unlabelled. It is not a 5u control anybody drew; it is worth ten
  minutes to find out what publishes it.

### Looked at

Six meeting frames rendered to PNG and read whole (`ui/build/screens/meeting-*.png`), plus the id
sweep. The ghost meeting is pixel-identical to the design with its bar now derived — twelve lit of
thirty over `0:24`, three cast rows bright with the mark, two STILL DECIDING at the lower step. The
scan is identical too: `6S LEFT` over twelve of twenty. The vote screen's rows are visibly taller
and the held row still reads as the design drew it. YOU ARE HERE and LOCKED IN read as spent rather
than as broken, which was the thing worth looking at rather than asserting.

Playtest variant built and launched on the simulator. It opened on `Boot` and fell through to
`Perms` on its own. Whole screen checked: status row level with the Island, nothing under the
cutout, `REQUEST MOTION ACCESS` — now 36u tall — clear of the home indicator, the PLAYTEST chip in
place. **It says nothing about this unit's work**: reaching a meeting needs taps the simulator path
cannot make, so the instruments here were the render harness, `MeetingInputTest` against the real
input stack, and `DeviceLayoutTest`.

## Unit 9 — the light signature, on every surface that names a Subroutine (D-106)

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `bf24629` — *"The phone says how much light it is about
to make"*. Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=866, failures=0, errors=0** across every module and both
targets, up from 863 — 3 new (`LightSignatureTest`, desktop). `DeviceLayoutTest` green across its
112 renders, both lints green, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`.

---

### ⚠️ THE TREATMENT — this is the thing to review

D-106 says a Subroutine's light signature is knowledge a player holds **in advance**, so that
Residents can plan a dark route. It was on none of the three surfaces it names. The sentence form
`SCREEN STAYS DARK` had been tried on the springboard widget and removed for how it read, and the
fact went out with the sentence.

**What is there now is a mark: three cells, one per rung of the ladder, lit up to this
Subroutine's own.** One cell for DARK, two for MEDIUM, three for BRIGHT.

```
   DARK  ▮        MEDIUM  ▮▮        BRIGHT  ▮▮▮
```

Four properties, and each is doing work rather than decorating:

1. **Unlit rungs are not drawn at all**, so the mark *emits in proportion to what it describes*.
   The bright mark is the brightest thing in its row and the dark mark is nearly nothing. On a
   screen whose governing rule is *minimise lit pixel area*, a legend spending the same light on
   every value would be saying "dark" in a bright voice.
2. **Constant width wherever it appears**, so on the work order it forms a column that reads DOWN
   the list. Choosing a route is a comparison between rows, and the last column is the only one
   that stays aligned when the names differ in length.
3. **Nothing on the ladder is zero cells.** The device has no off, and a phone emitting nothing is
   a phone whose player has been revoked — so an absent mark is not a value. That is what lets the
   two LOCKED rows, which name nothing on purpose, carry no mark without accidentally claiming
   their Subroutine is dark. Same fail-closed direction as the redaction schema: the failure mode
   is *my mark did not appear*, never *my mark said something I did not mean*.
4. **It is a mark, not a word, everywhere except one place.** The key at the foot of the work
   order is the only place in the game the mark is spelled out — on that screen because that is
   where the deciding happens, and not on the springboard, which is the screen you glance at while
   walking.

**Four surfaces. Three are D-106's; the fourth is mine and is flagged below.**

| surface | what it shows |
|---|---|
| the work order, every assigned row | its own mark, in a column of its own past the room and the marker glyph, at the row's own intensity — a completed row's light fades with the rest of it |
| the work order's foot | the key: `LIGHT  DARK ▮  MEDIUM ▮▮  BRIGHT ▮▮▮` |
| the NEXT SUBROUTINE widget | the mark inside the line the widget already had, after the marker glyph. The widget does not grow and the glance stays a glance |
| both Subroutine screens | one fixed slot, top right, where the sentence used to be. HANDSHAKE one faint cell, PARITY CHECK three bright ones — the two ends of the ladder in the same place |
| the caught screen | **not one of D-106's three.** Flagged below |

**Two small copy decisions inside the treatment, both flagged:**

- **`SubBright`'s trailing `LIT` word is gone.** It was the header's `SCREEN STAYS LIT` said a
  second time on the same screen; with the mark in the header the fact is in one place, in the
  slot it occupies on every Subroutine screen.
- **In the key the word comes before its mark**, which is not a preference. A mark keeps a
  constant width while only its lit cells are drawn, so with the mark first the space *after* it
  varies with the value: BRIGHT ends up welded to its own word while DARK floats free of one, and
  the eye starts binding each mark to the word on its right — reading the row as `LIGHT = one
  cell`. This was rendered both ways and looked at; word-first puts the constant gap where the
  binding is. The first render is what caught it.

### Where the light values came from

**Every value on screen is the design roster's own** (`gdd.md:565`): Replay bright, Short dark,
Jam medium, Sniff dark — the one *short dark*, the cell where a Resident can take a quick one
without becoming a beacon, which is why the fixture's current Subroutine reads DARK. Handshake
dark and Parity Check bright are the roster's too.

**One exception, and it is a ruling somebody owes:** `ARRAY WIPE` is a circuit rather than one of
the roster's ten, and the design rates it nowhere. BRIGHT on that row is this port's reading — the
work *is* the scanning, and the scan screen is a full amber field, one of only three lit screens in
the game. Logged as **E9-2**.

### The guard, and the four bugs written to make it fail

`LightSignatureTest` renders **all 56 screens in both roles** and counts the marks on each against
a table of what D-106 requires. It is a sweep rather than four assertions because the regression
this feature already suffered is *a surface quietly stopping carrying the value*, and an absent
mark looks exactly like a Subroutine nobody has rated — there is nothing on screen to notice.

Counting **per role** is the other half. A signature that differed by role would be a brightness
channel carrying alignment, which is the one thing the whole panel is built to refuse.

| injected | XML |
|---|---|
| the widget quietly stops carrying the mark — the regression that already happened once | `tests="3" failures="1"` — *"8 surface(s) disagree with D-106 about the light signature: Notify/Resident — shows {}, D-106 says {Dark=1}"* and seven more |
| a LOCKED row draws a mark | *"Work/Resident — shows {Dark=2, Medium=3, Bright=2}, D-106 says {Bright=2, Medium=1, Dark=2}"* |
| the mark becomes a brightness channel carrying the role | *"Home/Insider — shows {Bright=1}, D-106 says {Dark=1}"* — **Insider rows only**, which is the shape a leak takes |
| the key loses a rung | *"Work draws the wrong key … expected `{Dark=1, Medium=1, Bright=1}` but was `{Dark=1, Medium=0, Bright=1}`"* |

Each was reverted with a targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`. `git diff` was grepped
for the injection marker before committing.

**The sweep found a surface I had not enumerated.** My first table had the widget on three screens;
the test failed on `Banner`, which draws the springboard too. Four, not three — which is exactly
what a sweep is for and is not a thing anybody would have listed by hand.

### Flagged for a person — decisions I made that somebody should agree with

- **The caught screen carries the mark and D-106 does not name it.** `ScanCaught` is where BEGIN
  and NOT THIS ONE are both live: it is the last moment the choice is still a choice, and a route
  planned on the work order is re-decided there by anyone who walked past somebody on the way. One
  line of code to remove if the answer is no.
- **The mark on `Sub` is nearly invisible, by construction.** One cell at the faint step on the
  darkest screen in the game. That is the treatment being honest about itself, but it is the one
  place where "quiet" and "unreadable" are hard to tell apart on a desk, and it is a dark-room
  question rather than a screenshot one.
- **A player who never opens the work order never learns the mark**, because the key is only
  there. Deliberate — the springboard is a glance and the list is the decision — but it means the
  first mark most people see is the one on the widget, unexplained.
- **The fixture's current Subroutine is SNIFF with the instruction `PURGE THE MEDIA CACHE`**,
  while the roster's Sniff is haptic counting. Pre-existing, not touched, and worth a minute when
  the real Subroutines land.

### Looked at

Desktop render harness, all 56 screens × both roles (`ui/build/screens/*.png`), read whole for the
five that changed. The work order is the one that matters: the light column is legible from the
row above and below it, the done rows' marks fade with their struck-through names rather than
staying at full step, and the completion box on the left is not confusable with the mark on the
right — different column, different shape, different intensity.

**On the simulator, all four surfaces, driven by real taps** through the playtest picker rather
than left on `Boot`: `ui/build/simulator/sh-u9-{work,home,sub,subbright,scancaught}.png` (also
`/tmp/sh-u9-*.png`; `ui/build` is not tracked, so these are local artifacts). Whole screen checked
on each: status row level with the Island, nothing under the cutout, nothing under the home
indicator, the marks well clear of the curved corners, the PLAYTEST chip in its usual place. The
two Subroutine screens side by side are the treatment's best argument — one faint cell against
three bright ones, same slot, same size. **None of this says anything about the game**: the
simulator has no BLE, torch, camera or haptics, and the light signature is a promise about a lamp
this build does not have.



## Unit 10 (batch 1) — the first three Subroutines, and the fake proved from the pixels

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=893, failures=0, errors=0**
across every module and both targets, up from 866 — 27 new (11 in `SubroutineTest`, common, so
×2 targets; 4 in `SubroutineParityTest` and 1 in `ScreenshotTest`, desktop). `DeviceLayoutTest`
green across its now-118 renders, both lints green, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`.

---

### TRIAGE — all ten, done once so later batches do not repeat it

Read off the roster table (`gdd.md:563`) plus the five constraints (`:590`) and the notes at
`:576`–`:588`. **Specified** means: the design says enough to build the *interaction* without
inventing a rule a player can feel. Two things are excluded from "enough" by the charter rather
than by the design — **balance numbers**, and anything that needs a **new Effect** — and where
one of those is the only thing missing, it is said so rather than counted against the design.

| # | Subroutine | Light | Verdict | What is missing, precisely |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Replay** | Bright | **SPECIFIED** · **BUILT** | Nothing for the entry. The *flash* is a timed emission of light — an Effect — and the loop is frozen |
| 2 | **Interrupt** | Medium | **UNDERSPECIFIED** | The sweep is motion over time nobody has authored (an Effect), and *"a generous band"* is a balance number. Remove both and what is left is a static bar with no moment to tap |
| 3 | **Parity Check** | Bright | **SPECIFIED** · **BUILT** | Grid size and *which* regular pattern. Both are visual choices inside the design language, so decided here and flagged |
| 4 | **Sniff** | Dark | **UNDERSPECIFIED** | *"The phone buzzes N times; tap N"* is **two different Subroutines**. Tap N *times* is Handshake with the rhythm removed. Tap the *choice labelled N* puts a numeral on screen, which the bench's own rule calls suspect (*comparing quantities is perception; adding numbers is computation*, `gdd.md:588`). Needs a ruling before it is a distinct Subroutine — and it is the roster's only short dark, so it is the one that matters most |
| 5 | **Deallocate** | Bright | **UNDERSPECIFIED** | *"tap to even them out"* does not say what a tap does — move a dot off the column you touched, add one, remove one. Three different games, and the difference decides whether the answer is unique |
| 6 | **Drift** | Medium | **UNDERSPECIFIED** | Same wall as Interrupt: the drift is authored motion. Also unstated — path, speed, occluder layout, and what radius counts as *"where it is now"*, which is the whole difficulty |
| 7 | **Short** | Dark | **SPECIFIED, blocked on verification** | Nothing about the design: *hold N fingers for two seconds* is stated twice (`:571`, `:582`). What is missing is a way to **look at it** — N-finger multi-touch held for a duration cannot be driven on a desktop render and this unit may not use a phone. Building it would be building something nobody can check |
| 8 | **Signal Trace** | Medium | **SPECIFIED** | Graph size and decoy count are balance (`:586` says difficulty tunes by decoy count). **Node layout is not** — where nodes sit on screen is the entire legibility question at four luminance steps, and the design says nothing |
| 9 | **Jam** | Medium | **SPECIFIED** | Which two shapes, and the step size per tap. The step is a tuning value; the shapes are a visual choice inside the language. **The strongest remaining candidate** |
| 10 | **Handshake** | Dark | **SPECIFIED** · **BUILT** | Rhythm length (a presentation fixture, like every number in `Flow.autoAdvance`). The *buzz* is the house's and needs a phone |

**Six specified, four underspecified.** Built three; the design's own instruction is *"Build
Handshake first"* (`gdd.md:584`) and it was.

**Remaining specified and unbuilt, for the next batch: `JAM`, `SIGNAL TRACE`, `SHORT`** — in that
order. Jam is clean. Signal Trace needs somebody to pick a graph size and a node layout. Short is
a design that is finished and a verification route that does not exist.

---

### ⚠️ THE TREATMENT — this is the thing to review

**Rule 8 says every Subroutine ships with its fake in the same change. The way that is true here
is that there is no fake.**

There is one screen per Subroutine and it takes no role. `SubroutineModel` has no `isFake`,
`PanelActions` passes no role into any of the three new actions, and none of the three screens
reads `PanelState.role`. An Insider's Handshake *is* a Resident's Handshake — one code path,
nothing to keep in step, and no state reachable by only one role. The distinction lives in the
authority's ledger and nowhere a player can stand.

That claim is checked in the strongest form available: **the two roles are rendered through the
identical input and compared pixel for pixel, at every step of the input** — not only at the end,
because a difference that opens halfway through a sequence and closes again is exactly what a
final-state check misses. Pixels rather than semantics, because the language here is four
luminance steps of amber with no hue: a tell in this game is far likelier to be a *shade* than a
word.

#### The second property, and the one that makes the first one hold

**Nothing on the device says whether you were right.** `SequenceEntry` and `ChoiceEntry` hold the
player's own input and **have no field for the answer to sit in** — rule 3's discipline applied to
adjudication rather than to redaction. A Subroutine's pattern arrives as an Effect, the screen
echoes taps, the entry returns as an Intent, and the *server* verifies it (D-042). D-043 is the
one documented exception to server authority and this is not a second one.

It matters more here than it looks. The moment a screen decides for itself that a sequence was
correct, a real Subroutine and a fake need two behaviours, and the difference is on a lit screen
in a dark room. Keeping the judgement on the authority is *what makes one screen serve both
roles.*

`ParityGrid` is the same move for the one Subroutine whose answer is on screen: the generator
flips one cell of a checkerboard and **discards the index before it returns**, so the screen
drawing the grid cannot mark the odd cell however carelessly it is later edited. The answer is not
secret — finding it is the work — but the *judgement* is not the phone's.

#### What is built, and what is honestly the house's

| | built | the house's |
|---|---|---|
| **HANDSHAKE** (dark) | the return: one large target, uniform cells lighting as beats go back | the buzzed rhythm (haptics, and no phone attached) |
| **REPLAY** (bright) | the entry: four dots, a tapped one lights and stays lit, cells count the sequence | the flash (a timed emission of light is an Effect, and the loop is frozen) |
| **PARITY CHECK** (bright) | **all of it.** No timed presentation exists, so the only thing outstanding is the verdict | the verdict |

#### Six treatment decisions, each flagged

1. **Handshake's beat cells are uniform, and this is a real fix.** The ported fixture drew its
   beats at *differing widths* — which reads as long beat, short beat: **the rhythm itself, on
   screen, in the one Subroutine whose whole instruction is *by feel*.** A player who can see the
   pattern is doing a bright Subroutine with the brightness turned down.
2. **Every numeral is gone.** `3 OF 5 RETURNED` and `FOUND 4 OF 7` both sat beside a display
   already carrying the same fact. The bench's own rule: *any subroutine showing a numeral is
   suspect — the display should carry the arithmetic, not the player.* Cells now do, in the same
   grammar as the light mark one row above them.
3. **STOP NOW, on all three, same words, same place, never changing.** Somebody walked in and the
   player is heads-down, lit or blind, both hands occupied, and needs out **without reading the
   screen**. In the dark that is muscle memory. It goes to the work order, where NOT THIS ONE
   goes from the screen before it. It is not a claim about the work — whether anything banked is
   the house's, and the button neither asks nor says.
4. **A sequence hands itself over on its last element; a single choice takes SUBMIT.** The rule is
   *an entry goes when it can no longer change*: a sequence's last beat ends it and a confirm
   would be a second gesture in the dark for no decision, while a single mark can move right up
   until it is sent. The second half is the vote's shape (`choice` / `handedOver` / `locked`) for
   the vote's reason — a mis-touch must not be final in a game whose input vocabulary is
   explicitly no twitch timing and no precise dragging.
5. **The motion budget row is now on all three** rather than on one. It calibrates on entry to
   *every* Subroutine (`gdd.md:928`). It is a readout and **not a draining bar**: there is no
   motion on a desktop render, and a bar animating on nothing — on the one client-adjudicated
   value in the game (D-043) — is the worst possible place for decorative movement.
6. **Parity's filled cells sit at `Amber.Mid`, over roughly 40% of the screen.** That is the
   brightest non-inverted screen in the game and it is deliberate — *a bright Subroutine makes you
   a beacon for its whole duration* is the point of having bright ones. **It is also the one value
   here that is a dark-room question rather than a screenshot one.**

#### Four structural changes, larger than the screens

- **`ScreenId.Sub` / `SubBright` → `SubHandshake` / `SubReplay` / `SubParity`.** The old pair was
  the light ladder used as an identity, and it stops describing anything at the third Subroutine.
  **One id per Subroutine is also what keeps the sweeps honest:** every guard in `:ui` walks
  `ScreenId.entries`, so a Subroutine hiding behind a field on a shared screen would be a
  Subroutine that `LightSignatureTest`, `MeetingDisclosureTest`, `MeterDisclosureTest` and
  `DeviceLayoutTest` never looked at. Cost: nine call sites.
- **The roster is data now** — `enum class Subroutine`, the design's ten with structure and light,
  and the work order and the springboard widget read their light off it. They were literals
  agreeing by hand, which is the arrangement D-106 already lost once. `CurrentSubroutine` no
  longer stores a name and a light beside a Subroutine that could contradict both.
- **`PanelVals.concealed` asks the roster rather than naming a screen.** It named `Sub`, so a
  second dark Subroutine would have shipped with a bright status bar over a screen built to emit
  nothing (D-077), and the fault would have lived in a file nobody opens when adding a Subroutine.
- **BEGIN moved to the actions layer.** Which Subroutine opens is a fact about the card that was
  read, so the screen cannot name a target — the same shape as a tap on the plan opening the room
  under the finger. `Flow.viaActions[ScanCaught]` is **derived from the roster**, so building a
  Subroutine cannot leave its screen an orphan, and an unbuilt one opens **nothing** and the phone
  stays put. `FlowTest` walks all ten and asserts exactly that.

#### The fixture's current Subroutine: SNIFF → HANDSHAKE

U9 flagged it and this is the minute it was worth. The port named `SNIFF` while carrying the
instruction *purge the media cache*, and the roster's Sniff is haptic counting — a name and a
piece of work that were never the same Subroutine. Handshake keeps the **DARK** signature the four
D-106 surfaces already show (so `LightSignatureTest`'s widget expectations are untouched), is the
design's own *build this one first*, and is one of the three with an interaction behind it — so
**BEGIN now reaches the screen it names**, which it did not before.

### The guards, and the six bugs written to make them fail

| injected | XML |
|---|---|
| `SequenceEntry` refuses an element that does not match its position — the model adjudicating | `tests="11" failures="5"` — *"the two entries disagree about how far in they are at step 1 expected:&lt;2&gt; but was:&lt;1&gt;"*, plus four more |
| `ParityGrid` flips a second cell | *"seed 0 breaks the pattern in 2 places, not one: [0, 7]"* and *"some cells can never be the odd one expected:&lt;36&gt; but was:&lt;29&gt;"* |
| Handshake's returned beats draw a step brighter **for Insiders** | `tests="4" failures="2"` — *"HANDSHAKE after 1 tap(s) — 88 pixels differ by role, first at (115,274)"*, and once per step to six. **Not** at zero taps, because no cell is lit yet — the guard is precise about where the tell starts |
| the parity screen adjudicates and prints VERIFIED / MISMATCH | *"PARITY CHECK/Resident after 0 tap(s) says \"MISMATCH\""* — every step, both roles |
| the parity grid marks the odd cell as well as the chosen one | *"after 0 tap(s) the grid carries 1 marks expected:&lt;0&gt; but was:&lt;1&gt;"* |
| a dot tap walks the phone to the work order | *"a tap inside a Subroutine moved the phone expected:&lt;[]&gt; but was:&lt;[Work]&gt;"* |
| SNIFF (unbuilt) borrows `SubReplay` | `SubroutineTest`: *"two Subroutines share one screen expected:&lt;4&gt; but was:&lt;3&gt;"* and `FlowTest`: *"every built Subroutine opens on its own screen, and no unbuilt one opens at all"* |

Each was reverted with a targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, and the sources were
grepped for every injection marker before committing.

### Looked at

`ui/build/screens/`, whole-screen, at 600×1300: the three new screens in both roles, plus six
frames the `ScreenId` sweep structurally cannot reach — untouched and handed-over for each — added
to `ScreenshotTest` beside the lobby's and the meeting's, which exist for the same reason.

The handed-over frames are the ones that needed a person. On HANDSHAKE all five cells are lit,
`RETURNED . WAITING` has appeared in a slot that was already there so nothing moved, TAP has
visibly gone inert while keeping its place, and **nothing on the screen says whether it worked** —
which is easy to assert and much harder to be sure reads as deliberate rather than as a screen
that failed to update. Also checked: the work order's light column still reads down the list with
HANDSHAKE current, and the caught scan now names the Subroutine BEGIN actually opens.

**No simulator run.** Nothing in this unit changes the status row, the insets or the chrome, and
`DeviceLayoutTest` renders all 59 screens against a fake notch on every build — it went from 112
renders to 118 and stayed green. **None of this says anything about the game**: the flash and the
buzz are both the house's, and this build has no house.
