## Afternoon — Unit A2

**Notifications, reworked to the ruled model — the dim becomes a two-member vocabulary.**

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Full `./gradlew check`: **tests=953, failures=0, errors=0** across
every module and both targets, up from 935 — 18 new (`FlowTest` +4 on both desktop and
`iosSimulatorArm64`, `NotificationInputTest` 8→14, `NotificationsTest` 4→7, one `ScreenshotTest`
viewer). Both lints clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green
throughout, and the playtest variant still builds, installs and launches on the simulator.

### What the rework actually changed

U7 built the dim as *a banner is up*. D-118 says the dim is **not a notification style, it is a
two-member vocabulary**, and everything downstream of that had to move.

- **`NotificationKind` carries three rulings, one per field.** `heavy` (D-118: does the house dim —
  exactly two, and `FlowTest` names the pair rather than counting it), `stored` (D-119: does it
  stand on the lock screen until swiped), `heldBy` (D-105: where the thing survives). Five kinds
  now: `Opening` and `Egress` heavy; `Text`, `Unblocked` and `Notice` quiet.
- **`Notifications.arrivals`** replaces `onScreen`'s `when`: which notification is up, **what is
  underneath it** (a dismissal is a move to `Arrival.under`, not a constant `Home`), and **how it is
  presented** — a banner over the panel, or under the clock on the lock screen.
- **`PanelFrame` takes `dimmed` separately from `overlay`.** The two were one argument on the
  argument that they could then never drift apart; D-118 is precisely the ruling that they are
  different questions, so they are two arguments with one answer each and `Notifications.dims` is
  the only place the second is answered.
- **The quiet banner is drawn dark** — black ground, one hairline, the panel's own ink. *"No dim,
  no brightness spike"* is a claim about emitted light, and a quiet notification lighting a bright
  amber block would be the broadcast without the meaning.
- **`Quiet` does not buzz.** D-118 says quiet is *"nothing world-observable at all"*, and in a house
  where nobody may speak a buzz is about as world-observable as a phone gets. Six pockets going off
  at once is the same broadcast arriving through a different sense.
- **The ten seconds live in `Flow.autoAdvance`**, as three rows landing on each arrival's `under`.
  Reusing the house's own timing table means the undim is a transition of the whole panel rather
  than a timer inside a composable — and **`Quiet` has no row**, which the table's fail-closed
  default (a screen with no row waits) already gets right.
- **The lock screen shows what has arrived, under the clock.** Its two hardcoded messages became
  the two stored notifications, single-sourced with Messages; the arrival lands above them; swipe
  **left** takes any of them away. `NotificationsModel` holds what is still standing and can answer
  no other question — see the reflection guard below.

### Two screens rather than two flags

`Quiet` and `LockNotify` are new `ScreenId`s. Every sweep in this module walks `ScreenId.entries`,
so a quiet banner hiding behind a boolean on `Notify` would be a presentation none of them ever
looked at — and both of the guards that caught something below caught it *because* the screen had
an id. `LightSignatureTest` found the springboard's NEXT SUBROUTINE mark missing from `Quiet`'s
expected set the first time it ran.

### The lock screen's dim is the ground, not the glyphs

On a dark-field panel the dim is `alpha` over everything. On the lantern it cannot be: the amber
field **is** the emitted light, and fading black ink over a bright ground looks dimmer to the person
holding the phone and changes nothing in the room. So the ground drops to `NOTIFIED_DIM` and the
arriving notification stays at full amber — the one bright thing on a darkened screen.

### Injection log — eleven bugs written, watched to fail, reverse-replaced

Each was reverted with a targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`.

| injected | caught by | XML |
|---|---|---|
| the dim keyed to a banner being up again | `NotificationInputTest` | `tests="14" failures="1"` — *"Notify is one of the two that dim the house and the panel behind it is at 2.584, against 2.584 for a quiet banner on the same layout"* |
| the dim wrapped in a 400ms tween | `NotificationInputTest` | `tests="14" failures="1"` — *"one frame after the notification arrived the panel was at 8.734 and it settles at 2.584 — the house is fading down"* |
| a ten-second expiry given to `Quiet` | `FlowTest` | `tests="34" failures="1"` — *"Quiet clears itself after 10000ms — a quiet notification sits until it is swiped"* |
| the heavy hold moved to seven seconds | `FlowTest` **(after a fix)** | `tests="34" failures="1"` — *"D-119 gives a heavy notification ten seconds expected:<10000> but was:<7000>"* |
| the lock swipe answering right as well as left | `NotificationInputTest` | `tests="14" failures="1"` — *"dragging the notification RIGHT dismissed it, and right is the way the phone unlocks"* |
| `dismiss` marking a notification instead of removing it | three tests, three files | `FlowTest tests="34" failures="1"` — *"the swipe left it on the lock screen"*; `NotificationsTest tests="7" failures="1"` — *"expected:<[dismiss, getStanding]> but was:<[dismiss, getSeen, getStanding]>"*; `NotificationInputTest tests="14" failures="1"` |
| a `readAt` field on `Notification` | `NotificationsTest` | `tests="7" failures="1"` — *"a field arrived on Notification — if it can say when or whether, D-105 is gone"* |
| a NEW tag on the newest lock screen row | `NotificationsTest` **(after a fix)** | `tests="7" failures="1"` — *"LockNotify/Resident carries a NEW tag: HOUSE . NOW . NEW"* |
| the Egress made a stored notification | `FlowTest` | `tests="34" failures="1"` — *"expected:<[Text, Unblocked]> but was:<[Egress, Text, Unblocked]>"* |
| the lantern fading its glyphs instead of its ground | `NotificationInputTest` **(after a fix)** | `tests="1" failures="1"` — *"the lantern is at 473.96 and stays at 480.26 with the house's opening message on it — the amber field IS the light"* |
| the quiet banner drawn as a bright amber block | `NotificationInputTest` | `tests="14" failures="1"` — *"the top of the phone went from 24.67 to 92.26 when a quiet notification arrived — quiet means no dim AND no brightness spike"* |

**Three passed green, and two of those were real bugs rather than weak tests.**

1. **The seven-second hold went through.** The test compared the table against `HEAVY_HOLD` — a
   value asserted equal to itself, which is the same mistake U7's tap test made and the same fix:
   the number is written out, `assertEquals(10_000, HEAVY_HOLD)`, because ten seconds is the
   design's and not the constant's.
2. **⚠️ A NEW tag was already shipping.** The house thread's newest message has been stamped
   `21:02 . NEW` for as long as that screen has existed — an unread badge in the plainest form
   there is, on the one message in the game whose *words differ by role*. D-105's own guard could
   not see it: `onAllNodesWithText("NEW")` matches a node whose text **is** NEW, written that way so
   MAP A NEW HOME would not trip it, and this NEW shares a node with a timestamp. **The tag is
   gone** — the message is still last in the thread and still the only one at full intensity — and
   the guard now matches the word *inside* a node's text with the one legitimate use excused by
   what follows it (`\bNEW\b(?!\s+HOME)`).
3. **⚠️ The lock screen was being dimmed twice, and the notification with it.** `PanelFrame` dims
   whenever `Notifications.dims(screen)`, which is true on `LockNotify` too — so the frame dimmed
   the whole lantern *including the arriving card*, and the lock screen's own ground-dim landed on
   top of that. The screen was dark for the wrong reason and the notification was never at full
   amber, which is the entire point of the presentation. The frame now dims only for a
   `Presentation.Banner` arrival; the render before and after is in `ui/build/screens/`.

### The no-read-state property, held three ways

D-105 says no read/unread indicator anywhere, ever, and each of the three catches what the others
cannot:

- **Rendering** — every screen, both roles, for UNREAD/UNOPENED/UNSEEN and now a real NEW tag. This
  is the one that catches a mark a player can actually see, and it is the one that missed for a
  unit because of an exact-match.
- **The types** — `Notification`'s fields and `NotificationsModel`'s public surface are named in
  full and compared. This catches the field a badge would be drawn from, a year before the badge.
  Named rather than counted: *no more than seven fields* is satisfied by swapping one out, and a
  swap is exactly what a read flag looks like when it arrives.
- **The model** — `dismiss` removes; there is nothing to mark and nothing that could answer *was
  this one seen*.

### Decisions taken (minor, inside the established design language) — flagged, not silent

- **`Opening` and `Text` are two kinds, not one.** D-118 dims for *the house's opening text
  message*, and a second dim on the second text would spend the first one's meaning. So the opening
  text is heavy and every later one is quiet — which also gives the lock screen its second row and
  Messages its second house line.
- **The heavy two do not stand on the lock screen.** They clear themselves after ten seconds and
  what they were about is in its own home (Messages, the Egress widget); what stands under the
  clock is exactly the quiet stored ones. This is what keeps the design's lock screen at two rows.
- **Both house rows in Messages are drawn identically.** The older one dimmed would be
  indistinguishable from a read mark — the eye reads *bright above, faint below* as attended-to,
  nobody would call it a feature, and that is exactly why it would survive review.
- **`NUMBER WITHHELD` is a second sender**, which the old KDoc said there would never be. It is the
  design's own, on the messages that are about work rather than about the evening, and it is the
  better of the two names for a phone in a dark room.
- **A stored notification prints the time it arrived rather than NOW.** It can sit under the clock
  for twenty minutes; NOW would be a lie by then. The heavy two keep NOW, because a heavy
  notification is only ever read while it is arriving.
- **A notification on the lock screen is read, not opened.** No tap target: the phone is locked,
  the screen has one control and it is SLIDE TO OPEN, and a card that navigated would be an unlock
  nobody performed.
- **`SWIPE_DISMISS` is one distance for both directions.** Two numbers would be two things to learn
  in the dark, and the hand does not know which screen it is on.
- **Four stale "56 screens" counts** in KDocs became "every screen in the game". They were already
  wrong before this unit (60), and a number in a comment beside a sweep that walks `entries` is a
  number that only ever rots.

### Flagged for a person — not escalations, but somebody should look

- **⚠️ The NEW tag above is worth a second opinion**, because removing it is a visible change to a
  drawn screen. What is left marking the newest message in the thread is its position and its
  brightness. If the design wants a recency mark that is not a read mark, it needs a form that
  cannot be read as one, and I did not invent one.
- **The house notice still has a DISMISS button and its own meeting screen.** Unchanged from U7,
  and still the same question: is a notice a screen the meeting stops at, or a banner over the
  meeting? Under this unit's model it is quiet and ephemeral, which is expressible either way.
- **The Perms screen still lists NOTIFICATIONS as PENDING**, and after this unit that reads worse
  rather than better: everything here is the phantom OS drawing its own, and nothing in the app
  asks for or uses the real one. Whether the shipped app ever needs it — a banner while
  backgrounded — is still unanswered, and it is still on a screen telling every player it will be
  asked for.
- **A lock screen row slides off the left edge unclipped.** The banner's departure is clipped by
  `PanelFrame` because it crosses the status band; this one crosses the panel's own left padding
  into the corner radius. It looks right in the render and nothing measures it.

### Looked at

Every screen re-rendered (`ui/build/screens/`), and four read whole: `quiet-resident` (dark banner,
springboard behind at full brightness, status row untouched), `lock-resident` (two stored
notifications under the clock, bright amber field), `locknotify-resident` (field at `NOTIFIED_DIM`,
the arriving card the one thing at full amber — this is the frame that showed the double-dim), and

Playtest variant built, installed and launched on the simulator: opened on `Boot`, fell through to

screens × 2 roles against a faked notch and stayed green.

### Not in this unit

**The loop stayed frozen.** No `Event`, no `Effect`, no redaction-schema row, no `reduce()`. Every
line of this is `:ui` presentation and client-side state: `PanelState` is still flat and inert, the

## Loop — Unit L1

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`5f3930c`**. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`:
**tests=982, failures=0, errors=0**, up from 953 — **29 new** (model 332, core 38, harness 95,
ui 431, platform 79, app 7). `DeviceLayoutTest` green, `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean,
`:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`. **The freeze is back on** at the boundary named below.

**The loop is no longer frozen for one thing: what happens when a Subroutine entry lands.**
E10-1 and E10-3 are closed by implementing D-109 and D-110; D-130 came with them because it had to.

---

### What the wire looks like now

`Event.SubroutineCompleted` → **`Event.SubroutineReturned(at, actor, marker, entered: List<Int>)`**,
and the rename is the ruling rather than tidying. A client asserting a *completion* is a client
grading its own work, which is the one thing the whole Subroutine treatment keeps off the phone —
U10's entry types are physically incapable of holding the answer, so the phone could not honestly
send the old event even if it wanted to.

**One canonical entry shape for all six built Subroutines**, not a shape per Subroutine: an
element sequence, a chosen cell, a finger count, a signed offset and a walked route are all ordered
integers, and the *kind* of work is the client's presentation problem. Six wire types would have
been six chances to write a seventh that leaks.

**`Effect.SubroutineGraded(seat, accepted)`** is the answer. Two fields and deliberately no more —
no `remaining`, no *which elements were wrong*, no attempt count, no reason. A mismatch reports
only that it was a mismatch (`gdd.md:608`), and every extra field is one more thing that could be
generated differently for a fake.

**Redaction rows: `SUBROUTINE_GRADED to LIVING`** — both living classes, and addressed to the one
seat that returned the entry. Narrowing the row to either role is a one-word edit that reads like
a tightening and is a role oracle after a single Subroutine; broadcasting the effect would publish
who is completing work and how often, which is the per-player read the percentage-only meter
(D-103) exists to keep out of aggregate. Both are injected below.

### Rule 1 is the unit

The verdict is constructed **once, outside every branch**, exactly as the cooldown is in `contact`.
Five ways an entry can be refused — never scanned, scanned another card, returned at another card,
a seat the house assigned nothing, an entry already spent — each produce exactly one verdict of the
same shape. Every one of them is a place where `if (…) return Reduction(state, emptyList())` looks
like ordinary hygiene.

**The Insider's verdict is emitted, never omitted.** D-109's two rejected alternatives are both
worse than they look: *say nothing back about a fake* is rule 1's forbidden shape, and *roll a
plausible failure distribution* is a random number generator standing where the rule should be —
legible to the one reader D-047 never covered, somebody watching over a shoulder, who learns that
this player's work always works.

**The one asymmetry is server-side and appears on no screen.** A plain Resident's accepted entry
decrements SystemIntegrity; an Insider's writes nothing (`gdd.md:382`). `Effect.SubroutineGraded`
has no field that could carry it.

### ⚠️ D-130 came with it, and the reason is not tidiness

The meter total is now `seats × METER_PER_SEAT`, not `(seats − insiders) × 7`. Under D-103 the
Insider count can be hidden — drawn at arming, locked, told to nobody — and **a total built from
`seats − insiders` IS that count**, recoverable by division from any absolute meter value that ever
escapes. The percentage-only display rule closes the panel; this closes the arithmetic behind it.

Reachability now rests on **D-129's `K`** rather than on the operand: each Resident gets
`⌈M ÷ (seats − bandMax)⌉ + slack`, computed from public lobby facts only, so the Residents who
actually exist are never fewer than the number the order was sized against. Five per seat puts `K`
at 7 for an eight-seat home — where the old placeholder sat, deliberately, so the number moving is
a decision somebody makes rather than a side effect of this change. **Coefficient and slack are
playtest's** and are named as such. `K` itself is not built: it is drawn at arming, which is L3's.

`SystemIntegrityTest` was rewritten around the property rather than the number. Its sharpest
assertion is new: **arm the same home with every lawful Insider count and the bar must not move.**

### The copy — drafted, register ruled, wording flagged

| | |
|---|---|
| success | **`THE HOUSE GROWS WEAKER`** |
| failure | **`REJECTED . RESCAN THE MARKER`** |

Both are the decision log's own candidates. The register is D-109's and is not a draft: success is
a **damage report**, house-voiced, the house narrating its own decline — ***never*** *"the house
has it"*, because the house is not on the residents' side and must never be made to sound like an
ally. Failure is rejection plus the instruction to re-scan **in place**, and the instruction is
load-bearing rather than helpful: D-110 makes the walk back the price of a wrong answer, so the
line has to say where the player must stand. **The final wording is a build-time call — somebody
should agree with these two strings.** They are named constants (`HOUSE_WEAKER`, `RESCAN`) so
whoever settles it changes one place, not six.

**Three of the six had no return line at all.** Parity Check, Jam and Signal Trace ended on
SUBMITTED and nothing else; they have one now. It is also `fillMaxWidth` and centred — on those
three it sat at the root of the screen's column and rendered hard against the left edge while the
other three had it in the middle, which was visible only in the render.

**Nothing re-arms on a rejection.** The entry is still handed over, so every control is already
inert and stays inert; the only way back to ready is a fresh scan. There is no RETRY and there must
never be one — D-110 rules out the phone re-arming work on its own schedule in the same breath.

**The six screens take a `SubroutineVerdict?` and deliberately NOT `PanelVals`.** Every other
screen that needs anything derived takes the whole thing, which carries `insider` — and handing
that to a Subroutine screen would turn *"no role reaches this file"* from a fact about what the six
functions can see into a convention somebody maintains.

### ⚠️ The differential harness told the truth, and four of its tests had to be rewritten

`with the Insider count fixed no effect depends on who the Insiders are` failed on the first run —
**exactly as its own KDoc predicted it would**, *"the day it fails, a rule has started branching on
identity rather than on population."* D-109 is that day, and the KDoc's instruction was *rewrite
this rather than deleting it.*

The meter genuinely depends on **which** seats hold the role, and it reaches players outside the
system, who see the real bars live (`gdd.md:1014`). So `METER_ASYMMETRY` names that one kind and
subtracts it from **both** runs before diffing. That is not loosening: one withheld line shifts
every later line, so a raw diff of two runs that banked at different moments reports lamps and
meetings as divergent too. Subtracting asks the question that is actually open — *is there anything
OTHER than the meter that depends on who the Insiders are?* — and it is paired with two
complements so nothing is hidden by being filtered:

- **the divergence is asserted to exist** (`without the subtraction the exchange is visibly dirty`),
  so the filter cannot go vacuous;
- **it is asserted to reach no living player** (`the meter asymmetry reaches no living player`).

The role-oracle test that replaces the two retired ones is **sharper than either**: every seat's
verdicts, filtered out of the per-client transcripts, must be byte-identical under a role exchange —
including the two seats that traded. A fake that never failed, a fake graded on a rolled
distribution, a verdict withheld, or a verdict a beat late all show up as a differing or missing
line. It runs on the fixture round and over 150 fuzz rounds.

**The fuzzer now scores sometimes.** It reads the opening entries off the rules and hands over what
was actually asked for about half the time, having scanned about half the time. Without that, the
graded-and-correct path — the only one that moves the meter, and the one D-109's whole asymmetry
hangs off — would never once have run, and every property above would have held over 150 rounds in
which the rule under test never fired. `the generator produces both verdicts` is the coverage
assertion that keeps it honest.

Recording format **4 → 5**: a version 4 recording holds an event this build cannot construct and a
state row missing the field a verdict is computed from. The state row now carries the work order
**answer key included** — omitting `expected` would leave the one piece of state a verdict is
computed from outside the replay guarantee, which is the `ended` omission again in a new costume.

### The injections — thirteen bugs written, watched to fail, reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the Insider's verdict withheld in `reduce()` | **6 tests**, core and harness — *"the Insider's verdict was withheld, changed, or arrived a beat later — a fake that is met with silence is a role oracle after one Subroutine. Expected `<[SubroutineGraded(seat=Seat(index=1), accepted=true)]>`, actual `<[]>`"* |
| grading inverted | **6 tests** — *"the entry the house asked for was graded wrong"*, *"never scanned was graded correct"* |
| the entry is never spent | *"the entry re-armed itself, so an attempt costs nothing"* |
| an Insider banks | *"an Insider's fake advanced the meter (gdd.md:382). Expected `<40>`, actual `<39>`"* |
| the total reads the Insider count again | *"5 seats: the bar moved with the Insider count across band 1..2 — [20, 15], so the hidden count is recoverable from the meter by division"* |
| the schema row deleted | *"Expected `<[Resident/Live, Insider/Live]>`, actual `<[]>`"* — **fails closed** |
| the schema row narrowed to `Insider/Live` | *"actual `<[Insider/Live]>`"* |
| the verdict broadcast to every seat | *"Expected `<[Seat(index=3)]>`, actual `<[Seat(index=0), Seat(index=1), …]>"* |
| the Insider's verdict withheld on the **screen** | *"HANDSHAKE Accepted (handed over) — 337 pixels differ by role, first at (96,297)"* |
| a rejection re-arms TAP | *"HANDSHAKE: Rejected changed the live controls from [1] to [2]"* |
| the verdict survives a navigation | *"REPLAY: a re-scan left the previous attempt's verdict standing"* |
| a screen adjudicates (`RETURNED . CORRECT`) | *"REPLAY/Resident after 4 tap(s) says \"CORRECT\""* |
| one screen drops the line | *"PARITY CHECK/Resident/Accepted never drew \"THE HOUSE GROWS WEAKER\""* |

Each reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, and the sources were grepped for
the injection marker before committing.

**⚠️ One reverse-replace failed and left the tree damaged for two runs.** Deleting the schema row
was written as a replacement with an *empty* string, so the reverse replaced every empty position
in the file and did nothing. The row stayed deleted, the next two injections ran against a tree
that was already broken, and their results were meaningless. Caught by the marker grep and by the
diff being purely additive after the row was restored by hand; the tool now refuses an empty needle
and the three schema injections were re-run from a verified-green tree. **The two invalid runs are
not in the table above** — the entries there are from the clean re-run.

### Looked at

`ui/build/screens/`, at 600×1300: **18 new frames** — all six Subroutines × waiting / accepted /
rejected, added to `ScreenshotTest` beside the lobby's and the banner's, for the same reason. What
needed a person's eye was not the words but the light: whether a longer line at the same intensity
reads as the same slot on the near-black screens as on the lit ones, and whether anything above it
moved when the answer arrived. Nothing does — the slot is fixed height in all three states. The
left-edge alignment on three of the six was found here and nowhere else.

**No simulator run, no phones.** Nothing in this unit changes the status row, the insets or the
chrome, and `DeviceLayoutTest` renders every screen against a fake notch on every build.

---

### ⚠️ THE L3 BOUNDARY — what is spine and what L3 replaces

`GameState.openSubroutines` holds one `OpenSubroutine` per seat: the seat, what was asked, and the
marker it was armed at. **The shape is not provisional** — a seat has one open Subroutine, a scan
arms it, an entry spends it. Everything else is:

1. **Arming draws one placeholder entry per seat**, two integers over four values, derived from the
   round seed and the seat index. Seeded and recorded, and **it does not read `Role`** — which is
   load-bearing, not incidental: an Insider's fake order is drawn and sized by the same rule
   (D-129 makes order length role-independent on both axes), so a derivation that took the role
   would put the answer key itself on the role axis.
2. **That one Subroutine stays open all round, so the spine lets one assignment be completed
   repeatedly** — scan, return, scan, return. The meter is farmable without moving. This is the
   loudest thing L3 closes: advancing the work order is arming's job.
3. **Any card arms whatever that seat has open.** L3 resolves `(seat, card)` to *that player's*
   current Subroutine (D-123) and gives the other case D-124's `NOTHING FOR YOU HERE`.
4. **A re-scan re-arms the same question.** In play the house draws a fresh instance.
5. **`K` is not computed anywhere.** D-129's formula is written down in `SystemIntegrityTest`'s
   assertion and in `METER_PER_SEAT`'s KDoc, and drawn nowhere.

### Escalations

**E-L1-1 · Does an out player's live meter plus the presence plane add up to a role oracle? NOT
DECIDED, implemented the only way D-109 allows.**

D-109's asymmetry has to be visible somewhere, and where it is visible is the meter. Players
outside the system see the real bars live (`gdd.md:1014`), so they see a return that banked and a
return that did not — as an aggregate, with no attribution. **On its own that is nothing.** The
worry is D-111's presence plane feeding D-136's spectator map: a player who is out watches
*performing at room X* open and close, and then watches whether the bar moves. Over a round that is
a correlation, and the thing it correlates to is alignment.

Three things make it less than it looks and none of them closes it: the map is inference rather
than knowledge (D-136), two players can be performing at once, and the out cannot speak. But a
ghost who works out who the Insider is has learned the one fact the whole app is built not to tell
anybody, and *ghosts are in the room*.

What was implemented is the choice that leaks least of the two available: **`SubroutineProgressed`
is emitted only when the meter actually moves.** The alternative — emitting it on every return with
an unchanged value, which would have made the effect stream role-independent in *shape* and kept
the old differential tests passing untouched — is worse: it tells the out that *somebody returned
something that did not score*, which is a per-return count nobody designed and is E10-2's
behavioural channel arriving through the meter instead of through abandonment.

**Cheap now, expensive later**: if the answer is that the out must not see live progress at all,
that is one row of the allowlist. If it is that the presence plane must not feed a surface at the
same time as the meter, that is a design constraint on L3's spectator map, and it is much cheaper
to know before that map is built than after.

**E-L1-2 · A revoked player's correct entry still banks. NOT DECIDED, deliberately not guarded.**

`returned()` does not ask whether the actor is revoked, so a player who is out of the system can
still hand over an entry that moves the meter. In play their phone is on D-134's screen and has no
Subroutine open, so nothing can produce the event — but the *rules* accept it, and the fuzzer
proves it by doing exactly that. Adding `&& !state.isRevoked(actor)` is a state branch rather than
an effect branch and would be safe under rule 1 (the verdict is still emitted, and the seat is
`Out`, so the allowlist denies it either way). **It was not added because it was not asked for**,
and because per-seat round-state gating belongs with whatever answers F-005's other half —
orphaned Subroutines from a revoked player are meant to be auto-satisfied, which is the same
question from the other side.

**E-L1-3 · The two copy lines want a person.** Register ruled, wording drafted. See above.

## Afternoon — Unit A4

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit `1b62f39` — *"The house learns where to gather, and
stops pretending any home will do"*. Full `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1097,
failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** across every module and both targets, read from the test XML. The
same count run against `HEAD` (`c3f14e3`) in a throwaway `git worktree` gives **982**, so this unit
adds **115**. Both lints green, `:ui:boundaryCheck`
still reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green throughout. No Events, no Effects, no schema, no
`reduce()` — the freeze held.

### What it is

The second reserved shape, the gate that needs it, and the number the gate prints beside itself.

- **`MarkerShapes.MEETING` is `u_shape`, and that is a ruling this unit had to make.** D-121
  reserved a second shape without naming which one. The argument mirrors D-120's: the shape is the
  only field of a payload that can say what *kind* of card this is, so it has to be something
  printed — and the roster's U is a couch seen from above, which is the design's own word for
  where a meeting happens (GDD "Couch / meeting area", D-134). It is a letterform like the T, so
  the two reserved cards read as a pair on a printed sheet and neither reads as a marker.
  **Written up as provisional, for ratification, because it decides what is printed on paper.**
- `MarkerShapes.reserved` is a set of two and `registrable` is 42, both asserted in the object's
  own `init` rather than trusted — reserving a shape twice would leave the registrable count
  silently right and the reserved set silently wrong.
- **`HouseMap` carries `meeting` beside `terminal`**, with `MeetingTaken`, `moveMeeting`,
  `forgetMeeting`, and every carrier taking it through: `forget`, `forgetIn`, `renamedRoom`,
  `holdsAnything`, `rooms`, `of`. The one place it differs from the terminal is asserted rather
  than assumed: **a shape is reserved, a room is not** — ordinary markers may share the meeting
  card's room, which is the expected case, not an edge one.
- **`HouseMapText` is version 3** with an `M` row. **`SavedHomesText` is version 3** and refuses
  v1 and v2 *each by name*: a v2 home has no meeting card, D-127 makes one a condition of hosting,
  and there is nothing honest to invent — where a meeting is called is a place in a real house.
- **`HomeReview` in `:model` is the gate** (D-127): one terminal, one meeting card, at least eight
  ordinary markers, with `hosts = markers − 3` beside it. It lives in `model` because both the
  editor and a stored `SavedHome` ask it, and two copies of "eight" would be two copies that drift.
- **`ScreenId.NoTerminal` became `ReviewNeeds`** and names **every** missing requirement at once,
  not the first that fails. A host sent back for a terminal, then a meeting card, then three more
  markers walks their own house three times over a fact the app knew on the first pass (D-126).
  Each keeps the copy that says *where the thing belongs* — PUT IT SOMEWHERE AWKWARD for the
  terminal, PUT IT WHERE EVERYONE FITS for the meeting card, and WHY EIGHT for the markers.
- **`MeetTaken` and `MeetRemove` mirror `TermTaken`/`TermRemove`**, and the marker sheet's
  terminal row became a `ReservedRow` drawn twice — the token is passed in rather than switched on
  inside, so a third reserved card cannot get there by reusing the terminal's mark.
- **HOSTS UP TO N** at REVIEW, on Save Home and on Home Detail; the lobby draws a guidance line
  when more have joined than the home is sized for. **It never blocks** — LIGHTS OUT and HOST WITH
  THIS HOME are asserted to still be live controls in the tests that read the line.
- The playtest deck is eight cards and carries **both** reserved cards *and a duplicate of each*,
  so a walk on a phone reaches both refusals rather than only both placements.

Never `git checkout`; the files were uncommitted throughout. Read from the test XML, never an exit

### Three injections came back ASLEEP, and two of them became tests

**1. `moveTerminal` could delete the meeting card and nothing failed.** Every test written for the
new card walked the *meeting card's* own operations, so a shared-field bug reached through the
terminal's. A host would meet it by moving their terminal and finding the house had nowhere to hold
a meeting. `the terminal and the meeting card do not disturb each other` now walks both directions
and fails with *moving the terminal lost it expected:`<Room(name=LIVING)>` but was:`<null>`*.

**2. The scan readout's token could be deleted and nothing failed — for BOTH reserved cards.** This
is U5's own lesson arriving one card later: the tests read the *line* and the printed id and never
the mark, so a scan could have shown the host a result with no glyph on it at all, on the one screen
whose job is matching what the app says against the paper in their hand. Two tests now read the
token off the rendered screen; deleting either fails with *could not find any node that satisfies
… 'U'* / *… 'T'*.

**3. Not a guard: the first `saveLabel` injection.** Renaming `missing` without truncating it
changed nothing, which is a fact about the injection rather than about the code. Re-aimed
(`.take(1)`) and it failed by name.

### ⚠️ Two harness faults, both of which left an injection in the tree

Recorded because the recovery is the interesting part and because the next unit will use the same
script. **A reverse-replace whose anchor is empty cannot restore anything**, and a deletion spelled
as `old → ""` has exactly that shape; **a replacement string that already appears elsewhere in the
file restores the wrong line, or refuses and leaves the injection standing.** Both happened, both
were caught by reading the file rather than by trusting the script, and both were undone by
targeted reverse-replace — never `git checkout`, since the tree was uncommitted throughout. The
harness now asserts both sides are non-empty and that the replacement is absent before writing.

A third fault was quieter and is the one worth keeping: **the runner reported a build failure as
"nothing failed"**, because it inferred "asleep" from an empty result set. `:app:jvmTest` does not
exist — `app` has only iOS targets — so two deck injections looked like sleeping guards for a whole
sweep. It now distinguishes "no XML at all" from "XML with no failures", and says which.

### Decisions taken — flagged, not silent

- **`u_shape` is the meeting card, and this is the unit's one real ruling.** D-121 reserved a
  second shape without naming it. Written up as provisional for ratification, exactly as `t_shape`
  was, because it decides what is printed on paper and paper cannot be patched.
- **A v2 saved-homes file is refused rather than migrated**, and the refusal names the meeting card.
  This deletes any home saved by this morning's build — today that is a simulator container and
  nothing else, but it is the second time this format has done it and the second time is worth
  noticing.
- **`ScreenId.NoTerminal` was renamed rather than joined by a sibling.** A screen named after one of
  three requirements is a screen that tells a host about the terminal and says nothing about the
  other two.
- **The gate screen's copy for each requirement was written here**, in `ui`, as an exhaustive
  `when` over `HomeReview.Missing` rather than as strings on the model. A fourth requirement is a
  compile error on that screen rather than a blank box on the screen a host is stuck on.
- **The sample homes went from four markers to eight.** They predate the gate; a list where two of
  three rows read HOSTS UP TO 1 is a fixture teaching a host the wrong thing about their own list.
  Eight is deliberately the minimum rather than a comfortable number.
- **The lobby's guidance is the host's phone only.** A client has never been sent the home's marker
  count, and there is no wire field for it — adding one is an Effects change and the freeze is on.
- **`show(model)` in `HostSetupInputTest` now hands over the model's own lobby.** It did not, which
  was harmless while no host-setup screen read one and would have silently defeated every capacity
  test by rendering a different lobby than the test set up.

### Flagged for a person

- **The meeting card has no in-round behaviour yet.** D-121 says a meeting is *called* by scanning
  it; this unit builds the card, the map, the format and the gate, and nothing that answers a scan
  of it during a round. That is loop work and the loop is frozen.
- **`MarkerShapes.MEETING` needs ratifying before anything is printed.** See above.
- **Story 4.5's Array Wipe designations are still not built** — U5 flagged it; D-122 has since
  ruled that the house draws the three stations itself at arming, which may mean there is nothing
  for a host to designate and the story is smaller than it looks. Somebody should close that.
- **`HouseMapStore` and `HousePlanStore` still have no callers** — units 4 and 5 flagged it; three
  stores for two pieces of data, and the map is now a third version deep inside `saved-homes.txt`.
- **`IntegrityWidget`'s KDoc is still stale after D-103** — units 1, 3, 4 and 5 flagged it.
- **The Maps list rows still show only `FL . RM`.** Capacity is on the detail screen and the save
  screen; whether the list itself should carry it is a design call nobody has made.

### Escalations

**None raised.** Every question this unit met was already answered — D-120 and D-121 for the
reserved set, D-122 and D-123 for what markers are, D-125, D-126 and D-127 for the gate and the
guidance. The one thing not written down was *which* shape the meeting card is, and that is
recorded above as a provisional ruling rather than as an escalation because D-121 delegated it by
reserving a shape without naming one.

### Not in this unit

No Events, no Effects, no schema entries, no `reduce()`. Nothing in `core` or `harness` was
touched. The freeze held.
