## Loop — Unit L2

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`ec752fb`** — *"The house calls everyone in, hears the
room, and decides who stays"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1144, failures=0, errors=0**
across every module and both targets, up from 982 — **162 new** (model 416, core 61, harness 96,
ui 483, platform 79, app 9). `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` still
reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green. Recording format **5 → 6**.

**The freeze lifted for one thing: the meeting, from the card being scanned to the lights going
out.** D-104, D-117, D-121, D-133, D-134 and D-135 are implemented; D-102's takeover timing and
D-075's attribution rule are honoured where the code touches them.

---

### The wire, end to end

**Nine events, and one of them is a rename that is a ruling.** `VoteCast(voter, target)` — a single
shot, *changeable until the clock ends* — is gone, replaced by **`VoteSelected(voter, target)` plus
`VoteLocked(voter)`**. D-117 superseded that model, and splitting the event is what makes the split
real: **`VoteLocked` carries no target**, so it can only convert what was already transmitted, and
a client cannot lock a vote the couch never saw it make. The rest are `MeetingCheckedIn`,
`ReadyToVoteDeclared`, `DiscussionClosed`, `VoteWindowClosed`, `TallyHalfwayReached`, and
`MeetingCalled` gaining a `MeetingTrigger`.

**Twelve effects, and the count is the point rather than the cost.** Every split below is a
permission set that differs, and `EmitSchema`'s own rule is that a kind is permitted to a class in
full or not at all — *if two classes need different content, that is two kinds*. Merging any pair
would be redaction by nulling fields under another name.

| the fact | the living get | the out get |
|---|---|---|
| a meeting was called | `MeetingOpened` — the ring, and who called it | `StandAndWalkIn` — no phone call (D-134) |
| the gate | `CheckInProgressed` — one gate, both screens (D-104) | the same |
| your own ballot | `VoteHeld`, addressed to one seat | — |
| the vote as it happens | `VoteProgressed` — **locked seats**, a count | `VoteSelectionShown` — the selections (D-117) |
| the outcome | `MeetingResult` — who, and nothing else | `MeetingResolved` — the ballot with names on it |
| the room's decision landing | — | `RestrainedTakeover`, at T-half, one seat |

**`MEETING_OPENED` narrowed from `LIVING + OUTSIDE` to `LIVING`**, which is a real disclosure change
and is on the **round-state axis only**: both roles hear the same ring, both roles get the same
walk-in. `EmitSchemaTest` asserts that neither row splits on role, because a meeting's very first
push splitting on role would be an alignment tell delivered before anybody had said a word.

### Rule 1 is the vote, and the shape is `contact`'s

D-117 makes READY irrevocable, so **a later tap from that seat is refused** — and the whole weight
of this unit is in what *refused* means. It does not mean nothing is emitted. `Effect.VoteHeld` and
`Effect.VoteSelectionShown` are constructed **once, outside every branch**, and what they carry on a
locked ballot is the selection the house holds rather than the one the finger just landed on. The
authority re-asserts.

Written the tempting way — *if the ballot is locked, return no effects* — the player is left looking
at a row they lit themselves that the house has never heard of, and the absence of the answer is the
answer. `an accepted tap and a refused tap have the same shape` is the assertion that keeps it
honest: same kinds, same order, only the field values differ.

### Two refusals live in the admission gate, and one of them was a judgement call

D-133's *no meeting during an Egress* and D-121's *one meeting at a time* are refusals **above** the
rules, where D-066 put the first two. `RefusalReason` gained three: `EgressRunning`,
`MeetingAlreadyRunning`, `WrongMeetingPhase`. Each passes that enum's entry condition — *a player
standing in the dark could have worked it out without their phone* — an Egress is audible and dims
every panel, and a meeting is the whole house standing in one room on one phase.

**`WrongMeetingPhase` is the boundary of that enum and is flagged as such at the declaration.** The
next reason added will probably be over the line, because the ones that suggest themselves — *you
are revoked*, *you already locked*, *that target is already out* — are alignment leaks in refusal
clothing. Those stay in the rules and come back as an effect the same shape as a success.

### The Restrain lands at the halfway mark, not at the buzzer

`GameState` gained **`restrained`, a second list, never a second use of `revoked`** (rule 9), which
closes the gap `RoundState.Out` documented as live — *nothing in GameState stores a restrained
player* — the exact way that comment prescribed. It also gained `newlyRevoked`, which is D-134's
split between the newly Revoked (STAND AND WALK IN, long haptic) and the couch.

**The seat leaves the round at T-half rather than at the tally**, and that is the design's own
sequence: *the group holds them; the house deauthorises them moments later* (`gdd.md:1009`). Doing
it at the buzzer would put an out client class on a phone whose owner is still looking at the
living's result screen — and the attribution list meant for the couch would land there.

### The four transitions stopped being timers

D-134's E8-2 recorded the gate closing, the talk ending, the window closing and the ghost walk-in as
**authority pushes the loop must carry**. They were `Flow.autoAdvance` rows with delays on them, and
they are now `Flow.housePushes`, keyed on the `MessageKind` that carries each one — so a push
waiting on a message nobody emits **fails the build** rather than stranding a player on a screen.
That is the strongest thing `ui` can say about the authority without importing it, and
`:ui:boundaryCheck` still reports `ui clean`.

**The bench needed a stand-in and it is named as one.** With no house attached the meeting now stops
at the check-in screen and stays there — correct, and unwalkable. So `FlowHost` takes
`standingInForTheHouse`, which the debug root passes and nothing else does, and
`withNoHouseAttachedTheMeetingWaitsAtTheGate` is the test that keeps the default honest.

**`Countdowns.windowOf` now has two sources**, and the split is the ruling: where a timer on this
phone ends the screen the window is still read off it (the scan's ten seconds, unchanged), and where
the house ends it the window is the design's own number. The meeting's four were never really the
flow table's — it was borrowing 90 and 45 rather than owning them.

### Copy — the build-time call D-117 left open, taken

| | |
|---|---|
| the vote button | **`READY`**, replacing LOCK IN |
| once cast | **`VOTE CAST`** |

D-117 named READY as the rename candidate *for symmetry with the discussion's READY TO VOTE* and
left the final wording to build time. This is that call. Not *YOU ARE READY*, which is the
discussion's button on the previous screen. Both are named constants so settling it is one edit.
**Somebody should agree with these two strings.**

### The four controls reach the house, and none of them names a seat

`MeetingModel` publishes a **`MeetingRequest`** — seat-free — and `asIntent(seat, names)` attaches
the seat one layer out. That is not ceremony: *intents are attributed by connection, never by a
client naming itself*, and a screen that could construct `Intent.CheckIn(Seat(4))` is a screen that
could check somebody else in, which is the only cheat in this game that is remote, undetectable and
requires no physical act.

The client half of D-117 came with it: **a locked phone stops echoing.** Echo reflects a player's
own input, and a tap the house will refuse is not input the game accepted — a row lit and then
snatched back is worse than a row that never lit.

### Injections — nine, each reverted after the named test failed

| # | the bug | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | a tap after READY returns quietly (rule 1's forbidden shape) | `a tap after READY is refused with the house re-asserting`, `an accepted tap and a refused tap have the same shape` — `tests=61 failures=2` |
| 2 | the gate closes one player short | `the gate will not close a player short`, `a player who is out holds the gate open like anybody else` +2 — `failures=4` |
| 3 | the takeover fires at the buzzer | `the takeover reaches the losing seat and only at the halfway mark`, `a meeting that restrained nobody takes nobody over` — `failures=2` |
| 4 | auto-lock banks the FIRST selection | `the buzzer banks the latest selection and silence is a Skip` — `failures=1` |
| 5 | neither unanimity closes early | `unanimous readiness opens the ballot…`, `readiness is unanimous among the living`, `unanimous locks close the window early` — `failures=3` |
| 6 | the living are given the live selections | model `live selections reach the couch and no living class`; harness `a player who is never out never receives an out-only message` — `failures=2` + `2` |
| 7 | the couch is denied them (the other direction) | model, same test — `failures=2` |
| 8 | the takeover widened to the living | model `the takeover reaches exactly the restrained seat`; harness completeness — `failures=2` + `1` |
| 9 | the gate closing becomes a device timer again | ui `theMeetingsOwnTransitionsAreTheHousesAndNotTimers`, `theReadinessControlsWalkNowhere`, `nothingWalksToAScreenOnlyTheHouseCanPush`, `withNoHouseAttachedTheMeetingWaitsAtTheGate` — `tests=483 failures=7` |

### ⚠️ Injection 6 caught a test that agreed with itself, and the fix is the finding

The harness's *never-out seat receives no out-only message* property listed two kinds as literals —
`SubroutineProgressed` and `MeetingResolved` — and did not grow when the meeting did. So the first
version of the fix **derived** the list from `EmitSchema`. Injection 6 was then run against it and
**caught nothing**: widening a row to a living class removes that kind from the derived list, so the
property went on passing over the leak it was pointed straight at.

**A test derived from the thing it is testing agrees with itself.** That is exactly why
project-context describes the allowlist as *independently required, not a second opinion* — and a
harness that re-derives the allowlist is a second opinion after all. The list is written out again,
as a decision, with `the out-only kinds are exactly the ones named` as its completeness check
against the table.

That check earned its keep on its first run: it named **`StandAndWalkIn` and `RestrainedTakeover`**
as out-only kinds nobody had covered. Both are addressed to one seat as well as permitted only to
the out classes, so a living player was never going to receive one by accident — but *never by
accident* is the argument this project keeps refusing, and the row is what actually denies them.

### Three existing tests were widened because a Restrain now reaches state

Not loosened — corrected, and each in the direction the code moved. `ClientTranscriptTest`'s
`revokedSeats()` and `FuzzPropertyTest`'s `everRevoked` called a Restrained seat *living* and then
asserted a living seat receives no attribution, against a client the allowlist was correctly
treating as out. Both are `outSeats` / `everOut` now, reading **both lists**.

The third is sharper than it was. `the round-state axis actually moves during the round` required
every mover to be a **Resident** going Live → Out, which was true only while the fixture's one way
out was a Revoke; a Restrain lands on whoever the ballot lands on, so an Insider moves too. It now
asserts that **every mover moves along the round-state axis and never along the role axis** — a
class list holding two roles would mean a seat's alignment changed mid-round — plus that at least
one Resident still goes out, so the common case cannot quietly stop being exercised.

### Recording format 5 → 6

A version 5 recording holds an event this build cannot construct (`VoteCast`), and its state rows
are missing the ballots a tally is computed from. The state row gained the whole meeting — caller,
trigger, phase, check-ins, hands up, **every ballot with its selection and lock mark**, and the
pending Restrain — plus `newlyRevoked`, `restrained` and the Egress flag. Live selections are the
one thing at a meeting only a player outside the system may read, which is exactly why they belong
in an authority-side state row: a recording that stopped short of them could not tell a replayed
vote from a different one that reached the same result.

The fuzzer walks a **whole meeting about half the time** — everybody checks in, because D-104's gate
does not close a player short and a meeting one short would stall in CheckIn and make every later
phase a refusal, which is a fuzzer quietly testing the admission gate and nothing else. Inside the
vote it stays absurd: seats select more than once, some press READY, some tap again afterwards.

---

### ⚠️ Escalations

**1. THE TIE — built provisionally, awaiting the owner's ruling. Read this one.**

The charter said to escalate a tie if the design and log were silent. **They are not silent**, and
what is built follows them: `gdd.md:413` and `:1007` both say *most votes is Restrained; ties resolve
to Skip*, and D-075 leans on that same sentence — *combined with ties already resolving to Skip, the
whole weight of inaction sits behind restraining nobody*. So the tally is **plurality with Skip as a
candidate, and any tie at the top Restrains nobody.**

**The word that is unresolved is D-131's.** It says *at parity the Insiders can always block a
Restrain, so **Restrain is majority-decided***. Read as *a strict majority of votes cast*, the rule
is different from what is built, and the difference is real: at seven seats with two Insiders voting
together and five Residents splitting three-to-two, plurality Restrains the leader and strict
majority Restrains nobody. Under strict majority a divided Resident bloc could almost never Restrain
anybody, and Restraining every Insider is one of D-131's own two Resident win conditions — which is
the argument for reading *majority-decided* as describing the consequence (you need a Resident
voting majority in practice) rather than replacing the stated mechanic.

**That is a reading, not a ruling.** It is one line — `tallyOf` in `model/Meeting.kt` — and one
test. If the owner means strict majority, say so and it changes in a single place.

**2. The Egress is a stub, and two things are owed.** `GameState.egressRunning` exists because D-133
is a rule about *meetings* phrased about the Egress, and a meeting engine that could not express
*the card is inert, the report is not* would have the gap hidden inside it rather than named.
**Nothing writes it** — reachable only through `withEgress`, whose callers are tests. L5 owes a
writer, and D-133's *a reported Revoke pauses the Egress timer and never resets it* needs a timer to
pause. Neither is here.

**3. `MeetingResult` and `MeetingResolved` both reach the out.** The couch gets the outcome and the
attribution as two messages. That is two kinds because the living may have one and not the other,
and the redundancy for the out is the price. Recorded rather than resolved.

**4. Who counts as unanimous for READY TO VOTE.** Built as *unanimous among the living*, because a
player outside the system has no READY TO VOTE control on their screen (D-134 lists what the couch
watches and readiness is not on it). If the intent was unanimity across everyone at the meeting, the
gate is one word in `readyDeclared`. `ReadyProgressed` is denied to the out classes for the same
reason — the fail-closed direction, deliberately.

**5. The vote window is not wired to the lobby setting.** D-117 makes 45 seconds host-changeable and
the lobby draws the control, but the wire does not carry it. `Countdowns.MEETING_WINDOWS` holds the
default; when the setting is really sent, the house's number arrives with the phase and replaces it.

**6. Presence, arming and the spectator map stayed frozen.** No `Egress` mechanics, no presence
plane, no BLE map. The one place this unit touches an out player's screen is D-134's push, and it
carries nothing about where anybody is.

---

## Loop — Unit L3

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`559f48d`** — *"The lights go out for real, and the
house deals everybody a night's work"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1192, failures=0,
errors=0, skipped=0**, up from 1144 — **48 new** (model 426, core 86, harness 97, ui 493, platform
81, app 9), read from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck`
still reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green throughout. Recording format **6 → 7**.

**The freeze lifted for one thing: round start.** D-103 as amended, D-114, D-122, D-123, D-128,
D-129, D-130 and D-132 are implemented; D-112, D-118, D-124, D-125 and D-135 are honoured where the
code touches them.

---

### The two Insider numbers, and why the draw sits above the rules

`Event.RoundArmed` carries **`chosenInsiders`** — the host's setting, public, on every phone's lobby
row — beside **`insiders`**, the hidden draw. The whole of D-129 is in the difference: work-order
length is computed from the first and never from the second, so **order length is
count-independent by construction** rather than by a rule somebody keeps applying. The differential
harness rewrites `insiders` and never touches `chosenInsiders`, which makes *"the order did not
move with the draw"* a property the harness proves rather than one a test asserts about itself.

**`insidersFor` is called above `reduce` and its result is recorded; the stations, the active set
and the work orders are re-derived inside `reduce` from the same seed.** The asymmetry is
deliberate and has one reason: **the harness's whole method is rewriting the arming event's Insider
list.** A count drawn inside the rules could not be exchanged between two seats, and the harness
would lose the only handle it has on role. Stations and orders are role-independent by
construction, so nothing needs to rewrite them.

### ⚠️ The band amendment, and the one place revision 29 disagrees with itself

`InsiderBand.of` pins **5 and 6 seats to exactly 1** (D-103 as amended). From 7 the band resumes.

**Revision 29's own words are ambiguous above eleven seats and this build took the conservative
reading.** It says *"from 7 seats the band resumes, `1..⌈seats/4⌉`, **unchanged**"* — but the band
it is calling unchanged is revision 21's `max(1, ⌊seats/6⌋)..⌈seats/4⌉`, whose floor is 1 up to
eleven seats and **2 from twelve**. The literal formula and the word *unchanged* therefore differ
at 12+ seats: literal gives 12 → 1–3, unchanged gives 12 → 2–3. **What is built is the second**,
because the amendment says it changed the band only at 5 and 6, and because the engineering posture
is *designed for 6–10* where the two readings are identical. **Somebody should say which.** It is
one line and one test.

### What the house draws, in one place

| | |
|---|---|
| the count and who holds it | clamped by the band **at the draw**, not only at the setting |
| Spares, Rack, Disposal | three distinct ordinary markers, **re-drawn every round** (D-122) |
| the active set | `Balance.activeMarkers`, stations included; **the rest sits dark** (D-123) |
| every seat's work order | `Balance.orderSize` lines, same length for every seat and both roles |
| every Insider cooldown | already running, at **half** (D-132) |

**`Balance` is new and holds every playtest-owned number in one place** — `MINIMUM_SEATS` (D-128,
5), `METER_PER_SEAT` (D-130, 5, moved out of `core`), `ORDER_SLACK` (D-129), `STATIONS` (3),
`ACTIVE_MARKERS_PER_SEAT` (2), `REVOKE_COOLDOWN` (60 ticks, the lobby's own settings row). Three
layers read them and none may hold its own opinion: the rules draw against them, the lobby's gate
reads the party floor off them, and the reachability test computes both sides from them.

**⚠️ `ORDER_SLACK` is 1, which moves L1's eight-seat `K` from 7 to 8.** L1 parked `K` at 7
deliberately, where the old placeholder sat, so that the number moving would be *a decision
somebody makes*. This is that decision and it is recorded as one: with slack 0 the term is inert
and `K` is exactly the ceiling, so one entry nobody ever completes — a Revoked player's orphans,
an order whose tail nobody reached — puts the bar out of reach for the rest of the round. **Playtest
owns it.**

### The work order, and the two types that keep it honest

`WorkOrder` holds `OrderEntry` rows with the answer key, the anchor and the dependency graph.
`asLines()` narrows to **`OrderLine`**, a sealed pair: `Known(index, subroutine, done)` and
**`Blocked(index)`** — a known unknown that is a *different type* rather than the same type with
its name nulled (rule 3). The order sent to a phone is always as long as the order the house holds,
because an order that shortened while work was blocked would make its own length a tell (D-114).

**`blockedBy` holds earlier indices only, so the graph is acyclic by construction** rather than by a
check somebody remembers to run. The only edge source ruled today is D-123's self-dependency: anchors
are handed out round-robin through a per-seat shuffle of the active set, so an order no deeper than
the home is a flat menu and an order deeper than it wraps, and the second visit to a card is blocked
by the first. **A dependency graph between *different* Subroutines is not drawn, because nothing
rules one** — the mechanism is general and only that one edge is populated.

**D-112 is why the light signature is not on the wire.** `model` gained `SubroutineKind` (the ten),
`ui`'s roster gained `kind`, and `Subroutine.of(kind)` is total — `SubroutineRosterTest` holds the
bijection in both directions, which is what makes it total rather than lucky. No field arrives, no
effect grows a member, the schema gains no row for light.

### Two effects, two rows, and the one that had to be its own kind

- **`WorkOrderIssued(seat, lines)` → `LIVING`, addressed to the one seat.** Both living classes and
  it must never be narrowed: an Insider's fake order is drawn by the same rule at the same length,
  so a row narrowed to Residents is a role oracle delivered by the allowlist — an Insider whose
  phone shows no work at all.
- **`OpeningMessage(seat, haptic)` → `LIVING + OUTSIDE`, one per seat.** A separate kind from
  `MessageDelivered` rather than a flag on it, for A2's reason: *a second dim on the second text
  would spend the first one's meaning*, and a flag is a thing somebody passes `true` for by
  accident. **It carries no words** — the client holds the copy, as it holds the roster — and its
  haptic is **Short**, because D-135 closes the long one to five events and this is not among them.

**It is emitted on every return, not only when it changed.** Sending it only when something moved
would make its *presence* a second verdict, arriving beside the real one and readable without being
told — rule 1's forbidden shape wearing an optimisation's clothes. Injection-verified below.

### The cooldown is a rule, not a field

D-132 would have been another `egressRunning` if nothing read it. `RevokeArmed` now refuses while
the cooldown is running and starts a full one when it arms — **silent in both outcomes**, so the
branch is on state alone and rule 1 is untouched. A seat with no cooldown row has no ability to
arm, which is the fail-closed direction.

**⚠️ It aged three test files, and two of them were passing for the wrong reason.** `RulesTest`'s
differentials compare effect streams that are identical whether a revoke lands or not, so with every
`RevokeArmed` silently refused inside the opening stretch of peace they went on passing over a round
where **nobody was ever revoked**. `contacting a revoked target is indistinguishable from
contacting a live one` was comparing two live targets. It has a positive control now
(*"the fixture never actually revoked anybody"*), and every Revoke in `RulesTest`, `ReplayTest`,
`MeetingTest` and the fixture round is scheduled off the state's own `readyAt` or off `READY`.

### The fixture round and the fuzzer both walk the order now

Both used to read the opening state and scan one card forever. A seat can complete each line once,
so that banked once per seat in the first few events and never again — putting **every banked entry
before anybody had been revoked**, and the meter only reaches a player who is out. That is how
`a role exchange leaks nothing but the meter` went vacuous: *"no round in 150 diverged on the meter
at all."* Both now replay what they have built so far through the **admission gate** and ask for the
line the house would actually open next, and both moved their clocks to a stride wider than half a
cooldown so their Revokes land.

### Recording format 6 → 7

A version 6 arming has no marker list, so it replays into a round with no work in it. The state row
gained the whole draw — **orders with their answer keys**, stations, lit markers — plus every
cooldown, and `open` gained the order line it came from. `Transcript.escape` now escapes the
**comma** as well: lists are comma-joined and a marker id is external input read off a card, so one
containing a comma would have forged an element in the arming event's marker list. Same fault the
pipe had, one separator down.

### The injections — sixteen bugs written, watched to fail by name, reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the band consulted for the setting but not the draw | **4** — *"5 seats, seed 1: drew 2 Insiders — at this party size two of them reach parity before the first meeting"* |
| the order sized off the hidden draw | **3** — *"order length moved with the count D-103 hides. Expected `<7>`, actual `<8>`"* |
| the stations drawn with replacement | *"seed 0 drew the same card twice: [m3, m3, m3]"* |
| a blocked entry actionable anyway | **3** — *"at step 0 the actionable set was [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7]"* |
| the opening message to the first seat only | *"the opening message missed a seat"* |
| the opening message given the long haptic | *"the opening message joined D-135's closed set of five"* |
| cooldowns at full instead of half | **3** — including *"the fixture never actually revoked anybody"* |
| `WORK_ORDER_ISSUED` deleted from the allowlist | **4** — *"Expected `<[Resident/Live, Insider/Live]>`, actual `<[]>`"* — **fails closed** |
| the same row narrowed to `Resident/Live` | **2** — *"actual `<[Resident/Live]>`"* |
| the order broadcast to the house | *"Expected `<[Seat(index=3)]>`, actual `<[Seat(0)…Seat(5)]>`"* |
| the desk arming below the minimum | *"a party one short of the minimum armed a round"* |
| the lobby gate ignoring the lines | **3** — *"four of six was enough"* |
| the lobby gate ignoring the party floor | **4** — *"4 seats armed a round with every line in"* |
| two roster rows claiming one kind | **6** — *"Sniff resolves to 0 roster rows"* |
| the order re-issued only when it changed | *"a wrong entry came back a different shape from a right one. Expected `<[SubroutineGraded, WorkOrderIssued]>`, actual `<[SubroutineGraded]>`"* |
| a scan opening work wherever the player stands | *"scanning m1 opened work the house did not put there"* |

Each reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, and the sources were grepped for
the marker before committing.

### ⚠️ One came back ASLEEP, and it is the finding

**Deleting the work orders from the state row failed nothing.** That is the `ended` omission in a
new costume, one draw further on: **the answer key is what a verdict is computed from**, and
`Effect.SubroutineGraded` carries a boolean, so a build whose draw had quietly changed — different
Subroutines, different cards, different answers — replays byte-identically. `replay detects a work
order that was drawn differently` is the guard: mark every line done in the state rows, change
nothing else, and the replay must refuse it. On a build that does not render the orders there is
nothing to mark, and the test fails on that rather than passing quietly. The injection was re-run
against it and fails by name.

**Two harness faults, both recorded because the next unit will use the same script.** A replacement
that *contains* its own original defeats the reverse's own guard and leaves the injection standing —
it did, and it was undone by targeted reverse-replace after reading the file. And **the runner
reported a compile error as a sleeping guard**, because a build that never compiled leaves the
previous run's XML on disk; it now refuses any run whose output carries an `e:` line. A4 recorded
the same class of fault and this is the third member of it.

### Decisions taken (inside the design language) — flagged, not silent

- **`ORDER_SLACK = 1`**, moving the eight-seat `K` from 7 to 8. See above.
- **`ACTIVE_MARKERS_PER_SEAT = 2`** and `REVOKE_COOLDOWN = 60` ticks. The second is the lobby's own
  settings row read as a number; the first is invented and is playtest's.
- **The band's floor above eleven seats** — the conservative reading of revision 29. See above.
- **The lobby's third waiting line, `NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS`,** drawn on the host's screen as well as on
  a client's. A host three people short was looking at a inert button and a lobby that said nothing
  about why, on the screen where an evening either starts or does not.
- **`LobbyModel.everyLineIn` became `readyToArm`**, with `waitingFor()` beside it. Two conditions
  that fail in different words, and the party is named first: a lobby three people short that said
  WAITING FOR EVERYONE'S LINE sends the host looking for a phone that is not the problem.
- **`GameState.withOpenSubroutine` now creates a row.** It refused to, which was right while arming
  drew one per seat; the round now opens with nothing open and the row is created by a scan.

### Flagged for a person

- **⚠️ The band above eleven seats needs a ruling.** One line, one test.
- **⚠️ `ORDER_SLACK` and `K` want an owner.** The number a player counts on their own screen.
- **The lobby's `SUBROUTINES EACH 7` row is now visibly wrong.** `Balance.orderSize` is computable
  from public lobby facts, so that row could draw the real number; it was left alone because it is
  one of the unwired settings rows U6 already flagged and changing it is a visible screen change.
- **Nothing renders a work order yet.** The effect and its narrowed type exist and are guarded; the
  screens that draw a work order still draw `PanelState`'s fixture. Wiring them needs the host→client
  push, which does not exist for any effect (L2 left the same seam).
- **`armingFor` has no production caller.** LIGHTS OUT walks this phone to ARMED; the gate is real,
  the event is real, and the socket between them is transport work.
- **The Egress cooldown.** `InsiderAbility` has one member because Revoke is the only built ability.
  D-132 says *every*; when the Egress lands it brings its own duration and this enum gains a row.

### Escalations

**E-L3-1 · The band's floor above eleven seats — revision 29 disagrees with itself.** See above.
Conservative reading built, both readings identical at 6–10 seats.

**E-L3-2 · A re-scan does not re-draw the question.** L1's boundary listed it. D-139 and D-140 put
the instance parameters — band position, speed, phase, path, hidden duration — on the **scan** and
re-draw them on every re-scan, which is scan work rather than arming's, and none of those four
Subroutines is built. `OpenSubroutine.expected` is therefore fixed per entry for the round. Recorded
rather than guessed.

**E-L3-3 · F-005's other half is still open.** Orphaned Subroutines — a Revoked player's, or a chain
whose blocker can no longer be completed — are meant to be silently auto-satisfied so the bar stays
winnable. `ORDER_SLACK` buys margin for it and is not a substitute. Same standing L1 left it in.

### Not in this unit

**Egress, presence and the spectator map stayed frozen.** No `egressRunning` writer, no presence
plane, no BLE map. The one-lines stayed where they were: `LobbyDesk` holds them in memory through
arming, nothing moved them into `GameState`, and nothing needed to.

---

## Loop — Unit L4

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`2ab47b3`** — *"The house answers every card, and starts
writing down where people stand"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1226, failures=0,
errors=0, skipped=0**, up from 1192 — **34 new** (model 432, core 112, harness 99, ui 493, platform
81, app 9), read from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck`
reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green. Recording format **7 → 8**. No `ui` change at all.

**The freeze lifted for two things: in-round scanning and the presence plane.** D-109, D-110,
D-111, D-121, D-123, D-124, D-136, D-139 and D-140 are implemented or extended; D-071, D-072,
D-068, D-114 and D-129 are honoured where the code touches them. Egress and the BLE spectator map
stayed frozen.

---

### The silence that was doing the talking

A scan emitted **nothing at all**, in both outcomes, and the file said so proudly: *"deliberately
silent in BOTH outcomes … NOTHING FOR YOU HERE, held one layer below the copy."* That was rule 1's
forbidden shape wearing a citation. A scan that opened work produced a state change and no message;
a scan that found none produced neither — so **the existence of the message was the answer**, the
phone had nothing to distinguish an empty card from a failed radio, and anything downstream counting
messages could separate the two without ever seeing a payload.

`Effect.ScanAnswered(seat, opened: SubroutineInstance?)` is the fix and the shape is the whole
ruling. **One kind, one allowlist row, one message per scan**, content differing and existence
never — `VoteHeld`'s nullable selection is the same decision one system over. Two kinds were the
tempting alternative and are the same leak one layer out: two rows, and the day somebody narrows one
of them *registered but not mine* becomes separable from *this opened work* from the permissions
table alone.

**D-124's other vocabulary never reaches the rules.** Unregistered paper is refused above the gate,
produces no event, and is reported to nobody (D-072) — which is what keeps the two answers from
collapsing into the detector D-124 refuses.

### The question moved onto the scan, and the answer moved with it

E-L3-2, closed. `OrderEntry` **lost `expected`**: an entry is an *assignment* now — this Subroutine,
at this card, behind this dependency — and the *question* lives on `OpenSubroutine` for exactly as
long as one instance lasts. `instanceFor` draws both halves from one stream at scan time and
`SubroutineInstance` is the narrowed view that carries the parameters and is physically incapable of
carrying the answer.

**The re-draw hangs off `mintId()`.** Seeded, recorded, monotonic (rule 4), so the second scan of a
card cannot draw what the first drew and a replay draws exactly what it drew before. A clock reading
would have worked and is worse: two events at one tick draw one question, and the tick's resolution
silently becomes a game rule. **The mint happens on every scan and not only on the ones that find
work** — minting inside the branch would put a record of *which scans found something* into the one
piece of state a replay compares byte for byte.

**⚠️ The parameters and the answer are both still stand-ins, and the grading is equality.** D-139
grades a tap *inside a band* and D-140 *within a hit radius* — tolerances, not equality — and
neither Subroutine is built. What is real is the plumbing: the parameters ride the scan, they
re-draw, they reach the client narrowed, the answer stays behind. The four unbuilt Subroutines slot
in by giving their numbers meanings; **the tolerance grading is owed when they do.**

### The presence plane exists and reaches nobody

The window opens on a work-opening scan and closes three ways: hand-over, STOP NOW, and scanning
somewhere else. **Closing spends what was armed**, which is D-111 read literally — *there is no
half-returned sequence to resume, so the next scan restarts* — and without it D-110's cost, standing
at the marker in the dark, is refunded by walking off with a live question.

**`Effect.PresenceChanged` is named in `kindOf` and given no row in `ALLOWED`.** That is the
fail-closed hinge used deliberately rather than by accident, and it is written down in the table
where its row would be, because an absent row is indistinguishable from a forgotten one. It is also
**addressed to the performing seat**, so the two denials are independent: a row written by mistake
still could not carry seat 3's window to seat 5, and an addressing widened by mistake still delivers
to nobody. When the map lands the row becomes `OUTSIDE` and not one class more.

**It carries no reason and must not grow one.** STOP NOW, a step away and a hand-over are one fact
here; a `reason` field is an abandonment record, and an abandonment record is the behavioural
channel separating a real Subroutine from a fake.

### `routeScan`, above the rules beside `armingFor`

Three cases. The meeting card calls a meeting (D-121) — routed through the work plane it would
resolve against an order that cannot contain it, answer NOTHING FOR YOU HERE, and present as *the
meeting card is broken*. Every other registered card is work. **The Terminal routes to work too**
and answers NOTHING FOR YOU HERE until its screen exists: it is a registered card, so D-124's
registered vocabulary is the honest one, and sending it to the unregistered alert would tell a
player the T card in their hand is not part of the home, which is false.

It takes the `HouseMap` and lives outside `reduce` for `armingFor`'s reason: the rules have never
held house geography and this is not the change to start with.

### The Revoked seat's gate, and why it is not rule 1's shape

A seat outside the system opens nothing. **The emitted shape does not move** — the same two effects,
the same fields, and the null that NOTHING FOR YOU HERE already uses — and the allowlist declines
`SCAN_ANSWERED` to an out class anyway, so this is the second of two denials rather than the only
one. Round-state is publicly observable (D-068).

### Recording format 7 → 8

`PerformanceEnded` is an event a version 7 build cannot construct. `open` gained the parameters
beside the answer, `orders` lost `expected`, and the state row gained the whole presence plane —
which is the **only** artifact that can hold it, since no client ever receives one. Without that row
a build whose windows never closed would replay clean forever.

**The fixture round and the fuzzer both had to learn where the answer lives.** Both read `expected`
off the work order, which no longer has one; both now read it off the instance the scan just opened.
A generator that kept reading the order would hand over the answer to a question the house never
asked and every entry in the round would grade false — this project's recurring failure, one draw
further on.

### The injections — twelve bugs written, watched to fail by name, reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the null answer withheld | **6** — *"a scan that found nothing came back a different shape from one that found work"* |
| `PRESENCE_CHANGED` permitted to `LIVING` | **3** — *"a presence fact was delivered to a phone"* |
| presence broadcast, row to `OUTSIDE` | **5** — including the differential harness: *"a seat that stayed in the round saw the meter asymmetry"* |
| the instance seeded off the entry, not the scan | **2** — *"the re-scan re-used the first instance's parameters"* |
| the meeting card routed to work | *"Expected `ScanRouting.Meeting`, actual `ScanRouting.Work`"* |
| the Revoked-seat gate removed | **2** — *"a Revoked seat's scan opened work"* |
| STOP NOW not spending the arming | *"the arming outlived the walk"* |
| the question dropped from the state row | *"the state row does not carry the question the scan drew"* |
| presence dropped from the state row | *"a build whose windows never closed replays as correct"* |
| `SCAN_ANSWERED` narrowed to Residents | *"Expected `<[Resident/Live, Insider/Live]>`, actual `<[Resident/Live]>`"* |
| presence reported only on an opening | **2** — shape differs |
| a nothing-scan leaving the arming standing | *"the arming followed the player"* |
| the hand-over not closing the window | *"the window survived the hand-over"* |

Each reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, with the runner refusing any run
whose output carries an `e:` line and the reverser refusing a replacement that contains its own
original — both faults L3 recorded, both scripted this time rather than remembered.

### ⚠️ One guard was covering half of what it claimed

`replay detects an instance that was drawn differently` tampered the whole `open=` field at once. It
passed — but it would have passed on a build whose state row carried **only the answer**, because
the question and the answer come off one stream today and a build that changed one changes the
other. The coverage was real and accidental, and it would have lapsed silently the day somebody
split the draws. It runs **two tampers now, one segment each**, and the question's half fails by
name when the field is removed.

### Decisions taken (inside the design language) — flagged, not silent

- **A scan is a placement, so a scan that finds nothing spends what was armed elsewhere.** D-136
  says a performer's own scan is ground truth; the corollary is that you cannot be armed at a card
  you are not standing at. Nothing rules this explicitly. It is the conservative direction — the
  alternative lets a live question be carried around the house — but somebody should say so.
- **`PerformanceEnded` spends the arming**, for the same reason, off D-111's *the next scan
  restarts.*
- **The instance is `List<Int>`, one canonical shape for all ten Subroutines**, exactly as
  `expected` already is: a wire type per kind is ten chances to write an eleventh that leaks, and
  *which* Subroutine is the client's presentation problem (D-112).
- **`PARAMETER_LENGTH = 3`, `PARAMETER_VALUES = 16`** — invented, and playtest's the moment a real
  Subroutine reads them.

### Escalations

**E-L4-1 · The presence row records the CARD, not the room name — and the charter asked for the
room.** D-111 says the phone reports *performing at room X*; D-123 says *a card is a place, not a
container*, and the rules have never held house geography. Recording the card keeps `core` free of
host-authored room names and keeps the arming event's marker list as it is; the card → room
translation is one `HouseMap` lookup and lives with the map, which is where the frozen consumer
lives too. **If the spectator map wants the room name inside round state, `Event.RoundArmed.markers`
has to carry marker → room and the recording format changes again.** Recorded rather than guessed,
and flagged because it is a deliberate deviation from the unit's wording.

**E-L4-2 · The screen still says NOT YOURS / *"Nothing of yours opens here"* (D-071), and D-124's
vocabulary is NOTHING FOR YOU HERE.** `ScreensWork.ScanBadScreen` predates revision 27. The two say
the same thing and the ruling named different words; changing them is a visible screen change and
copy is a build-time call (D-109's verdict lines are in the same position). One string, one test.

**E-L4-3 · The grading is equality and D-139/D-140 rule tolerances.** See above. Owed when Interrupt
and Drift are built, along with meanings for the parameter numbers.

**E-L4-4 · `Transcript.escape` does not escape the colon, and marker ids can contain one.** The
payload alphabet is QR alphanumeric minus SPACE, which includes `:` — and the state row's `open=`
and `presence=` fields use `:` as a sub-separator around an escaped marker id. Two different states
could therefore render identically, which is the same fault the pipe and the comma each had. It is
a state-row injectivity hole rather than a forged-event hole, and it is one line in `escape` plus
one in `unescape`. Not taken in this unit because it moves every recorded state row.

**E-L3-3 is still open**, unchanged: orphaned Subroutines are not auto-satisfied.

### Not in this unit

**Egress and the BLE spectator map stayed frozen.** Nothing writes `egressRunning`, no map, no
BLE. **`ui` was not touched** — nothing renders a scan answer or a work order yet, and wiring them
still needs the host→client push that L2 and L3 both left as a seam.

## Loop — Unit L5

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`7f2c1c1`** — *"The house calls somebody, and two
people who cannot speak have to answer it together"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1264,
failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**, up from 1226 — **38 new** (model 440, core 135, harness 104, ui
495, platform 81, app 9), read from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean,
`:ui:boundaryCheck` reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green. Recording format **8 → 9**.

**The freeze lifted for one thing: the Egress lifecycle.** F-001 as ratified, D-131, D-132 and
D-133 are implemented; D-076, D-109, D-118, D-121, D-124, D-135 and D-136 are honoured where the
code touches them. F-002's amendment is **recorded and not built** — Isolate does not exist.

---

### The stub became the state, and both debts it named are paid

`GameState.egressRunning` was a `Boolean` with no writer, and its KDoc named what was owed when the
Egress landed: *something must set it, and D-133's pause needs a timer to pause.* It is
**`egress: Egress?`** now — type, both nodes, the deadline, the pause mark, the held Sync Pulse
offers, the lockouts — and `egressRunning` is derived from it, so D-133's admission-gate rule reads
exactly as L2 wrote it and there is no second field that could disagree about whether a countdown is
on every widget in the building.

### The nodes are chosen above the rules, for `routeScan`'s reason

**`egressFor` sits beside `routeScan` and `armingFor`.** Choosing two markers in non-adjacent rooms
needs the home's *shape*, and the rules have never held house geography. Its answer rides
`Event.EgressFired` and lands in the recording as an input, exactly as `RoundArmed.insiders` does.

**Non-adjacency and the small-home degrade are ONE rule, not a rule and an exception.** The pair is
*the two markers whose rooms are farthest apart* in the adjacency graph, and non-adjacent is simply
a distance of two or more. An ordinary home gets a non-adjacent pair by construction; a home too
small to have one gets the farthest thing it has — the owner's *small homes are lawful; never fail
to fire* — with no second code path that only runs in a house nobody tested in. Distances come from
`Floor.neighboursOf`, which is E4.9's *adjacency without a line of geometry*; different floors are
unreachable and therefore rank as maximally far, which is both true and correct.

**It declines exactly one thing:** a round that cannot supply two *distinct* markers. That is not
the degrade (D-127 floors a home at eight markers) — it is an Egress physically impossible to
contain that would look exactly like one that was not, and the Residents would lose to a shape they
could never see.

### The Sync Pulse: the phone hands over taps, the house decides everything else

**`Event.SyncPulseReturned` carries no node.** Where a player is standing is what the house recorded
when they *scanned* the node's card — D-136, *a performer's own scan is knowledge* — and a phone
naming its own node is the cheapest cheat in the game: sit on the couch, claim the Landing, contain
an Egress from a chair. `PerformanceEnded` carries no marker for the same reason and this is the
same decision. It carries no verdict either: a boolean *"I was on the beat"* is the phone grading
its own work.

The beat schedule is arithmetic on the tick the Egress fired at — **house-wide**, so concurrent
pairs forming a minute apart are still in time with each other, and nothing has to be recorded for a
replay to grade the same taps the same way. A pause does not move it.

**Five ways to fail, one shape for all of them.** Outside the system, not at a node, serving a
lockout, off the beat, on the beat with nobody opposite: every one produces
`Effect.SyncPulseAnswered(seat, held)`. Written the tempting way — silence unless the beat was good
— the absence reports *the house does not think you are at a node*, to a living phone, which is the
presence read D-111 split the two planes to close.

### ⚠️ The tap window exposed a constant that could not exist, and the tick rate had to be named

**`Balance`'s durations silently assumed one tick per second.** `REVOKE_COOLDOWN` was the bare
literal `60` for the lobby's `REVOKE COOLDOWN 60S`; nothing needed finer than a second, so nothing
noticed — and at `FixedTimestep.SUGGESTED_STEP_NANOS`'s 20 Hz that literal is a three-second
cooldown.

The Sync Pulse cannot be written at that floor **at all**: its tap window has to be a fraction of
its beat interval, and at one tick per second the only interval leaving room for a window is slower
than any heartbeat two people could catch.

**So `TICKS_PER_SECOND` is named, and it adopts 20 Hz.** `SUGGESTED_STEP_NANOS` is deliberately
named apart from `FixedTimestep.of` so that adopting it is a visible act; this is that act, and
**somebody should confirm it.** Every duration in `Balance` now reads through it, `REVOKE_COOLDOWN`
included — so the number on screen has not changed and the number in ticks has.

**The window has a hard edge and it is now guarded.** At half the beat interval every possible tap
is inside *some* beat's window and the grading stops grading: a player hammering the screen passes,
a player who never looked up passes, and the Sync Pulse is a four-tap button with a delay in front
of it. Nothing about that failure is visible — every test still passes and every pair still
contains. `Balance.syncPulseIsGradeable()` holds the line and `the tap window cannot swallow the
whole beat` asserts it, because the person who widens the window to be kind is not reading the KDoc
when they do it.

### One clock for the whole house, and it is not a `Cooldown`

The panel's *SHARED WITH THE OTHER INSIDER* is `GameState.egressReadyAt` — **one value, not a row
per seat.** `Cooldown` is keyed on a seat, so a house-wide clock held that way would be N rows that
must move together and *shared* would become a convention somebody maintains. It starts the round
already running at half (D-132), and no effect carries it: every phone learns the shared clock
restarted from `EgressOpened`, which every phone receives.

### The pause, and the arithmetic that is the whole of D-133

`deadline += (now − pausedAt)`. Fired at 60 with a 120-second timer, called in at 80 with the clock
reading 100, resumed ninety seconds later with the clock **still reading 100**. A reset would read
120, which makes the report a free Egress cancellation and every Egress ends the same way. Held
offers do not survive the meeting: everybody walked to the meeting area, and a beat held across it
would pair with somebody who has not been in the room for ninety seconds.

### Five effects, and the audiences are why there are five

| the fact | who gets it | why |
|---|---|---|
| `EgressOpened` | everyone, per seat | D-118's second dimming event; a dim reaching fewer than everyone is a beacon (D-076). Long haptic, D-135's first. **The actor included** — a phone that did not dim is the loudest tell in the design |
| `EgressHeld` | everyone | the countdown stopped, or started; the whole party is in one room when it fires |
| `SyncPulseAnswered` | the one seat that tapped | rule 1's shape; broadcast it publishes who is at a node, live |
| `EgressContained` | everyone | **no seats, no node, no attribution of any kind** |
| `EgressSucceeded` | everyone | the terminal fact — Insiders win outright (D-131) |

**`EgressContained` carries nothing and that is the design of it.** Naming the pair would mint two
players an alibi at the one moment in the round when everybody is moving and nobody may speak.

**A running Egress outlives its Insiders** (D-131): nothing in the type refers to who fired it and
nothing cancels it on a Restrain. That is a rule expressed as an absence, so it is asserted rather
than trusted — `restraining every Insider does not stop a running Egress`.

### ⚠️ The round is NOT ended here, deliberately

`EgressSucceeded` is emitted, recorded and replays; `GameState.ended` is untouched. The win
conditions as a set, and the screens that follow, are L6's — splitting them across two units would
put the round's most consequential transition in two places. Said plainly rather than left as an
omission: **today an uncontained Egress emits its fact and the round keeps running.**

### `ui`: the two node-naming surfaces cannot disagree, structurally

`Notifications.EGRESS_NODES` was a constant that both the alert and the widget read, which proved
only that two fixtures agreed with each other. The pair is round data now — `PanelState.egressType`
and `egressNodes`, pushed, with the port's drawn pair as the fallback, exactly `secondsLeft`'s
arrangement. Every drawing site goes through **`PanelVals.arrival`**, the one door onto the arrivals
table, so a composable cannot reach past it and get the fixture. `EGRESS . BEACON` was hard-coded on
the widget and is the house's word now, which matters precisely because Beacon and Tether are
mechanically identical: hard-coding one looks right in every screenshot and is wrong in half of all
Egresses. `ui` still never sees `core`; `PanelState` is still flat and inert.

### Recording format 8 → 9

Three events a version 8 build cannot construct, and `EgressFired` carries the type and both nodes
as **inputs**. The state row's `egress` was a boolean and is the whole lifecycle now — deadline,
pause mark, held offers, lockouts — beside `egressReady`. A version 8 recording says only that a
house was on fire, which cannot tell a contained Egress from one that ran out. The fixture round
walks a whole Egress: fired, paused by a report meeting, resumed, contained.

### F-002, recorded and not built

**Egress nodes are excluded from Isolate's target list during an Egress — outright, no fake
success.** The owner's ruling *amends* F-002, whose proposal was immunity with a fake success; the
exclusion leaks nothing because the two nodes are already named to every phone in the house, and a
fake success would spend a cooldown on a shot the house had already decided could not land. Isolate
does not exist. The note is on `Egress.isNode` and on `InsiderAbility`, which are the two places its
builder will be standing.

### The injections — nineteen bugs written, watched to fail by name, reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the same-node condition dropped from pairing | *"two people at ONE marker contained an Egress"* |
| the pause resetting the deadline instead of moving it | **2** — *"the countdown did not pick up exactly where it stopped"*, and the recorded-deadline test |
| no lockout on a missed beat | *"a missed beat cost nothing; stalling at a node is free"* |
| the shared cooldown ignored at fire | *"an Egress fired inside the opening cooldown"* |
| firing not spending the shared cooldown | *"a second Egress started immediately after the first was contained"* — **this one initially survived; the test it should have failed did not exist and was written** |
| nearest pair chosen instead of farthest | *"the only non-adjacent pair in this strip is WEST and EAST"* |
| the node read off the event instead of the presence plane | **3** — including *"two people at two nodes did not contain it"* |
| expiry emitting no terminal fact | **2** — including *"an Egress with no Insiders left to win it was quietly cancelled"* |
| the type rendered as a constant in the recording | *"a Tether Egress did not survive its own recording"* — **also initially survived; the fixture only ever fires Beacon, so a both-types round-trip was written** |
| the meeting card no longer inert during an Egress | **2** |
| silence on a failed beat | *"Expected `[SyncPulseAnswered(held=false)]`, actual `[]`"* |
| `SYNC_PULSE_ANSWERED` widened to the couch | *"Expected `[Resident/Live, Insider/Live]`, actual `[…, Resident/Out, Insider/Out]`"* |
| `EGRESS_OPENED`'s row deleted | **2** — *"EgressOpened has no row: the Egress would run and reach nobody"* |
| the widget reading the port's pair instead of the house's | *"EgressWidget did not name CELLAR"* |
| the tap window widened past half the beat | **3** — including *"the tap window is at least half the beat interval, so every tap is on some beat and nothing can miss"* |
| the Egress admission gate removed | *"Expected `[EgressNotRunning, EgressNotRunning]`, actual `[]`"* |
| the pause mark dropped from the state row | *"no state row records the moment the countdown stopped"* — **also initially survived; replay compares the build against itself, so a field-presence test was written** |
| an attribution added to containment | *"an effect other than the beat answer and the containment reached a phone"* |
| the report meeting not pausing the timer | **2** |

**Three injections survived their first run and each one bought a test.** All three are the same
class: a guard whose failure is invisible to the tests that already existed — a cooldown that only
matters after containment, a label that only matters when it is the other one, a state-row field
that both sides of a replay drop together.

### Escalations

1. ⚠️ **The tick rate is now decided in `Balance`, at 20 Hz, and nobody has confirmed it.**
   `FixedTimestep` refuses a default on purpose; `Balance` had one anyway, silently, at 1 Hz. This
   makes it explicit and adopts the architecture's suggestion. It changes `REVOKE_COOLDOWN` from 60
   ticks to 1200 — the same sixty seconds either way, but only if the rate is what this now says.
2. ⚠️ **`EGRESS_COOLDOWN` at 180s is invented.** Nothing in the design fixes it. `EGRESS_TIMER`'s
   120s is F-009's; the cooldown's is not, and playtest owns it.
3. ⚠️ **The Sync Pulse's shape is playtest's and is guessed:** a 1.5s beat, a 400ms window, a 2s
   lead, an 11-second run of four beats. Only *four taps* and *2–3s lockout* come from the design.
4. ⚠️ **F-009's better half is not built** — the app proposing the Egress timer from the painted
   grid, which is free because the longest marker-to-marker path is already computable. Nor is the
   lobby setting.
5. **An uncontained Egress does not end the round.** L6's, and stated above.
6. **Containment requires having scanned the node's card.** That falls out of D-136 and is the only
   way the house can know where somebody is — but nothing in the GDD says the node has to be
   scanned, and a player who walks to the right room and taps without scanning is told `held=false`
   with no explanation. Worth a design confirmation.

## Loop — Unit L6

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`ecc005b`** — *"The house says who it owned, and the
evening gets a second round"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`:
**tests=1304, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**, up from 1264 — **40 new** (model 450, core 149,
harness 107, ui 508, platform 81, app 9), read from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and
`redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` reports `ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green. Recording
format **9 → 10**.

**The freeze lifted for one thing: how a round ends.** D-131's four routes, D-153's percentages,
D-157's NEW ROUND and the reveal sequence at `gdd.md:1051-1063` are implemented; D-102, D-116,
D-134, D-135 and D-156 are honoured where the code touches them. The BLE spectator map stays frozen.

---

### `ended` was a boolean with no writer. It is the outcome now, and the rules write it

`GameState.ended` shipped as a flag nothing set, with a KDoc naming what was owed: the round-end
condition was open, so no event set it, and the field existed only so the allowlist could name
[RoundState.Ended] and deny it things. Both halves are paid. It is **`outcome: Outcome?`** — winner
and route — and `ended` is derived from it, so there is no second field that could disagree with the
reason about whether the evening is still going. The state row prints the outcome, and a replay that
ended a round the right way for the wrong reason no longer matches.

### One wrapper, not four branches, and a new route is one clause

The round can end on a Subroutine returning, on a takeover at a meeting, on a Sync Pulse pairing and
on a countdown reaching zero. A check written into each of those is a check the fifth one is
missing, and the symptom of missing it is a round that carries on after it is over, in a dark house,
with nobody able to say so out loud. So `reduce` is `ending(event, dispatch(state, event))`: every
handler is inside it the moment it joins `dispatch`, and **D-131's four routes are one `when` in one
function**, in the order they are checked, with the ordering argued at the clause.

**`?: return base` is rule 1's forbidden shape and it is allowed to live here**, for `takeover`'s
reason in a wider form: the branch is on the single most public fact in the game. A round that ended
takes over every screen in the house at once with the lights coming on; a round that did not end
emits nothing and every phone carries on. There is no audience for whom the *absence* is news.

**Route (d) is the one that reads the event.** Once `egressExpired` clears the Egress, nothing left
in `GameState` can tell an expiry from a containment, and a second field to remember it by would be
a flag beside `egress` that a build could de-synchronise.

**(b) yields to a running Egress and is checked before (c).** *A running Egress outlives its
Insiders* is a rule expressed as an absence — the clause simply does not fire — so both halves are
asserted: restraining the last Insider mid-Egress does **not** end the round, and then containment
ends it for the Residents while expiry ends it for the Insiders, from a state in which not one of
them is left to enjoy it.

### The reveal, and D-116 kept in the artefact that outlives the evening

Three effects, because three audiences are told different amounts: `RoundEnded` per seat with the
winner and the route, `InsidersRevealed` to everybody with the names **and the lines**, and
`HouseSignedOff` to the Insiders alone — *Thank you for your cooperation*, or *Unfortunate*.

**All three are permitted to the ENDED classes and to nothing else, which is a stronger guarantee
than any of their contents.** `RoundState.Ended` is reachable only through `GameState.outcome`,
whose only writer is the transition that emits these — so a reveal constructed at any other moment
is offered to classes that do not exist yet and is delivered to nobody. Rule 2 doing the work rule 3
would otherwise have to: the audience is bounded by *time*, in the type.

**The house holds the lines and drops them in the same transition that publishes them.** They ride
`RoundArmed` as a recorded input and live on `GameState`; `endRound` is the only writer of `outcome`
and the only thing that empties them, so the last read of a one line and its deletion are one
instruction apart. That is *deleted when the round ends* when the publish **is** the round ending.

**And the recording cannot hold one.** `Transcript` renders the *seats* that handed a line over and
never the text — in the arming row, in the state row, and in the reveal — because a recording is the
one artefact of this game that outlives the evening, and the promise printed on the screen where a
line is typed does not survive being written into one. The parser rebuilds blanks, the reveal renders
as seats alone, and **a round that ends replays byte-identically**: the game is reproduced without
the words. A replayed reveal therefore publishes blanks, said plainly rather than left to be found.

**Only Insiders' lines are in it, and not because a filter removes the others.** The list is built by
mapping `insiderSeats`, so a Resident's line was never in it — a filter is a thing somebody removes
while tidying. Every Insider is named, living or not: somebody Restrained at the first meeting was
working for the house just as much as the two still walking around at the end.

### ⚠️ `EgressSucceeded` lost its allowlist row, and the deletion is what keeps D-156

Every emission of it now sits in the same reduction that ends the round, and classification uses the
state after the event — so by the moment of delivery every seat is already `Ended` and the old
`LIVING + OUTSIDE` row could not have delivered it to anybody. **A row that cannot fire is worse than
no row**: it reads as a permission somebody granted, and the next person to widen it would be
"fixing" a delivery that was never missing. Written down where the row was, exactly as
`PRESENCE_CHANGED` is.

The deletion is also what keeps D-156. `EgressSucceeded` rides `Haptic.Long`; a widened row would put
**two** buzzes on every phone on one of the four routes and one on the other three. The ending's own
haptic is `Haptic.Short`, because **D-135's long haptic is closed at five and the round ending is not
one of them** — it is the most tempting sixth in the design, which is the reason the set is closed.

### ⚠️ ESCALATION — the differential harness had to be narrowed

`diff` now compares two role-swapped runs **only up to the first ending in either**, and this is a
real narrowing of a leak instrument. The reason: **parity counts living plain Residents against
living Insiders, so the round ending is arithmetic on the assignment.** Exchanging seats 1 and 3 in
the shared fixture moves the restrained seat from one side of parity to the other — `3 > 1` becomes
`2 <= 2` — and the second round is over at that meeting. Every line after that was reported as
divergent: 411 lines, 283 "unexplained", none of it actionable.

**`METER_ASYMMETRY`'s treatment cannot express it.** The meter is one message *kind*, so it can be
dropped from both runs with the surrounding lines still aligned. An ending is not a message; it is
the round stopping, and the gate refuses everything afterwards, so the shorter run's transcript
simply ends for every seat at once.

**The argument that it is not a channel**, for a second reader to agree or reject: in any real round
exactly one assignment exists, nothing follows the ending, and the reveal on that very message states
the alignment outright. **What is kept:** everything while both rounds were still running is compared
exactly as strictly as before, and the truncation detection is untouched in every case where neither
run ends. **What is paired with it:** `the exchange really does end one round and not the other`,
which fails loudly if the subtraction ever becomes a filter over two identical rounds — the same
protection `without the subtraction the exchange is visibly dirty` gives the meter.

`untilTheRoundEnds` is `internal` so the two direct comparisons in `DifferentialTest` and the fuzz
oracle use the same rule rather than three copies of it.

### `ui`: percentages, the reveal, and the control that was never there

**D-153's two defects are gone.** `SYSTEM INTEGRITY 14 / 32` and `0 / 32` are percentages, computed
from the bar's own lit count against `METER_SEGMENTS` so the number and the bar cannot disagree —
which is how a row reading `14 / 32` came to sit under a bar drawn at a different fraction with a
denominator that was a fossil of the count F-005 corrected. `MeterDisclosureTest` covers the endings
now, in both directions: no `/total` anywhere, and the percentage is really drawn, so a screen that
answered by saying nothing about the meter would fail rather than pass.

**The reveal is pushed, and the port's fixture is a fallback**, exactly `egressNodes`' arrangement.
`NamedInsider` prints the line under the name; it used to print `BLACKMAILED` beside it, which is the
fact without the content — and the content is the half that turns a result into a person. The
Residents' ending draws the same reveal from the same `PanelVals.revealed` and gained the house's
message card, because the house has something to say when it loses too and its register does not
improve.

**PERIMETER DISARMED needed no new code and did need a test.** Landing on `WinResidents` is the whole
of the wiring; the two glyph booleans are asserted against each other, because a build that lit both
would draw two irises and one that lit neither would take the perimeter off the status row at the
moment somebody most wants to read it.

**NEW ROUND is the actions layer's, not the graph's** — LIGHTS OUT's reason exactly: whether the
screen publishes it as a control depends on whether this phone is running the house, and *is this the
host* is not a fact a screen graph can hold. `ScreenGraphTest` caught the first attempt to put it in
`exitsOf`. The two endings left `awaitingTheHouse`, which is the change: they were terminals and the
evening finished wherever the round did.

**`LobbyModel.roundEnded` used to call `link.leave()`** — it kept D-116's promise by taking the whole
house down with it, which was correct while the app had no second round and became an evening that
ends after one the moment NEW ROUND existed. `LobbyLink` gained `roundEnded()` beside `leave()`: the
desk drops every line, the connection stays up, and the home, the seats and the settings survive
(D-157). The transport re-sends this phone's name afterwards, because the desk drops names with the
lines (D-115) and the protocol's answer has always been that the name rides back up.

**The ending is not a row in `Flow.housePushes` and must not become one.** That table is keyed on the
screen you are standing on; the ending can land on any of them, and `Tally` already has a row — a
second one keyed on the same screen replaces the first *silently*. `Flow.endings` is keyed on the
winner instead, which is what decides the screen.

### Injections

Eight, each one a targeted reverse-replace rather than a `git checkout`, each one read off the test
XML by name.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1a | parity written `<` instead of `<=` — fires one seat late | `parity fires at the boundary and not one seat early`: *living plain Residents fell to the number of living Insiders and the round ran on* |
| 1b | parity written `<= n + 1` — fires one seat early | the same test, on its **first** assertion: *three plain Residents against two Insiders is not parity, and the round ended*; plus `parity counts living Insiders and not the draw` |
| 2 | `&& !state.egressRunning` dropped from the Insiders-run-out clause | `containment ends a round whose Insiders are all Restrained`: *a held offer ended the round on its own* — the Egress stopped outliving its Insiders |
| 3 | `systemIntegrity == 0` → `< 0` | `the meter reaching zero ends the round where the player is standing` **and** `the last accepted entry ends the round in the reduction that banks it` |
| 4 | the publish built from `state.seats` instead of `insiderSeats` | `the publish carries no Resident's line`: *seat 0 is a Resident and the house published their line* |
| 5 | `endRound` stops emptying `oneLines` | `the desk is empty the instant the round ends`: *the round is over and the house is still holding everybody's line* |
| 6 | `INSIDERS_REVEALED` given a `LIVING` row | `nothing that states an alignment is permitted before the round ends` **and** `the reveal reaches nobody while the round is running`: *the app named an Insider to a living phone* |
| 7 | the meter printed as `14 / 32` again | `theEndingsSpeakOnlyPercent` **and** `bothEndingsStillReportTheMeter`: *expected `0%` but was `0 / 32`* |
| 8 | `newRound` stops calling `lobby.roundEnded()` | `newRoundGoesBackToTheLobbyWithTheLinesGone`: *WinInsiders: the line survived the round it was for* |

**1b is the one worth reading.** A boundary written one seat wrong in *either* direction fails, and
the two directions fail different assertions of the same test — which is what makes the test about a
boundary rather than about a case.

### Open, and what this unit did not do

1. ⚠️ **The differential narrowing above.** Named again here because it is the one thing in this unit
   that makes a leak instrument see less than it did.
2. **`NEW ROUND`'s label and whether it takes the two-second hold are copy-pending** (revision 33).
   Built without the hold: D-141's argument is about a control *pressed in the dark*, and this one is
   pressed in the light with everyone watching.
3. **Seat-to-name resolution for the reveal is the transport's and is not built.** The effect carries
   seats and lines; `PanelState.revealed` carries names and lines; nothing joins them yet, so the
   port's fixture pair is what the screens draw. Same shape as every other pushed field on that class.
4. **Nothing pushes the meter yet**, so the endings' percentages come from a screen-dependent fixture
   rather than from the house. The rule they are drawn under is the built half; the number is not.
5. **The `NEW` tag guard gained a second exemption.** `NEW HOME` was excused because a home is a thing
   the host makes; `NEW ROUND` is excused for the same reason. Neither is a claim about what anybody
   has looked at, which is what that guard is for.


---

## Client — Unit C1

*Revision 32's two rulings made real: D-141's hold family across five controls, and D-142's
Resident page 2 that is identical at rest and inert under the thumb. Plus the lobby's stale `7`,
and the last button dismissal in the app.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check` clean at **1318 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors** (all modules,
`--rerun-tasks`), `DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest` among them. No Events, Effects, schema
rows or `reduce()` touched — this is `:ui` and `:model` only, over the wire L1–L6 built.

### The hold is one clock now, and the furniture is the caller's

`HoldToConfirm` was the Delete screen's block with the two-second loop inside it. The loop is now
`rememberHold`, which returns how far a hold has got and **the pointer input to put somewhere** —
because three of D-141's five want the design's bar-and-note block on a dark field, and two are
springboard tiles that must go on looking exactly as they looked. Five separate ideas of two seconds
was the failure mode; there is one, and `HOLD_MILLIS` is still written down once.

| control | what it looks like |
|---|---|
| the vote lock | the block, dark-field. `HOLD TO CAST` → **`VOTE CAST`**, one composable through all three states so the thing under the thumb never moves or changes height |
| arming a Revoke | the tile, unchanged at rest. A hairline along its foot while a finger is down, and nothing otherwise |
| arming an Egress | the same |
| the host's LIGHTS OUT | the block, and it keeps the filled slate — it is the design's one saturated control and this is the screen it was drawn for |
| UNREGISTER AND CONTINUE | the block, outlined, beside a lit MOVE THEM FIRST — the Delete screen's inversion, same argument |

**`enabled` is refusal at the surface, not at the end.** With it false there is no pointer input at
all. That is D-142 for a Resident's page 2 and it is the whole ruling — a hold that filled and then
declined would be a self-test, and a bar filling in a dark house is world-observable to whoever is
behind the shoulder. The same mechanism now expresses the ballot's empty-selection refusal and the
lobby's closed gate, which is why `PanelButton`'s null-handler branch is down from three call sites
worth testing to two.

### Two graph edges moved, and both had to

The ability tiles and the notice publish no click action any more, so `ScreenGraphTest` cannot read
them off the screens. `Page2 → Banner` and `Notice → Discussion` left `ScreenGraph.exitsOf` for
`Flow.viaActions`, walked by `FlowTest` through new `FlowModel.armEgress()` and
`FlowModel.dismissNotice()`. **The egress tile is still the only role-asymmetric edge in the game**,
and the asymmetry is now capability rather than appearance: rendering a Resident's page 2 and firing
everything on it finds nothing, which is the ruling and not a gap.

### The two copy calls revision 32 left open

- **The ballot's pre-lock label: `HOLD TO CAST`**, with `HOLD THE BUTTON FOR TWO SECONDS.` under it —
  the same sentence the Delete screen teaches with the lights on, now a constant (`HOLD_NOTE`) so a
  player learns the gesture once and meets it four more times in the dark.
- **The Resident's page-2 resting line: `READY . ALL NOMINAL`**, replacing `READY . TAP TO TEST`,
  which invited a press on a tile that now takes no pointer input whatever. Same brightness, same
  slot, **same nineteen characters** as the Insider's — which also changed, to `READY . HOLD TO ARM`,
  because the instruction was wrong and a false instruction there costs a whole cooldown with no
  cancel.

### The lobby's `7` was right by coincidence

`SUBROUTINES EACH` is `Balance.orderSize(joined, chosenInsiders)` now — D-129's `K`, computed from
public lobby facts alone. The literal happened to be correct at six seats and at no other party
size. An empty lobby reads `NOT YET` rather than the `1` the arithmetic floor produces for a house
with nobody in it.

### Injections

Seven, each a targeted reverse-replace, each read off the test XML by name.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `while (spent < millis)` → `< 0` — every hold fires the instant a finger lands | **five** by name: `revokeArmsOnAFullHoldAndNotBefore`, `egressArmsOnAFullHoldAndNotBefore`, `unregisterAndContinueTakesTwoSeconds`, `mostOfAHoldDoesNotTurnTheLightsOut`, `mostOfAHoldCastsNothing` — plus the two pre-existing Delete guards |
| 2 | the tiles' `enabled = armable` → `enabled = true` | `aResidentsPageTwoAnswersNeitherTileAndDrawsNothingUnderTheThumb`, on the pixels |
| 3 | the ballot's `live` drops `&& !meeting.locked` | `aCastBallotRefusesBothTheTapAndASecondHold` |
| 4 | `StopNow` turned into a `HoldToConfirm` | `stopNowIsOneTapOnEveryScreenThatOffersIt`, plus `ScreenGraphTest` and three `SubroutineParityTest` guards |
| 5 | the lobby row pinned back to `"7"` | `theSubroutinesEachRowIsAskedOfTheBalanceRules` and `anEmptyLobbySizesNothing` |
| 6 | the notice's `onDragEnd` never calls `dismissNotice()` | `theHouseNoticeIsSwipedAwayRatherThanDismissed` |
| 7 | the hold block forced to 20 units tall | `everyControlAtAMeetingIsBigEnoughToHitInTheDark` |

**3 is the one worth reading, because the first version of that test did not catch it.** It held the
cast control for a full two seconds and then looked for the bar — by which time a hold that ran and
then declined has reset itself, and is indistinguishable from one that never started. The check has
to be made **with the finger still down and half the window spent**. Two other inertness assertions
(the empty ballot, the closed lobby gate) had the same shape and were fixed the same way.

**7 is why `HOLD_BLOCK` exists.** The meeting's tap-target sweep reads controls off click semantics,
and a hold has none — so retiring the ballot's tap would have quietly taken the meeting's most
important control out of the one measurement that keeps it hittable in the dark, and the sweep would
have gone on passing while measuring one control fewer. The block carries a test tag, beside
`HOLD_SURFACE` which is the same idea for the Subroutine that counts fingers. It also gained
`heightIn(min = TAP_TARGET)`: the design's own padding leaves it at 34 units, and a hold that cannot
be *started* reliably in the dark is a hold nobody finishes.

### ⚠️ ESCALATION — the vote window cannot reach arming without a wire change

**Item 6's second half is not built.** The host's `VOTING` row moves a number `LobbyModel` holds and
nothing reads, and there is no path for it to travel:
