- `LobbyLink` has `host`, `join`, `handOver`, `setInsiders`, `roundEnded`, `leave` — **no arm**, so
  arming does not go over the lobby wire at all.
- `LobbyBody` has `Handover`, `Naming`, `Standing` — no arming body.
- `Event.RoundArmed` carries `seed`, `seats`, `insiders`, `chosenInsiders`, `markers`, `oneLines` —
  **no settings**, and no vote window.

Carrying it needs either a new `LobbyBody` case or a new `RoundArmed` field. Both are wire changes,
the freeze forbids them, so this is logged and skipped. Nothing about the round's vote clock changed;
`Flow.autoAdvance` still stands in with the design's 45.

### Open, and what this unit did not do

1. **`NEW ROUND` still takes no hold**, as L6 left it and for L6's reason: D-141's argument is about a
   control pressed in the dark, and that one is pressed in the light with everyone watching. Revision
   33 has it copy-pending; nothing here decided it.
2. **The Revoke tile's hold reaches `stepRevoke`, which is still the fixture's state cycle.** Real
   arming is silent, spends the cooldown at the moment of arming and has no cancel — all three are
   the house's, and none of them exists yet. The gesture in front of it is now the right one.
3. **The hold is not gated on the Revoke's own state.** An Insider can hold an already-armed or
   cooling tile and it cycles. Refusing there is a decision about an ability, which is not the
   screen's to take, and the states are already legible on the tile either way.
4. **`SWIPE UP TO DISMISS` is written on the notice** where the button was. It is a line of text with
   no border and no tap target, not a control — but it is copy nobody has ruled on.

---

## Client — Unit C2

*The two screens that drew a fixture now draw what the house sent: the work order (L3's
`Effect.WorkOrderIssued`, carried since `559f48d` and consumed by nothing) and the couch's meeting
view (L2's ghost effects, same standing). Plus Ghost3's two literal percentages.*

**Outcome: green, pushed.** Commit **`b197368`** — *"The house deals the work, and the couch watches
the vote happen"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1331, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(model 450, core 149, harness 107, ui 535, platform 81, app 9), up from 1318 — **13 new**, read from
the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` reports `ui clean`,
`DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest` green. **`:ui` only — no Event, Effect, schema row or
`reduce()` touched.**

---

### Three fields, and the arrangement was already written down

`PanelState` gains `order: List<OrderLine>?`, `ballots: List<Ballot>?` and `meetingPhase:
MeetingPhase?`. Each is **null-means-the-house-has-said-nothing**, with the port's drawn values
standing in — `egressNodes`' and `revealed`'s arrangement exactly, and the point of copying it is
that the fallback stays a fixture rather than becoming a second source of truth.

**`ui` never consumed an `Effect` before this and still does not.** These are the shapes the
effects carry, resolved at the boundary, which is what `PanelState`'s own KDoc asks for: *every
field here arrives already decided*. What is missing is the transport, and it is missing for every
effect in the game (C1 and L2 and L3 all left the same seam).

### The blackout is the type, and that is the whole of it

`OrderRow.Blocked` carries **one integer**, mirroring `OrderLine.Blocked`. The `when` that maps one
to the other is a translation rather than a decision: there is no field on either side that could
name a blocked entry, so no composable, no branch and no later refactor can put a name on one.

**Why it is worth being this strict about a two-case sealed interface.** D-146 made dependencies
**cross-player**, so a blocked entry that named its Subroutine would name *the person upstream of
you* as surely as the work — and D-114's *known unknown* would be neither. The row also draws **no
light mark**, because a signature is a property of a particular Subroutine and drawing one describes
exactly what the row exists not to describe. Nothing on the ladder is zero cells, so the empty
column reads as *nothing is claimed* rather than as a dark Subroutine.

**The blocked-entry treatment is the established one, unchanged**: outlined box, `LOCKED`, `WAITING
UPSTREAM`, no mark, full-length row. It was already right; what changed is that it is now
structurally impossible for it to say more. Rendered at three scattered positions in an eight-line
order (`build/screens/work-order-dealt.png`) rather than the fixture's two trailing rows, which is
the shape D-123's same-card reuse actually produces.

### ⚠️ ESCALATION — E-C2-1 · Nothing on the wire says where a piece of work is

**`OrderLine` carries no card and no room, in either case, deliberately.** Its own KDoc: *the house
answers a scan and never publishes a map of the round.*

**The drawn design puts a room and a marker glyph on every work-order row and on the springboard
widget.** Those are the two things a player in a dark house navigates by. So the moment a real order
arrives, both surfaces lose the destination — uniformly, on every row at once, which is what the
screenshots show.

**What is missing, exactly:**

- `OrderLine.Known` would need the `MarkerId` — `OrderEntry` already holds it authority-side.
- And the client would need **room-name resolution for a marker**, which it does not have either:
  the home is the host's, and no effect publishes a marker→room map.

Both are wire changes; the freeze forbids them; logged and skipped. **It is nulled rather than
filled from the fixture on purpose** — letting the port's `GARAGE` sit beside a Subroutine the house
chose is a screen that lies in the dark to a player with no way to check, and doing it on some rows
and not others is worse. `anOrderFromTheHouseSaysNothingAboutWhereTheWorkIs` holds it **in both
directions**: it fails if somebody refills the column from a fixture, and it fails the day a card
really arrives, at which point the test that has to change says exactly why.

### ⚠️ ESCALATION — E-C2-2 · Nothing carries System Integrity to any client

D-134 gives the couch **live** Resident progress. **No effect carries the meter at all.**
`SubroutineGraded` carries a boolean about one entry and deliberately says nothing about the meter
(D-109: an Insider's accepted entry writes nothing), and there is no other candidate. `PanelVals`
already refuses to hold `integrity` for exactly this reason — *a field with no sender*.

So Ghost3's number is still the port's fraction. The **shape** is now right (percentage, computed
off its own bar) and the sender is missing. The Egress half is one step better and still short:
`EgressHeld(remaining, running)` reaches `LIVING + OUTSIDE`, but the **duration it is a fraction of**
is a lobby setting the wire does not send — the same gap C1 escalated for the vote window, one
setting over (F-009).

### Ghost3's two numbers disagreed with their own bars

`66%` was drawn over a bar lit to **21 of 32** (65). `71%` over one lit to **22** (68). Not a
rounding slip — two hand-maintained facts about one meter, on the screen where a meter is read most
closely, by the reader with the whole round to read it. Both come off `outsidePercent` /
`egressPercent` now, which are the same fraction the bars are drawn at. That is `integrityPercent`'s
own argument (D-103, D-153) applied one screen further out, and it is a **covered fix**: D-153 says
no screen prints a total and D-103's revision-21 wording is that the bar and the number cannot
disagree.

### The couch's clock was wrong for ninety seconds of every meeting

The living walk a screen per phase. The out watch the whole meeting from **one** screen, so
`GhostMeeting` is the only surface in the game whose clock changes meaning without the screen
changing — and it did not know. It said **VOTING ENDS IN** through every phase, counting down a
window that had not opened while the room was still talking. D-134 puts *the discussion and vote
timers* on the couch, plural.

`Countdowns.onGhostMeeting(phase)` looks the window up by phase; the label is the phase's own words.
**Check-in has no window** — D-104's gate closes when the last player walks in, not when a clock runs
out — so it draws no readout and no bar.

**That last one was caught by rendering and by nothing else.** The first version drew the block
unconditionally and the check-in frame came out with a lone `0` above an unlit bar. Nothing failed.
It renders, it is plausible, and it says the **opposite** of what is true: a clock that has run out
rather than a gate waiting on somebody still walking. `aPhaseWithNoWindowDrawsNoClock` is the guard,
with the three phases that do have a window asserted beside it so the fix cannot become *draw no
clock ever*.

### ⚠️ The meter sweep was pointed straight at its own bug and would have walked past it

Injection 4 rewrote a couch readout as `21 / 32`. `MeterDisclosureTest.assertNoDenominator` swept for
`/32` — **without a space** — and the rows D-153 was written against read `SYSTEM INTEGRITY 14 / 32`,
**with** spaces. The sweep named for that defect could not have caught it; only the percentage
assertion beside it failed, which is a different guard doing this one's job by luck.

It sweeps both spacings now, and the injection was **re-run** to watch the sweep itself fail by name
(*"Did not expect any node but found '1' node that satisfies ... contains '/ 32'"*). Same class as
L2's derived-allowlist finding: a guard that agrees with itself about the thing it was written for.

### One test was asleep on arrival

`theOrderScreenIsTheSameScreenInBothRoles` mapped over the roles **inside**
`runDesktopComposeUiTest`, which returns `Unit` — so it collected a `List<Unit>` and compared `Unit`
to `Unit`. Green forever, over any difference at all. It collects into a list declared outside the
harness now, and carries two positive controls (the text read is non-empty, and it contains the
order that was dealt) so it cannot quietly go back to agreeing with itself.

### Injections — five, each a targeted reverse-replace, each read off the XML by name

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | a blocked row drawn as a named one (`Sniff`) | **2** — `aBlockedEntryNamesNothingAndRatesNothing` (*"could not find any node ... 'LOCKED'"*), `everySurfaceThatNamesASubroutineCarriesItsLightAndNoOtherScreenDoes` (*"Work/Resident — shows {Dark=4, Medium=1, Bright=2}"*) |
| 2 | `PanelVals.ballots` ignores the push | `theCouchIsShownTheVoteTheHouseIsPushing` — *"HOLLIS cast a ballot the house pushed and is not on the outside view"* |
| 3 | the vote screen draws the selections (the other direction) | **2** — `onlyAPlayerOutsideTheSystemIsEverShownWhoVotedForWhom` (*"expected `<[GhostMeeting]>` but was `<[Vote, GhostMeeting]>`"*), `theVoteScreenCountsAndDoesNotAttribute` (*"expected `<0>` but was `<5>`"*) |
| 4 | a couch readout as `21 / 32` | `theOutsideViewSpeaksOnlyPercent` — twice: first on the percentage assertion, then on the sweep itself once both spacings were covered |
| 5 | the destination column refilled from `CURRENT` under a real order | `anOrderFromTheHouseSaysNothingAboutWhereTheWorkIs` — *"Work drew a destination for work the house dealt ... expected `<0>` but was `<6>`"* |

Each reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, and the sources were grepped for
the marker before committing. Injection 3's revert was verified by grepping for `CAST_FOR` and
`vals.ballots` in `ScreensRoom.kt` (absent).

### Flagged for a person — no covering ruling, so nothing was changed

1. **⚠️ The couch's vote window is 60 while the living's is 45.** `Countdowns` carries a
   `GhostMeeting` row of its own beside `Vote`'s D-117 default — two numbers for one clock, in a lit
   room where the couch can see a living player's phone. **Left alone deliberately**: the 60 is what
   reproduces the design's drawn picture (`VOTING ENDS IN 0:24` over twelve lit segments of thirty),
   and moving it to 45 redraws that same `0:24` at sixteen. Reconciling is a visible screen change.
2. **⚠️ `NEXT SUBROUTINE` is the queue D-114 replaced with a menu, surviving as a heading.** The
   house designates nothing — the player chooses among what is actionable, and choosing *how much
   light to make* is the whole point of D-106's always-visible signature. The widget names the first
   actionable line so the slot is not blank, which is a presentation and is flagged where it is
   derived rather than defended. Changing it is a copy and layout decision.
3. **⚠️ ARRAY WIPE is on the work-order list and the house can never send it.** It is a circuit, not
   one of the roster's ten, has no `SubroutineKind`, and is drawn by D-122's station draw at arming.
   So it survives only in the port's fixture. Whether it belongs on this list is unruled — it *is*
   real work a Resident does, and the list is where a dark route is planned. Its `BRIGHT` is still
   this port's reading (D-113 ratified the value, nothing ruled the row).
4. **`NOTHING OPEN`** — new copy, for an order with nothing actionable. Unreachable by construction
   (`blockedBy` holds earlier indices only, so line 0 is always actionable until done) and written
   down anyway, because the alternative is a blank slot on the screen a player navigates by.
   Deliberately **not** D-124's NOTHING FOR YOU HERE, which is a scan's answer about a card.
5. **The couch's ballot rows render during check-in** in the screenshots, because the fixture holds
   them. In play the house would have sent nothing yet. Screen behaviour is correct; noted so the
   frame is not read as a defect.

### Not in this unit

**No transport.** Every field added here is fed by a fixture until something pushes it, which is
true of every field on `PanelState` and is the seam C1, L2 and L3 all stopped at. The spectator map
(D-136) stayed frozen: Ghost3's occupancy is still `Plan.trueCounts` and nothing carries presence.

---

## Subroutines — Unit S1

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`741eeb8`** — *"Two buzzes in the dark, and a column you
can take too far"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1349, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(model 450, core 149, harness 107, ui 553, platform 81, app 9), up from 1331 — **18 new** (8 in
`SubroutineTest`, common, so ×2 targets; 2 in `SubroutineParityTest`, desktop), read from the test
XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` reports `ui clean`,
`DeviceLayoutTest` green across its now-61 screens × 2 roles. **`:ui` only — no Event, Effect,
schema row or `reduce()` touched.** THE FREEZE HELD.

**Built: SNIFF (D-137) and DEALLOCATE (D-138).** Eight of the design's ten now have their
interaction; Interrupt and Drift remain, and their blocker is not the design — it is E-L4-3's
tolerance grading, which neither of these two needed.

---

### The wire expressed both, and neither needed a change to it

**Entries ride L1's canonical ordered-integer shape.** Sniff's entry is one integer — *which group*
— and reuses `ChoiceEntry`, the vote's shape and Parity Check's, because *which group was bigger* is
one selection out of two made in the dark by somebody who may have to change it. **An eighth entry
class holding a boolean would be a second way to say the same thing.** Deallocate's is a list of
per-column removals, which is `List<Int>` exactly as `OpenSubroutine.expected` already is.

**Instance parameters ride the scan.** Sniff is `[first, second, gapTenths]` — three small integers,
which is what L4's stand-in already draws — and **the pause arrives in tenths of a second so that
nothing on the wire is ever milliseconds.** Deallocate's parameters *are* the column heights, so the
column count is the list's length and there is no second field to disagree with it. Neither needed
a shape L4's plumbing does not already have. **No escalation: both were expressible.**

What is *not* real yet is the arrival. `:ui` still holds these as fixtures on `SubroutineModel`
beside `TRACE_SEED` and `SHORT_FINGERS`, written as **the parameter list the house sends rather than
as named numbers**, so the fixture is the same shape as the arrival that will replace it and the
reader that will read the real one is the reader being exercised now.

---

### ⚠️ THE SNIFF ANSWER GESTURE — this is the thing to review

D-137 leaves the answer gesture to the builder and flags it. Here it is, and it is the most
aggressive treatment in the port.

**The screen is drawn in full and painted black.** Header, both halves, the return slot, the motion
row, SUBMIT, STOP NOW — every one of them is in the place it occupies on the other seven Subroutine
screens, and **every colour on the screen is `Amber.Black` until the player has answered.** Nothing
is hidden and nothing is added later: the difference between the dark screen and the lit one is a
palette, and **nothing moves at the moment it lights.** A screen that *grew* its controls when the
answer arrived would be a change in lit area on the frame a player is most likely to be looking away
from, on the one Subroutine that exists to keep somebody invisible.

**The answer is the two halves of the panel.** Left is the group that buzzed first, right the one
that buzzed second — reading order, which is also the order they arrived in, and the only mapping a
player can be expected to hold while watching a doorway. The target is half a phone: no precision,
no boundary to find, nothing to look at. **A mark cannot be made on the darkest screen in the game,
so the screen answers instead** — the first tap is what lights it, and what it lights is which side
the finger landed on.

**The tap does not send it.** A single choice takes SUBMIT (batch 1's rule, the vote's shape) and
here it earns it twice: the tap is made blind, on a target the size of a hand, by somebody who
cannot see what they hit. Tapping the other half moves the mark, exactly as Parity Check's does.

**⚠️ STOP NOW is drawn in black and is live from the first frame.** The strongest claim on this
screen and the one most worth a person's judgement in an actual dark room. It is in its usual place,
with its usual words, doing its usual thing — it simply cannot be seen. *In the dark that is what
STOP NOW already was*: the reason it never changes is that it is pressed by muscle memory rather
than read. An invisible live control is the strongest form of that rule rather than an exception to
it, but somebody should stand in a dark corridor and try it.

**Everything the lit state draws is at most `Amber.Dim`** — Handshake's ceiling. A screen that went
dark for the perception and then flared for the answer would be making the dark-rung claim about
half of itself.

**⚠️ There is no instruction on this screen and there cannot be one.** *Which group was bigger* is a
sentence made of light. The instruction has to live on the screen before this one — `ScanCaught`
already names the Subroutine and its signature — and **that is recorded as owed rather than quietly
solved here**, because it is a change to a screen this unit was not asked to touch.

---

### The refusals: one loud, one quiet, and the difference is the whole of D-125

**Equal groups are refused loudly, by name, at the read.** `SniffGroups.of` throws
`MalformedSubroutineParameters` naming the ruling — *a tie is a coin flip the house would then
grade, and D-109 grades entries on their merits or not at all.* Three other malformations are
refused the same way: the wrong length, a group with no buzzes, a pause of nothing.

**It is not defensive decoration.** The stand-in draw in `core` produces three numbers out of one
range with no idea what they mean (E-L4-3), so **it can draw an equal-groups Sniff today**.

**The throw is at the READ and never at a draw**, and the format doctrine is why it is loud while
rule 6 says errors are silent to the player: a screen that threw mid-composition would blank, and a
phone that blanks in a dark house is indistinguishable from a revocation. Today the read happens
once at class-load against the port's own fixtures, so a malformed one fails the *build*. **When the
push exists this belongs at the effect boundary with the rest of the arrival.**

**Deallocate's quiet refusal is the picture's, not the answer's.** A column with no dots left is not
a target. Refusing at *level* would be the screen saying *that would be wrong*; refusing at *empty*
is that there is nothing under the finger to take. The alternative is worse than a verdict — an
entry counting removals the picture cannot show would send the house a number the player was never
shown and grade them on it. **What is drawn and what is sent stay one fact**, in one expression.

**Equal columns are deliberately NOT refused.** A distribution arriving level asks for no removals,
which is trivial rather than malformed; refusing it would be this client deciding how much work a
piece of work has to contain. `gdd.md:569` says *unequal* and D-138 does not, and the difference is
left where the ruling left it.

---

### `ColumnEntry` is told the column COUNT and never the heights

The required entry is the sum of each column's excess over the shortest, so **the shortest column is
the answer key**, and an entry handed the heights would find it with one `min()`. That is
`HoldEntry`'s ruling one Subroutine along — `SHORT_FINGERS` lives on the model and not on the entry
— and here the ask is a whole list and the subtraction is a one-liner, which makes the narrowing
worth more rather than less.

**There is no `level`, no `excess` and no `owed` anywhere in `:ui`.** Every one of them is one line
and every one of them is the answer. `DotColumns.of` returns the heights and computes nothing. The
only place the two halves of a Deallocate instance appear in one expression is a test, which says so
in its own name.

---

### Sniff's haptic script, shaped for the device unit

`HapticStep.Buzz(millis)` / `HapticStep.Rest(millis)` — alternating durations, no intensities,
nothing to interpret, which is the vocabulary every platform vibration API already has. `SniffGroups.
script` is the first group, the pause, the second group, **ending on a buzz** rather than on silence,
because *when the question is over* is the moment the player is expected to answer.

**Every buzz is the same length.** Not a detail: a script whose pulses varied would be a rhythm, and
D-137 makes Sniff a magnitude judgment precisely so there is no pattern to hold. D-135 also reserves
the long haptic for five named events and this is not among them.

**⚠️ It lives in `:ui`.** `platform` sees `model` and never `ui`, so a script the platform layer
consumes directly would have to be a `:model` type — which is a wire-adjacent addition and the
freeze was on. It is pure data with no role and no answer in it, so **moving it to `:model` when the
device unit lands is a file move**, and that is the flag.

---

### The injections — nine bugs, each watched to fail by name, each reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the equal-groups refusal removed | *"SNIFF accepted two groups of 1, which is a tie the house would then grade"* |
| `ColumnEntry` given the distribution, refusing past level | **2** — *"a removal was refused expected:`<[0, 9, 0]>` but was:`<[0, 0, 0]>`"*, and *"a tap on DEALLOCATE reached nothing"* |
| the script buzzing the second group longer | *"the buzzes are not all the same length, so the script carries a rhythm"* |
| a too-tall column clamped instead of refused | *"DEALLOCATE accepted [3, 8] — a column taller than the panel draws"* |
| SNIFF's header lit before the answer | *"SNIFF/Resident is emitting light before the answer — brightest pixel 138 at (9,31)"* |
| a column refusing a tap that would take it below level | *"a tap on the shortest column was refused … the screen adjudicated one"* |
| DEALLOCATE grading itself — COMPLETE once level | *"DEALLOCATE/Resident after 6 tap(s) says \"COMPLETE\""*, both roles, open and handed over |
| SNIFF's chosen half a step brighter for Insiders | **2** — *"SNIFF after 1 tap(s) — 3243 pixels differ by role, first at (8,41)"*, and the same under both verdicts |
| SNIFF built, and dropped from the dispatch | *"a tap on SNIFF reached nothing — it has a screen, a roster row and no wiring in SubroutineModel.tap"* |

Each reverted by **targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`**, with the runner refusing any
run whose output carries an `e:` line — and one addition this unit made to L4's script: after the
reverse-replace **the file is compared byte for byte with what it was before the injection**, and a
run that cannot restore it exactly fails loudly. That is the check a `git checkout` would hide.

#### ⚠️ One injection failed to fail, and the guard was widened rather than the injection dropped

**`COMPLETE` on a levelled Deallocate passed every test in this file.** The parity sweep drove the
columns *in order*, `step.mod(columns)`, which never levels the fixture — so **the one state a
screen would be tempted to grade was the one state the sweep never rendered.** It now drives **the
tallest column that is left** (take from the biggest pile, which is also what a player does), which
reaches level at exactly six taps, and the injection fails there naming both roles.

#### The guard caught a real leak before any injection did

**`MotionBudgetRow` drew its outline at `Amber.Edge` unconditionally**, so the first run of the
luminance assertion reported *brightest pixel 36 at (8,536)* on a screen that is supposed to emit
nothing. A hairline is still emitted light and D-137 carves no exception for chrome. The outline is
a parameter now, black on Sniff until the answer.

---

### Looked at

`ui/build/screens/` at both aspect ratios, whole-screen, both roles: SNIFF untouched (black but for
the D-077-dimmed status bar), answered, handed over, and under both verdicts; DEALLOCATE the same
five. Two treatment decisions came out of looking rather than out of reasoning:

- **Deallocate's column field is a fixed height**, exactly as tall as the tallest distribution the
  reader accepts, centred in whatever room the body has. Stretched with the panel it drew the same
  five dots against a different amount of nothing on every handset — and *how tall is that column*
  is the entire question. It also guarantees a full column is never clipped.
- **The floor is a hairline under the columns.** Without it they are lengths hanging in a box and
  the comparison is a judgement about where each one ends.

**Simulator run, both screens, at real device metrics** — a temporary launch-screen change in
`Cheats.kt`, reverted by targeted reverse-replace and confirmed absent from the diff before
committing (`git diff --stat -- app/` empty). Status row level with the Island, nothing under the
cutout or the home indicator, DEBUG chip in place, no exceptions in the device log. Deallocate's
dots are comfortably thumbable at phone size and the baseline reads at a glance. **Sniff's untouched
screen on a real panel is a black rectangle with a dimmed status bar and nothing else**, which is
the whole ruling made visible and is also exactly what a screen that failed to draw looks like —
which is why the answered frames are in the viewer beside it.

**None of this says anything about the game.** The two group sizes, the pause and the column
distribution are all the house's in play, the buzzing is the house's, and this build has no house.

### Escalations and flags

**E-S1-1 · SNIFF has no room for its own instruction.** *Which group was bigger* cannot be written
on a screen that emits nothing, so the instruction has to be on `ScanCaught` before BEGIN. Not
taken here: it is a visible change to a screen outside this unit, and copy is a build-time call.

**E-S1-2 · The haptic script is in `:ui` and the device unit will want it in `:model`.** `platform`
cannot see `ui`. Pure data, no role, no answer — a file move, flagged so it is a decision rather
than a discovery.

**E-S1-3 · The parameter read belongs at the effect boundary and is at class-load.** Today a
malformed fixture fails the build, which is the strongest available place while nothing pushes an
instance. When the push exists, `SniffGroups.of` and `DotColumns.of` move to where the arrival is
resolved — and the refusal must stay loud to the authority and silent to the player.

**E-L4-3 stands and is now sharper**: the stand-in draw in `core` knows nothing about what its three
numbers mean, so it can draw an equal-groups Sniff or an undrawable distribution. The client refuses
both by name. **The per-kind draw is owed.**

### Not in this unit

**Interrupt and Drift.** Both need the tolerance grading E-L4-3 records as owed — inside the band,
within the hit radius — and both are authored motion, which is the wall batch 1's triage named.
Neither was touched.

---

## Subroutines — Unit S2

**Outcome: done, green, pushed.** Commit **`36a26c6`** — *"A sweep with no clock on it, and a dot you
have to keep in your head"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1373, failures=0, errors=0,
skipped=0** (model 450, core 149, harness 107, ui 577, platform 81, app 9), up from 1349 — **24 new**
(9 in `SubroutineTest`, common, so ×2 targets; 6 in the new `SubroutineMotionTest`, desktop), read
from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, `:ui:boundaryCheck` reports `ui
clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` green across its now-68 screens × 2 roles. **`:ui` only — no Event,
Effect, schema row or `reduce()` touched.** THE FREEZE HELD.

**Built: INTERRUPT (D-139) and DRIFT (D-140). With these two, all ten roster Subroutines are
built.** The blocker batch 1's triage named — authored motion with no Effect to carry it — is closed
by D-139's architecture rather than worked around: the parameters ride the scan, the client renders,
the house grades.

---

### The motion is arithmetic the house authored, which is why it is allowed to exist at all

Rule 5 forbids a fade nobody authored. These two move, and the reason that is not an exception is
that **the house authored the numbers and the client is doing division**: `InterruptSweep.at` and
`DriftPath.at` are pure functions of the instance and the milliseconds since the screen opened, in
integer arithmetic, with no tween, no easing and no floating point to round differently on two
handsets. The same instance on the same clock draws the same frame on every phone and on every
replay — which is the second promise in `project-context`, and it is now asserted at the pixel level
rather than reasoned about.

**The milliseconds come from the composition and never from a wall clock.** `Clock.System.now()` is
unreplayable by construction; a screen reading one would render differently on every replay of the
same recording. That is [HoldSurface]'s discipline with one difference, and the difference is the
interesting part.

### ⚠️ `withInfiniteAnimationFrameMillis`, and why the choice is load-bearing rather than stylistic

`HoldSurface` uses `withFrameMillis` because a hold ends. **D-139's sweep does not end**, and an
ordinary frame loop that never stops means a composition that never goes idle — which would hang
`waitForIdle` in every sweep in this module, not only in the new ones.

Compose's own idiom for an animation with no end solves it in both directions:

- **Under an auto-advancing test clock** — which is every existing sweep — the
  `InfiniteAnimationPolicy` stops the frames and both screens render their opening position.
  Deterministic, identical for both roles, no hang. `ScreenshotTest`, `DeviceLayoutTest`,
  `ScreenGraphTest`, `LightSignatureTest` and `SubroutineParityTest` all took the two new screens
  without a line of change to how they run.
- **Under `mainClock.autoAdvance = false`** the frames arrive exactly where a test puts them, which
  is how `SubroutineMotionTest` samples an animated screen at the *same instant* for both roles.
- **On a device** there is no policy and it runs forever, which was checked on the simulator rather
  than assumed — see below.

**This was probed before anything was written**, because a hang would have been expensive to
discover late. That probe is the only reason the file does not use `withFrameMillis`.

### ⚠️ THE PARITY SWEEP WAS BLIND TO EVERYTHING AFTER FRAME ZERO — and an injection proved it

The first injection — `INTERRUPT`'s sweep running at double speed for Insiders — **passed
`SubroutineParityTest` with the bug in the tree.** Under the auto-advancing clock both roles sit at
the opening frame, and two identical frame-zeros compare equal however differently the two roles
would have moved a second later.

That is the batch-1 lesson in a new place: *a sweep that renders one state cannot see the states it
never renders* (S1 hit the same shape when Deallocate's drive never reached level). The fix was a
second file rather than a widened one — `SubroutineMotionTest` owns the clock, and every claim in it
is about the frames the older sweep cannot reach. The older sweep is not redundant: an animated
screen that fails to draw its first frame is a bug all of its own, and it is the one guard that
still sees it.

### Interrupt: the panel is the target, and the tap is the entry

**No SUBMIT.** A confirmation step would ask the player to ratify a moment already seconds gone, so
the hand-over belongs to the entry type: Interrupt's is a `SequenceEntry` of **length one**, whose
rule already is *an entry goes when it can no longer change*. **The screen cannot forget to send**,
which a screen that had to remember could.

**The whole body is the control**, outlined `Amber.Dim` while live and `Amber.Edge` once spent —
exactly how `PanelButton` draws that difference. A button under the bar would put the finger where
the eye is not, on the one Subroutine where *when* is the entire answer.

**There is no timeout anywhere**, and it is asserted rather than asserted-about: a minute of
standing still leaves the entry untouched, the return line empty, every control live, and the sweep
still moving. A screen that quietly stopped animating would pass the first three.

### Drift: the lane is the target, and a tap before the buzz is echoed and goes nowhere

**⚠️ Two treatment decisions, both flagged.**

**The entry is a position along the path, not a point on the glass.** The path is straight and
drawn, so *where the dot is vertically* is not a question anybody is asking — and a tap taxed for
missing a line the screen already shows would grade a blind one-handed finger on something the
question never contained. So the strip is the control at its full height and the hit radius becomes
a distance along the path. It also keeps the entry one integer on L1's canonical ordered-integer
shape, which a two-coordinate tap would not have been.

**A tap before the pulse moves the mark and sends nothing.** The alternative — refusing it — is
honest too, and it was rejected on the dark-room test: a control that swallows a tap in silence is
indistinguishable from a phone that has failed, and this is the one Subroutine where the player is
watching the screen rather than the doorway. *This is not the phone refusing an answer; it is the
phone not inventing a question.* `RETURNED . WAITING` is what says it went, and it says nothing
until it has.

**The motion stops when the entry goes.** A dot that kept drifting after the hand-over would emerge
from cover at a known speed and hand back the position the player was asked for. Frozen, nothing
appears that the player was not already looking at.

**The canvas has no inset**, and that is a guard rather than a layout preference: the step the house
receives is the tap's offset over the surface's width, so a picture drawn narrower than its own
touch surface would put the mark somewhere the finger was not and send the other one of the two. It
was written with a `padding(horizontal = 8.u)` first, caught by looking, and there is now a test
that measures the mark's own pixels against the fraction of the lane the finger landed on.

### The refusals: four apiece, and D-140's is the one with teeth

Every one is at the READ and never at a draw, for `SniffGroups`' reason (a screen that threw
mid-composition would blank, and a blank phone in a dark house is indistinguishable from a
revocation).

**A wait longer than the crossing puts the dot back in sight before the buzz**, and the player then
answers by looking. That is D-140 inverted rather than relaxed — the Subroutine still renders, still
accepts a tap, still gets graded, and tests nothing. **The stand-in draw in `core` produces exactly
that today** (E-L4-3), which is why the guard sweeps seeds × speeds × waits rather than checking the
fixture: every accepted instance has the dot under cover at the pulse, and the sweep fails if no
instance was refused, so it cannot quietly stop exercising the refusal.

Interrupt's three are the band running off the end of the bar (`DotColumns`' clipped column one
Subroutine along — the player answers the picture, correctly, and is graded against a band they were
never shown), a sweep at no speed, and **a phase outside the cycle, which is deliberately not
wrapped**: the house grades a position this client draws, so a parameter the client silently
reinterprets is the two of them disagreeing about where the sweep began.

### Neither class can be asked how the player did

`InterruptSweep` draws the band and draws the sweep and has **no `caught`, no `inside`, no
`wasRight`**. `DriftPath` renders the motion and holds no answer, though `at(askAtMillis)` is one
expression — which is the `SniffGroups` situation for the third time: the client must render, so the
client *can* compute; what must never happen on the device is the judgement. **The only place either
instance meets a tap in one expression is `theFixtureQuestionsHaveAnAnswerAndItIsNotOnThePhone`**,
which says so in its own name and never ships.

Two guards hold the softer version of it — that the echo may not *look* like a verdict. The word
sweep reaches both new screens (proved: an injected `COMPLETE` on a catch inside the band fails
naming both roles), and `theEchoIsTheSameMarkWhereverItLanded` counts the echo's own lit pixels, so
a mark drawn brighter for a good catch or wider for a tap near the dot fails by pixel count. A
verdict in this game is far more likely to be *a shade* than *a word*.

### The fixture that had to be a catch rather than a live sweep

`openingASubroutineAgainClearsEveryEntry` requires every entry in `SubroutineModel.sample` to hold
something — *an entry the fixture never touched is one whose clearing proves nothing* — and
Interrupt has no state between untouched and sent. So the sample catches the sweep **a third of the
way along, outside the band**, and that placement is asserted: a default frame marking the middle of
the band would read as this phone having said *well done*.

The mirror of it is asserted too, and it is S1's Deallocate lesson: **the parity sweeps drive
Interrupt to half way along the bar precisely because that is INSIDE the band** — the one frame a
screen tempted to grade could be caught on. Both facts live in the one test that owns the answer
key, so a fixture edited for some other reason cannot take the coverage with it silently.

### Looked at

`ui/build/screens/` at both aspect ratios, whole-screen, both roles: INTERRUPT untouched, caught,
and under both verdicts; DRIFT the same. One treatment decision came out of looking rather than out
of reasoning — **the bar field went from 64 units to 110**, within twenty of Drift's lane field,
because the two moving Subroutines should have the same weight on the glass or the roster's light
ladder acquires a second axis nobody rated.

**Simulator run, both screens, at real device metrics** — a temporary launch-screen change in
`Cheats.kt`, reverted by targeted reverse-replace and confirmed absent from the diff before
committing (`git diff --stat -- app/` empty). Status row level with the Island, nothing under the
cutout or the home indicator, DEBUG chip in place, no exceptions in the device log.

**Two screenshots a second apart, and the dot had moved.** That is the check the desktop cannot
make: `withInfiniteAnimationFrameMillis` behaves differently in the presence of a test policy, so
*the animation actually runs on a device* is a claim that had to be made on a device. It does.

**A bench artefact worth knowing about:** the cheat picker jumps straight to a screen without
`beganAgain()`, so INTERRUPT opens there already caught and spent. In play `beginSubroutine` clears
every entry at BEGIN, which is why the device shot of DRIFT is the live one.

**None of this says anything about the game.** The band, the speed, the phase, the layout and the
hidden wait are all the house's in play, the buzz is the house's, and this build has no house.

### The injections — twelve bugs, each watched to fail by name, each reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| INTERRUPT's sweep twice as fast for Insiders | *"INTERRUPT frame 1 — 768 pixels differ by role, first at (102,280)"* — **and the older parity sweep passed** |
| the sweep opening at a random offset (rule 4) | **2** — *"drew something different the second time through … reading a clock the harness cannot put back"* |
| a ten-second timeout on the sweep | *"a minute of waiting took a control off INTERRUPT, so something on it expired"* |
| DRIFT sending a tap made before the buzz | *"a tap before the buzz went to the house, which is an answer to a question nobody has asked"* |
| a fat mark for a catch inside the band | *"INTERRUPT draws 643 lit pixels for a catch inside the band and 213 for one outside it"* |
| a fat mark for a tap near the hidden dot | *"DRIFT draws 1147 lit pixels for a tap on the dot and 357 for one nowhere near it"* |
| the visible-at-the-buzz refusal removed | **2** — *"seed 0 at speed 9 buzzes 12 tenths in, and the dot is at 32 with the cover at [22..31, 48..57, 74..83]"* |
| a clipped band clamped instead of refused | *"INTERRUPT accepted [7, 14, 30] — the band runs off the near end"* |
| the sweep wrapping instead of bouncing | *"the sweep jumped from 99 to 0 at 5000ms — a bar that leaps at the edge is a wrap"* |
| DRIFT dropped from the dispatch | *"a tap on DRIFT reached nothing — it has a screen, a roster row and no wiring"* |
| the lane drawn inside its own touch surface | *"a tap at 0.8 of the lane drew its mark at 0.769 of it"* |
| INTERRUPT saying COMPLETE for a catch in the band | *"INTERRUPT/Resident after 1 tap(s) says COMPLETE"*, both roles |

Each reverted by **targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`**, with the runner refusing any
run whose output carries an `e:` line, refusing to inject into a tree whose target tests already
fail, and comparing each file **byte for byte** with its pre-injection bytes afterwards — S1's
addition, kept.

### Escalations and flags

**E-S2-1 · The band's WIDTH is a client fixture and the house has to hold the same number.** D-139
sends *band position, speed, phase* and leaves the width to playtest with the speed, so
`InterruptSweep.BAND_HALF` is written in `:ui`. That is only honest while nothing grades: **when
E-L4-3's tolerance grading lands, a client and an authority disagreeing about how wide the band is
would reject taps the player watched land inside it.** Either the width joins the parameters or the
constant becomes shared — a decision, not a discovery.

**E-S2-2 · Drift's entry is a position along the path, and the hit radius therefore is too.** Named
above. If the design wants a euclidean radius, the entry has to become two numbers and the lane
stops being the target — which is a different screen, not a different constant.

**E-S2-3 · A tap before the buzz is echoed rather than refused.** The design language supports
either; this took the dark-room reading. Worth a person's judgement, because it is the one place in
these two screens where the phone accepts a gesture it will not send.

**E-S2-4 · The elapsed clock keys on the composition, and should key on the instance.** A re-scan
re-draws band position and phase (D-139) and the layout and wait (D-140), and today a re-scan always
disposes the screen on the way to the marker, so the clock restarts for free. When the push exists,
`screenMillis` must restart on the instance id instead — one line, not expressible now because
nothing in `:ui` carries an instance identity.

**E-L4-3 stands and is now at its sharpest.** Both new readers refuse instances the stand-in draw
produces today, and **the grading is still equality where D-139 and D-140 rule tolerances** — inside
the band, within the hit radius. Every Subroutine that needs it is now built, so nothing is waiting
on anything else: **the per-kind draw and the tolerance grading are the whole of what is owed.**

**E-S1-2 is now bigger, not different.** Two haptic scripts live in `:ui` rather than `:model`
(Sniff's groups and Drift's *now*), and both move in one file move when the device unit lands.

### The roster is complete

**All ten of the design's Subroutines are built.** Replay, Parity Check, Short, Signal Trace, Jam and
Handshake (batch 1), Sniff and Deallocate (S1), Interrupt and Drift (here). `Subroutine.built` is
`Subroutine.entries`, `SubroutineModel.tap` has **no `else` branch left** — an eleventh Subroutine is
now a compiler error in the same change that adds the row rather than a screen that silently never
echoes — and every sweep in the module walks all ten.

What is *not* real is still the arrival: the four instances are fixtures on `SubroutineModel`,
written as the parameter lists the house sends, so the reader that will read the real one is the
reader being exercised now.

---

## Device — Unit D1

**Outcome: done, green, pushed, and it ran on the phone.** Commit **`0fbc4a9`** — *"Five long buzzes
on a real phone, and one place to get a pattern from"*. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`:
**tests=1386, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (model 450, ui 577, core 149, harness 107, platform
88, app 15), up from 1373 — **13 new** (6 in `HouseBuzzTest`, 5 in `HapticsTest`, 2 in
`CoreHapticsMotorTest`), read from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean, both
boundary checks clean. **No Event, Effect, schema row or `reduce()` was touched. THE FREEZE HELD.**

**Built: the iOS haptic actual, the effect-to-motor wiring above it, and a bench that drove both on
an iPhone 13 Pro.** D-135's five Longs, the Short, and the two Subroutine scripts that exist.

---

### There is one place to get a pattern from, and that is the whole of D-102 as code

D-102 says *the buzz is identical for every player — same pattern, same duration, including when an
Insider's own Revoke lands — or it is an audible tell in a silent house.* Phrased as a rule it is a
rule every call site can break by accident, and the symptom is one screen buzzing slightly longer
than the others in a room where nobody may speak and nobody can see.

So it is not phrased as a rule. **`HouseBuzz` holds one pattern definition per kind, the durations
are private, `script(Haptic)` is the only way out of a kind, and the platform seam takes a `Haptic`
rather than a number.** A call site cannot ask for a buzz of its own length because there is no
parameter for one. The `when` is exhaustive, so a sixth kind is a compile error until somebody
writes its pattern down — D-135 says adding one is *a decision, not a parameter*, and that sentence
is now enforced by the compiler rather than by a reviewer remembering it.

**`SniffGroups.PULSE_MILLIS` reads `HouseBuzz.SHORT_MILLIS` instead of carrying its own `90`.** It
was a literal sitting a few files from an identical literal — the arrangement D-106 already lost
once. D-140's own words are *"it is the same short buzz Sniff's groups are made of, because there is
one of them in this app rather than one per Subroutine"*; that sentence is now true structurally
rather than by two constants agreeing by hand.

**`HapticStep` moved `ui` → `model`.** That is **E-S1-2 closed**, and it was exactly the file move
the flag predicted: `platform` sees `model` and never `ui`, and the motor is on the far side of that
seam. Pure data, no role, no answer, not `@Serializable` — nothing puts a script on the wire; a
Subroutine's parameters travel and the client builds the script from them.

---

### `HouseBuzzer` is one exhaustive `when`, and it is deliberately the only funnel

An effect arrives; if it carries a `Haptic`, the motor plays that kind. Twelve of the twenty-nine
carry one. **Nothing branches on role, seat or content**, and there is no filter asking whether the
effect was *meant* for this phone — what reaches a client is `EmitSchema`'s decision, made once, on
the authority, and a second opinion on the device is rule 1's exact shape.

**It is not `PanelVals.BUZZING`, and joining them would be the obvious wrong move.** `ui` already
holds a set of screens that buzz and wiring *that* to the motor is one line. D-134 forbids it: the
buzz rides the effect rather than living in a table in `ui`, because a client-side lookup that could
say *"…and if it was you, buzz differently"* is the device deciding a game answer. `BUZZING` stays
what it is — a description the flow derives `houseDriving` from — and it reaches no motor.

The `when` being exhaustive is the schema's fail-closed direction at this layer: a new `Effect` does
not compile until somebody has written down whether it buzzes. The failure mode is *"my effect
doesn't buzz"*, found the first time it runs.

---

### CoreHaptics, not the feedback generators, and the reason decides it

`UIImpactFeedbackGenerator` is a fixed vocabulary of taps whose durations **Apple** owns. This
game's buzzes are durations **the design** owns: D-135's long haptic is long because a pocket must
be able to tell it from the short one, and `.heavy` is not a duration. Sniff's script — eight buzzes
and a pause, alternating — has no expression in the generators at all.

One `CHHapticEngine`, started once, under `autoShutdownEnabled` so a phone in a pocket for four
minutes between meetings is not holding the motor awake. Every buzz is **one continuous event at one
intensity and one sharpness** — duration is the only thing D-102 lets a buzz differ in, so it is the
only thing that varies. **The motor replaces rather than queues**: two events at once must not become
two buzzes in a row, because *how many times a phone went off* is precisely the channel D-156 closed
when F-003 found the reveal leaking through it.

**A target with no Taptic Engine goes silent and tells the log.** Not a throw: rule 6, and rule 5
behind it — a screen that blanked because the motor threw is indistinguishable from a revocation to
everyone looking at it across a dark room.

---

### The injections — four, each watched to fail by name, each reverse-replaced and byte-compared

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| a call site reaching past `HouseBuzz` for a pattern of its own | **2** — *"an event buzzed with a pattern of its own: `[[Buzz(millis=1500)]]` is neither the Short (`[Buzz(millis=90)]`) nor the Long (`[Buzz(millis=750)]`)"*, and *"an effect carrying Short was played as something else"* |
| the walk-in buzzing differently depending on the seat it reached | **2** — *"STAND AND WALK IN at Long buzzed differently by seat"*, and the kind test beside it |
| every delivered message buzzing | **2** — *"the reveal buzzed 5 time(s) for an Insider and 2 for a Resident — F-003 exactly"*, and *"an effect with no kind on it reached the motor anyway"* |
| the no-op actual refusing loudly instead of going quiet | **2** — `IllegalStateException` out of both silence tests |

Reverted by **targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`**, with S1's addition kept: after the
restore each file is compared byte for byte with what it was before, and a run that cannot restore
it exactly fails loudly. The runner refuses any run whose gradle output carries an `e:` line — a
compile error is not a test failure.

**What the recordings compare is scripts, not kinds.** A test asserting two call sites used the same
`Haptic` label would be asserting that they agreed about a word. *Identical for every player* is a
claim about what a room can feel, and a room feels durations.

---

### On the phone — iPhone 13 Pro, playtest build, `devicectl` console

The bench cannot be tapped by a unit running overnight, so it drives itself when the process is
launched with `SOMEONES_HOME_HAPTIC_SWEEP` set (`launchSwitch`, cheat-source-set only, and the KDoc
says in as many words that it is never configuration — balance locks at arming and stamps into the
recording, and nothing a round depends on may come from a launch environment). The sweep fires **the
same rows a finger fires**: the same effects, the same `HouseBuzzer`, the same motor.

```
[haptics] engine started
[haptic-bench] EgressOpened → Long
[haptics] LONG played — 1 event(s), 750ms
[haptic-bench] MeetingOpened → Long
[haptics] LONG played — 1 event(s), 750ms
[haptic-bench] StandAndWalkIn → Long
[haptics] LONG played — 1 event(s), 750ms
[haptic-bench] RestrainedTakeover → Long
[haptics] LONG played — 1 event(s), 750ms
[haptic-bench] MeetingEnded → Long
[haptics] LONG played — 1 event(s), 750ms
[haptic-bench] OpeningMessage → Short
[haptics] SHORT played — 1 event(s), 90ms
[haptic-bench] StandAndWalkIn → Short
[haptics] SHORT played — 1 event(s), 90ms
[haptic-bench] SNIFF script → 8 buzz(es), 15 step(s)
[haptics] SCRIPT played — 8 event(s), 2840ms
[haptic-bench] DRIFT script → 1 buzz(es), 2 step(s)
[haptics] SCRIPT played — 1 event(s), 2606ms
[haptic-bench] SWEEP DONE — only a hand proves a buzz
```

**Twenty-eight lines of console, no exception, no refusal, no `engine would not start`, and not one
`silent — no haptic hardware`.** Sniff's `3, 5, 8` came through as eight buzzes over 2840ms, which
is the fixture's own arithmetic arriving at the motor unchanged.

The **same binary in the Simulator** takes the other branch and takes it quietly —
`this target has no haptic hardware — every buzz will be a silent no-op`, then `LONG silent`,
`SHORT silent`, `SCRIPT silent`, and the app carries on. One class, one code path, no build flag
deciding which implementation a phone got.

**The screenshot is the Simulator's** (`shots/haptic-bench.png`) and that is the honest division
this repo already keeps: `devicectl` has no screenshot command, and a screenshot proves layout while
the device log proves the engine fired. Neither proves the third thing.

---

### ⚠️ FELT VERIFICATION AWAITS A HUMAN HAND

**I cannot feel the phone.** Everything above says the engine was asked and did not refuse. Nothing
above says what the phone *felt* like, and the two questions that matter most are both of that kind:
**is the Long unmistakable through a pocket, and are Sniff's two groups countable without looking.**

The phone is on the desk with the playtest build installed. Open it, tap the **PLAYTEST** chip
(bottom-left), then **HAPTICS**. In order:

1. **The five Longs** — EGRESS, INCOMING CALL, STAND AND WALK IN, RESTRAINED TAKEOVER, LIGHTS OUT.
   All five must feel **exactly the same**. If any one of them reads as heavier or longer than
   another, that is D-102 broken and the five stop being one signal.
2. **OPENING MESSAGE** (the Short), then **COUCH WALK-IN** immediately after. These are the *same
   effect* at the two kinds. The question is whether a Long and a Short are still obviously
   different **with the phone in a trouser pocket and your hand not on it** — that is the condition
   D-135's closed set is built for, not a phone held in the palm. `LONG_MILLIS` is **750** and it is
   a placeholder: playtest owns it, as it owns the 7.
3. **SNIFF** — three buzzes, a pause, five buzzes. **Count them without looking at the screen.** If
   the groups are not countable, D-137's magnitude judgment has no channel, and the two numbers to
   argue about are `PULSE_MILLIS` (90) and `PULSE_GAP_MILLIS` (220).
4. **DRIFT** — a long wait, then one short buzz. It should feel like the same short buzz as OPENING
   MESSAGE, because it is.
5. **STOP** — tap it during SNIFF. The script should cut mid-group rather than finish.

---

### Escalations and flags

**E-D1-1 · Nothing in the app reaches the motor except the bench, because nothing pushes effects
yet.** `HouseBuzzer` is real, tested and in `commonMain` — but the only construction of it is in the
cheat source set, so a **release build today has no haptic wiring at all**. That is correct rather
than broken: there is no transport-fed loop in the app root to wire it to. **The day the push
pipeline lands, `HouseBuzzer` moves to the app root beside it and the bench keeps its own.** Flagged
so it is a decision rather than a discovery.

**E-D1-2 · The Sync Pulse has no haptic script and one was not invented here.** L5's beat structure
lives in `core` as `Balance.SYNC_PULSE_BEATS`; there is no `HapticStep` script shape for it anywhere
in `ui`, the way Sniff and Drift have one. Writing one would be choosing a beat length and a gap for
a Subroutine whose feel is the whole mechanic — **a design decision wearing the shape of a
parameter**, and this unit had no ruling to read it off. The bench has no SYNC PULSE row for that
reason. It is one small addition once somebody decides what the beat is.

**E-D1-3 · `LONG_MILLIS` is a placeholder and only a hand can settle it.** 750ms was chosen to be
comfortably more than 2× the Short so that a pocket can separate them, and the test asserts exactly
that relationship rather than the number. **Playtest owns it.** If the morning's hand says the Long
is too subtle through denim, the constant moves and one test's threshold moves with it.

**E-D1-4 · The bench builds its own effects, which makes it the house's stand-in and nothing
better.** `FlowHost` carries the same caveat under `standingInForTheHouse`: the `Haptic` on each
bench row was written down in the cheat file rather than constructed by `Rules.kt` in the same
reduction that constructed the push — the arrangement D-134 forbids in play. What the bench proves
is the half below the effect: *given an effect carrying a kind, does this phone buzz that kind.*
Which kind rides which push is `core`'s, and `core` tests it.

### Not in this unit

**The torch, the lamp, BLE and the camera.** All hardware, none of it haptics. The one thing this
unit touched outside `:platform` and the app root is the `HapticStep` file move E-S1-2 asked for.

---

## Device — Unit D2

**Outcome: built, green, pushed, on the phone — and stopped one tap short of a scan.** Commit
**`56db5d2`** — *"The front camera reads the paper, and the paper the house prints"*.
`./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1410, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (model 450, ui 577,
core 149, harness 107, platform 95, cards 17, app 15), up from 1386 — **24 new** (17 in `:cards`, 7
in `platform`'s iOS test set), read from the test XML. `vocabularyLint` and `redactionLint` clean,
both boundary checks clean, `verify-guards.sh` **6 passed, 0 failed**. **No Event, Effect, schema
row or `reduce()` was touched. THE FREEZE HELD.**

**Built: the iOS camera actual behind U5's `CardScanner` seam, and the printable deck it reads
(story 4.11).** Plus a camera bench that drives both on an iPhone 13 Pro, and a host-side script
that reads the finished PDF back with Apple's own decoder.

---

### The lit screen is the scanner, so there is no preview and there must never be one

`ScanScreen` already says it in copy — *the lit screen is your light; your front camera reads the
card, so you will not see this while it works* — and gdd M1 says it as a requirement:
`AVCaptureSession` with a metadata output and **no preview layer attached**. `CameraCardScanner`
is that sentence as code. The session carries an `AVCaptureMetadataOutput` and nothing else, the
device is `AVCaptureDevicePositionFront`, and no `AVCaptureVideoPreviewLayer` is constructed
anywhere in the file. A preview would be a picture nobody can look at, drawn out of the frame
budget, on the one screen whose brightness is a game value.

One ordering detail is load-bearing and is commented as such: **`metadataObjectTypes` is set after
`addOutput`.** Before the output belongs to a session its available types are empty, so setting the
types first sets them to nothing — a scanner that runs, sees, and says nothing at all.

---

### It hands over the payload and asks nothing about it

U5's seam says the scanner yields *nine characters as printed* and that decoding is `CardPayload`'s.
That is enforced here by there being nothing else in the path: `stringValue` goes to the listener
**not trimmed, not upper-cased, not length-checked**, and a symbol whose `stringValue` is null goes
through as the empty string rather than vanishing.

That is what makes the refusals reachable. A foreign code — a URL, a wifi sticker, somebody's
boarding pass — is *delivered*, and `CardPayload.decode` refuses it with a reason the scan surface
already knows how to say (D-071). A scanner that tidied a payload first would turn a misread into a
card: `1A SEED01` trimmed is nine characters that look like ours. `CameraCardScannerTest` asserts
the bytes survive, and separately asserts that *the bytes surviving is what lets the screen say
something true about the piece of paper in the host's hand*.

---

### What the phone found: a camera that switches on behind a screen nobody is looking at

The first device run turned up a real defect, which is the only reason the run was worth making.
iOS asks for the camera **once ever**, in a dialog, and the answer arrives whenever the person
answers it — after reading a sentence, thinking about it, possibly walking into another room. The
sweep asked, waited twelve seconds, released the lens, and would have been handed a grant
afterwards: `begin()` fires from the permission callback, and it had nothing to consult about
whether anybody still wanted a camera. **A capture session would have opened behind a screen the
host had already left**, holding the lens and the ISP on a phone that has to last an evening.

`wanted` is now set by `start` and cleared by `stop`, and the callback checks it. It is deliberately
a second flag beside `listener`: a listener is *what a reader is told about*, and this is *whether
there is anything to read for*. `aScreenThatLeftWhileThePromptWasUpDoesNotStartACamera` is the named
test, and the injection that flipped `stop`'s assignment is in the table below.

---

### `:cards` — the printable deck, and it is a module because paper cannot be patched

A seventh Gradle module, **host-side, JVM only, and it ships in nothing**: it has no iOS target, so
nothing in it can ever be linked into the app. Everything else in this repository can be fixed in
the morning; a printed card is in somebody's hallway, so the numbers are all written down with the
reason attached and every one of them is read back by a test.

**Forty-four cards, four pages of US Letter, twelve to a page.** The reserved pair first — the T
card (D-120) and the U card (D-121, D-152) — so the two cards a host has to find are the two at the
top of page one, and they are the only two that say what they are, because they are the only two
whose shape is spoken for. Then the roster's 42, in roster order. A `100pt` symbol is 21 modules
plus its four-module quiet zone, which lands a module at about 1.2 mm of ink: D-069 said **buy scan
margin with card size, not with symbol version**, and that is what buying it looks like.

**THIS SIDE UP is printed on every card and it is load-bearing, not polite.** The roster kept
`semicircle_up`/`semicircle_down` and `arrow_up`/`arrow_down` — two pairs that are rotations of each
other — *only because the printed card says which way is up*. A sheet that dropped the line would
quietly collapse 44 shapes into 42, and the collisions would surface as a player standing in the
wrong room, inside the injected error the Terminal already carries on purpose.

#### A reprint hands out different ids, and that is the whole reason the id exists

D-069: a host who mislays a card and prints a replacement creates two physical cards showing the
same shape, and the old one found behind a shelf next year would report a player as standing in
whichever room the new one was registered to. **The id only saves anybody from that if the reprint's
ids are different.** So a printed id is **four characters of run tag and three of card number**, the
tag is fresh on every printing, and `-Prun=QK7M` reprints an old one — the single case where
repeating an id is right, a host reprinting the one card the dog ate.

#### One third-party library, and the reason it is there is the reason there are not two

`com.google.zxing:core`, host-side only. A QR encoder written by hand is a Reed-Solomon
implementation over GF(256), a mask-penalty search and a format bit sequence — a few hundred lines
no test here could meaningfully check, because the only way to know a symbol is right is to read it
back with something written independently. **Two QR implementations that disagree is D-070's
collision, printed.** The version is not requested and hoped for either: `QrSymbol` asserts
`versionNumber == 1` after encoding, because a tenth character or a stray punctuation mark silently
grows the symbol and the failure surfaces months later as *the cards do not scan like they used to*.

The **PDF is written by hand** — four pages of filled rectangles, filled paths and single-line
labels in a format that has not changed since 1993, against a dependency whose transitive tail would
sit in a repo with one host-side library and none in the app. It draws the glyphs from
`MarkerShape.path` itself, so the sheet and the screen are the same roster rather than two.

---

### Two things the first rendered sheet got wrong, both found by looking at it

Neither was caught by a passing test, and both are now.

- **The reserved word printed below the cut line.** TERMINAL and MEETING sat four points *outside*
  the rectangle a host is told to cut along — the two cards on the sheet that say what they are were
  the two that would have said it on the offcut.
- **`ORDINARY MARKERS ? ONE PER PLACE`.** An em dash in a line of copy, silently substituted by the
  single-byte encoding the font is declared with. Nothing failed and nothing warned.

`escape` now maps the two dashes and the curly quotes to the bytes WinAnsi actually holds them at
and **refuses anything else**, and `everythingPrintedOnACardIsInsideItsCutLine` measures every text
baseline against the cut rectangle rather than the cell. A printable that is wrong on paper and
green everywhere else is the whole class of bug worth guarding here.

---

### The injections — seven, each watched to fail by name, each reverse-replaced

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the camera tidying a payload on its way past (`payload.trim()`) | `everySymbolArrivesExactlyAsItLeftTheCamera` — *"the scanner changed a payload on its way past. Expected `[1A SEED01, 1aseed001, 1ASEED001 , …]`, actual `[…, 1ASEED001, …]`"* |
| a stopped scanner still wanting the camera | `aScreenThatLeftWhileThePromptWasUpDoesNotStartACamera` — *"a stopped scanner still wants the camera, so a grant arriving now opens a session behind a screen the host has walked away from"* |
| the generator accepting an id it cannot print | `aRunTagOutsideTheAlphabetIsRefused` — no `IllegalArgumentException` for `qk7m` |
| one shape missing from the sheet (`registrable.drop(1)`) | `everyShapeInTheRosterIsOnTheSheetExactlyOnce` — *"the sheet is not the whole roster expected:<44> but was:<43>"* |
| the glyphs filled non-zero (`f*` → `f`), so the ring becomes a disc | `everyGlyphIsFilledEvenOdd` — *"the sheet does not carry one even-odd fill per card expected:<44> but was:<0>"* |
| the reserved word back below the cut line | `everythingPrintedOnACardIsInsideItsCutLine` — *"a line on the T card sits at 585.0, below the cut line at 586.0 — a host who cuts where they are told loses it"* |
| a character the font cannot print, substituted silently | `aCharacterTheFontCannotPrintIsRefusedRatherThanSubstituted` — no refusal for `→` |

Reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, with the file hashed before and after.
**One restore failed loudly and that is worth writing down**: the `wanted = false` injection turned
it into a second `wanted = true`, so the reverse anchor matched twice and the runner refused to
guess — it printed `!!! FILE NOT RESTORED`, the line was put back by hand, and `:platform`'s tests
were re-run green before anything else happened. A restore that silently picked one of two would
have left a live defect in the tree wearing a green build.

---

### On the Mac — the finished PDF, read by Apple's decoder, card by card

`./verify-cards.sh` generates a sheet, rasterises it at 300 dpi the way a printer would, crops each
cell **out of the manifest the generator wrote** — payload, page and rectangle, from the same
`CardSheet.slotOf` the pages were drawn with — and decodes it with `CIDetector`, which is the
CoreImage family the phone's camera pipeline belongs to.

```
generating run VRFY...
reading cards/build/deck/someone-is-home-cards-VRFY.pdf at 300dpi, card by card...
PASS — 44 of 44 cards read back as the card they were printed from,
       each one out of the cell the generator says it drew it into.
```

**Card by card rather than page by page, and that was not a preference.** A whole page handed to the
detector came back with seven or eight of its twelve symbols and no indication which: a QR detector
is built to find *a* code in a scene, and a scene with twelve of them is not what it optimises for.
Cropping to the declared cell turns one ambiguous answer into forty-four unambiguous ones — and it
checks the position as well as the payload, so a card printed into the wrong slot fails here.

The command:

```
./gradlew :cards:sheet                 a fresh run tag, so fresh printed ids
./gradlew :cards:sheet -Prun=QK7M      that exact run again
./verify-cards.sh [run-tag] [dpi]      generate, rasterise, decode, diff
```

The PDF lands in `cards/build/deck/` and **is not committed** — the generator is.

---

### On the phone — iPhone 13 Pro, playtest build, and the wall it hit

```
[scan-bench] watching
[scan-bench] [scan] asking for the camera
[scan-bench] SWEEP — opening the session with nobody holding a card
[scan-bench] watching
[scan-bench] [scan] asking for the camera
[scan-bench] released
[scan-bench] SWEEP DONE — frames are not a scan; only a card held to the lens is
[scan-bench] watching
[scan-bench] [scan] asking for the camera
[scan-bench] LEFT WATCHING — tap ALLOW if the camera was never asked for, then hold a card up
```

**That is the whole log, and the honest reading of it is that the camera never started.** iOS asks
for the camera in a system dialog; `devicectl` has `install`, `launch`, `reboot`, `orientation` and
no way whatsoever to answer one. There is no `simctl privacy` for a physical device. The unit
reached the permission gate, asked correctly with the right string in the bundle —

```
$ /usr/libexec/PlistBuddy -c "Print :NSCameraUsageDescription" SomeonesHome.app/Info.plist
The house reads the printed cards through the camera. No picture is taken, kept or sent anywhere.
```

— and stopped there, because the next event in the sequence is a finger.

**So nothing below is claimed:** no `session running=true`, no `configured — front camera`, no
`[frames] 30 frame(s) delivered`. The frame counter exists precisely so that *the session started*
and *the pipeline is moving pixels* are distinguishable in a log from a room with nobody in it, and
it has not yet printed a line. **AN END-TO-END PAPER SCAN AWAITS A HUMAN HAND, AND SO DOES THE
FIRST FRAME.**

The phone was left in the best state available for that hand: **the app is running on the CAMERA
bench with the permission alert up and the scanner armed** (`LEFT WATCHING`), so the tap and the
card are the whole of the morning's work.

---

### ⚠️ THE TWO-MINUTE MORNING SCRIPT

1. **Pick up the 13 Pro and tap ALLOW.** The app is on the CAMERA bench with the alert waiting. If
   it has been closed, reopen it, tap the **PLAYTEST** chip, then **CAMERA**, then **START**.
   Expect on screen and in the log: `permission granted`, `configured — front camera '…'`,
   `session running=true`, and then `[frames] 30 frame(s) delivered` climbing about once a second.
   **If the frame lines do not appear, the pipeline is not delivering and everything else here is
   theatre** — that is the single most important line to look for.
2. **Get a card.** Either `./gradlew :cards:sheet` and print it at **100%, no scale-to-fit**, or
   open `cards/build/deck/someone-is-home-cards-VRFY.pdf` full-screen on the Mac and use the screen.
3. **Hold a card up to the TOP of the phone, screen facing the card.** The front camera is beside
   the screen, which is the whole point and also the thing that feels wrong the first time.
   Expect: `read '1AVRFY003' → circle · VRFY003` and **D1's Short buzz**. Reading it off a lit
   display is easier than off paper; if paper fails where the screen works, the number to argue
   about is `CardSheet.QR_SIZE` (100pt) and the fix is a bigger card, never a bigger symbol version.
4. **Hold up the T card and the U card.** Both should read and both should say `(reserved)`.
5. **Hold up something foreign** — a wifi sticker, a parcel label, any QR in the house. It must be
   *delivered and refused*, `refused: WrongLength`, not silently ignored. That is the camera
   declining to decide what a card is.
6. **Then the real screen.** PLAYTEST chip → the panel → walk to **SCAN MARKER**. The camera starts
   with the screen and stops when you leave it. Hold a card up: the viewfinder should register it,
   and a second card of the same shape should be refused by the map's own reason. The seeded
   shutter chip is still there beside it and still walks the tour of refusals no stack of paper
   guarantees.
7. **The distance question, which only a house answers.** Standing where a marker would actually be,
   at arm's length, by lamplight: does it catch? That is what the card size was chosen for and
   nothing on a desk tests it.

---

### Escalations and flags

**E-D2-1 · A refused camera is invisible to the host, and there is no channel to make it visible.**
If a host taps *Don't Allow*, the scan screen behaves exactly like one that is working: lit,
instructive, counting down, registering nothing. The `CardScanner` seam has no error path and the
freeze forbids a new `Effect`, so the refusal goes to the log and nowhere else. **This is host
setup in a lit room, where D-071 already allows the screen to be specific about a piece of paper** —
so a specific message is probably lawful here in a way it would not be in a round. It needs a
ruling, not a guess.

**E-D2-2 · The camera prompt must be warmed during setup, in the light.** iOS asks once ever, and
today the ask happens on whichever screen first opens a scanner. That could be the in-round scan, in
a dark house, mid-round — the same mistake the architecture already flags for
`NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription`, with a worse blast radius: a dialog appearing on one phone in a
dark room at the moment somebody scans is both a light nobody authored (rule 5) and a tell.

**E-D2-3 · This is the host-setup scan. E5's is a different thing and is not built.** Hold-to-scan,
the ~0.5 s acquire, the aim haptics, routing, check-in and the motion budget are stories 5.1–5.11.
What exists today is a continuous reader that starts with the screen. The seam's own KDoc says the
in-round scan *must not grow into* the setup one; wiring the same class into E5 without the hold
gesture would be exactly that.

**E-D2-4 · The printed id's shape is now decided by code, and no ruling covers it.** D-069 says the
id exists so a stale card is recognisable, which is only true if reprints differ — but *four of run
and three of card* was chosen here, not ratified. It is on paper the moment anybody prints, and the
decision log should either adopt it or replace it before that happens.

**E-D2-5 · `CardSheet.QR_SIZE` is a placeholder in the way `LONG_MILLIS` is.** 100 points was chosen
so a module lands near 1.2 mm, comfortably above what a phone camera needs on a desk. Whether it
catches at arm's length in a dark corridor is a playtest question, and the answer moves the card
size rather than the symbol version.

**E-D2-6 · `:cards` is a seventh module, and the module list is the architecture.** It is host-side
with no iOS target and ships in nothing, and `settings.gradle.kts` says so where the list is. Named
here so that it is a decision somebody agreed to rather than a module that appeared.

### Not in this unit

**The torch, the lamp, BLE, and E5's motion budget.** The one thing this unit touched outside
`:platform`, the cheat source set and the new `:cards` is `iosApp/Info.plist`, which needed the
camera usage string or the app is stopped on the first frame it asks.
