## Every escalation, L1 through D2, in one place

**Thirty-one raised. Ten are already closed** — nine by the owner's rulings in revisions 32 and 33,
one by a later unit. **Twenty-one are open.** The closed ones are listed too, because a reader
coming to this cold needs to know which questions are settled as much as which are not.

### Closed — do not re-litigate

| # | what it asked | closed by |
|---|---|---|
| E-L1-1 | does an out player's live meter plus the presence plane add up to a role oracle? | **D-145** — the out watch everything live; the residual correlation is the ghost's game |
| E-L2-1 | the tie: plurality, or D-131's *majority-decided* read strictly? | **D-144** — plurality, Skip is a candidate, ties resolve to Skip; the parity veto survives |
| E-L2-2 | the Egress is a stub; a writer and a pausable timer are owed | **L5** (`7f2c1c1`) — both paid |
| E-L2-4 | who counts as unanimous for READY TO VOTE | **D-147** — the living only |
| E-L3-1 | the band's floor above eleven seats; revision 29 disagreed with itself | **D-143** — `max(1, ⌊seats/6⌋)`; two Insiders at twelve seats |
| E-L3-2 | a re-scan does not re-draw the question | **L4** (`2ab47b3`) — the question moved onto the scan |
| E-L3-3 | F-005's other half: orphaned Subroutines auto-satisfied? | **D-146** — nothing auto-satisfies; `K × Residents` plus slack is the whole rescue |
| E-L5-5 | an uncontained Egress does not end the round | **L6** (`ecc005b`) |
| E-S1-2 | the haptic script is in `:ui` and the device unit will want it in `:model` | **D1** (`0fbc4a9`) — `HapticStep` moved, exactly the file move the flag predicted |
| A4's one provisional ruling | is the meeting card `u_shape`? | **D-152** — ratified |

### Open — the twenty-one

**Wire-shaped, and every one of them blocked by the freeze rather than by a question (5).**

1. **E-C2-1 · Nothing on the wire says where a piece of work is.** `OrderLine` carries no card and
   no room in either case. The drawn design puts a room and a marker glyph on every work-order row
   and on the springboard widget — the two things a player in a dark house navigates by — so the
   moment a real order arrives both surfaces lose the destination on every row at once. Needs
   `MarkerId` on `OrderLine.Known` **and** a marker→room map the client does not have.
2. **E-C2-2 · Nothing carries System Integrity to any client.** D-134 gives the couch live Resident
   progress and no effect carries the meter at all. The shape is right (percentage, computed off
   its own bar); the sender is missing. L6 item 4 is the same gap from the endings' side.
3. **C1 · The vote window cannot reach arming.** `LobbyLink` has no `arm`; `LobbyBody` has no
   arming case; `Event.RoundArmed` carries no settings. The host's `VOTING` row moves a number
   nothing reads. **This is L2's escalation 5 with the mechanism named.** Same shape as F-009's
   Egress-timer setting.
4. **E-L4-1 · The presence row records the CARD, not the room name** — a deliberate deviation from
   L4's own brief, to keep `core` free of host-authored geography. If the spectator map wants the
   room inside round state, `RoundArmed.markers` has to carry marker→room and the format changes
   again.
5. **L6 · Seat-to-name resolution for the reveal is the transport's and is not built.** The effect
   carries seats and lines; `PanelState.revealed` carries names and lines; nothing joins them.

**Numbers somebody has to own (6).**

6. **E-L5-1 · The tick rate is decided in `Balance`, at 20 Hz, and nobody has confirmed it.**
   `FixedTimestep` refuses a default on purpose; `Balance` had one silently, at 1 Hz. This makes it
   explicit. It moves `REVOKE_COOLDOWN` from 60 ticks to 1200 — the same sixty seconds **only if
   the rate is what this now says.** The sharpest of the six.
7. **E-L5-2 · `EGRESS_COOLDOWN` at 180s is invented.** `EGRESS_TIMER`'s 120s is F-009's; this is not.
8. **E-L5-3 · The Sync Pulse's shape is guessed** — 1.5s beat, 400ms window, 2s lead, an
   11-second run of four beats. Only *four taps* and *2–3s lockout* come from the design.
9. **E-S2-1 · Interrupt's band WIDTH is a client fixture** (`InterruptSweep.BAND_HALF`, in `:ui`)
   and the house has to hold the same number. Honest only while nothing grades: when tolerance
   grading lands, a client and an authority disagreeing about the width rejects taps the player
   watched land inside the band.
10. **E-D1-3 · `LONG_MILLIS` is 750 and is a placeholder.** The test asserts the *relationship*
    (comfortably more than 2× the Short) rather than the number. Only a hand settles it.
11. **E-D2-5 · `CardSheet.QR_SIZE` is 100pt and is a placeholder**, chosen so a module lands near
    1.2 mm. Whether it catches at arm's length in a dark corridor moves the **card size**, never
    the symbol version (D-069).

**Rulings owed on behaviour (6).**

12. **E-L4-3 · The grading is equality where D-139 and D-140 rule tolerances** — inside the band,
    within the hit radius — and the stand-in draw in `core` knows nothing about what its three
    numbers mean, so it can draw an equal-groups Sniff or a Drift whose dot is visible at the buzz.
    Both clients refuse those by name. **Every Subroutine that needs it is now built, so nothing is
    waiting on anything else: the per-kind draw and the tolerance grading are the whole of what is
    owed.** The single largest open item in the loop.
13. **E-L1-2 · A revoked player's correct entry still banks.** `returned()` does not ask whether
    the actor is revoked. Unreachable in play, accepted by the rules, and the fuzzer does it.
    Deliberately not guarded — it belongs with whatever answers per-seat round-state gating.
14. **E-L5-6 · Containment requires having scanned the node's card.** Falls out of D-136 and is the
    only way the house can know where anybody is, but nothing in the GDD says the node has to be
    scanned, and a player who walks to the right room and taps is told `held=false` with no reason.
15. **E-S2-3 · A tap before the buzz is echoed rather than refused.** The design language supports
    either; S2 took the dark-room reading (a control that swallows a tap in silence is
    indistinguishable from a flat phone). The one place in these two screens where the phone
    accepts a gesture it will not send.
16. **E-D2-1 · A refused camera is invisible to the host, and there is no channel to make it
    visible.** *Don't Allow* leaves the scan screen lit, instructive, counting down and registering
    nothing. This is host setup in a lit room, where D-071 already allows specificity — so a
    specific message is probably lawful here. **It needs a ruling, not a guess.**
17. **E-D2-2 · The camera prompt must be warmed during setup, in the light.** iOS asks once ever,
    and today the ask happens on whichever screen first opens a scanner — which could be the
    in-round scan, in a dark house, mid-round. A dialog appearing on one phone in a dark room is
    both a light nobody authored (rule 5) and a tell.

**Instruments and structure (4).**

18. **⚠️ L6 · The differential harness had to be narrowed**, and it is the one change all night
    that makes a leak instrument see *less* than it did: `diff` now compares two role-swapped runs
    only up to the first ending in either. The reason is that parity counts living plain Residents
    against living Insiders, so **the round ending is arithmetic on the assignment** — exchanging
    two seats in the fixture ends the second round a meeting early and reported 411 divergent
    lines, 283 of them "unexplained" and none actionable. The argument that it is not a channel —
    in any real round exactly one assignment exists, nothing follows the ending, and the reveal on
    that very message states the alignment outright — **wants a second reader to agree or reject.**
19. **E-S1-3 · The parameter read belongs at the effect boundary and is at class-load.** Today a
    malformed fixture fails the build, which is the strongest place available while nothing pushes
    an instance. When the push exists, `SniffGroups.of` and `DotColumns.of` move — and the refusal
    must stay loud to the authority and silent to the player.
20. **E-S2-4 · The elapsed clock keys on the composition and should key on the instance.** A
    re-scan re-draws band position, phase, layout and wait; today a re-scan always disposes the
    screen, so the clock restarts for free. One line, not expressible until `:ui` carries an
    instance identity.
21. **E-L4-4 · `Transcript.escape` does not escape the colon**, and marker ids can contain one —
    the payload alphabet is QR alphanumeric minus SPACE. Two different states can render
    identically. A state-row injectivity hole rather than a forged-event hole; the same fault the
    pipe and the comma each had, and the third time this class has appeared.

**Recorded rather than escalated, but do not let them silt up.**

- **E-L2-3** · `MeetingResult` and `MeetingResolved` both reach the out — two kinds because the
  living may have one and not the other; the redundancy is the price.
- **E-D1-1** · Nothing in the app reaches the motor except the bench, so **a release build today
  has no haptic wiring at all.** Correct rather than broken; the day the push pipeline lands,
  `HouseBuzzer` moves to the app root.
- **E-D1-2** · The Sync Pulse has no haptic script and one was **not** invented — choosing a beat
  length for the Subroutine whose feel *is* the mechanic is a design decision wearing the shape of
  a parameter.
- **E-D1-4** · The haptic bench builds its own effects, so what it proves is only *given an effect
  carrying a kind, does this phone buzz that kind.*
- **E-D2-3** · This is the **host-setup** scan. E5's in-round scan — hold-to-scan, the ~0.5 s
  acquire, aim haptics, routing, check-in, the motion budget — is stories 5.1–5.11 and is not built.
  Wiring this class into E5 without the hold gesture is exactly what its KDoc forbids.
- **E-D2-6** · `:cards` is a seventh module, host-side, no iOS target, ships in nothing.
- **E-L4-2** · The screen still says NOT YOURS / *"Nothing of yours opens here"* where D-124's
  vocabulary is NOTHING FOR YOU HERE. One string, one test.

### Still-wrong documentation, from revision 33's own sweep

Six sites the docs batch could not take, listed so they are not lost: `gdd.md`'s *Open the Status
panel* / *Adjust the lamp* rows (**needs a ruling** — D-142 moved arming to page 2 and said nothing
about whether a Status panel survives for the lamp dial); the tier layout described as the halves
of that panel; three orphan-auto-satisfaction sites plus the F-005 line at the head of the decision
log (covered by D-146, unswept); `epics.md` 7.15 *Graceful collapse* (**no covering ruling either
way**); F-001's *Beacon/Tether differ in … the containment beat* (retired by D-149); and `epics.md`
6.2 calling Sniff *haptic counting* when D-137 made it a magnitude comparison with no count at all.

## Every flagged design treatment

**The three the brief singled out, first.**

**1. ⚠️ S1's Sniff answer gesture — the most aggressive treatment in the port.** D-137 left the
gesture to the builder. What was built: the screen is drawn **in full and painted black** — header,
both halves, the return slot, the motion row, SUBMIT and STOP NOW all in the place they occupy on
the other nine Subroutine screens, every colour `Amber.Black` until the player answers, and
**nothing moves at the moment it lights**. The answer is the two halves of the panel, left for the
group that buzzed first; the target is half a phone, with no precision and no boundary to find. The
tap does not send — SUBMIT does. Two things want a person in an actual dark corridor: **STOP NOW is
live from the first frame and cannot be seen** (the claim is that this is what STOP NOW already
was, pressed by muscle memory rather than read), and **there is no instruction on the screen and
there cannot be one** — *which group was bigger* is a sentence made of light, so it has to live on
`ScanCaught` before BEGIN (E-S1-1, unbuilt, and a screen S1 was not asked to touch).

**2. C2's blocked-entry treatment — the established one, and now structurally impossible to
loosen.** Outlined box, `LOCKED`, `WAITING UPSTREAM`, **no light mark**, full-length row.
`OrderRow.Blocked` carries one integer mirroring `OrderLine.Blocked`, so no composable and no later
refactor can put a name on one. Worth the strictness because **D-146 made dependencies
cross-player**: a blocked entry that named its Subroutine would name *the person upstream of you*
as surely as the work. The empty light column reads as *nothing is claimed* rather than as a dark
Subroutine, because nothing on the ladder is zero cells.

**3. S2's four choices, all flagged at the source.** *Interrupt has no SUBMIT* — a confirmation
step would ask the player to ratify a moment already seconds gone, so the entry type carries it
(`SequenceEntry` of length one, whose rule already is *an entry goes when it can no longer change*)
and the screen cannot forget to send. *The whole body is the control*, because a button under the
bar puts the finger where the eye is not on the one Subroutine where **when** is the entire answer.
*Drift's entry is a position along the path, not a point on the glass* (E-S2-2) — a tap taxed for
missing a line the screen already draws would grade a blind one-handed finger on something the
question never contained, and it keeps the entry one integer on L1's canonical shape. *A tap before
the pulse moves the mark and sends nothing* (E-S2-3) — the dark-room reading. Plus: **the motion
stops when the entry goes**, or a dot still drifting would emerge from cover at a known speed and
hand back the answer; and **the canvas has no inset**, which is a guard rather than a preference,
because a picture drawn narrower than its own touch surface puts the mark where the finger was not.

**The copy that is drafted, register-ruled, and waiting on somebody to agree with the words.**
Every one is a named constant, so settling it is one edit rather than six.

| where | the strings |
|---|---|
| L1 · the Subroutine verdict | `THE HOUSE GROWS WEAKER` / `REJECTED . RESCAN THE MARKER`. The register is not a draft: success is a **damage report**, house-voiced — never *"the house has it"*, because the house is not on the residents' side. The failure line's *rescan* is load-bearing rather than helpful: D-110 makes the walk back the price of a wrong answer, so the line has to say where the player must stand |
| L2 · the ballot | `READY`, replacing LOCK IN; `VOTE CAST` once locked |
| C1 · the ballot's pre-lock label | `HOLD TO CAST`, with `HOLD THE BUTTON FOR TWO SECONDS.` under it — the Delete screen's sentence, now a constant, so the gesture is learned once in the light and met four more times in the dark |
| C1 · page 2 at rest | `READY . ALL NOMINAL` (Resident) and `READY . HOLD TO ARM` (Insider) — **same nineteen characters**, replacing a Resident line that invited a press on a tile that now takes no pointer input |
| C1 · the notice | `SWIPE UP TO DISMISS` — a line of text with no border and no tap target, and copy nobody has ruled on |
| C2 · an order with nothing actionable | `NOTHING OPEN` — unreachable by construction, written anyway, and deliberately **not** D-124's NOTHING FOR YOU HERE, which is a scan's answer about a card |
| L6 / revision 33 | **`NEW ROUND` takes no two-second hold**, and its label is copy-pending. D-141's argument is about a control pressed *in the dark*; this one is pressed in the light with everyone watching |

**Presentation calls with no covering ruling (C2's four, plus two).**

- **The couch's vote window is 60 while the living's is 45.** Two numbers for one clock, in a lit
  room where the couch can see a living player's phone. Left alone deliberately: the 60 is what
  reproduces the design's drawn picture (`VOTING ENDS IN 0:24` over twelve lit segments of thirty),
  and moving it to 45 redraws that same `0:24` at sixteen.
- **`NEXT SUBROUTINE` is the queue D-114 replaced with a menu, surviving as a heading.** The house
  designates nothing; the widget names the first actionable line so the slot is not blank.
- **ARRAY WIPE is on the work-order list and the house can never send it** — it is a circuit, not
  one of the roster's ten, has no `SubroutineKind`, and is drawn by D-122's station draw at arming.
  It survives only in the port's fixture. Whether it belongs on that list is unruled.
- **`MarkerShapes.MEETING` is `u_shape`** — was provisional, **ratified by D-152**. A U is a couch
  seen from above, and it is a letterform like the T so the two reserved cards read as a pair.
- **THIS SIDE UP is printed on every card and is load-bearing, not polite.** The roster keeps two
  pairs of shapes that are rotations of each other (`semicircle_up`/`_down`, `arrow_up`/`_down`)
  *only because the card says which way is up*. A sheet that dropped the line would collapse 44
  shapes into 42, and the collisions would surface as a player standing in the wrong room.
- **E-D2-4 · The printed id is four characters of run tag and three of card number**, and no ruling
  covers it. D-069 says the id exists so a stale card is recognisable, which is true only if
  reprints differ — the shape was chosen in code, not ratified. **It is on paper the moment anybody
  prints.**

## The screenshots — eighteen, and what looking at them found

`_bmad-output/overnight/shots2/`, all at real device metrics on an iPhone 17 Pro via
`./run-app.sh simulator playtest`. `simctl` still has no tap, so the instrument was the same one
U11 used with one improvement: instead of one marked line rewritten per screen and one build per
shot, a single marked `SHOT-DRIVER` block read `ScreenId.entries` against `launchSwitch`, so **one
build served all eighteen** and each shot is a `simctl launch` with an environment variable.
`SH_SHOT_INSIDER` sets the role, `SH_SHOT_CAST` calls the ballot's own `readyToVote()`, and
`SH_SHOT_FRESH` calls `beginSubroutine`'s `beganAgain()`. Every line carried the marker; the
restore was a **targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`**, and afterwards `git diff` is empty
and `git grep SHOT-DRIVER` finds only this worklog's own prose.

| shot | what it is |
|---|---|
| `vote-hold` `vote-cast` | the ballot before and after the hold — one composable through both states, so the thing under the thumb never moves |
| `page2-resident` `page2-insider` | D-142, side by side |
| `work-order` | seven lines with two blocked ones |
| `couch-meeting` `couch-round` | GhostMeeting and Ghost3 |
| `sub-sniff` `sub-sniff-untouched` `sub-deallocate` `sub-deallocate-untouched` `sub-interrupt` `sub-drift` | the four new Subroutines, Sniff and Deallocate in both states |
| `ending-residents` `ending-insiders` | both endings, as a Resident |
| `ending-residents-as-insider` `ending-insiders-as-insider` | the same two on the phone the house speaks to |
| `lobby-subroutines-each` | the derived row |

**What is right, briefly.** `VOTE CAST` sits at exactly the height `HOLD TO CAST` did, with the
note under it changing from `HOLD THE BUTTON FOR TWO SECONDS.` to `THIS PHONE HAS VOTED. IT CANNOT
BE TAKEN BACK.` and the control dropping to its spent outline. Page 2's two roles are the same
layout, the same brightness and the same slot, down to `READY . ALL NOMINAL` and `READY . HOLD TO
ARM` occupying identical width. The work order's two blocked rows draw `LOCKED` / `WAITING
UPSTREAM` with the light column empty. Ghost3's readouts are `65%` and `68%` over bars lit to 21
and 22 of 32 — the numbers and the bars agree, which is the whole of C2's fix. Both endings print a
percentage and no total (`0%`, `43%`), and `WinResidents` swaps the status row's filled iris for the
hollow one. **The untouched Sniff on a real panel is a black rectangle with a D-077-dimmed status
bar and nothing else** — the ruling made visible, and also exactly what a screen that failed to
draw looks like, which is why the answered frame is beside it. The lobby's `SUBROUTINES EACH` reads
`NOT YET` on an empty lobby rather than the `1` the arithmetic floor produces.

### ⚠️ Visually off — recorded, not fixed

1. **The house's sign-off card is amber on the bone ending.** `HouseSignOff` hard-codes
   `Amber.Dim`, `Amber.Faint` and `Amber.Bright` (`ScreensOut.kt:578`) and is drawn on **both**
   endings — but `WinResidents` is a lights-on bone screen where every sibling got a bone variant:
   `BoneSummaryRow`, `BoneNamedInsider`, `NewRound(Amber.BoneInk, Amber.BoneDim, border =
   Amber.BonePale)`. So `UNFORTUNATE.` is the one amber element on a sage-and-slate screen. See
   `ending-residents-as-insider.png`. **It reaches only an Insider's phone**, which is why no
   Resident-role render caught it and why a two-role layout sweep would not either — the sweep
   compares geometry, and this is a palette. One `HouseSignOff` call needs the bone palette passed
   in, the way `NewRound` already takes it.
2. **`EGRESS . TRUE` on the couch reads like an Egress type and is not one.** Ghost3's literal
   (`ScreensOut.kt:353`) means *the true Egress number* — the adjective, matching `TRUE OCCUPANCY`
   at the foot of the same screen. But the springboard widget draws `"EGRESS . ${vals.egressType}"`,
   where the second half **is** the type (`EGRESS . BEACON`), and L5 changed it from a hard-coded
   literal to the house's word precisely because Beacon and Tether are mechanically identical. Two
   meanings in one shape, one screen apart. A copy call, not a bug.
3. **The couch's status carrier reads `UNREGISTE…`.** A bench artefact — jumping to `GhostMeeting`
   with no `outBy` renders the fallback, and in play the carrier is only ever REVOKED or
   RESTRAINED. Worth recording anyway, because it shows the field truncates at eleven characters
   and nobody has checked that RESTRAINED (ten) is the longest word that will ever go there.
4. **Interrupt and Drift claim very different amounts of glass.** S2 raised Interrupt's bar field
   from 64 units to 110 so the two moving Subroutines would have the same weight, and the *fields*
   do now match. But Interrupt outlines the **whole body** as its control (correct — the tap is the
   entry, and a target under the bar would put the finger where the eye is not) while Drift outlines
   only the lane, so side by side one screen reads as a full-height panel and the other as a strip.
   Both are as ruled; the pair has not been looked at together until now.
5. **The list screens leave the lower half of the panel empty.** The ballot's six rows end 35% down
   a 6.3" panel; the work order's seven end at 40%. Content is top-anchored, controls are
   bottom-anchored, and the black between them is the largest single area on both screens. On a
   game whose whole discipline is *make less light* that is arguably right, and it is the most
   consistent thing across the set, so it should be right **on purpose** rather than by default.
6. **The bench opens Subroutine screens already answered and spent** — S2 recorded this and it
   still holds: the cheat picker jumps without `beginSubroutine`, so `sub-sniff` and
   `sub-deallocate` show the lit states. That is why `SH_SHOT_FRESH` exists and why the untouched
   pair is in the set. Not a defect; a frame that would be misread without this note.

**Two things I thought I saw and did not.** A second `PLAYTEST` chip under the status row, and a
duplicated status row at the foot of the bone screens. Both were artefacts of reading a downscaled
render; cropping the regions out of the original pixels at full resolution shows neither. Recorded
because the next person looking at these frames will see the same thing, and because a screenshot
pass that reports phantoms is worth less than one that reports nothing.

## What is waiting for a person this morning

### 1. The phone, for the things only a hand can settle (~5 minutes)

The iPhone 13 Pro has the playtest build on it. **PLAYTEST chip → HAPTICS.**

1. **The five Longs** — EGRESS, INCOMING CALL, STAND AND WALK IN, RESTRAINED TAKEOVER, LIGHTS OUT.
   All five must feel **exactly the same**. Any one heavier or longer than another is D-102 broken.
2. **OPENING MESSAGE, then COUCH WALK-IN.** The same effect at the two kinds. The question is
   whether a Long and a Short are still obviously different **with the phone in a trouser pocket
   and your hand off it** — that is the condition D-135's closed set is built for. `LONG_MILLIS` is
   750 and is a placeholder (E-D1-3).
3. **SNIFF** — three buzzes, a pause, five buzzes. **Count them without looking at the screen.** If
   the groups are not countable, D-137's magnitude judgment has no channel; the numbers to argue
   about are `PULSE_MILLIS` (90) and `PULSE_GAP_MILLIS` (220).
4. **DRIFT** — a long wait, one short buzz. It should feel like OPENING MESSAGE, because it is.
5. **STOP during SNIFF.** The script should cut mid-group rather than finish.

### 2. The camera, which stopped one tap short (~2 minutes)

`devicectl` cannot answer an iOS permission dialog and there is no `simctl privacy` for a physical
device, so D2 reached the gate, asked correctly, and stopped. **The app was left running on the
CAMERA bench with the alert up and the scanner armed.**

1. **Tap ALLOW.** Expect `permission granted`, `configured — front camera '…'`, `session
   running=true`, then **`[frames] 30 frame(s) delivered` climbing about once a second. If the
   frame lines never appear, the pipeline is not delivering and everything else is theatre** —
   that is the single most important line to look for.
2. **Get a card**: `./gradlew :cards:sheet` printed at **100%, no scale-to-fit**, or open
   `cards/build/deck/someone-is-home-cards-VRFY.pdf` full-screen on the Mac.
3. **Hold it to the TOP of the phone, screen facing the card.** The front camera is beside the
   screen — the whole point, and the thing that feels wrong the first time. Expect
   `read '1AVRFY003' → circle · VRFY003` and D1's Short buzz.
4. **The T card and the U card** — both read, both `(reserved)`.
5. **Something foreign** — a wifi sticker, a parcel label. It must be **delivered and refused**
   (`refused: WrongLength`), never silently ignored.
6. **Then the real screen**: PLAYTEST → the panel → SCAN MARKER. The camera starts with the screen
   and stops when you leave it.
7. **The distance question, which only a house answers.** Standing where a marker would be, at
   arm's length, by lamplight: does it catch? Nothing on a desk tests it, and the answer moves
   `CardSheet.QR_SIZE` — the card size, never the symbol version.

### 3. Two decision-log diffs to read

- **`1ff2c98` — revision 32**, D-141 through D-147: the two-second hold family, page 2, the band
  floor, plurality, the ghost's game, cross-player dependencies with nothing auto-satisfied, and
  unanimity among the living.
- **`404452e` — revision 33**, D-148 through D-158: F-001 ratified, Beacon/Tether identical in v1,
  F-002 amended to exclusion, the shared Egress cooldown, `u_shape` ratified, **no screen ever
  prints a total**, the meter moves on completions only, ghosts invisible by state, F-003 closed,
  NEW ROUND, and degrading Subroutines deferred to v2.

Both were written by the run and neither has been read by their author.

### 4. The rulings that unblock the most work, if you only answer three

1. **E-L4-3 — the per-kind draw and the tolerance grading.** Ten Subroutines are built against a
   stand-in that draws three meaningless integers. This is the largest single thing between the
   loop and a playable round.
2. **E-L5-1 — is the tick rate 20 Hz?** Every duration in `Balance` now reads through it.
3. **E-D2-4 — the printed id's shape.** It goes onto paper the first time anybody prints, and paper
   cannot be patched.

### 5. And the evening that is still waiting on hardware

**The two-phone D3 evening awaits the second 16 Pro.** Nothing in this run touched BLE, the torch,
the lamp or E5's motion budget, and the spectator map (D-136) has stayed frozen since L1 named it.
A single phone on a desk has now been taken about as far as it goes.

## Loop — Unit L7

**E-L4-3 is closed, and with it E-S2-1 and E-L1-2.** The house's draw knows what it is dealing, the
grading knows what a near miss is, and the band on the glass is the band the house grades against.
1428 tests, 0 failures (`core` 166, `model` 225 ×2, `ui` 371 desktop + 207 native, `harness` 107,
`platform` 95, `cards` 17, `app` 15). `DeviceLayoutTest` green. No phones.

### What moved, and the one structural decision behind all of it

**The parameter readers left `:ui` for `:model`.** `SniffGroups`, `DotColumns`, `InterruptSweep`,
`DriftPath`, `ParityGrid`, `SignalGraph` and `MalformedSubroutineParameters` are now
`model/SubroutineQuestion.kt`, joined by four new ones — `ReplayPattern`, `HandshakeRhythm`,
`ShortHold`, `JamRings` — so **all ten kinds have a reader that refuses by name.**

The move is what makes the rest possible, and it was forced twice over. *The draw must be checkable
against the clients' refusals*, and no module could see both: `harness` cannot see `:ui`, `:core`
cannot see `:ui`, and `:ui` cannot see `:core`. And *the house has to compute the answer from the
picture the client draws* — the odd cell of a grid, the shortest route across a lattice, where a dot
is behind cover — which cannot be a second implementation without being a second implementation to
get wrong. `model` is the one place both ends see.

**What did NOT move is the judgement.** `answerTo` and `grades` are in `:core`, which `:ui` cannot
see, so a screen still cannot ask *which cell breaks the pattern* or *was that tap inside the band*.
`OpenSubroutine.accepts` is gone: what is left on the model type is `standingAt`, the half of grading
that is a fact about a place. The other half became a rule the moment it became a tolerance.

### 1. The per-kind draw (`core/Subroutines.kt`)

`drawParameters(kind, draw)` deals each kind's own shape inside each kind's own bounds; `answerTo`
answers the question it just dealt **by reading it with the client's own reader.** Three consequences
worth stating:

- **A tie is not rejected and re-rolled, it is inexpressible.** Sniff draws a smaller group and a
  gap and then chooses a side. A rejection loop would be a draw whose length depends on what it drew,
  which is a stream that stops replaying the day the bounds move.
- **Drift's wait is drawn inside the room the seed and the speed left**
  (`DriftPath.hiddenTenthsAvailable`), because how long the cover lasts is a fact about the layout.
- **Signal Trace deals only lattices with exactly one shortest route.** Equality grading plus two
  optimal routes is a player walking a correct route and being refused — D-137's tie in another
  Subroutine's clothes. The client cannot refuse those (computing the optimum is the one thing
  `SignalGraph` exists not to do), so the house never deals them: `traceSeeds` sifts 512 seeds at
  class-load and 100+ survive.

### 2. The tolerance grading, and the one number that closes E-S2-1

Equality for eight; **band membership** for Interrupt; **hit radius** for Drift. The tolerances are
**parameters** now: Interrupt is 4 numbers (`bandAt, bandHalf, speed, phase`) and Drift is 4
(`seed, speed, hiddenTenths, radius`). `InterruptSweep.BAND_HALF` is deleted. The client renders
`bandFrom`/`bandTo` from the number it was sent, and `SubroutineModel` carries `sweep` and `path` on
the **instance** rather than the companion, so what a screen draws is what arrived — which is where a
pushed instance will arrive, and what makes the rendering claim testable.

Drift's radius rides the parameters too and is deliberately **not drawn**: it is centred on the
position the player is being asked for, so a radius on screen is the answer's neighbourhood painted
around the answer.

### 3. The revoked-seat gate (E-L1-2)

`returned` now asks `state.isOut`. Rule 1's shape and not its forbidden one: an out seat's return
emits the same three effects with the same fields as a **refused** living seat's, the meter does not
move, and the allowlist already declines delivery — two independent denials, neither visible as an
absence. The test asserts the differential against a wrong answer rather than a correct one, because
`SubroutineProgressed` is emitted when the meter moves and the meter did not.

### 4. Injections — seven, each reverted after watching it fail

1. Sniff draws equal groups → `no Sniff is dealt with two groups of the same size` **and** the fuzz
   oracle **and** `the drawn answer grades as accepted` — 6 failures.
2. Drift's wait drawn past the cover → `no Drift is dealt whose dot is visible at the buzz` + 5.
3. Interrupt graded by equality → `a tap inside the band is caught and a tap outside it is not`
   (1 failure, and it is the one-integer-off assertion that catches it).
4. Tolerance widened by one step → both tolerance tests, both edges.
5. `isOut` gate removed → both revoked-seat tests.
6. `isOut` written as an early return → the shape differential, alone: the meter test still passed,
   which is precisely why rule 1 needs its own assertion.
7. The band drawn from the old `8` → `theBandIsDrawnFromTheWidthTheHouseSentAndNotFromAFixture`
   (`:ui` desktop, 371 tests, 1 failed).

### 5. The tick rate (E-L5-1, closed)

20 Hz, confirmed by the owner 2026-08-22. `Balance`'s ⚠️ is gone and the number is asserted in
`SubroutineDrawTest`, beside the assertion that **no Subroutine parameter is in ticks** — a wait is
tenths, a buzz is milliseconds, a cooldown is ticks, and three clocks in one system fail by being
multiplied together.

### 6. Escalations this unit opens

1. **⚠️ E-L7-1 · Handshake's rhythm is drawn, sent and played — and graded as a COUNT.** The entry
   carries the ordinal of each tap and nothing about when it landed, so a rhythm returned at any
   tempo passes. The timing is not in the entry and cannot be graded until it is. This is the one
   place in the roster where the question the player is asked is strictly richer than the answer the
   house can read.
2. **E-L7-2 · `answerTo` reads through the client's reader, so a malformed draw would throw inside
   `reduce`.** Unreachable by construction and proved so by the fuzz, and the posture is the intended
   one — loud to the authority, and never on a client-visible path (rule 6). Recorded because *the
   rules can throw* is a sentence somebody should have agreed to.
3. **E-L7-3 · The fuzz oracle is circular for BOUNDS and not for RELATIONS.** Draw and reader share
   their limits now, so *inside 1..7, refuse outside 1..7* proves nothing. Everything the stand-in
   actually got wrong is relational — the groups differ, the dot is hidden, the band fits, no dot
   flashes twice — and those are properties of what was drawn. Stated in the test's own KDoc so
   nobody reads more into a green fuzz than it says.
4. **E-L7-4 · The placeholder values are now numerous and they are all mine.** Sweep speed 11–17,
   drift speed 10–15, hit radius 3–12, band half 4–25, Sniff gap 1–3 and groups ≤7, Handshake 3–6
   beats with gaps of 0.2–0.9s, Jam's opening ±3–12, Deallocate's floor spread. **Not one has been
   played with.** The structure is what this unit owns; every one of these is playtest's.
5. **⚠️ E-L7-5 · Jam had E-S2-1's fault too, and it was found by giving the house an opening to
   draw.** `JAM_OPENING` was a third size in `ScreensSubroutines` — eleven steps of daylight on the
   glass — while `JamRings.opening` is what the house grades. The press that visibly put one ring on
   the other would have been refused, on the Subroutine the design calls *slow, forgiving,
   satisfying*. The screen now sizes the ring at the target plus the drawn opening, so the rings
   coincide exactly at the answer. **Two of these have now been found the same way; nobody has swept
   for a third.** Every other kind reads one fixture list per Subroutine, which is single-source but
   is on the companion rather than the instance — `sweep`, `path` and `rings` are the three that
   moved to the instance because a number the client *draws* is graded.
6. **E-L7-6 · Six client fixtures became parameter lists, and Replay's is a pattern nothing plays.**
   `REPLAY_PARAMETERS` is a real order now (`2, 0, 3, 1`) and the screen still does not flash it —
   the flash is an Effect and the push does not exist, which is the deliberate hole `ReplayScreen`
   already documents. The fixture is right and the picture is unchanged; when the push lands the
   pattern is already there to play.

## Device — Unit D3

**Outcome: done, green, pushed — and the evening ran.** A physical iPhone hosted a house on the
owner's Wi-Fi; the other physical iPhone and three Mac instances found it by mDNS, seated, named
themselves, handed their lines over, and the gate walked
`NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS → WAITING FOR EVERYONE'S LINE → READY` one seat at a time until LIGHTS OUT went
live. `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1433, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (model 450,
ui 578, core 166, harness 107, platform 95, cards 17, app 20), read from the test XML — **5 new**,
all in `LineWatchTest`, `app` going 15 → 20. Both lints clean (`vocabularyLint` across
`model`/`core`/`ui`/`app`/`cards`, `redactionLint clean — 45 file(s)`), `boundary: ui clean` and
`boundary: core clean`, and both other variants swept.

**No `Event`, `Effect`, schema row or `reduce()` was touched. THE FREEZE HELD.** What is new is one
`expect`/`actual`, one cheat that presses the buttons a finger presses, one probe that watches the
socket, one defaulted parameter on the app's lobby link, and one more frame in the screenshot viewer.

Running the same binary a second time found the best thing on this page, which is E-D3-1.

---

### The finger the second phone did not have

D1's bench exists because a buzz has no picture. This one exists because **a second phone has no
finger**: discovery, joining, a name going up and a line being handed over each need a person on one
device while a person is on the other, and a unit driven from a Mac has neither. `devicectl` can set
an environment on launch and nothing else, so that is what the evening is made of.

- `platform/.../Haptics.kt` — **`launchValue(name)` beside `launchSwitch`**. A flag is enough for a
  bench that does the same thing every time; five instances differ only in what they are called,
  which home they are looking for and what their owner typed, and a bench that hard-coded those
  would be one phone's evening compiled in five times. Its KDoc carries `launchSwitch`'s warning and
  sharpens it, because a *value* is so much more obviously configuration than a flag is. It is not
  configuration: what the callers take from it is a resident's name, a home's name and a line —
  three pieces of **input a finger would have supplied**, none of them a balance value, none of them
  read by any rule.
- `app/src/cheats/.../LobbyCheat.kt` — the evening. **Every step is a call a screen already makes**:
  `FlowModel.go` is the navigator, `nameResident` is the field on JOIN, `hostHome` is HOST WITH THIS
  HOME, `attachToHome` is a tap on a network row, `handOverLine` is HAND IT OVER, `cycleInsiders` is
  the INSIDERS row, `lightsOut` is the slate button. There is no second route into the lobby here
  and no state it reaches that a screen does not, which is what makes the log evidence about the app
  rather than about the bench.
- `app/src/cheats/.../LineWatch.kt` + `LineWatchTest` — the probe and its positive control.
- `app/.../LobbyOverTransport.kt` — **one defaulted parameter, `onInbound`, empty in production**,
  handed every body arriving at this client before anything is done with it.
- `ui/src/desktopTest/.../ScreenshotTest.kt` — `lobby-two-phones`, the frame two real phones reach.

### What the logs are, and the one thing they are not

The lobby's numbers are read back out of the same `LobbyModel` the composition draws from, so
`2 JOINED · 2 OF 2 HANDED THEIRS OVER` is the text on the screen rather than a second opinion about
it. **A log is still not a photograph**: it says what the screen was told to draw and never what it
drew. `devicectl` has no screenshot command — D1 recorded the same division — so the rendered proof
is `lobby-two-phones.png`, and the phones' own screens still want an eye.

**The one line is never printed**, at no level, not even a prefix of it. A line in a device log is a
line on a phone. What is printed is that one went and how long it was, which separates *nothing was
typed* from *something was typed and did not arrive* without putting anybody's confession in a file
on a Mac. `nothingItSaysEverContainsTheLine` is the assertion that keeps it that way, fragments
included — U6's reason, one layer down.

---

### Five seats, and the phone in the middle of them

Host: iPhone 16 Pro, `vadManuel-Phone`, at `192.168.1.189`. Seats: iPhone 13 Pro `Manuel's iPhone`,
plus three Mac instances. Playtest build on all five.

```
PHONE HOST
[lobby-bench] EVENING — HOSTING 'THE BUNGALOW' AS 'ELLIOT'
[INFO] (io.ktor.server.Application): Responding at http://0.0.0.0:50562
[lobby] HOSTING 'THE BUNGALOW' ON :50562        [lobby] ADVERTISED AS 'THE BUNGALOW'
2 JOINED · 2 OF 2 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA]                        · NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS · LOBBY
3 JOINED · 3 OF 3 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS]                · NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS · LOBBY
4 JOINED · 4 OF 4 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI]          · NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS · LOBBY
5 JOINED · 4 OF 5 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE]    · WAITING FOR EVERYONE'S LINE · LOBBY
5 JOINED · 5 OF 5 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE]    · READY · LOBBY
[lobby-bench] LIGHTS OUT — READY=true · NO REFUSAL
[lobby-bench] ARMED THIS PHONE ONLY — ON ARMED

THE OTHER PHONE
[lobby-bench] SAW 'THE BUNGALOW' AT 192.168.1.189:50562
[lobby-bench] SEATED — 2 JOINED · 1 OF 2 HANDED THEIRS OVER · [ELLIOT, PRIYA]
[lobby-bench] INBOUND — 12 FRAME(S) {STANDING=12} · 0 CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE
```

**`192.168.1.189` is the load-bearing string on this page.** It is the host phone's address on the
owner's Wi-Fi, resolved by four other processes — not `127.0.0.1`, which is every previous run of
this code. U6's `LobbyLoopbackTest` ran one host and two clients through a real socket on one
machine; this is the first time the ends were different pieces of hardware.

| what the unit asked for | what happened |
|---|---|
| discovery across the real LAN | `SAW 'THE BUNGALOW' AT 192.168.1.189:50562` on four separate seats |
| join | five seated, and the count agreed on every screen at every step |
| the resident name riding up | the host lists `[ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE]`; it typed one of them |
| the one line reaching the desk | `linesIn` climbed 0→5, one per seat, and nothing else moved |
| never in a client-bound frame | every seat: `{STANDING=n} · 0 CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE` |
| names and counts on both phones | the same standing printed on both, off the model the screen draws |
| the Insider setting cycling and clamping | `UNKNOWN → 1 → UNKNOWN → 1`, band `1..1`, never outside it |
| LIGHTS OUT refused at 2 seats | `REFUSED: NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS`, and the button stayed inert |
| LIGHTS OUT armed at 5 seats | `READY=true · NO REFUSAL`, then **ARMED THIS PHONE ONLY** — see E-D3-3 |

**The refusal is the pass, and it is rendered rather than only computed**: `lobby-two-phones.png`
draws the lobby at the count two real phones produce — `2 JOINED` over `NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS`, LIGHTS
OUT dim beneath it — because what needed an eye is whether that frame reads as an instruction rather
than as a fault. It does. *(One artefact of the viewer, not of the screen: the fixture leaves YOUR
ONE LINE at REQUIRED while the count above says 2 of 2, a combination no real phone can produce.)*

**`SUBROUTINES EACH` moved with the party on real hardware** — 6, 11, 9, 8, 8 as the seats filled.
That is L3's `Balance.orderSize` being asked rather than the design's `7` being printed, seen on a
phone rather than in a test.

### The probe: a verdict, and never a body

`LobbyWire` already makes the promise structurally — `Standing` has no `String` field to put a line
in, so *the lobby leaked a line* is not a bug that can be introduced by filling one in. That is the
real guarantee and `LineWatch` does not replace it. What it adds is the half a Mac cannot see: five
processes on a real network is where a wrong turn in the host's broadcast would show, and **from a
Mac an absent leak and an absent connection look identical**. So the count of frames is as
load-bearing as the count of carriers — *zero carriers out of zero frames* is what a phone that
never connected reports, and it is the answer this probe is most likely to give by accident.

The obvious instrument logs the frame and lets somebody read it. **That instrument writes the thing
it is guarding onto a Mac the first time it works** — the leak, arriving through the leak detector.

### The injections — four, each watched to fail by name, each reverse-replaced

Two on a phone and two in the test suite, because the probe is the one new guard here and a guard
with no positive control is the standing U6's never-persisted sweep refused for itself.

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the host echoing a `Handover` back down to every client | on **both phones**: `!!! A CLIENT-BOUND FRAME WAS HANDOVER · CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE=true`, and `INBOUND — 11 FRAME(S) {STANDING=9, HANDOVER=2} · 1 CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE` |
| the alarm narrowed to undecodable bodies | `aHandoverComingBackDownIsCaughtAndNamed` — *"the echoed handover did not set the alarm off. Expected `<1>`, actual `<0>`"* |
| the alarm printing the body it caught | `nothingItSaysEverContainsTheLine` — *"the probe printed the line it is guarding: … `{"type":"handover","line":"i still have priya's spare key"`"* |
| *(the first injection, again, after the fix below)* | the alarm reached the console rather than only the screen |

Reverted by targeted reverse-replace, never `git checkout`, and the sources were grepped for the
marker before committing.

**The first phone injection nearly passed for the wrong reason, and that is worth writing down.**
`LineWatch` was handed the transport rig's log, which draws to the screen and does not print — so
the one line in this app that shouts reached the screen of a phone nobody was holding and **never
reached the console**. The injection was caught, but by its summary line rather than by its alarm.
The wiring was changed and the injection re-run before anything was believed. Same family as A4's
and L3's instrument faults; the fourth member of it.

---

### ⚠️ E-D3-1 · The same binary, launched twice, strands every phone — and says nothing

**Run 1**, fresh containers: everything seated in under a second. **Run 2**, same build, nothing
changed, phones untouched: the client discovered the host over mDNS correctly and then **nobody
seated — not the client, and not even the host's own loopback client.** Ninety seconds of
`NOT SEATED YET` on both phones, with the server up and `Responding at http://0.0.0.0:49744` in the
host's own log. **Run 3**, after clearing the containers: the host seated instantly again. The only
variable between 2 and 3 is what was stored on the phones — and the host's loopback seat needs no
permission and no radio at all, which removes every other explanation.

**A phone that played a round presents its stored seat token on the next launch; a host that has
brought a new house up has never issued that token; the resume is refused; and the lobby says
absolutely nothing.** `LobbyOverTransport.onPhase` acts on `Seated` and on nothing else, so
`ClientSession.Phase.Dismissed` reaches no log, no screen and no host.

It is D-084's failure shape wearing a lobby's clothes, and it is **rule 6 exactly inverted: silent to
the player and silent to the authority**. What a player sees is a lobby that never fills, forever,
with no reason. What a host sees is a party that never arrives. It will happen on the second evening
of every household that plays twice.

**Not fixed, and the easy half is not the hard half.** Reporting `Dismissed` through the same
`onEvent` the token-not-saved failure already uses is one line; **what the lobby should say to a
player whose seat was refused is a decision about what a client receives**, and the freeze is on.
Note the token is doing its job — D-087 wants a resume to present *that* seat and a house that never
issued it must refuse — so the bug is entirely in the silence, never in the refusal.

`LobbyCheat.FORGET_SWITCH` exists because of this and says so in its KDoc. **Its first version was
wrong in an instructive way**: it cleared the token from a `LaunchedEffect`, one composition after
`LobbyOverTransport`'s constructor had already read it, so the phone reported `TOKEN FORGOTTEN` and
then presented the token anyway. It is acted on in `CheatRoot` before the link is built.

### ⚠️ E-D3-2 · Two homes with one name: the advertisement fails outright, and the host is not told

```
[lobby] ADVERTISE FAILED {NSNetServicesErrorCode=-72001, NSNetServicesErrorDomain=10}
```

`-72001` is Bonjour's **collision** error: a house called THE BUNGALOW was already on the air, so the
second one **never got published at all**. Its host sat in a lobby with one seat in it and no reason
given; every other phone searched for a home that was not there.

This contradicts something the code already believes. The nearby list dedupes by address and port
**and never by name**, with the reason written at the call site: *two households on one network may
both have called theirs THE BUNGALOW.* The screen is built for a duplicate name that the radio
cannot publish. One evening in a block of flats, or one household running two phones as hosts by
accident, and the second host is invisible with no symptom.

A ruling is wanted on both halves: whether the advertisement should carry a disambiguator (Bonjour
will auto-rename if asked), and — the part that matters more — **what the host's screen says when
its own house could not go on the air.** Same shape as E-D2-1: a refusal that reaches only a log.

### ⚠️ E-D3-3 · Five seats armed one phone, because there is nothing to arm five with

`LIGHTS OUT — READY=true · NO REFUSAL` and then `ARMED THIS PHONE ONLY`. That is not a shortfall of
the run; it is the state of the tree, and the bench prints it in those words on purpose.

L3 built `armingFor` and flagged that it has no production caller; `FlowModel.lightsOut` is
presentation only and its KDoc says so — it walks *this* phone to ARMED and tells nobody anything.
**There is no host→client effect push for any effect** (L2 and L3 left the same seam), so the arming
draws and the opening message cannot reach a second seat today, at any party size. What five seats
proved is that **the gate opens on the house's own counts and the button goes live** — which is real
and was worth five processes — and it is not what the unit's item 4 asked for. Recorded rather than
quietly reported as something else.

### ⚠️ E-D3-4 · An uninstall resets Local Network, and mDNS then fails with no symptom whatsoever

Clearing the containers for E-D3-1's experiment reset iOS's Local Network grant. On the next run the
host's `ADVERTISED AS` line was simply **absent** and the client logged nothing at all from its
browser for a minute — no error, no prompt, no empty-result event. Exactly the fail-closed OS
behaviour `Info.plist` already carries a comment about, met in the wild. It took two taps from a
person to clear, and the advertisement went out the moment they happened.

This is E-D2-2 arriving for real, and the good news is where it arrived: **on setup, in the light**,
which is the one place it is survivable. The warning stands and is now evidenced rather than
predicted — the same dialog at arming, in a dark house, is both a light nobody authored and a tell.

### A seventh source directory, and the sweep that demanded it

`LineWatchTest` was written into `app/src/commonTest`, both cheat-carrying variants went green, and
**`variantSweepRelease` refused the build**: release does not compile the cheat source set, so a
test for a cheat is an unresolved reference in the one variant that has to build without them. That
is story 0.10b's fail-closed arrangement working on a case nobody had hit — the sweep exists because
a plain `check` certifies one variant of three and reports green, and this is the second time it has
earned itself.

The answer is `app/src/cheatsTest/kotlin`, wired into `commonTest` by the same
`if (variant != "release")` that wires the cheats into `commonMain`. **The tests are now compiled
exactly where the thing they test is compiled**, which is the only arrangement that cannot drift.

### Two smaller things

- **The console carries two bare `0.5` lines** on a host when a second seat connects. The whole tree
  has exactly one `println` outside the benches, so they come from a dependency. Noted so the next
  person does not go looking for them.
- **The machine's WindowServer crashed mid-unit and took the filesystem with it.** For about
  twenty minutes every process on this Mac — shell, python and the editor alike — got `EPERM` on
  anything under the project directory while *writes* still succeeded, which is a macOS
  Files-and-Folders grant, not any sandbox this session controls. It cost an escalation and a wait.
  **There is an untracked `crash-log.txt` sitting in the repository root**, 4,279 lines, written at
  16:35 and reporting a WindowServer stackshot at `15:17:04` — the minute the reads stopped. It is
  not this unit's file and has been left where it was found rather than deleted or committed;
  somebody should decide whether it wants a `.gitignore` line or a bin.
- **A `devicectl` install can leave a phone with no app at all.** One `run-app.sh` run reported *"it
  launched and failed"*, and the app was in fact **not installed** — `process launch` answered
  `NSLocalizedFailureReason = The requested application is not installed`. Re-installing fixed it.
  The script's liveness check is honest about the symptom and cannot tell those two apart.

### Not in this unit

**The torch, the lamp, BLE, the camera and the loop.** The lobby's transport is pre-arm bookkeeping
and stays that way here: no `Event` was minted, no `Effect` left anywhere, no schema row was
consulted, and nothing was redacted, because before arming there is no round to redact anything out
of.

---

## Docs batch 4

**Decision log revision 34 — D-159 through D-171 — records the owner's daytime Q&A of 2026-08-22,
and the fossil sweep that goes with it.**

The scan gesture is corrected at the root (**D-159**): tap SCAN, turn the lamp onto the code, and
the subroutine opens by itself on acquire — the front camera shares the lamp's face, so the player
cannot see their own screen while scanning and a hold-and-release gesture was asking them to judge a
read on evidence pointed at a wall. The ported ten-second window was always right and now bounds an
unattended read. **D-160** gives the house a word for *no*: one vibrate yes, two vibrates no, and a
phone call vibrates LONG–pause–LONG until answered, which amends D-135's five-longs set.

**D-168 deletes the Status panel and the lamp dial** — revision 33's flagged-not-ruled item, now
ruled. Carry state moves to the work-order widget, which says on its face that carrying blocks every
other entry. That took four fossils out of `gdd.md`, the tier table's *top and bottom half* among
them.

**Six of this run's escalations close:** E-D2-1 and E-D2-2 to **D-161** (no seat until every
permission is granted, at setup, in the light; mid-round flips become house notices), E-D2-4 to
**D-169**, E-L5-6 to **D-162**, E-S2-3 to **D-165** (an early Drift tap is graded REJECTED and
spends the attempt), and L6's harness question to **D-166**.

**This unit's four escalations are recorded in the log as open, awaiting rulings** — E-D3-1, E-D3-2
and E-D3-3 as open items, **E-D3-4 as D-161 met in the wild**, on setup, in the light, which is the
one place it is survivable.

Also: **D-163** one vote clock at 45 s (`Countdown.kt`'s ghost 60 is now a code site owed a sweep),
**D-164** the Array Wipe circuit as a real work-order entry, **D-167** the ratified numbers,
**D-170** the new vocabulary word **SHIFT**, and **D-171** authorizing W1's six wire widenings.
Story 7.15 is deleted, `epics.md`'s ±50 ms becomes ±150 ms, and the empty-lower-halves layout
question is closed as correct-on-purpose under the minimise-lit-area rule.

**`crash-log.txt` was left exactly where this unit found it.** Still untracked, still undecided.
