## Restore

**Outcome: done, green, and the evening is back up.** Two rulings landed — **E-D3-1 to D-172** and
**E-D3-2 to D-173** — both injection-verified, and the evening restored on the same five seats D3
ran, on the same two phones.

`./gradlew check`: **tests=1445, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (model 450, ui 588, core 166,
harness 107, platform 95, app 22, cards 17), read from the test XML — **12 new**, and the arithmetic
is worth one line because it is not 6: `StaleSeatTest`'s two are `app` (20 → 22), and the four in
`LobbyModelTest` are `commonTest`, so they run on **both** `iosSimulatorArm64Test` and `desktopTest`
— 4 × 2 plus `HostSetupInputTest`'s two desktop-only ones is the +10 that takes `ui` 578 → 588. Both
lints clean (`vocabularyLint` across `model`/`core`/`ui`/`app`/`cards`, `redactionLint clean — 45
file(s)`), `boundary: ui clean`, `boundary: core clean`, and both other variants swept.
`verify-guards.sh`, run in the same worktree: **6 passed, 0 failed** — both vocabulary injections,
the unmarked-wire-type injection, both boundary injections and the release cheat-reachability
injection all rejected as they should be.

**No `Event`, `Effect`, schema row or `reduce()` was touched. THE FREEZE HELD.** Nothing here is the
loop: the lobby's transport is pre-arm bookkeeping, and before arming there is no round to redact
anything out of.

### The unit ran beside another unit, in one working tree

The first thing this unit found was not a bug in the tree, it was **a second agent writing to it.**
At 16:57 `git status` was clean but for `crash-log.txt`; by 17:00 six `model/` files and a new
`Destination.kt` had been written, and two compiles of `:model` minutes apart failed with *different*
errors — `Unresolved reference 'VOTE_WINDOW_SECONDS'`, then two non-exhaustive `when`s in
`EmitSchema.kt`. Neither was this unit's; both were snapshots of somebody else's edit in flight.

It is **W1, D-171's six wire widenings**, and by 17:14 it had reached `ui/.../Lobby.kt` — a file this
unit was editing in the same minutes — adding `LobbyLink.setVoteWindow`.

**Nothing about that was worked around quietly.** It was reported to the lead twice, the whole change
was kept as a patch outside the repository, and the verification was moved somewhere the collision
could not reach it: **a detached git worktree at `9d1f287`, with this unit's patch and nothing else
in it.** W1's `setVoteWindow` was removed from the copy of `Lobby.kt` in that worktree, so what the
numbers below describe is HEAD plus these two rulings — no W1, no half-landed widening, nothing this
unit did not write.

That is also where the app on the phones was built from, and for the same reason: **the build the
owner will pick up is one whose behaviour was verified**, not one carrying another unit's unfinished
work into a dark house.

### E-D3-1 → D-172 · the stale seat, dropped where nobody can see it

The refusal was always right and stays untouched: `ClientSession` still presents *that* seat or
nothing, still goes terminal, and `SeatLedger.resume` still answers a token it never minted with
`UnknownToken`. **What is new is everything after the refusal.**

- `LobbyOverTransport.onPhase` now has a `Dismissed` arm. On `UnknownToken` it forgets the stored
  token, builds a fresh `ClientSession`, and reconnects — a Hello, from a phone that has never
  played. Nothing reaches a screen: `onStanding` is the only channel this class has to a lobby, and
  it carries counts.
- **Once.** `mayStartOver` is false on the second attempt. A Hello cannot be refused for holding an
  unknown token, so the recursion could not run twice anyway — but a bound that is an argument about
  somebody else's protocol is not a bound, and `theFreshJoinIsTriedOnceAndARefusedStrangerStopsThere`
  is the one that would notice.
- **Only `UnknownToken`.** A locked round and a full house refuse a stranger for the reason they
  refused the token; starting over on those is a phone knocking at a door it has been told is shut.
- `TransportHost` gained `onRefused`, and `LobbyHouse` wires it to the host's log. **The reason, never
  the token** — a host's log is not where the one thing that identifies a seat belongs, and D3 already
  caught the shape of that mistake once in `LineWatch`.
- The client records it too (`REFUSED BY THE HOUSE — UnknownToken`, then `THE STORED SEAT IS NOT THIS
  HOUSE'S — JOINING FRESH`). `onEvent` is a console-and-rig channel; in release the lobby is not wired
  to a screen at all, so this is loud to the authority and silent to the player, which is rule 6 the
  right way up for the first time on this path.

The one structural change is `SeatStore` — the three top-level token calls behind one interface, with
`DeviceSeat` as the default. It exists so that *a phone carrying a seat from a house that no longer
exists* is a state a test can construct. Without it the ruling could only ever be checked by playing
two evenings.

### E-D3-2 → D-173 · the home that could not go on the air

**HOST WITH THIS HOME is a refusable control now** — the shape SAVE HOME and HAND IT OVER already
have, and it is the same reason in a different coat: where the button lands depends on an answer the
screen does not have.

- `HostAdvertiser` gained `onAir` and `onFailed`. **Exactly one fires, and neither at `start()`** —
  the iOS actual already implemented `didNotPublish` and threw the reason away in a log line.
- `advertiseFailureOf(code)` maps the OS's number to the two answers a *host* can act on. It is in
  `commonMain` with `BONJOUR_COLLISION = -72001` beside it, so the one decision in this path is
  testable without a network stack under it; `iosMain` only pulls the code out of the error dictionary.
- `LobbyHouse.start` no longer calls `onUp` when the socket binds. It calls it when the home is on
  the air, and on a refusal it **takes the server back down** — a bound port under a home nobody can
  find is not a house, and leaving it up would make the next attempt under a better name fail for a
  second, unrelated reason.
- `ScreenId.HomeDetail` loses `Lobby` from `ScreenGraph.exitsOf` and gains it in `Flow.viaActions`,
  which is `ScreenGraphTest` reading the screen correctly rather than a bookkeeping edit: the button
  no longer names where it goes.
- The refusal is drawn on the detail screen beside the store's own, in the same ink. Two lines rather
  than one merged line because they belong to two different things and at most one is ever set.

**The words** are `ANOTHER HOME HERE IS ALREADY CALLED THE BUNGALOW` and `THIS HOME DID NOT GO ON THE
AIR`. The first is the refusal a host will really get, and it is one RENAME away — **the RENAME row is
on that same screen**, which `aHomeThatCannotGoOnTheAirRefusesInTheLightAndStaysPut` asserts is still
there. A refusal that is honest and leaves no way out is a worse screen than the one it replaced.

**D3's own log is where this bug is visible**: in `host.log` from the 16:25 run,
`[lobby] ADVERTISED AS 'THE BUNGALOW'` arrives **three lines after** `SEATED`. The host was joining
its own lobby before the home was on the air. That ordering is now reversed.

### The third outcome, which neither ruling names

The ruling names a refusal, and **the OS does not always refuse.** With the Local Network grant gone
iOS publishes nothing and reports nothing — no error, no prompt, no callback (E-D3-4, and the very
first device run before it). Waiting on two callbacks that may both never arrive would have turned
HOST WITH THIS HOME into a button that does nothing when pressed, which is a *worse* failure than the
one being fixed and would have been shipped in the name of fixing it.

So there is a third end: `ADVERTISE_PATIENCE_MILLIS = 10_000`, after which an unanswered
advertisement is refused as one. **Exactly one of three outcomes always reaches the host.** Bonjour
answers in well under a second on a working stack — D3's own logs have the publish landing between
two lobby lines — so this is a bound on a callback that never comes, not a timeout on a slow network.

**Ten is a number nobody ruled, and it wants the owner's eye.** The owner ruled two outcomes; the OS
has three, and this is the third — an extension of D-173 made to keep the ruling honest rather than a
decision handed down. It is the one number in this unit with no authority behind it. Two questions
for a person: is ten seconds the right wait for a host standing in a lit room with a party in front
of them, and is `THIS HOME DID NOT GO ON THE AIR` the right thing to say to somebody whose real
problem is a permission they can still grant. Recorded under D-173 in the decision log as built but
not ruled.

**It is also the one piece of this unit with no test behind it**, and that is said plainly rather
than left to be discovered: `HostAdvertiser` is an `expect class` built inside `LobbyHouse.start`, so a
silent advertiser cannot be injected without a factory seam this unit did not think was worth adding
mid-collision. On the simulator and on both phones the advertiser answers, so the positive path is
device-proven below and the silent path is reasoned. Flagged for whoever wants it closed.

### The injections

Three, each watched to fail by name, each reverse-replaced by targeted string swap — never
`git checkout`, which in a worktree whose files are deliberately not HEAD would have taken the whole
unit out with the injection.

| injected | what failed |
|---|---|
| the automatic rejoin, skipped | **both** `StaleSeatTest` tests, by timeout at 30 s — the phone stranded exactly as it was on 2026-08-21 |
| HOST WITH THIS HOME walks to the lobby whatever the radio said | `aHomeThatCannotGoOnTheAirRefusesInTheLightAndStaysPut` — *"the lobby opened over a home the radio never put on the air expected:`<HomeDetail>` but was:`<Lobby>`"*, and `theEdgesTheActionsLayerOwnsAreEdgesItReallyWalks` with it |
| the house records no refusal | `aSeatTokenThisHouseNeverIssuedIsDroppedAndThePhoneSeatsItselfFresh` — *"the house turned a connection away and recorded nothing. Expected `<UnknownToken>`, actual `<null>`"* |

**The third injection found a fault in this unit's own instrument first, and that is the entry worth
keeping.** It did not fail — it **hung**, for ten minutes, and was stopped. The record assertion was a
bare `Channel.receive()` sitting outside the `withTimeout` block, so a build where the house recorded
nothing left the test waiting forever instead of failing. A guard that hangs is a guard that gets
stopped by a CI timeout and read as infrastructure trouble. It is a `tryReceive` now, with the reason
written at the line, and the injection was re-run against the fixed test before anything was believed.
Fifth member of the silent-instrument family this run keeps finding — after A4's, L3's and D3's two.

Stopping the hung run also left injection 3 in the tree, which is the failure mode `verify-guards.sh`
already carries a warning about. It was restored by hand and then **diffed against the main tree's
copy of the same file** rather than eyeballed; the three injection sites were counted afterwards and
the sources grepped for the marker.

### The evening

Five seats, D3's own: iPhone 16 Pro `vadManuel-Phone` hosting at **192.168.1.189:50709**, iPhone 13
Pro `Manuel's iPhone`, and three Mac instances. Playtest on all five, built from the isolated
worktree. The bundle was looked for in each phone's own installed-apps list rather than inferred
from a launch, because D3 logged an install that reported success and left a phone with no app.

**`SOMEONES_HOME_LOBBY_FORGET` was set on nobody, and that is the whole design of this run.** D3's
script cleared every stored token before every launch — because without that a second run seated
nobody. All five devices were carrying tokens from the 16:25 attempt against a house that no longer
exists, so the switch being off makes this run *the failing case*, not a rehearsal with the failure
switched out of the way.

```
PHONE HOST
[lobby-bench] EVENING — HOSTING 'THE BUNGALOW' AS 'ELLIOT'
[lobby] HOSTING 'THE BUNGALOW' ON :50709
[lobby] ADVERTISED AS 'THE BUNGALOW'
[lobby] A CONNECTION WAS REFUSED — UnknownToken
[lobby] REFUSED BY THE HOUSE — UnknownToken
[lobby] THE STORED SEAT IS NOT THIS HOUSE'S — JOINING FRESH
[lobby-bench] HOSTING 'THE BUNGALOW' — ON THE AIR · WAITING FOR THIS PHONE'S OWN SEAT
[lobby-bench] SEATED — 1 JOINED · 0 OF 1 … · [ELLIOT]
… 3 JOINED …  4 JOINED …
5 JOINED · 4 OF 5 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE] · WAITING FOR EVERYONE'S LINE · LOBBY
5 JOINED · 5 OF 5 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE] · READY · LOBBY

THE OTHER PHONE
[lobby-bench] SAW 'THE BUNGALOW' AT 192.168.1.189:50709
[lobby] REFUSED BY THE HOUSE — UnknownToken
[lobby] THE STORED SEAT IS NOT THIS HOUSE'S — JOINING FRESH
[lobby-bench] SEATED — 2 JOINED · 1 OF 2 … · [ELLIOT, PRIYA]
```

**All five seats printed those three lines.** Both phones and all three Mac instances presented a
token from a defunct house, were refused, dropped it and walked in as strangers — and the gate walked
`NEEDS 5 RESIDENTS → WAITING FOR EVERYONE'S LINE → READY` one seat at a time anyway.

| what the ruling promised | what the phones did |
|---|---|
| a stale token is refused, and the house records it | `A CONNECTION WAS REFUSED — UnknownToken`, on the host, five times |
| the phone drops it and joins fresh | `THE STORED SEAT IS NOT THIS HOUSE'S — JOINING FRESH`, then `SEATED`, on every seat |
| the player sees nothing | the only bench line between launch and `SEATED` is the one that says it is waiting; no refusal reached `LobbyModel` on any device |
| **the second evening works** | five seats, from five phones that had all played before, in under a minute |
| the home is on the air *before* anyone joins it | `ADVERTISED AS` now precedes `SEATED`; in D3's log it came three lines after |
| the line never comes back down | `INBOUND — 17 FRAME(S) {STANDING=17} · 0 CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE` on the host, `{STANDING=12} · 0` on the client |

**`SUBROUTINES EACH` moved with the party again** — 6, 11, 9, 8, 8 — which is `Balance.orderSize`
being asked rather than a literal printed, on hardware.

**This run was not repeated a second time on purpose.** It already *is* D3's Run 2 — the one that
stranded every phone — and re-running would have torn down the full lobby the owner is meant to walk
up to. A log is still not a photograph: this says what the screens were told to draw.

### What is left on the phones

**Both phones are in the lobby, seated, with the room full and the gate open**, which is where this
unit was asked to leave them:

```
16 Pro   5 JOINED · 5 OF 5 HANDED THEIRS OVER · INSIDERS UNKNOWN · SUBROUTINES EACH 8 ·
         [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE] · READY · LOBBY
13 Pro   5 JOINED · 5 OF 5 HANDED THEIRS OVER · INSIDERS UNKNOWN · SUBROUTINES EACH 8 ·
         [ELLIOT, PRIYA, MARCUS, DANI, ROSE] · READY · LOBBY
```

`devicectl device info processes` says `SomeonesHome` is alive on both. The three Mac instances are
holding their seats. The host is **ELLIOT on the 16 Pro**, LIGHTS OUT is live and **has not been
pressed** — `SOMEONES_HOME_LOBBY_LIGHTS_OUT` was left unset deliberately, because arming walks the
host's phone off the lobby and the lobby is what was asked for.

**No permission dialog appeared on either phone.** The grants survived the reinstall of the same
bundle id, exactly as expected, so no tap from a person was needed anywhere in this unit.

The five bench processes are still attached to their devices' consoles; stopping them is what ends the
evening. Logs: `r-host.log`, `r-client.log`, `r-sim1..3.log` in this session's scratchpad.

### Not done, and flagged

- **The ten-second patience has no test**, for the reason above. Flagged, not hidden.
- **The collision code is read out of the error dictionary by key name**, through `toString`, because
  both ends of that entry are bridged Foundation objects rather than Kotlin values. `advertiseFailureOf`
  is tested; *the extraction* is not, because reproducing a real `-72001` needs two hosts of the same
  name on one network. **It degrades in the right direction**: a dictionary this cannot read is still
  a refusal, and the host reads THIS HOME DID NOT GO ON THE AIR instead of the sharper sentence. The
  failure mode is a less specific true statement, never a missing one.
- **HOST WITH THIS HOME is silent while the radio thinks.** On a working stack that is under a
  second, but it is now a button that can take a moment to answer and the screen says nothing while
  it does. No spinner was invented for it: this app has no loading idiom, and inventing one on a lit
  setup screen is a design decision rather than a bug fix. Raised rather than taken.
- **`LobbyOverTransport.setVoteWindow` is W1's, written by this unit.** `LobbyLink` grew the method
  under this unit's hands mid-edit, and `app` could not compile without an implementation. It
  forwards to `LobbyDesk.setVoteWindow` and says at the site that W1 owns it and should overwrite it.
- **`crash-log.txt` was moved out of the repository, and it is safe.** The decision D3 left for a
  person was made by a person: the owner and the orchestrator moved it to the Desktop as
  `crash-log-2026-08-22-windowserver.txt`, out of the repository root but preserved for a possible
  report to Apple. **Verified by this unit rather than taken on trust** — 5,766,132 bytes, 4,279
  lines, written at 16:35, first record a WindowServer stackshot stamped `2026-08-22 15:17:04`. That
  is D3's file exactly, down to the line count D3 wrote down, intact.

  **This unit reported it lost, and that report was wrong.** It saw the file at 16:57, saw it absent
  by roughly 17:14, found no copy in git — it was never tracked — and, knowing another unit was
  rewriting `model`, `core` and `harness` in the same working tree at exactly that hour, wrote down
  that a `git clean` was the likely cause. **W1 did not touch it and that suspicion is withdrawn
  here in the same words it was made in.** The move happened in the minutes after this unit's first
  `git status` and before its second, which is a window narrow enough to look like a disappearance
  and wide enough to be an ordinary act by somebody else. `032cf9a` records the wrong version and
  stays in the history where it can be read against this correction.

  **The lesson is not about the file.** A missing artefact and a concurrent editor is a coincidence,
  and this unit turned it into an accusation on the strength of nothing but timing. The right report
  was the first half — *it is not here, I did not move it, here is when it went* — and the naming of
  a culprit was the part that had no evidence under it. Nothing was lost except accuracy, this once.
- **E-D3-3 is untouched and still open.** There is still no host→client effect push, so LIGHTS OUT
  still arms one phone. Nothing here changes that and nothing here pretends to.

---

## Wire — Unit W1

*Six authorised widenings (D-171), every one of them already ruled — this unit only made the wire
able to say it. The freeze lifted for exactly these six and for nothing else.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check`: **tests=1482, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (model 450,
ui 602, core 183, harness 108, platform 100, app 22, cards 17), up from **1410** — **72 new**, read
from the test XML and never from an exit code. `vocabularyLint` clean (18 + 24 + 46 + 60 files),
`redactionLint` clean (46 files), `core boundaryCheck` clean, `ui boundaryCheck` clean,
`DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest` green. Recording format **10 → 11**.

### This is a finished unit, not a fresh one — what was inherited and what was fixed

**A parallel session's W1 failed mid-flight with its work uncommitted in the working tree**: 41
modified files, `Destination.kt` untracked, +1559/−226, and a worklog draft in scratch with
`TESTS_TOTAL`, `INJECTIONS_HERE` and `ESCALATIONS_HERE` still in it. **It failed before it ran a
single injection**, which is the part of it that could not be inherited on trust.

**The six widenings themselves were reviewed as inherited work and kept.** Every claim in the
sections below was read against the diff rather than adopted from the draft, and the design of them
is right: the narrowing lives in one function, the two meter kinds are two kinds for a reason an
allowlist cannot express, and the blocked line's blackout is structural. The prose of §1–§5 below is
substantially the orphan's, verified line by line.

**What this unit found half-done and finished:**

- **Nothing had been injection-verified.** Twelve injections were run, listed below. All twelve
  failed the way they were supposed to, and one of them exposed a real hole (next item).
- **The circuit was unguarded in the state row.** `Transcript.work()` renders `ArrayWipe` as its own
  word, and **nothing tested it** — because nothing deals a circuit, no replay ever renders one. A
  `work()` that spelled the circuit as one of the roster's ten would have certified a replayed round
  with an eleventh Subroutine in it as byte-identical, silently, forever. `a circuit renders as a
  circuit and carries all three of its stations` is the new guard, built by hand because the draw
  cannot build it.
- **`MemoryLobbyLink.windows` was a recorder nothing read.** The fixture was added to prove the
  host's setting leaves the screen and no test ever asserted on it. Two now do — the host's taps
  arrive in order, and a client's arrive not at all.
- **`LobbyDesk.namedSeats()` and `LobbyDesk.voteWindowSeconds` were untested.** Both are what arming
  reads off the desk; five tests now cover them, including the one that matters — **what arming
  takes is the `names` map and there is nothing in it that could be a line.**
- **`LobbyOverTransport.setVoteWindow` was another unit's stopgap**, marked *W1 owns this and should
  overwrite it*. Adopted: the body was already right, and the KDoc now says why **the absence of
  `publish()` is the decision** rather than saying it was written in a hurry.
- **Three stale sites** left mid-thought: the springboard's *nothing on the wire says where an entry
  is* comment, a `LobbyModelTest` KDoc still predicting its own failure, and fully-qualified
  `home.someoneshome.model.*` references in `Transcript.kt` and `CountdownTest.kt` where every
  neighbour uses an import.

### 1 + 2 + 6 · The order line learned to say WHERE, and what it may be

Three of the six are one change, because the destination, the map it is resolved from and the
circuit are one sentence: **a line of a work order is a piece of work, at some places.**

`Event.RoundArmed.markers` were bare card ids. They are **`Destination`s** now — card, glyph id and
the room the host named on their setup walk — which is E-L4-1's mechanism taken rather than
recorded. `GameState` holds the table, **nothing derives anything from it**, and the day something
in `core` starts making decisions about which room is where is the day the rules have acquired an
opinion about a house they have never been in. `renaming every room changes nothing the house draws`
is the guard.

**The narrowing lives in `WorkOrder.asLines(geography)` and is *which rows*, not which fields.** A
`Destination` is physically incapable of carrying a seat, a Subroutine, an answer or a dependency,
so there is nothing inside one to redact; what a client gets is the rows its own order names. The
whole home's geography on every phone is the map of the round the house has never published, and it
is the thing this unit was told not to do.

**A blocked line still carries nothing, the room included.** That is the half worth reading. A room
on a blocked row is the leak in its most plausible costume — it names no Subroutine, so it reads as
harmless, and under D-146's cross-player dependencies it points at the card the person **upstream of
you** is standing at. `OrderLine.Blocked` has one field and must never gain a second.

**`AssignedWork` is a sealed pair rather than an eleventh `SubroutineKind`.** Array Wipe is a
*circuit* (`gdd.md:611`), not one of the ten the design rates for light, and an eleventh enum member
would have put a circuit everywhere a Subroutine is expected — the roster, the parameter draw, the
grading — with a special case at each. An entry's cards are a **list**: one for a Subroutine, three
for the circuit, in D-122's draw order. So a circuit line and a Subroutine line are the same shape
with a different number of places in it, and there is no second list to disagree with the station
draw.

**⚠️ The circuit is representable and is not dealt.** The design's mix — *1 circuit, 1 long, 3
medium, 2 short* — is not built at all: length is D-129's `K` and the kinds are drawn flat. What the
format can now carry is what lets the client's ARRAY WIPE row stop being a fixture; putting one in
an order needs the carried state it is graded against (F-014, E7). One consequence is written where
it bites: `AssignedWork.opensAs` is null for the circuit, so `WorkOrder.openAt` **steps over** a
circuit line rather than returning it — a later Subroutine at the same station still opens, which is
the better of the two wrong answers, and it is unreachable while nothing deals one.

### 3 · The meter has a sender, and two kinds because timing is not a permission

`SystemIntegrityLive` → the two classes outside the system, on every movement.
`SystemIntegrityBatched` → the two living classes, **once, when a meeting is called** — the shift
boundary — and frozen in between.

**One kind with both rows would have been wrong and the reason is the whole of it.** The two carry
the same fact and differ in *when*, which is the one thing an allowlist cannot express: a single kind
permitted to both audiences hands the living every movement the instant it happens, and a rate signal
is *who is working and how fast* — the per-player read the percentage form exists to keep out of
aggregate. `EmitSchema`'s own rule arriving from the other direction: two audiences told different
amounts is two kinds, and *how often* is an amount.

**The batch goes out whether or not the meter moved.** A meeting with no push on it would tell the
whole room that nobody banked anything all shift — a report on the house's work nobody asked the app
to make, delivered by an absence.

**Percentage only, and `Balance.integrityPercent` is the one place that arithmetic exists.** A total
of `seats × METER_PER_SEAT` is public, so an absolute value on any wire hands back the count D-103
hides. On the client `PanelVals.integrityLit` is now computed **from** the pushed percentage rather
than beside it — which is the fault revision 21 found as `14 / 32` under a bar at a different
fraction, and C2 found again as Ghost3's `66%` over twenty-one lit cells, closed by there being one
value instead of two.

**Ghost3's couch bar is live for real now** (D-134). The Egress bar beside it is not: `EgressHeld`
carries `remaining` but the duration it is a fraction of is still F-009's unsent lobby setting.

### 4 · The vote window reaches the round

The host's VOTING row moved a number nothing read. It rides `RoundArmed`, `GameState` holds it,
`openVote` is the **one** read of it, and it arrives on `Effect.MeetingPhaseOpened` when the ballot
opens — where `Countdowns` has said since L2 that it would come from.

**The field is `voteWindowSeconds` and not `windowSeconds`, deliberately.** The discussion's ninety
and the tally's fifteen are the design's own constants that the client still holds, so a field named
for *this phase's window* would be null on two phases that **do** have clocks, and the first client to
read null as *no clock* would take the discussion's countdown off the screen. Named for the ballot, it
cannot be mistaken for anything else.

On the client the pushed number replaces the default on **both** ballot clocks — the living's
`Vote` and the couch's `GhostMeeting`. They carry different drawn defaults (45 against 60) and that
flag stays open about the **fixture**; in play they can no longer disagree, which matters because the
couch can see a living player's phone across a lit room.

**It does not go down the wire in either direction, and that is a decision with a test on it.** The
Insider count travels because the house *clamps* it and a host who could not see the clamped value
would be reading a setting the house is not holding. The ballot's length is clamped by nobody and
read by nobody until the vote opens, so it stays off `LobbyBody.Standing` and travels by the better
road. `moving the vote window changes nothing the house published` holds that shut.

### 5 · The reveal says who

`InsiderNamed` carries a name. The names live on the desk (`LobbyDesk.namedSeats()`), ride the arming
event as a recorded input exactly as the one lines do, land on `GameState.names`, and are dropped by
`endRound` **in the same instruction as the lines** — D-115 and D-116 kept by one transition rather
than by two calls somebody can forget.

**It discloses nothing new.** Every phone in the lobby was already sent every name on
`LobbyBody.Standing`; what this pairs a name with is the alignment, and that is the effect's whole
job. **And the recording still holds seats and no names**, exactly as it holds seats and no lines —
so a replayed reveal publishes a blank name beside a blank line, and the round is reproduced without
the people.

**The rule the guard actually holds is not *no effect carries a name*.** Revision 34 authorised the
fourth widening as **seat → name *for the reveal***, citing D-115 — and D-115 put names in the
**lobby**, where six people standing in a hall can already see each other, and *wherever the
interaction needs them*. So the guarantee worth holding is that **nothing carries a name until the
round is over**: a name a phone sees before the ending is the lobby's, off `LobbyBody.Standing`, and
never the round's. `no effect carries a name until the round ends` sweeps a whole shift — scans, a
hand-over, a full meeting — and fails on the string it leaked.

### Recording format 10 → 11, and E-L4-4 closed on the way

A version 10 arming carries bare card ids, so it replays into orders that point nowhere. The row
gained destinations, the ballot's length and the seats that said what to call them; two effects a
version 10 build cannot construct arrive with it; `MeetingPhaseOpened` carries the window; a work
order line carries its destinations and can be a circuit.

**`Transcript.escape` covers the colon now, and that is E-L4-4 closed** — the third member of the
family the pipe and the comma opened. It has always been a sub-separator inside `open=` and
`presence=`, and a marker id may contain one (the payload alphabet is QR alphanumeric minus SPACE).
This unit made it sharper rather than merely latent: a destination renders as `card:glyph:room`, and
a **room name is text a host typed** — `KITCHEN: BACK` would have forged a fourth part.

### The injections — twelve, every one of them run

Every guard below was broken on purpose, the targeted tests were run, and the failures were read out
of the test XML. The tree was restored between each.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `SYSTEM_INTEGRITY_LIVE` widened to `LIVING + OUTSIDE` | `MeterPushTest.a banked entry pushes the meter live to the out and to no living phone` |
| 2 | `SYSTEM_INTEGRITY_BATCHED` widened to the out as well | `MeterPushTest.calling a meeting hands the living the meter once and reaches no out phone` |
| 3 | `SYSTEM_INTEGRITY_BATCHED` narrowed to Resident/Live alone | the same test — an allowlist narrowed to one role is an alignment tell |
| 4 | `asLines` returns `at = emptyList()` for every known line | 3 × `GeographyTest` (own rooms, blocked blackout, the circuit's three stations) |
| 5 | a blocked line given a room in `PanelVals.order` | `WorkOrderTest.aBlockedLineDrawsNoDestination`, `.aBlockedEntryNamesNothingAndRatesNothing` |
| 6 | `openVote` publishes `Balance.VOTE_WINDOW_SECONDS` instead of the round's | 2 × `VoteWindowTest` (the armed window, and the second meeting) |
| 7 | `cycleVoteWindow` stops calling `link.setVoteWindow` | `LobbyModelTest.every move of the vote window is handed to the link` |
| 8 | `LobbyDesk.namedSeats()` reads the `lines` map instead of `names` | `LobbyDeskTest.the seats arming takes are named and carry not one word of any line` |
| 9 | `Transcript.work()` renders the circuit as `Replay` | `RecordingTest.a circuit renders as a circuit and carries all three of its stations` |
| 10 | a seat's name put on a mid-round `MessageDelivered` | `EndingTest.no effect carries a name until the round ends` (+ 2 × `MeterPushTest`) |
| 11 | the reveal's `name` blanked | `EndingTest.the reveal names every Insider living or not with their line` (+ the sweep) |
| 12 | `Transcript.destination()` drops the room | **17 tests** across `RecordingTextTest`, `ResumeTest`, `RecordingTest`, `FuzzPropertyTest`, `FixturesTest` |

Injection 12 is the wire round-trip answering loudly: a destination that loses a field does not
produce a subtly different recording, it produces one that will not parse.

### Escalations and flags

- **⚠️ Nothing in production arms a round, so the far end of widenings 3 and 4 is the desk.** The
  host's ballot length and the seats' names reach `LobbyDesk`, and `LobbyDesk` is what a round is
  armed from — but **`armingFor` has no production caller at all**, and never had: `lineOf` has been
  in the same position since D-116 landed. The chain is proven at both ends (lobby → desk in
  `LobbyModelTest` and `LobbyDeskTest`, desk-shaped inputs → clock and reveal in `VoteWindowTest`
  and `EndingTest`) and the join in the middle is **E-D3-3**, still open, unchanged by this unit and
  not pretended otherwise.
- **⚠️ The circuit is expressible and is not dealt.** The format carries it; nothing puts one in an
  order. Dealing one needs the carry-state machine (F-014, E7), and the design's assigned mix — *1
  circuit, 1 long, 3 medium, 2 short* — is unbuilt in every one of its four parts.
- **⚠️ The two drawn ballot clocks still disagree: `Vote` 45, `GhostMeeting` 60.** Unchanged and
  still wanting a ruling — but the flag is now about a **fixture** rather than about the game, since
  the house's number replaces both at once. `withNoHouseAttachedTheTwoBallotClocksKeepTheirDrawnWindows`
  pins the fixture so the flag cannot quietly become about the game again.
- **⚠️ The springboard's meter reads `87%`, not `88%`.** A visible change to a drawn screen: the
  figure was a literal typed beside a bar computed from `integrityLit`, and 28 of 32 cells is 87.5
  floored. Same correction C2 made to Ghost3's `66%` over twenty-one lit cells. Recorded rather than
  absorbed.
- **⚠️ The Egress bar on the couch still has no denominator.** `EgressHeld` carries `remaining`; the
  duration it is a fraction of is F-009's lobby setting and the wire does not send it. One setting
  over from the vote window, and **not** in W1's authorised six.
- **`ARRAY WIPE`'s BRIGHT is still this client's reading.** D-113 ratified the value; nothing ruled
  the row. It is written down once now (`PanelVals.ARRAY_WIPE_LIGHT`) rather than typed into a
  fixture, so a house-dealt circuit and the drawn design cannot drift apart — but it is a port
  reading either way.

## Client — scan flow

*Revision 34's client half: D-159's corrected scan gesture and D-160's haptic vocabulary, built in
`:platform`, `:ui` and `:app`. No Event, Effect, schema row or `reduce()` was touched. **THE FREEZE
HELD.***

**Outcome: green, committed, pushed.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1497, failures=0,
errors=0, skipped=0** (model 450, ui 602, core 183, harness 108, platform 112, cards 17, app 25),
up from W1's **1482** — **15 new** (12 in `platform`, 3 in `app`), read from the test XML and never
from an exit code. `vocabularyLint` clean (7 + 18 + 24 + 46 + 60 files), `redactionLint` clean (46
files), `core` and `ui` `boundaryCheck` clean, `DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest` green,
`verify-guards.sh` **6 passed, 0 failed**.

**Device verification is deliberately deferred.** Both phones are seated in a live lobby their
owner is watching, so nothing here was built to, installed on or run against hardware. The two
outputs this unit changes — a vibration and a lens — are the two outputs a screenshot cannot show
and a headless test cannot feel, so **what is green is the shape, not the sensation**: the
durations, the gaps and the looping flag are arithmetic a test can hold, and whether a hand can
tell D-160's *yes* from its *no* through a pocket is a question only a phone answers. Both benches
carry the rows that ask it.

### The scan is a window, and the window is what stops the buzz storm

`ScanWindow` (`platform`) is D-159 as an object: `open` on the tap, **one read**, `expire` when the
ten seconds run out, `close` when the screen simply goes away. Acquire buzzes once and closes the
window itself; expiry buzzes twice and closes it; closing says nothing, and that silence is the
whole difference — the double vibrate is the house answering a scan, and a window closed because
the app backgrounded has no scan left to answer.

**The fix for the camera bench's buzz storm is the window and not a payload dedupe.** A camera
cannot tell *a new card* from *the same card, still in frame* — `CardScanner` says so at the seam
and refuses to guess — so the bench that buzzed per delivery vibrated at the frame rate. Deduping
on the payload would still let a *second* card in the same frame ring a second buzz out of one tap,
which is one scan producing two answers in a game where a scan is how the house learns where
anybody is. The first delivery closes the window, so every later one lands on nothing.

**The window's own guard is the thing under test, not the fake camera's.** `ScanWindowTest`'s fake
lens hands out a listener that survives `stop()`, because a real capture session is not a function
call: `stopRunning` is asynchronous and a frame already in the pipeline arrives after it. A window
that relied on the camera having gone quiet would be relying on the one thing the seam explicitly
refuses to promise.

**Across windows a repeat stays meaningful.** Scanning the same marker again in a new window is not
a duplicate, it is a player putting themselves at a known place at a known time — the alibi the
presence map is made of (D-136) — and `ScanWindow` remembers nothing between windows.

**It holds no clock.** Ten seconds is drawn on the screen, so it lives where the bar lives:
`PanelVals.SCAN_WINDOW_MILLIS = SCAN_SEGMENTS * SCAN_SEGMENT_MILLIS`, which `Flow.autoAdvance`
now reads instead of restating `* 500`. A second copy of that number in `platform` would be two
clocks for one window, disagreeing eventually.

### D-160's alphabet, and there is still one place to get a pattern from

`Haptic` gains **`Double`** and `HouseBuzz` gains its pattern beside the Short and the Long — same
structure, same privacy, same exhaustive `when`. That is the D-102 guard extending to the new word
rather than a new mechanism beside it: a call site still cannot compose two buzzes of its own,
because there is no parameter for one. The injection that proves it is unchanged and now covers the
`Double` — a `heard` that reaches past `HouseBuzz` for a 300 ms pattern fails
`noEventBuzzesWithAPatternOfItsOwn` by name.

**The call is the one thing in this game that repeats.** `HouseBuzz.RING` is LONG–pause–LONG and
`Haptics.play` grew a `repeating` flag for it; `Effect.ringing()` is a second exhaustive `when`
beside `Effect.haptic()`, so a new `Effect` does not compile until somebody has written down
whether it rings, and the answer they will write is the safe one. `HouseBuzzer.answered()` is the
only thing that stops it.

**The ring's trailing `Rest` is load-bearing and so is `loopEnd`.** A `CHHapticPattern`'s duration
stops at its last *event*, so a pattern ending in a buzz loops straight from one ring into the next
and the pairing that says *telephone* disappears. The iOS actual sets `loopEnd` to the script's
total rather than letting the pattern decide, and takes the **advanced** player only for this one
caller — a bug in the looping path cannot reach the four Longs and the Short a round depends on.

**D-135 is amended and not replaced, and the test says both halves.** The call rings; the Egress,
STAND AND WALK IN, the Restrained takeover and LIGHTS OUT are one Long each and stop on their own.

**`MeetingOpened` still carries `Haptic.Long` and that is not a contradiction.** `Rules.kt` put it
there under D-135 and D-160 did not go back and rewrite the reduction — which it could not have,
under the freeze. The kind on the effect is the house saying *this is one you must not miss*; the
client obeys the amendment about how a phone says it. It is a decision about the **effect type**
and nothing else: every phone receiving a `MeetingOpened` rings, and none of them consults a seat,
a role or who called the meeting.

### The copy sweep

`ScanScreen`'s **HOLD THE SCREEN UP TO THE MARKER** is now **TURN THE SCREEN TO THE MARKER**, and
its footer says the alphabet in the two sentences a player needs it in: *One buzz and it opens
itself. Two buzzes and it gave up — tap SCAN again.* The screen's KDoc, `PanelVals.SCAN_SEGMENTS`'s
and `Flow.autoAdvance`'s scan row all now say what the ten seconds are counting down **to**, which
is the only thing D-159 changed about them. The camera bench's instruction moved from *Hold a
printed card up* to *Turn the TOP of the phone to a printed card*.

### The two scans stayed two scans

`ScanCheat.scan` is D-159's window. **`ScanCheat.watch` is the host-setup scan and was left
continuous** — E-D2-3 is explicit that it is a different thing, and the screen's own instruction is
KEEP SCANNING TO ADD MORE. What it stopped doing is buzzing per delivery: a payload identical to
the one before it is the card that has not moved. A different card always buzzes, and coming back
to a card later still buzzes, because that is a host correcting themselves and it is a real event.

The in-round surface is wired on `ScreenId.Scan` in `Cheats.kt`, and **it deliberately does not
navigate**. What a read produces is `Effect.ScanAnswered` and the house's push to `ScanCaught` —
`Flow.housePushed` says so, and there is no house attached to this build (E-D3-3). A client that
walked itself there would be the device deciding which Subroutine a card holds. So the window ends
where it really ends on this build: at the buzz, which is the half below the effect boundary and
the half that is real.

### Injections, all reverted by targeted reverse-replace

| # | broken on purpose | the test that named it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `saw` no longer closes the window — the buzz storm returns | `theSamePayloadTwiceInOneWindowBuzzesOnceAndOpensOnce` |
| 2 | `open` refuses a second window — the alibi affordance fails | `aSecondWindowReadsTheSameCardAgain` |
| 3 | `expire` buzzes `Short` instead of `Double` | `expiryProducesExactlyTheDoubleAndCloses` |
| 4 | `MeetingEnded` also repeats | `theCallIsTheOnlyThingThatKeepsGoing` |
| 5 | the call plays with `repeating = false` | `theCallRingsAndTheOtherFourLongsDoNot` |
| 6 | `heard` reaches past `HouseBuzz` for a 300 ms pattern | `noEventBuzzesWithAPatternOfItsOwn` |

**Six run, six failed as intended, six reverse-replaced.** Injection 3's automated reverse collided
— after the swap, `ScanWindow.kt` held two identical `haptics.buzz(Haptic.Short)` lines and the
replace refused rather than guessing which one to undo. It was repaired by hand, at the call site,
and `check --rerun-tasks` was re-run afterwards on the repaired tree. **The refusal is the script
working**: a reverse-replace that had picked one of two matches would have silently restored the
wrong line and left a poisoned tree looking green.

### FLAGGED for the owner — one treatment, decided rather than guessed

**STOP NOW produces the double vibrate, and D-160 does not say it should.** The Scan screen has
exactly two exits that leave a window open — the countdown reaching zero, and the player tapping
STOP NOW — and the wiring treats both as *the window produced no read*, which is the sentence the
double is the word for. The alternative was a second clock in the app racing `FlowHost`'s, where
whether the refusal buzzes at all depends on which coroutine resumes first in a frame. **One rule
with no race beat two clocks with one**, but it is a reading of D-160 rather than a line in it. If
the owner wants STOP NOW silent, the app needs a channel from the button to the window and the
ordering question comes back with it.

### Where the code is still wrong, carried forward unchanged

`Countdown.kt`'s `ScreenId.GhostMeeting to 60` and its *left alone deliberately* KDoc (D-163, one
clock at 45) were **not** touched — revision 34 lists them as client work and they are not this
unit's four items. Everything else on revision 33's and 34's carried-forward list is likewise
untouched: `ScreensOut.kt`'s `14 / 32` rows (D-153), `epics.md` story 4.10 and the marker-taxonomy
blind zone row (D-155), and the three orphan auto-satisfaction sites (D-146).

## Push — Unit P1

*The freeze lifted for one named thing: the machinery that carries authority-produced `Effect`s from
the host to connected clients. Nothing here mints an `Event`, changes a rule, edits a schema row or
touches `reduce()`. **E-D3-3's first half — there is no host→client effect push for any effect — is
closed.** Its second half is not, and is named below.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1528, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(ui 602, model 466, core 183, platform 124, harness 108, app 28, cards 17), up from **1482** —
**46 new**, read out of the test XML and never off an exit code. The arithmetic: model +16 and
platform +24 are `commonTest`, so they run on both `iosSimulatorArm64Test` and `desktopTest`
(8 × 2 and 12 × 2); app's three are simulator-only and counted twice across the variant sweeps.
`vocabularyLint` clean (model 48, ui 60, core 24, app 20, cards 7), `redactionLint clean — 48
file(s)`, `boundary: core clean`, `boundary: ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest`
green. `verify-guards.sh`: **6 passed, 0 failed**. No phone was required for any of it.

### The shape of it, in one paragraph

`Effect` is deliberately not `@Serializable` and `redactionLint` fails the build on any attempt to
make it so — so the wire type is **constructed** rather than annotated onto the authority's own
shape, which is rule 3's *redact by constructing a narrower type* applied to the transport. The new
`RoundMessage` is that construction, one member per effect, exhaustive over the sealed type. The
host resolves each effect through `EmitSchema.deliveries` **before anything is rendered**, spends a
per-seat sequence number, retains the push, and writes it down that seat's socket. The client's
`RoundInbox` decides in order / already held / missing something, and `FlowModel.houseSaid` is the
one consumer that turns a message into a screen.

### 1 · The wire cannot say a kind the schema denies, because it is built out of the schema

`EffectWire.emit` has exactly one source of recipients and no API that accepts a ready-made frame.
A kind with no allowlist row produces no `Delivery`, so **no sequence is spent, nothing is retained
and nothing is written** — the pipeline is not *checked* against the allowlist, it is made of it.
The two row-less kinds prove it from inside a running house: `PresenceChanged` and `EgressSucceeded`
are projected (the `when` is exhaustive) and reach nobody, exactly as they are named in
`EmitSchema.kindOf` and granted nothing.

**The narrowing is real and it is the recipient's own seat.** Sixteen of the thirty-one effects are
addressed to one seat, and for every one of them the message has **no seat field at all** — the
socket is the address, and a seat riding inside the payload would be a second statement of the same
fact that can be wrong on its own. `aSeatAddressedMessageCannotTellWhichSeatItIsFor` asserts it the
only way that means anything: projecting the same effect for two different recipients has to produce
identical bytes. The kinds that keep a seat are the broadcast ones whose *content* is a seat — who
called the meeting, who was restrained, who cast what — and each of those is a disclosure its own
row already decided. That half is asserted too, so the first test cannot one day be "fixed" by
stripping seats off everything.

**Fourteen model types were marked `@ClientFacing` to let the projection reach through them**, and
they are gathered in one comment at the foot of `RoundWire.kt` so that marking each was a decision
rather than a chain of consequences. `Role` is not among them and never will be.

### 2 · The host's own phone is not a special case, and that is the whole answer to the charter's question

There is no local delivery path. The host joined its own house over loopback in Unit 6 for exactly
this reason, so **seat zero's client is a websocket client**: the host's own effects go through the
same projection, the same sequence counter, the same encoder and the same socket as the phone in the
next room. *The route no other phone uses is the route nobody tests*, and here it would have been the
route the authority's own screen depends on.

### 3 · One consumer, two sources

`FlowModel.houseSaid` is the only place in the app where a pushed message may touch a screen, and
`houseGraded` — the seam the bench has been driving since L1 — now goes **through** it rather than
setting a field of its own. `inProcessAndOverLoopbackLeaveIdenticalState` runs a thirteen-effect
shift both ways and demands one `PanelState`; it starts both phones at the assembly gate so the
meeting push has a screen waiting for it, and asserts the screen really moved, so the comparison is
not two phones agreeing about a round neither of them had.

**The `when` is exhaustive and most of it does nothing yet, deliberately.** A kind with no field on
`PanelState` and no route in either table is a **named no-op with its reason at the branch**, never a
missing case and never an `else`. Eight land: the verdict, the order, both meter kinds, the meeting
phase, the ballot window, the reveal, the Egress type — plus the takeover and the ending, which
move the screen. The rest are the honest map of what the surfaces still owe, and every one of them
is a design question rather than a wiring gap (see the escalations).

**A frame is never applied from a socket's thread.** `RoundOverTransport` queues and `run()` drains
on the caller's dispatcher — D4's one ordered event queue at the client end, and the reason the
client half is a channel rather than a callback.

### 4 · D-097's re-assertion, built as a replay because the snapshot is undecided

D-097 rules that a refusal is answered by re-pushing the client's authorized view, and **leaves the
payload of that view deliberately undecided**. So the mechanism here **invents no payload**: the
house replays its own per-seat outbox — the pushes exactly as they were sent — from wherever the
client says it is. Every byte passed `EmitSchema` at the moment it was produced, addressed to this
seat, and was already delivered to this phone once, so re-sending it discloses nothing that was not
disclosed. A *rebuild* would be the opposite: it would ask what this seat may see **now**, which is a
different question with a different answer once a class has moved, and answering it would be writing
the undecided snapshot under another name. **Nothing about disclosure was decided by this unit.**

The other half of D-097's requirement — *safe to re-receive redundantly* — is kept on the client:
`RoundInbox` drops any sequence at or below what it already holds.

**An out-of-order push is dropped rather than applied.** Applying push 9 while 7 and 8 are missing
leaves whichever fields 7 and 8 carried standing at their old values under a screen drawn from a
newer one — a phone that is *partly* up to date and cannot say which part. Dropping it and asking is
the recoverable failure, and what a screen draws is then always a **prefix** of what the house said
rather than a mixture.

**A phone that is not there keeps its place.** A failed write spends the sequence and retains the
message anyway, reports the seat to the authority at once (rule 6 — a failed radio must never make a
player silently invisible) and says nothing to the player. So an unreachable phone is a **delay, not
a hole**, and the client's own gap detector never sees a number that was never used.

Mid-round reconnect rides the same request by its other trigger (`reseated()`); lobby-phase rejoin is
untouched and still D-172's.

### 5 · The one line

`OneLineNeverPersistedTest`'s idiom, extended to round effects: `RoundOverTransport` carries the restore unit's
`onInbound` probe, empty in every production construction, and `noFrameCarriesALineUntilTheRoundEnds`
reads **every body that really reached a phone over a real socket** and sweeps it for the text three
people in the fixture house typed. Names are swept in the same pass (W1's *no effect carries a name
until the round ends*).

**The charter states the rule absolutely — *nothing in this pipeline may carry a one-line down* — and
read absolutely it forbids the reveal**, which is a ruled disclosure (D-116, `gdd.md:1063`) and the
half of the round that makes it land as people rather than as a result. It is therefore held in W1's
shape for the same question about names: **nothing carries one until the round ends.** The sweep is
**self-verifying** — the same probe is run across the ending, where a line is published on purpose,
and has to find it there, and has to find only the Insider's. A sweep that reads nothing passes for
the same reason a sweep that reads everything does.

### The injections — nine, every one of them run and reverted by targeted reverse-replace

Never `git checkout`. The tree was diffed and grepped for markers afterwards.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `emit` walks `state.seats` instead of `EmitSchema.deliveries` | **5 ×** `EffectWireTest` — the denied kind reaching four phones, the Insider-only row reaching everybody, the live count leaving the couch, the role exchange going vacuous, the numbering |
| 2 | `RoundMessage.kind` names `SubroutineProgressed` for a graded entry | `RoundWireTest.everyEffectProjectsToItsOwnKind` |
| 3 | `SubroutineGraded` given back the seat it is addressed to | `RoundWireTest.aSeatAddressedMessageCannotTellWhichSeatItIsFor` |
| 4 | the reveal's line blanked in the projection | `RoundWireTest.theRevealPublishesTheLineItIsThereToPublish` **and** `EffectPushTest.noFrameCarriesALineUntilTheRoundEnds` — *the reveal published no line, so the sweep above was looking at nothing* |
| 5 | an out-of-order push applied over the hole rather than asked about | `RoundInboxTest.aGapIsNotAppliedAndTheAskNamesWhatIsHeld`, and over a real socket `EffectPushTest.aDroppedFrameIsNoticedAskedAboutAndMadeGoodOnItsOwn` |
| 6 | a re-assertion answered out of **every** seat's stream | 2 × `EffectWireTest` — one seat was handed another seat's messages |
| 7 | a failed write swallowed | `EffectWireTest.aSeatThatCouldNotBeReachedKeepsItsPlaceAndCollectsOnAsking` |
| 8 | the socket path decides one kind for itself — **two consumers, not one** | 2 × `EffectPushTest` — the identity test and the convergence test both |
| 9 | `ABILITY_FIRED` widened from `Insider/Live` to both living classes | `EffectWireTest.exchangingTheRoleExchangesTheTwoStreamsAndNothingElse` |

**Injection 4 is the entry worth keeping.** It is the only one that breaks a *positive* control, and
it is the one that proves the one-line sweep reads real bytes rather than an empty list. Every other
guard here fails when something leaks; this one fails when the instrument stops looking.

**Injection 1's first attempt did not compile** and the runner reported the run as stale rather than
green — `state.clientClassOf(...)` is an extension function and cannot be reached by qualifying it.
A green report off an uncompiled tree is the sixth member of this run's silent-instrument family, and
it was caught by making the runner grep for `e:` before printing any counts.

### Escalations

- **⚠️ E-P1-1 · The Egress nodes reach a phone as card ids and the screen draws room names.**
  `Effect.EgressOpened.nodes` is `List<MarkerId>`; `PanelState.egressNodes` is text a player reads in
  a dark house. Only `egressType` lands, and the drawn pair still stands in. Resolving one to the
  other means `Destination`s on the effect — which is arguably D-171's fifth widening (*the spectator
  map and containment both resolve through the marker → room map*) but is a change to an `Effect`'s
  shape, which is the loop rather than the carriage. **Escalated, not guessed:** a nine-character
  card id on a dark-house screen is worse than the fixture it would replace.
- **⚠️ E-P1-2 · The couch's live ballot cannot land.** `Effect.VoteSelectionShown` carries seats and
  `PanelState.ballots` holds names, and `ui` mapping one to the other would be `ui` keeping a second
  copy of who is in the house. It wants either the reveal's treatment (names on the effect, D-115's
  shape) or a resolver on the transport side. Not closed by putting a roster in `ui`.
- **⚠️ E-P1-3 · The retained per-seat stream is unbounded within a round.** The re-assert replays from
  an outbox that grows for the length of the round and is wiped by `roundEnded()`. At this game's
  message rate that is tens of kilobytes a seat, against a whole-app budget of ~0.5 MB/s — but nothing
  caps it. **A cap was deliberately not added**: a bounded outbox answers a re-assert *partially*, and
  a silent partial is the failure class this project ranks worst. Wants a ruling, not a constant.
- **⚠️ E-P1-4 · Sixteen kinds have no route and no field.** The ring, the walk-in, the notification
  arrivals, the cooldown, the four counts, the vote's own re-assertion, the house's last word. Each is
  a named no-op with its reason in `houseSaid`; each needs a decision (*which* screen a `MeetingOpened`
  opens is not a wiring gap this unit may close by choosing). Written down rather than left as absent
  branches, because an absent branch is indistinguishable from a forgotten one.
- **⚠️ E-P1-5 · Sequencing and writing share one lock.** Per-seat order is structural rather than an
  emergent property of how fast sockets drain — the price is that a socket that *blocks* in `send`
  stalls the house's whole write path. Ktor throws on a closed socket rather than blocking, so this is a
  half-open-TCP concern rather than an observed one, and the fix (a per-seat outbound actor) is more
  machinery than the observed problem justifies. Recorded rather than built.
- **⚠️ E-D3-3's second half is untouched and still open.** `LobbyHouse.emit` is the authority's whole
  exit to the phones and **nothing in production calls it**, because nothing in production arms a
  round: `armingFor` still has no production caller, exactly as W1 left it. The pipeline below arming
  is real and proven end to end over a real socket; the join above it is the loop's.

### Flagged design choices

1. **`RoundBody.Gap` travels client → host and is deliberately not an Intent.** It names no game
   action, has no refusal, mints no `Event` and reaches no rule; the house's whole answer is bytes it
   already sent that phone. That is what lets it exist while the intent channel does not, and it is
   why it could be built inside a unit told not to build intents.
2. **`EgressOpened.type` is `egress` on the wire.** `type` is the polymorphic discriminator the whole
   sealed hierarchy already uses, and a payload field of the same name makes the type unserialisable.
   Caught by the round-trip sweep, which is what a round-trip sweep is for.
3. **`app` sees `ui`, `platform` and `model` and deliberately not `core`**, so `EffectPushTest` drives
   a hand-built effect list rather than a round. That is the right way round: what is under test is
   the carriage, and a fixture list is a *sharper* input than a round because it can contain kinds a
   fixture round would never reach — the two row-less ones among them.
4. **No new `ui` test.** The consumer is exercised end to end from `app`, and its exhaustiveness is a
   compile error rather than an assertion. `houseGraded`'s existing callers are what prove the
   delegation, and they stayed green.

## Push — Unit P2

*The freeze lifted for one named thing: the machinery that carries client-originated **intents** to
the host as **attributed** Events. P1 carried effects down; this carries requests up. Nothing here
edits a schema row, changes a rule, or arms a round — but it does add two `Intent` members and one
`core` translation, because a carriage with nothing to carry is not a carriage.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1565, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(ui 602, model 478, core 188, platform 133, harness 114, app 33, cards 17), up from **1528** —
**37 new**, read out of the test XML and never off an exit code. The arithmetic: model's 6 are
`commonTest` and run on both `jvmTest` and `iosSimulatorArm64Test` (6 × 2); core's 5, platform's 9,
harness's 6 and app's 5 run on the simulator target only. `vocabularyLint` clean (model 50, ui 60,
core 26, app 21, cards 7), `redactionLint clean — 50 file(s)`, `boundary: core clean`,
`boundary: ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest` green. No phone was required.

### The shape of it, in one paragraph

`Intent` is deliberately not `@Serializable` for the reason `Effect` is not, so the wire type is
**constructed** rather than annotated on. But where P1 constructed a narrower type to keep ground
truth off a phone, this constructs one to keep an **attribution** off a phone — and the field it
narrows away is the one field every member of `Intent` has. `IntentMessage` has no seat and no way
to acquire one; `IntentDesk` stamps the seat the *connection* was admitted to; `core`'s `factOf`
turns the attributed request into the one `Event` it names; and `admit(state, intent, at)` is
`admit(state, factOf(intent, at))` and nothing else, so there is one gate with two doors into it.

### 1 · The discipline stopped being a convention and became the wire's

L2 kept *intents are attributed by connection, never by a client naming itself* by having
`MeetingModel` publish a seat-free `MeetingRequest` and attaching the seat one layer out —
`asIntent(seat, names)`. **That function is gone.** Its replacement is `asMessage(names)`, and
losing the parameter is the point rather than a tidy-up: the old one was `ui`'s *promise* not to
name a seat, kept by nobody passing it a wrong one. `IntentMessage` **cannot** name a seat, so the
promise is now the shape of the frame, all the way to the desk. **There is no longer any function
in `ui` that turns a `Seat` into an `Intent`.**

The seats that *do* ride up are content and not attribution, and the rule is the same one P1 landed
on pointing the other way: a seat may be in a payload when the seat **is** what the message is
about, never when it is who the message is from. `SelectVote` carries a target because a ballot is
a list the house drew on this phone and publishes live to the couch (D-117).

### 2 · Three client-originated facts have no request at all, and each is a decision

The line `factOf` holds: **an `Intent` may exist only where the `Event` it becomes is fully
determined by what the phone did, which connection did it, and when it arrived.** Three fail it:

- **The Revoke firing** (`Event.ContactMade`) — *phone touched to phone. Physical, so it is
  reported, never requested.* It names a target seat that only the authority can resolve, because
  D7 puts every phone's advertised identity behind a token rotating every fifteen seconds. A client
  that could send this would be naming the seat it revoked.
- **The Revoked report** (`MeetingTrigger.RevokeReported`) — the same act, the same answer. This is
  why the meeting card and the report are **two wire members rather than one with a `trigger`
  field**: a trigger field would be a client choosing which admission rule it would like to be
  judged by, and the card is inert during an Egress while the report is not (D-133).
- **The Egress firing** (`Event.EgressFired`) — the finger is a client's, the event is not: its type
  and its two nodes are the house's draw, made above the rules against a home's shape the phone has
  never held.

`IntentAdmissionTest` classifies **every** `Event` through an exhaustive `when` — a new event does
not compile until somebody decides whether a phone may ask for it — and then checks the
classification against what `factOf` actually reaches, in both directions. The table is written by
hand and the coverage is computed, so neither can agree with itself (L2's injection-6 finding).

### 3 · Dedup, and the window it belongs to

D-097 makes intents fire-and-forget — *clients hold no request/response posture* — so nothing here
is an ack. `RoundBody.Act` carries a per-connection sequence and the desk keeps the highest it has
seen per seat: **at or below the mark is dropped**, because `ArmRevoke` applied twice is two
cooldowns spent on one press and `CheckIn` twice is a phone counted twice against a gate that
closes on a number (D-104). A number *above* the mark is put through even when it skips, because
refusing it would be rule 1's forbidden shape in transport clothing — the skip is reported to the
authority (rule 6) and the phone's screen is corrected the ordinary way, by `RoundInbox` noticing
the missing push and the house re-asserting.

**The window is the connection and it has to be.** A stopped app resumes its seat with a fresh
outbox counting from 1 (story 0.8), so a desk holding the old mark would take every request that
phone ever made again for a duplicate — not a leak, but a player pressing controls that reach a
house which has already decided it has heard them, forever, in silence. Both ends reset on the same
fact. **There is deliberately no `roundEnded` on either**: clearing at a round boundary would let a
stale frame from *that* round arrive at a desk that had forgotten it and be put to the next round's
rules, and D-157 keeps the sockets up between rounds anyway.

### 4 · The ordered queue is not a precaution — the test caught the bug on its first run

The first build ran each arriving request in its own `launch`. `IntentLoopTest`'s identity test
failed immediately: the desk and the emit boundary both suspend, so two requests from **one phone**
reached the rules in the order they *finished*. A `LockVote` overtaking its own `SelectVote` locks a
ballot the couch never watched being made, which is D-117's whole point undone by a scheduler.

`LobbyHouse` now has **one ordered queue with one consumer** (D4 made literal at the host end), and
a **seating rides the same queue** — a seating that raced the frames behind it would clear the mark
*after* the new connection's first request had already been judged against the old one, so the first
press after a reconnect would be dropped in silence.

### 5 · The round trip, proved in the one module that can see both ends

`app` sees `ui`, `platform` and `model` and deliberately not `core`, so it cannot run `admit` —
which is why `IntentPushTest` proves the client half and the stamp, and **`harness` is where the
join lives**, because it is the only module that sees `core` and `platform` at once. The three
round trips the charter named all run there over a real socket: a check-in filling the gate and
coming back to every phone; a vote selection reaching the couch and the voter and nobody else; an
entry graded with the verdict returning to the sender alone.

**States are compared as `Transcript.render` rows, not with `==`.** `GameState` is a plain class
with no structural equality, so `assertEquals` on two of them compares identities and can only ever
fail. The state row is what the replay guarantee already compares two rounds by, and it is *every
field of authority state, with no exceptions for fields nothing writes yet* — a stronger check than
a data class's `equals` would have been.

### The injections — ten run, every one reverted by targeted reverse-replace

Never `git checkout`. The tree was grepped for every marker afterwards and is clean.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | a wire member gains `actor: Seat? = null` and `attributedTo` prefers it | **nothing, at first** — see below. After the instrument was fixed: `IntentWireTest.noMessageCanSayWhoSentIt` |
| 2 | the dedup dropped | **4 ×** `IntentDeskTest` — the duplicate, the below-the-mark number, and *both* re-seating tests via their negative controls |
| 3 | a re-seating no longer clears the mark | **3 ×** `IntentDeskTest` — the resumed phone, the one-seat-only check, and the two-ends-agree walk |
| 4 | the desk stamps `Seat(0)` instead of the connection's | `IntentDeskTest.theSeatComesOffTheConnectionAndTheFrameContributesNothing` |
| 5 | `factOf` maps `CheckIn` to `ReadyToVoteDeclared` | 2 × `IntentAdmissionTest` — the translation, and the completeness table noticing a fact nobody could ask for |
| 6 | `admit(state, intent, at)` gets its own gate (`Admitted(reduce(…))`) | 2 × `IntentAdmissionTest` — *one gate* and *refused outside a round* |
| 7 | `RoundOverTransport.act` numbers the request and sends nothing | 3 × `IntentPushTest` — the control sequence, the resend, and the outbound sweep |
| 8 | the downward leg severed: `EffectWire.emit` walks `state.seats` | 2 × `IntentLoopTest` — the live selection reached all eight seats, the verdict reached all eight |
| 9 | the upward leg severed: every request taken for a duplicate | **6 ×** `IntentLoopTest` — the whole file, the trio included |
| 10 | the ordered queue reverted to a launch per request | **nothing** — see below |

**Injection 1 is the entry worth keeping.** The first version of `noMessageCanSayWhoSentIt` swept
the *rendered bytes* for a forbidden field name, and the injection walked straight through it:
`encodeDefaults` is off, so a nullable `actor` defaulting to null renders nothing at all. The bytes
were clean while the type had grown exactly the field the file exists to forbid — and a client that
set it would have been attributed to somebody else. **The test now reads the serial descriptor**,
which is the type as the wire sees it, and keeps a second bytes sweep with `encodeDefaults = true`
so the two instruments fail for different reasons rather than the same one. A guard that reads a
rendering is a guard an encoder setting can switch off.

**Injection 10 did not reproduce, and that is reported rather than dressed up.** The reordering is a
race: it failed on this test's very first run — which is how the production bug in §4 was found —
and passed when re-injected later under different scheduling. So the ordering guarantee rests on the
**structure** (one queue, one consumer, in both rigs and in production) and not on a test that can
be relied on to notice its absence. See E-P2-4.

### Escalations

- **⚠️ E-P2-1 · The Subroutine hand-over has a wire member and no control to send it.**
  `IntentMessage.HandOverEntry` is carried, translated and proved end to end from a fixture — the
  entry-graded round trip is real — but **no screen publishes one.** `SubroutineModel.handOver` sets
  `handedOver = true` on a per-entry type and sends nothing, and wiring it needs three things this
  unit may not decide: a `send` seam on `SubroutineModel` (`MeetingModel`'s shape), a projection
  from ten heterogeneous entry types to the one `List<Int>` the event carries, and **the marker**,
  which the model does not hold and which lives in the scan flow another unit is holding. Escalated
  rather than guessed: an entry sent against the wrong marker grades against a question the house
  never asked, and every Subroutine in the round would come back refused.
- **⚠️ E-P2-2 · `LobbyHouse` is mirrored by two test rigs and driven by neither.** `IntentPushTest`
  and `IntentLoopTest` each rebuild its arrangement — ledger, server, desk, wire, queue — because
  `LobbyHouse.start` waits on an advertisement no test has a radio for (P1's rig had the same
  problem and the same answer). So the production object's own wiring, including the ordered queue
  added by this unit, **is not under test**, and a divergence between it and the rigs is invisible.
  The fix is a seam that brings the house up without the radio; that is a change to `LobbyHouse`'s
  startup, which is the loop's rather than the carriage's.
- **⚠️ E-P2-3 · `MeetingModel.send` still has no production wiring, and the reason is a cycle.**
  `MeetingModel(send = …)` wants the round transport, which wants a `FlowModel`, which wants the
  `MeetingModel`. `RoundOverTransport` has no production caller either (P1 left it that way), so
  breaking the cycle means deciding how the app root holds the round's client half — which is the
  same join as E-D3-3's second half. Proved end to end in `IntentPushTest` with the real
  `MeetingModel` and the real `RoundOverTransport`; **not wired in `Cheats.kt`.**
- **⚠️ E-P2-4 · The per-seat ordering guarantee has no reliable test.** See injection 10. It is
  structural and it is documented at both queues, but nothing fails deterministically when it is
  removed. A deterministic version would need an admit step that can be made to suspend on demand,
  which is a seam in the rig rather than in the code under test.
- **⚠️ E-P2-5 · STOP NOW and the Sync Pulse are client controls with no wire member.**
  `Event.PerformanceEnded` and `Event.SyncPulseReturned` are both a phone's, and both would pass
  `factOf`'s test. Neither is in this unit's named scope, so both are classified as unreachable in
  `IntentAdmissionTest`'s table with the reason at the branch rather than left as a hole. Adding
  them later is two members and two rows.
- **⚠️ E-D3-3's second half is still open, and this unit did not close it.** `LobbyHouse.emit` and
  `LobbyHouse.onIntent` are now both real and both have no production caller, because nothing arms a
  round: `armingFor` still has none, exactly as W1 and P1 left it. **Both carriages are built and
  proved; the thing between them is the loop's.**

### Flagged design choices

1. **`core` gained a file, and it is a translation rather than a rule.** `factOf` reads no
   `GameState`, refuses nothing and chooses between no events. `admit(state, intent, at)` is two
   calls. The alternative — a mapper in `platform` or `app` — would have put the `Intent → Event`
   correspondence in a module that cannot see `Event`'s rules, and the exhaustiveness that makes it
   worth anything would have lived away from the type it is exhaustive over.
2. **The tick is an argument, not a field of `GameState`.** *The house's clock, sampled at the edge
   like every other.* Two requests reduced at one tick would be two events the recording could not
   order, and the recording is the only debugging instrument this game has.
3. **`RoundBody` grew a third member rather than the upward direction growing a wrapper.** `Gap` and
   `Act` are both client → host and are deliberately separate, so the desk's two answers — *replay
   what you missed* and *put this to the rules* — can never be reached by the same branch.
4. **The forged frame is refused rather than stripped.** `RoundWire`'s decoder is strict, so a body
   carrying a field the type does not have decodes to `null` and is left alone as *not a round
   body*. Either behaviour would be safe — the desk stamps the connection's seat regardless — but
   it is worth knowing which one is in force, so `IntentPushTest` asserts both that the forge fails
   **and** that the honest frame behind it on the same socket is still attributed correctly. A
   client that could stop its own session with one bad frame would be a denial nobody could
   diagnose.
5. **`verify-guards.sh` was not run.** It takes backups of the working tree and restores them, and
   other units were live in this repo at the time. Its six guards are unchanged by this unit.

## Push — Unit P3, the rehearsal

*The first complete round in this game's existence: five seats, one physical iPhone holding the
authority, four Mac instances joining it across the owner's LAN, and every act of the round run
over the real transport. The freeze held for game rules — what is new is one build-file edge, two
seams on the lobby's transport, and a bench that plays. **Everything the bench had to fake is
named at its own call site and listed below**, and that list is what this unit is actually for.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1575, failures=0, errors=0,
skipped=0** (ui 602, model 478, core 188, platform 133, harness 114, app 43, cards 17), up from
**1565** — **10 new**, all in `app`'s cheat tests, read out of the test XML and never off an exit
code. `vocabularyLint` clean (7 + 26 + 27 + 60 + 50 files), `redactionLint clean — 50 file(s)`,
`boundary: core clean`, `boundary: ui clean`, `variantSweepRelease` and `variantSweepPlaytest`
both green. `verify-guards.sh`: **6 passed, 0 failed** — including guard 6, *release cannot reach
the cheats*, which is the one this unit could most easily have broken.

---

### 1 · The reason nothing could arm a round was one missing line, and it was not a boundary

E-D3-3's remaining half has been open since L3 and was restated by W1, P1, P2 and Restore in the
same words: *`armingFor` has no production caller.* The reason is mechanical and nobody had said
it out loud — **`app` could not see `core`.** `app/build.gradle.kts` named `:ui`, `:platform` and
`:model` and stopped there, so no module that ships inside this application could reach
`armingFor`, `admit`, `factOf` or `egressFor` at all. It was not that somebody had forgotten to
call the function; there was no compilation unit in the app from which the call could be written.

**The architecture already ruled on this and the edge was simply never drawn.** The boundary table
reads *app roots · may depend on: everything · may NOT: —*, and the paragraph under it puts the
tick driver in the app roots specifically because `core` cannot import coroutines
(`game-architecture.md:651,661`). So the one build-file change in this unit is that line, with the
citation at it.

**No guarded boundary moved.** The three edges the modules exist for are `core ↛ coroutines`,
`core ↛ platform` and `ui ↛ core`; `:core:boundaryCheck` and `:ui:boundaryCheck` enforce them,
neither reads this line, and both still report clean. `core/build.gradle.kts` forbids `:platform`,
`:ui` and `:harness` to `core`, and `ui/build.gradle.kts` forbids `:core` to `ui` — `app` appears
in neither list, because `app` was never meant to be in one.

### 2 · Two seams on the lobby's transport, and one of them is about there being one socket

- **`RoundSeam`** — the round's client half, handed the lobby's own connection. There is exactly
  one socket per phone and the lobby owns it: a round that opened a second would be a second seat
  as far as `SeatLedger` is concerned, and a round that could not reach the first could not
  receive a push at all. Three methods, one per moment the round cares about — *a connection
  exists*, *a frame arrived*, *this phone has taken its seat*. Null in every production
  construction.
- **`LobbyHouse.armingInputs()`** — everything a round is armed out of, in one call rather than
  five getters, because it is one moment: balance values lock at arming, and a class offering five
  readers offers five chances to read them at five different instants. **It is the only reader of
  the lines outside the desk**, and that is the permitted caller `LobbyDesk`'s own KDoc names: the
