was temporary and has been removed — the repo gains the pictures, not a test.)*

```
surgetargets-insider / -resident      isolatetargets-insider / -resident
spoofrooms-insider   / -resident      winresidents-insider   / -resident
ghostegress-running                   ghostegress-empty
scancaught-sniff                      scancaught-handshake-no-briefing
homedetail-refused-names-the-cause
```

**Everything the units claimed is in the pixels.** The ghost Egress widget draws
`EGRESS . TETHER  68%` over a lit bar with a bright border while one runs, and a dim empty box with
no bar and no text otherwise. Sniff's briefing sits two lines above BEGIN on the caught scan and the
Handshake render has nothing in that slot. The Residents' sign-off is bone and slate with no amber
anywhere. The refusal draws both of D-180's lines. The Spoof plan draws cells with the stairs
hatched and unlabelled as a target, and no YOU ARE HERE.

**Six things a person should look at. Nothing was fixed.**

1. ⚠️ **The two percentages on the couch are the same number twice.** `SYSTEM INTEGRITY 65%` and
   `EGRESS . TETHER 68%` sit eight pixels apart, right-aligned, in the same ink, at the same size,
   on the one screen an out player stares at for a whole round. One is the game's progress and one
   is a crisis countdown. In a dark house at a glance they are not distinguishable.
2. ⚠️ **The empty Egress widget is an anonymous box.** With no type pushed it draws *nothing at all*
   — no label, no bar — so for the first part of every round the couch shows an unexplained empty
   rectangle. Reserving the space avoids a layout jump, which is presumably why; whether an
   unlabelled box beats no box is a person's call.
3. ⚠️ **The cover words carry into the picker headers.** A Resident's render titles the pickers
   **BUS**, **CACHE** and **THERMAL** (`PanelVals.tier2A`), over a body that is otherwise identical
   to the Insider's — the full roster, the full marker list, the full plan. **This is not a leak in
   the app**: D-142 makes a Resident's tier-2 tiles take no pointer input from the first
   millisecond, so there is no hold to run and no view to be caught on, and `HoldInputTest` guards
   it in pixels, in state and in what went up the wire. It is worth knowing anyway, because on this
   one screen the cover word is the *only* thing between the two roles.
4. **The briefing moves the artwork.** The caught scan's glyph-and-instruction block sits about 27px
   higher when a briefing is present, so two consecutive scans of different Subroutines will show
   the centre of the screen jump.
5. **The briefing is the dimmest line on its own screen.** It renders in the same faint ink as the
   caption under it, and it is the one sentence whose entire purpose is to be read at a card in the
   dark before a screen that cannot be read.
6. **Large blind zones at device aspect.** The pickers use the top ~40% with BACK pinned to the
   bottom; the Residents' ending leaves roughly 40% of the panel empty between EGRESSES CONTAINED
   and WORKING FOR THE HOUSE. None of it is wrong and none of it showed on the 3:4 sweep canvas.

**A note on parity.** `winresidents` differs between the roles and that is correct, not a leak: the
Insider's copy carries the extra `NUMBER WITHHELD / UNFORTUNATE.` block — their own last message,
after the round is over. `surgetargets`, `isolatetargets` and `spoofrooms` differ only in the header
word, per item 3.

### The morning's checklist

**Rule on this first — ⚠️ E-P3-1, the round is invisible.** It is the honest pick off the list above
and it is not close. Every other open item is a rule, a number or a wire member; this one is *a
complete, correct game that no player would see any of*, proved on five phones, and P1 refused to
guess it precisely because **which screen a `MeetingOpened` opens is the owner's to say**. It blocks
E-P3-2 as well (the walk-in seat has no check-in control because `StandAndWalkIn` is one of the
sixteen routeless kinds), and it is the one thing standing between this build and a playable
evening. It wants the owner before it wants an engineer.

Then, in order:

1. **The decision-log diffs.** Revision 35 landed in `bdcdb68` (*a home that cannot go on the air
   says so*) — note it predates P1 and its ⚠️ was closed later by D-179. Revision 36 is `e3cb2e6`
   (*the phone that is not an iPhone*), revision 37 is `fc1002f` (*four rulings the build was
   already obeying*), and **D-179 + D-180** are `b528392`. `git show <hash> --
   _bmad-output/planning-artifacts/gdds/gdd-someone-is-home-2026-08-16/decision-log.md` on each.
2. **The `shots4/` set**, with the six notes above beside it. The two drafted strings are visible in
   two of these shots — the cause line in `homedetail-refused-names-the-cause`, Sniff's briefing in
   `scancaught-sniff` — so the copy call and the legibility call can be made off the same picture.
   ⚠️ **`shots3/` is now stale**: F1 item 1 re-cut the couch's ballot to 45s and redrew the segments
   (D-163), so the countdown in those frames is a picture of a window that no longer exists.
3. **The hardware that still wants hands.** None of it can be checked in the simulator.
   - **The BLE Revoke handshake** — P3 stand-ins 1 and 2, and the largest unbuilt thing in the game.
   - **Surge's sound** (T1·5) — three questions written on `Speaker`: which audio route, whether it
     plays through the silent switch, what it does to the audio session. Every actual is
     `SilentSpeaker` today, deliberately, because a sound that plays *sometimes* would be worse than
     one that never plays.
   - **How long the slam lasts** (T1·6) — the two luminances are on the effect; the timing is the
     platform's.
   - **Every Tier-2 surface has only ever been rendered.** The pickers, the plan, the ghost Egress
     widget and the bone sign-off have never been on a phone in a dark room, and `shots4/` is a
     desktop render, not a device screenshot.
   - **A second rehearsal after E-P3-1 is answered**, since the first one could not see the round it
     was proving.
4. **The cheap engineering that is now unblocked.** `app` can see `core` (P3 §1), so **the tick
   driver** (E-P3-3) is a coroutine turning a handle that already exists — and five authority pushes
   are waiting on it. **The Sync Pulse and the Egress firing** (E-P2-5 / E-P3-7) are two wire members
   and two rows, and until they exist the Egress is a countdown nobody can answer.
5. **`egressFired`'s `cooldownStarted` lie** (T1·8 / T2·7) — flagged twice, unchanged twice under
   the freeze, and now the only handler of five that hardcodes `true`. It is a one-line fix waiting
   for a unit that is allowed to make it.

---

## Routing — Unit R1

*The freeze lifted for one named thing: **routing the routeless effect kinds to screens and fields**,
the tick driver, the missing wire members and the meeting rules the owner ruled on 2026-08-23 (**D-183 to D-185**,
recorded in decision-log revision 38 while this unit was running — the build and the log agree). This
is the unit that answers E-P3-1 — a client that received a hundred pushes across a complete round
and never left the lobby screen — and it is the first unit whose subject is what a **player** sees.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1681, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(ui 648, model 488, core 223, platform 133, harness 115, app 57, cards 17), up from **1636** —
**45 new**, read out of the test XML and never off an exit code. `vocabularyLint` clean (ui 67,
model 54, core 30, app 30, cards 7), `redactionLint clean — 54 file(s)`, `boundary: core clean`,
`boundary: ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest` and `ScreenGraphTest` green. **No phone was required.**

### The shape of it, in one paragraph

Eleven of E-P1-4's sixteen routeless kinds now move a screen or a field; the four timed meeting
transitions and the Egress's expiry are minted by a real clock; four controls that had no wire
member now have one; and a meeting became a hard stop for every Insider ability. **Nothing here
decides a game question** — every branch either copies a value the house sent onto `PanelState` or
moves the screen by a rule written down in `Flow.housePushes`, and the tick driver mints only
because a deadline the house already set has passed.

### 1 · The round is visible — E-P3-1 closed

`MeetingOpened` rings the living phone (`Found` on a reported Revoke, `Call` on the card — *same
layout, different reason*), `StandAndWalkIn` puts an out seat on `Ghost2`, the three counts move the
numbers the meeting screens were drawing as fixtures, `VoteHeld` re-asserts the VOTE CAST lock,
`MeetingResult`/`MeetingResolved` fill the tally, the two message kinds raise their banners, and
`HouseSignedOff` writes the ending's last line. **The caller is named off the roster** (D-121) —
both ring screens drew PRIYA and ELLIOT on every phone in every round until this landed.

### 2 · The gate can close — E-P3-2 closed, and the escalation was half wrong

The escalation reads as though the walk-in screen needed a check-in control built. **It did not.**
`Ghost2Screen` has drawn the living's own `CheckIn()` since the out screens were ported — what was
missing was **any way to arrive on it**, because `StandAndWalkIn` was routeless. One line of routing
closes it, and the correction is worth recording because the next reader of that entry would build a
second control.

**A second stall was found on the way and fixed**: the ring is a six-second presentation timer and
the gate is a count of phones, so the gate can close while one phone is still ringing. That phone
missed the phase push, fell through to `Assemble` on its own timer, and waited for a gate that had
already closed — E-P3-2 from the other end. The three ring screens now answer `MEETING_PHASE_OPENED`
and go straight to `Notice`.

### 3 · The tick driver — E-P3-3 closed, and it is two files on purpose

`HouseClock` is pure: it takes **elapsed nanoseconds** and a `GameState` and returns the events that
fell due. `tickHouse` is the loop — read a monotonic clock, sleep a step, pump whatever really
elapsed, call three seams — and it holds no deadline, no duration and no branch on the round. So
*when does a meeting end* is tested with **no coroutine, no delay and no wall clock**: a
ninety-second discussion is one call with a number in it.

**Two durations moved into `Balance`** because something finally had to read them:
`DISCUSSION_SECONDS = 90` (`gdd.md:412`) and `TALLY_SECONDS = 15`, both literals in `ui/Countdown.kt`
until now. `Countdown` reads them, so the bar on five phones and the transition that ends it count
against one number. The takeover's halfway mark is **derived** from the tally window rather than
typed, so a change moves both together.

**The driver found its own bug.** A transition fired on **every pump** once its window passed,
because in a live round the minted event changes the phase underneath it — the driver was relying on
the rules to shut it up, which holds right up until an event is refused. `MeetingClosed` arriving at
a meeting that has already ended is refused `WrongMeetingPhase`, quietly and correctly, and the only
trace would have been a refusal log nobody reads. The clock now remembers what it has spent on the
phase it is running, and the test holds the state still to prove it.

### 4 · The four wire members, and the one that needed a frame of its own

STOP NOW and the Sync Pulse are ordinary `IntentMessage` members: both pass `factOf`'s line more
plainly than anything already on it, so **the gap was a missing member and never a missing argument.**
The Subroutine hand-over got its three missing pieces — a send seam, `SubroutineEntry.entered` (the
ten vocabularies projected to one `List<Int>`, on the interface so a Subroutine cannot be built
without one), and the marker.

**The Egress firing is `RoundBody.Fire`, a fourth body member**, and the argument is `factOf`'s
rather than tidiness. An `IntentMessage` carrying the type and the nodes would let a client **choose
its own Egress**; one carrying nothing would need a `factOf` arm that composes a fact from a second
source. So the request keeps its own frame, the desk answers it at its own door (`Landing.Draw`), and
the two answers — *put this to the rules* and *make the draw, then put THAT to the rules* — cannot be
reached by one branch. It carries **nothing but its dedup sequence**, off the same counter an `Act`
uses, because an Egress delivered twice is the house's longest cooldown spent on one finger.

### 5 · A meeting is a hard stop — the owner's rulings, and three flags closed

- **T1·7 / T2·8 closed — D-183.** Tier-2 fires are refused at the gate during a meeting
  (`MeetingAlreadyRunning`). The ruling's argument is stronger than the one this unit first wrote and
  the code now carries it: *there is no surface to fire from* — page 2 does not exist during a
  meeting for any seat of any role, so the branch is not a client-visible path at all. **It costs nothing** — the event never reaches the
  handler that would spend the pool. The Revoke is deliberately not included; Tier 1 arms in place.
- **T1·9 / T2·9 closed — D-185.** Isolations and phantoms **fail at the call**. A card dark in a house nobody is
  walking and a false sighting of people standing in one room both spend a meeting affecting
  nothing, so *a meeting can eat one whole* is answered by not making anybody spend it. Nothing is
  emitted about either — asserted by comparing the effect lists of a meeting called with an
  isolation running and one without.
- **`gdd.md:373`'s open *pauses or resets* is answered — D-184: it resets.** Every per-seat clock comes out of a
  meeting at **half** — the round's opening handed to the Insiders again, `openingCooldowns` shared
  with arming so the two are one arithmetic. **The Egress's house-wide clock is frozen out of it
  while its Egress runs**; a reset mid-crisis would hand the Insiders a second Egress on top of the
  one the Residents are crossing the house to contain.
- **T1·8 / T2·7 closed.** `egressFired` hardcoded `cooldownStarted = true`, so an Egress fired into a
  running cooldown told that phone it had spent one it did not and the bar was wrong for three
  minutes. It reads the state transition now. **The shape did not move** — one `AbilityFired`,
  addressed to the actor, whatever happened — and three `EgressTest` assertions that encoded the lie
  were rewritten to assert the shape and the honest flag apart.

### 6 · Two hand-written completeness lists had silently stopped covering three abilities

`IntentAdmissionTest`'s `everyIntent` and `everyEvent()`, and `IntentWireTest`'s `everyMessage`, are
written by hand on purpose — *a list derived from the thing it is checking agrees with itself*
(L2's injection-6 finding). **All three had drifted**: the three Tier-2 fires were in none of them.
The completeness check therefore classified `SurgeFired` as requestable, found no request producing
it, and passed — because a fact absent from *both* lists is a fact the loop never reaches from either
direction. Fixed, with the drift named at each list. Hand-written stays right; what it needs is the
treatment the emit schema's own list gets.

### Injections — twelve, each caught by a different assertion

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `MeetingOpened` routed nowhere | the ring test — E-P3-1 reproduced exactly |
| 2 | both triggers → `Call` | the report lost its header |
| 3 | `ReadyProgressed.expected` → the seat total | the two denominators collapsed |
| 4 | a banner allowed over a house-driving screen | it interrupted the tally |
| 5 | `isTierTwo && false` | Tier 2 fired from the assembly; the pool test caught it too |
| 6 | the Egress clock reset mid-crisis | the frozen half |
| 7 | isolations survive the call | T1·9 |
| 8 | `cooldownStarted = true` restored | four assertions, three of them pre-existing |
| 9 | the check-in gate given a 30s timeout | D-104's *presence over proceed-without* |
| 10 | the phase clock reads the round's age | a late meeting ended instantly |
| 11 | a paused Egress allowed to expire | D-133 |
| 12 | the clock forgets what it spent | four timed transitions fired 20× a second |

**A thirteenth, on method rather than on the code.** `git checkout <file>` was used to revert an
injection on a file holding a large body of **uncommitted** work, and took all of it. Injections
after that restored from a copy, and the unit checkpointed to a commit first. *Reverting an
injection is not the same operation as reverting a file.*

### ⚠️ ESCALATIONS

**⚠️ E-R1-1 · The calling seat cannot be routed, because a phone does not know its own seat.** The
owner's table asks for *the calling seat → Calling*. `MeetingOpened` carries `caller: Seat`, and
`LobbyBody.Standing` carries counts and names and never *you are seat three* — deliberately. So the
comparison has one operand. Two shapes would close it and both are outside this unit's named scope: a
**per-seat `MeetingOpened`** carrying *you called this* (which is `EgressOpened`'s existing
arrangement), or **teaching a phone its own seat**. The caller lands on `Call` with everybody else
meanwhile — one header wrong on one phone, and not a leak.

**⚠️ E-R1-2 · The living's tally rows have no sender.** The drawn tally screen shows names with
counts — MARCUS 3, SKIP 2, PRIYA 1 — to *everybody*, and the design is explicit that this is lawful
(the living get *a count and nothing else*, which is a per-candidate tally without attribution). But
`Effect.MeetingResult` carries only `restrained`. Either the effect is a field short or the drawn
screen shows the living more than it should. **The fixture stays** and the couch's rows are real.

**⚠️ E-R1-3 · `GameState.accessPausedAt` is now unused machinery.** Two of the owner's rulings between
them make it unobservable: Tier 2 is refused at the gate during a meeting, so `accessReady`'s pause
is never consulted; and every cooldown resets to half at meeting end, which overwrites the resume the
pause existed to compute. It is **kept and flagged rather than deleted** — removing state is a
recording-shape decision and the Shift ruling is in the log.

**⚠️ E-R1-4 · Five kinds in the owner's table are not routed, and each is a decision rather than a
gap.** `Surged`'s **malfunction sequence** (1.5s bright / 1s dark / 2.5s boot / 5s total, mid-work
lost) is a render path rather than a field and wants its own unit; `LampSet` → the lock-screen lamp
level runs against the standing argument that the lamp is `ui/Lamp.kt`'s and must not pass through a
state field (rule 7); `SubroutineProgressed` → the ghosts' live meter carries a raw `remaining` and
turning it into a percentage needs `seats`, which is D-103's denominator; `ScanAnswered` → the
caught-scan flow and `EgressHeld`/`SyncPulseAnswered` → the containment screens both want surfaces
this unit did not build. Named rather than half-built.

**⚠️ E-P2-3 restated with a consequence.** `MeetingModel.send` and the new `SubroutineModel.send`
both exist and **neither has a production caller**, because the cycle is unchanged: the models want
the round transport, which wants a `FlowModel`, which wants the models. The seams are built and
proved from tests; the join is still the loop's.

## Static — the unit

*The freeze lifted for one named scope: **replacing Spoof with Static end to end** (D-186). Not a
tune and not a rename — the mechanic was cut, the slot was kept, and what went with the mechanic was
a screen, an enum row's worth of client surface, two effects, a state list, a recorded input and the
largest disclosure the emit table has ever held.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1689, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(ui 655, model 488, core 223, platform 133, harness 115, app 58, cards 17), read out of the test XML
and never off an exit code. R1 left **1681**, so this is **8 net** across five files added and one
deleted — the honest number for a unit whose subject is mostly subtraction. `vocabularyLint` clean
(ui 68, model 53, core 30, app 30, cards 7), `redactionLint clean — 53 file(s)`, `boundary: core`,
`boundary: ui`, `DeviceLayoutTest`, `ScreenGraphTest`, `ScreenshotTest`, `LightSignatureTest`,
`MeetingDisclosureTest` and `DifferentialTest` all green. **No phone was required.**

### The shape of it, in one paragraph

`Event.StaticFired` carries an actor and a tick and nothing else — the narrowest fire in the game —
and the handler writes **one nullable deadline** on the state and emits **one house-wide
announcement**. The announcement carries no seat, so the anonymity is structural rather than
filtered: two Insiders firing at the same tick produce byte-identical effects. Everything else in
this unit is what came out.

### 1 · What was deleted, and it is more than the ability

| gone | what it was |
|---|---|
| `Effect.PhantomPlaced` | an effect with no schema row and no audience |
| `Effect.HomePlanOffered` | **the largest disclosure in the emit table** — the whole home to one class |
| `HomePlanView`, `FloorView`, `RoomOutline`, `PlanCell` | the client-facing plan types, all four |
| `Event.RoundArmed.plan` | a recorded input the rules read once and dropped |
| `GameState.phantoms`, `withPhantom`, `phantomFor` | the phantom plane |
| `placementOf`'s inference arm | the phantom's half of D-136 |
| `Balance.SPOOF_DURATION`, `SPOOF_COOLDOWN`, `phantomIsTemporary` | its two weights and their guard |
| `ScreensSpoof.kt` | the map picker, whole |
| `ScreenId.SpoofRooms`, its two graph rows, `PanelState.homePlan`, `PanelVals.HOME_PLAN` | the route and the fixture |
| `Transcript.plan`, `RecordingText.plan` | five separators of picture in a line-oriented file |

**D-136 survives and is not swept with the rest**, which the ruling says outright. `Placement` is
kept — `Known` and `Inferred`, the file renamed to `Placement.kt` — with `Inferred` now having **no
constructor caller anywhere in the game**. That is the honest state of a grade whose only producer
will be a BLE sighting on a map nobody has built, and deleting it would delete the shape of the
ruling along with the ability. Written on the type, where the next reader will be.

### 2 · The effect carries no seat, and that is the ability rather than a shortcut

*Loud and anonymous* is two words and the second one is structural here. `Effect.StaticOpened(seconds,
seed)` has nowhere to put an actor, `GameState.staticUntil` has nowhere to put one, and the recording's
state row has nowhere to print one — **a recording of this game cannot say who jammed the house,
because the house never knew.** `gdd.md:398`'s no-actor-side-feedback rule is then satisfied by
construction rather than by suppression: the firing phone is told exactly what a bystander's phone is
told, because it is the same message.

`gdd.md:393`'s *every observable effect must be self-inflictable* needed no expression either. The jam
is house-wide, so it covers the firing Insider's own monitoring; an Insider standing at the Terminal
when their own Static lands reads the same noise a Resident does.

**A second fire extends and can never shorten.** `withStatic` takes the later deadline. A window a
second Insider could cut short would make its length on screen a report of how many of them reached
for it, which is the anonymity leaving through the render.

### 3 · The seed, and rule 5 on a surface that has to animate

The noise is **authored light**: `staticSeed(state.seed, event.at)` is minted from the round's seed
and the firing tick and **from nothing else** — no actor, no seat index — and rides the effect, so six
phones draw the same field and a replay draws it again. `Static.field(seed, frame)` is a pure
function with no `Random`, no clock and no platform source; the hash is written out by hand because
what it has to be is **the same on every target**, and a field that differed between Kotlin/Native
and the JVM would make the noise a fact about which phone you were holding.

⚠️ **The frame loop is bounded by the jam's own window, and the first build was not.** An unbounded
`withFrameNanos` loop hung `runDesktopComposeUiTest` outright — the composition never idles — which
is L5's recorded hang arriving from a different direction. It is `rememberHold`'s loop now, ending
where the house's seconds end, so the veil's last frame and the panel field's expiry agree by
arithmetic rather than by two timers lining up.

### 4 · The render, and exactly which surfaces got it

**The real data is drawn normally and completely, and the noise goes over it.** D-186 is explicit
that Static must be distinguishable from a disconnection — a jammed Terminal does not read as a
broken one — and `NO SIGNAL` already means *you are not at the station*. About one cell in six is
fully transparent and the holes move every step, which is what makes the counts *faintly* readable
rather than gone: glimpses of a number nobody can hold still long enough to add up.

**Two surfaces exist and both got it: `TermLive` and `Timelapse`.** ⚠️ **E8 is design-complete and
build-pending**, so both are drawn over fixture counts — and the Timelapse's is the narrower claim of
the two: a playback watched *during* a jam renders as noise, which is the right picture for a reason
narrower than the ruling's. **The seconds-crossing-the-window arithmetic is not built and cannot be**
— there is no recorded history behind the numbers and nothing on the phone knows which second of a
playback it is showing. Written on the screen and flagged here rather than half-built.

**The ghost surfaces are explicitly untouched**, and it is two independent denials: the allowlist
refuses the kind to both out classes, and `StaticRenderTest` hands `Ghost3` a jam it has no lawful way
to receive and asserts the couch's map stays clean anyway. The failure that catches is somebody laying
the veil over `LivePlan` itself, where all three maps would inherit it in one line.

### 5 · Page 2, and the one copy line that had to change

The tile fires in place under the same two-second hold (D-141) and **opens nothing**, which is the two
Tier-1 tiles' behaviour rather than the two pickers'. It does not navigate: a phone that walked
anywhere on a Static would be reporting the fire to whoever was looking over a shoulder.

⚠️ **`tier2Sub` said `HOLD TO SELECT A TARGET` and that line is now false.** Two of the three select
and one does not, and a line still promising a picker would send an Insider looking for a screen that
no longer exists. It reads **`HOLD TO USE THESE THREE`** — 23 characters, exactly the length of the
line it replaces, so the row's shape against the Resident's `WITHIN NOMINAL PARAMETERS` has not moved
(D-142). **The Resident's cover words are untouched**: `BUS`, `THERMAL`, `CACHE` describe a phone's
subsystems rather than the abilities behind them, and the one that changed would be the one a
returning player noticed.

### 6 · The lint learned both words

`spoof` is a rejected stem now, with `deactivat`'s reasoning: the old word reached code, tests and one
screen, and **a rename that is only a sweep is a rename somebody undoes by writing the familiar
word.** It caught 42 in `core`, 17 in `ui` and 5 in `model` on the first run, which is what the stem
is for. `Static` is deliberately **not** a stem — that list holds rejected forms, and the accepted
vocabulary is in the task's advice text, which gained `Static` beside `Override`.

### 7 · Recording format 13 → 14, and it is mostly a subtraction

The first bump in this format that removes more than it adds. `SpoofFired` out and `StaticFired` in
(an actor and a tick); two effects a version 13 build could name gone, one it cannot name new; **the
arming row lost the home's plan**, which is the largest thing this format has ever stopped carrying;
and the state row swapped a list of phantoms for one nullable deadline. That deadline is load-bearing
for the isolations' reason **inverted** — the jam does reach every living phone, but it reaches them
as a window that has already started, so a replay that did not hold it would come out of the jam at
the wrong moment.

### The injections — seventeen run, every one reverted by targeted reverse-replace

Never `git checkout`. The unit checkpointed to a commit first (R1's lost file), and the tree was
grepped for every marker afterwards and is clean.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `staticFired` spends the pool and opens no jam | 3 × `StaticTest` |
| 2 | the actor folded into `staticSeed` | `StaticTest.a jam is identical whichever insider fired it` |
| 3 | the jam paused at a meeting call instead of cleared | `StaticTest.a meeting call ends a running jam…`, `MeetingRulesTest.an isolation and a static jam fail at the meeting call` |
| 4 | `withStatic` takes the newer deadline unconditionally | `StaticTest.a second insiders static extends the window and never shortens it` |
| 5 | `STATIC_OPENED` widened to the out classes | `EmitSchemaTest.static reaches both living classes and neither out class` |
| 6 | the same row narrowed to Residents alone | the same test — **both directions, and neither is the other's** |
| 7 | the jam addressed to one seat | the same test's audience half |
| 8 | `ISOLATE_TARGETS_OFFERED` widened to both living roles | `EffectPushTest.noFrameCarriesTheHomesGeography` — **house geography on a Resident's socket, off real bytes** |
| 9 | the veil drawn over the couch's true-occupancy map | `StaticRenderTest.aGhostsMapStaysCleanEvenWhenHandedAJam` |
| 10 | `Static.field` ignores the seed | `StaticRenderTest.theNoiseFieldIsDeterministicFromTheSeed` |
| 11 | the veil pinned to frame 0 | `StaticRenderTest.theSameFrameRendersTheSamePixelsAndTwoFramesDoNot` |
| 12 | `fireStatic` navigates | `HoldInputTest.aStaticHoldFiresAndOpensNothingAndTellsTheActorNothing` |
| 13 | `staticFired` early-returns on a running pool (rule 1) | `AbilityRulesTest.a wasted fire…`, `StaticTest.a static into a running pool…` |
| 14 | `StaticFired` dropped off `isTierTwo` | `MeetingRulesTest.every Tier-2 fire is refused at a meeting…` |
| 15 | a fifth run added to the arming burst | `StaticTest.the arming burst is four runs and none of them is a plan` |
| 16 | a plan-shaped kind back in the emit table | `EmitSchemaTest.no kind in the table carries the home's plan` |
| 17 | the client keeps its jam across a meeting call | `RoutingTest.a static jam is raised on any living phone and dropped at a meeting call` |

**#8 is the one the unit was written around.** The home's plan crossing to nobody cannot be asserted
by deleting a type — a type can be deleted and the disclosure can come back on another one — so the
claim is made about **frames**: `EffectPushTest`'s probe reads every body that really reached seat 0
over a real socket and sweeps it for a room seat 0's own work order never names. It is
**self-verifying**, `noFrameCarriesALineUntilTheRoundEnds`' discipline: the same probe has to *find*
the room this seat's order does send it to, in the same run, or a sweep that read nothing would pass
for the same reason a sweep that read everything does.

### ⚠️ ESCALATIONS — unruled, skipped, and owed a decision

1. **`Balance.STATIC_COOLDOWN` is 120s and the number is this build's.** D-186 ruled the *direction*
   — *heavier than old Spoof's; constant raised; playtest tunes it* — and named no figure. What is
   built is an **ordering** rather than a value: Surge 150 > Static 120 > Isolate 90, argued on the
   constant. Not escalated as a blocker because the ruling authorises the raise; flagged because the
   figure is not the owner's.
2. **The Timelapse's window arithmetic is unbuildable today.** *Seconds crossing a jam render as
   static* needs a playback that knows which second it is showing, and E8 is build-pending. What
   ships is a jam over a fixture playback. See §4.
3. **`Transcript.staticFires` counts a metric whose question has changed.** `gdd.md:1347` asked
   whether the third ability was ever used; D-186 **took that risk** and replaced the mechanic.
   Nobody expects Static to go unused, so what is worth counting now is the other direction — how
   often the house goes dark — and no threshold is encoded, which is deliberate. Whether the row is
   still worth watching at all is the owner's.
4. **A jam has no expiry event, and the client runs the window.** `FlowHost` drops the panel field
   after the seconds the house sent, which is the ring's presentation timer one message over. The
   alternative is a second kind for *the monitoring is back*, and a message whose arrival said so
   would be a second thing to time and a second thing to miss. Decided, argued on
   `FlowModel.staticLapsed`, flagged.
5. **`GameState.accessPausedAt` is still unused machinery** — E-R1-3, inherited unchanged. Static
   spends the same pool and touches nothing about it.
6. **A phone that reconnects mid-round is re-sent no Isolate list** — T2·6 / D-190's other half is
   not this unit's, and the plan half of that escalation **dissolves** with the plan.

### Flagged design choices

- **`Placement.Inferred` has no caller and is kept anyway** (D-186 says so). See §1.
- **The veil is a layer over the surface, never a replacement for it.** A blanked view is a broken
  view, and D-186 forbids Static resembling one.
- **No haptic and no sound**, both rulings rather than omissions: D-176 gives the game one sound and
  it is the Surge; D-135 closes the long buzz to five events and this is not one. A buzz in every
  pocket at the moment an Insider chose would be a timer telling a dark house that *now* was when
  somebody moved.
- **No house notice** — D-187 narrows notices to detectable deliberate evasion, and Static is an
  ability.
- **`STATIC_DURATION` is the only owner-set number in this unit.** Ten seconds, off the ruling's own
  table, and the one constant here that is not a placeholder.

## Client — Unit C3

*Three things at once, and only one of them lifted the freeze: **the briefings inversion** (D-188),
**the caller's own message** (E-R1-1, ruled by the owner 2026-08-23 — OPTION 1), and **the five
kinds R1 left unrouted** (E-R1-4). Everything except the caller is client work; the caller is one
effect, one wire member, one schema row and one recording-format bump.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1711, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0**
(ui 674, model 490, core 224, platform 133, harness 115, app 58, cards 17), read out of the test XML
and never off an exit code. The Static unit left **1689**, so this is **22 net**. `vocabularyLint`
clean (ui 69, model 53, core 30, app 30, cards 7), `redactionLint clean — 53 file(s)`,
`boundary: core clean`, `boundary: ui clean`, `DeviceLayoutTest`, `ScreenGraphTest`,
`MeterDisclosureTest` and `ScreenshotTest` green. **No phone was required.**

### ⚠️ THE NINE BRIEFING LINES, FOR THE OWNER'S EYE

D-188 orders nine lines drafted in Sniff's register and nine in-screen instructions stripped. Both
are done. **Sniff's stands unchanged.** The nine below are the builder's words inside a ruled shape
— second person, imperative, **what the phone will do and then what you do about it**, two lines
each, no nouns from the fiction — and they are drafts, not decisions. They live on
`Subroutine.briefing` (`ui/Subroutines.kt`) and nowhere else.

| # | Subroutine | The line, as drafted |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | REPLAY | `DOTS WILL FLASH IN ORDER.` / `TAP THEM BACK IN THAT ORDER.` |
| 2 | INTERRUPT | `A SWEEP WILL RUN BACK AND FORTH.` / `TAP WHILE IT IS INSIDE THE BAND.` |
| 3 | PARITY CHECK | `A GRID WILL FILL IN A PATTERN.` / `TAP THE ONE BLOCK THAT BREAKS IT.` |
| 4 | SNIFF | *(the owner's existing line, unchanged)* |
| 5 | DEALLOCATE | `THE COLUMNS WILL STAND UNEVEN.` / `TAP THEM DOWN TO THE SHORTEST.` |
| 6 | DRIFT | `A DOT WILL MOVE, THEN HIDE.` / `WHEN THE PHONE BUZZES, TAP WHERE IT IS.` |
| 7 | SHORT | `THE PHONE WILL ASK FOR FINGERS.` / `HOLD THAT MANY UNTIL IT RETURNS.` |
| 8 | SIGNAL TRACE | `TWO ENDS WILL BE MARKED.` / `TAP NODE TO NODE FROM ONE TO THE OTHER.` |
| 9 | JAM | `TWO RINGS WILL SIT APART.` / `TAP UNTIL THEY SIT ON ONE ANOTHER.` |
| 10 | HANDSHAKE | `THE PHONE WILL BUZZ A RHYTHM.` / `TAP IT BACK BY FEEL.` |

**The nine deletions are the other half and they are not cosmetic.** *TAP TO CATCH THE SWEEP INSIDE
THE BAND*, *RETURN THE RHYTHM BY FEEL*, *FIND THE BLOCK THAT BREAKS THE PATTERN* and six more are
gone from the Subroutine screens. That is D-188's argument as pixels: a rating of bright, medium or
dark is a promise about how much light you stand in while you work, and an in-screen line is lit
pixels for the **whole duration** of the task — it makes a dark Subroutine less dark and a bright one
longer. Reading happens at the card, where the scan has already paid for the light.

### 1 · The field is non-null now, and that is the structural half

`Subroutine.briefing` was `String?` with nine nulls; it is `String` with ten values. **Total over the
roster** means a Subroutine cannot be added without somebody writing the sentence a player reads
before a screen they may not be able to read — which is exactly how nine of them came to say nothing
at the card in the first place. The compiler now holds what a test used to.

`SniffBriefingTest` **is renamed `BriefingTest`**, because its subject changed: it asserted *Sniff is
the only one briefed and the nine say it on their own screens*, and it now asserts *all ten are
briefed at their own card, only at their own card, and no Subroutine screen draws its own line*. A
file whose name says the opposite of its assertions is a file the next reader trusts wrongly.

### 2 · E-R1-1, OPTION 1 — `Effect.MeetingCalling`, and the seat comes off at the wire

The escalation was that *the calling seat cannot be routed, because a phone does not know its own
seat*: `MeetingOpened.caller` is a `Seat` and the comparison that would pick the caller out has one
operand. The owner chose the per-seat message over teaching a phone its seat, and **the built shape
is `RestrainedTakeover`'s exactly**: addressed to one seat, and **the wire member has no field at
all**. Two meetings called by two different people put byte-identical bytes on two different sockets.

- **`MEETING_CALLING to LIVING`**, and the class is not what narrows it — `deliveries` does, to the
  calling seat. The row would grant nothing wider even if somebody widened it, because a caller is
  living by construction; it is written narrow anyway so the row can be pointed at a rule.
- **No haptic, and that is a ruling rather than an omission.** The ring is `MeetingOpened`'s and the
  caller receives it like everybody else. One phone in a silent house buzzing differently from the
  other five is the caller named by hardware, at the one moment the design already made public by
  making them walk to the card. D-135's five stay five.
- **Emitted after the ring**, deliberately: the ring is what carries the caller's name onto the panel
  and clears that phone's jam, so a caller pushed to `Calling` first would arrive with a header the
  next message was still on its way to fill. Injection 5 is that ordering.
- **Recording format 13 → 14 → 15.** One effect a version 14 build cannot name; the smallest bump
  this format has had. The transcript row carries the seat and the wire member does not, which is the
  one place the two deliberately differ: a client may not be told which seat it is, and a recording
  that could not say which seat called the meeting could not replay one.

### 3 · The routing tail — five kinds, and two of them needed a surface built

**(a) The Surge is five seconds of the phone malfunctioning** (D-182: 1.5s full-bright, 1.0s dark,
2.5s of the cold-start Boot render, no input, the open Subroutine instance lost).

- **`ui/Lamp.kt` grew `LampState`**, two `MutableIntState`s and a `lampArgb(0..100)`. Respecting R1's
  note: **the lamp does not pass through a state field.** `FlowModel.surged` writes the slam in the
  call the message arrives in, not a frame later — rule 7's *same frame*, on the one effect whose
  whole point is that it is instantaneous. `Lamp` is drawn over every screen in the game,
  unconditionally, and at `UNSET` it is one `drawRect` of nothing: a lamp that appeared and
  disappeared from the tree would be a recomposition at the moment there must not be one.
- **The three durations are the platform's**, as the ruling says, and live beside `HOUSE_STAND_IN_MS`
  rather than in `Balance` — `core` has no clock to say a duration with.
- **The sequence suppresses the screen's own timer**, which is the one place in the app that happens.
  `Boot` falls through to `Perms` after 2.4s and the Surge holds it for 2.5, so without it the player
  gets a hundred milliseconds of the cold-start permissions walk mid-round. Injection 8, and the test
  that first "caught" it only read the end of the sequence — it now collects every screen the phone
  stands on.
- **The instance is lost at the reboot**, not at the slam: the fiction is the phone coming back with
  nothing in its hands, and it is after any stray tap the two dark seconds could take.
- ⚠️ **Where the phone lands afterwards is the build's, not the ruling's.** D-182 sizes the downtime
  and says what it costs and does not say which screen comes back. It lands on the springboard, which
  is where a rescan starts. Flagged.
- ⚠️ **The sound still has no route and nothing to route to.** `Speaker` is a stub and every actual of
  it is silent. Carried forward unchanged.

**(b) `LampSet` → the lock screen's ground**, through the same `Lamp.kt` path. *Locked IS the lamp,
and the house sets the level, not you* — so the amber field is read inside a `drawBehind` off
`LampState.authorised` rather than through `background()`, which would have made every luminance
change a recomposition of the one screen whose whole job is to emit. `UNSET` is `0` and a real
blackout is `0xFF000000`: a lamp nobody has set and a lamp the house set to nothing are opposite
claims, and collapsing them would open a bench's lock screen black.

**(c) `SubroutineProgressed` → the couch's meter as a percentage.** The count itself reaches no
screen (D-103: `seats × METER_PER_SEAT` is public arithmetic). It goes through
`Balance.integrityPercent` — the only place that arithmetic exists — into the same
`PanelState.integrityPercent` field `SystemIntegrityLive` writes, so bar and figure stay one value.

- **The denominator is the house's or there is none.** `PanelState.seats` is written from
  `CheckInProgressed.expected`, the only message in the game that carries a seat total, and it is
  **nullable where `MeetingCounts.seats` has a fixture default of six** — a phone that has not been
  told leaves the bar where it is rather than dividing by a bench's number. Both are written from one
  branch, so they cannot drift.
- ⚠️ **The seat total only arrives at the first meeting.** A phone put outside the system before one
  has a bar that does not move until then. It is a **carrier gap**, not a routing one — no message
  before `CheckInProgressed` carries a seat count. Flagged rather than closed by inventing one.
- **A real defect fell out of this, found by the new test and fixed.** `PanelVals.outsidePercent`
  computed the couch's figure from `outsideLit` **against 32 segments** — a round trip — so a house
  saying 70% printed 68%, and every value landing on a segment boundary hid it. That is C2's
  `66%`-over-21-cells fault in a quieter form: the number the house sent is the fact and the bar is a
  rendering of it, and a rendering may not be the source. Injection 14.

**(d) `ScanAnswered` → the caught scan.** One flow: scan → caught, with the briefing on it → BEGIN →
the work. It deliberately does **not** open the Subroutine — that is `beginSubroutine`'s answer off
the roster, driven by the player, and a push that opened the work here as well would be two routes to
one Subroutine with one untested, and would take away the last screen NOT THIS ONE is still a choice
on. **A null answer opens `ScanBad`** (D-124), which is the other half of the single-kind shape.

- **The row is a join of two pushed facts, not a derivation.** The house names the entry, the kind and
  the parameters; the work order it already sent names where that entry is and whether it is done, and
  the index they are joined on is the house's. An answer naming an entry the order cannot place moves
  nothing — fail-closed — and a `Blocked` line is unplaceable by construction (D-114).
- ⚠️ **`CurrentSubroutine.instruction` is now nullable and is always null in play.** *RESYNC THE
  KEEPALIVE* is flavour copy and **nothing on the wire carries it**: neither `ScanAnswered` nor
  `OrderLine.Known` holds a sentence. A real caught scan is one line shorter, which is the fail-closed
  direction — the fixture's sentence printed over somebody else's Subroutine is a lie a player cannot
  check, and what to do is on the briefing and is never absent. Whether it wants a carrier or belongs
  on the client's roster beside the label is **a copy call nobody has made. Escalated, not guessed.**
  `room` is nullable for the same shape of reason (an empty `at` is lawful).

**(e) `EgressHeld` / `SyncPulseAnswered` → the containment screen.** `running` becomes
`PanelState.egressHeld` and the widget's clock reads **HELD** in place of the number, dimmed with the
bar — a paused countdown drawn as a still number is indistinguishable from a phone that stopped
receiving, and that difference is the whole of what a player is reading there. `held` becomes
`PanelState.beatHeld` and draws `ON THE BEAT` / `OFF THE BEAT`, **copied and never interpreted**: false
is the answer for a missed beat, a lockout, a seat not at a node and a seat outside the system alike,
and a screen that split it finer would have rebuilt the presence detector the single shape prevents.

- ⚠️ **`EgressHeld.remaining` is still not routed, and it is R1's line rather than a new one.** It is
  in ticks and `PanelState.secondsLeft` is in seconds; `Balance` says outright that a screen dividing
  by `TICKS_PER_SECOND` is a screen holding an opinion about the rate. E7's, with the tick rate — the
  same gap `egressPercent` has one field over. Only the two **states** the unit was asked for landed.

### The injections — fourteen run, every one reverted by targeted reverse-replace

Never `git checkout`. The unit checkpointed to a commit first (R1's lost file), files were copied
before each injection and restored from the copy afterwards, and the tree was diffed against the
checkpoint at the end — one file changed, and it is the two tests strengthened below.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | a stripped Subroutine screen draws its instruction again | `BriefingTest.noSubroutineScreenDrawsItsOwnBriefing` |
| 2 | the caught scan draws all ten briefings, not this card's | `BriefingTest.everyCaughtScanDrawsItsOwnBriefingBeforeBegin`, `LampRoutingTest.theScansAnswer…` |
| 3 | `MEETING_CALLING` addressed to `state.seats` | `EmitSchemaTest.the caller's own message reaches exactly the calling seat` |
| 4 | the seat rides **inside** the wire member | `RoundWireTest.aSeatAddressedMessageCannotTellWhichSeatItIsFor` |
| 5 | the caller's message emitted **before** the ring | `MeetingTest.the calling seat is told it is calling - once and after the ring` |
| 6 | the Surge's bright and dark phases swap length | `LampRoutingTest.theSurgeRunsBrightThenDarkThenBootAndComesBack` |
| 7 | the reboot leaves the open Subroutine standing | `LampRoutingTest.aSurgeEatsTheSubroutineInYourHands` |
| 8 | the cold-start timer not suppressed during a Surge | `LampRoutingTest.aSurgedPhoneDoesNotFallThroughTheColdStart` + the sequence test |
| 9 | `LampSet` writes a `PanelState` field | `LampRoutingTest.theHouseSetsTheLampAndNothingOnThePanelMoves`, `RoutingTest.the routeless kinds…` |
| 10 | the meter divides by a guessed denominator (`?: 6`) | `RoutingTest.the ledger's count reaches the meter as a percentage…` |
| 11 | `SurgeSlam` loses `nth` — two Surges become one | `LampRoutingTest.aSecondSurgeMidWindowRestartsTheSequence…` |
| 12 | a null scan answer opens the card anyway | `RoutingTest.a caught scan opens the card and a null answer opens the refusal` |
| 13 | the containment widget reads `running` backwards | `RoutingTest.the containment screen learns that the clock stopped…` |
| 14 | the couch's figure round-trips through the bar again | `MeterDisclosureTest.theCouchsMeterMovesOnTheLedgersCountAndStillPrintsNoTotal` |

**Two of them were not caught on the first run, and both tests were wrong rather than the code.**

- **#6 passed** because the sequence test walked the clock in terms of `SURGE_PEAK_MS` and its
  siblings — *a list derived from the thing it is checking agrees with itself*, L2's injection-6
  finding arriving on a duration instead of a list. D-182's three figures are now typed out beside
  the walk, so moving one is a diff that says what it is moving.
- **#8 was caught by a neighbour rather than by the test named for it.**
  `aSurgedPhoneDoesNotFallThroughTheColdStart` only read the end of the sequence, and the fall-through
  is **transient**: the phone lands on `Perms` for a hundred milliseconds and then comes back to
  `Home` anyway. It now collects every screen the phone stands on across the whole five seconds.

### ⚠️ ESCALATIONS — unruled, skipped, and owed a decision

1. **The nine briefing lines are copy and want the owner's eye.** Listed in full at the top of this
   section. D-188 ordered them drafted in Sniff's register; the register is a ruling and the words
   are not.
2. **`CurrentSubroutine.instruction` has no carrier and is now always null in play.** *RESYNC THE
   KEEPALIVE* is a sentence per work-order line and nothing on the wire holds one. Either the effect
   is a field short or the flavour line belongs on the client's roster beside the label — a copy call,
   not a wiring one. See §3(d).
3. **Where a surged phone lands is the build's.** D-182 sizes the downtime and does not name the
   screen it comes back on. `Home`, because that is where a rescan starts. See §3(a).
4. **The seat total only reaches a phone at the first meeting**, so a phone outside the system before
   one has a meter that does not move. Carrier gap, not routing. See §3(c).
5. **`EgressHeld.remaining` stays unrouted** — ticks versus seconds, E7's with the tick rate.
   Inherited from R1 unchanged. See §3(e).
6. **The Surge's sound has no route and nothing to route to** — `Speaker` is a stub. Inherited
   unchanged.
7. **`GameState.accessPausedAt` is still unused machinery** (E-R1-3), and **`egressFired` still lies on
   `cooldownStarted`** was fixed by R1 but its Egress-clock half is D-184's. Neither is this unit's.

### Flagged design choices

- **The lamp is drawn over every screen and is transparent for all but five seconds of a round.**
  Unconditional on purpose: a conditional lamp is a recomposition at the one moment rule 7 forbids
  one. It publishes no pointer input, so a tap during the two dark seconds still reaches the screen
  underneath — harmless, because the sequence ends by pushing to a known screen either way, and
  recorded here rather than solved with an input blocker nobody asked for.
- **`PanelState.seats` exists to be a denominator and is drawn by nothing.** It is fed to
  `Balance.integrityPercent` and to nothing else; `MeterDisclosureTest` reads the panels.
- **The caught scan's location line drops the room rather than blanking it.** A leading separator over
  an absent room reads as a rendering fault rather than as a room nobody sent.
- **`Placement`, the Static unit's flags and everything on revision 38's carried-forward list are
  untouched.** This unit's scope was the three named items and nothing else.

## Rehearsal 2

*P3 played the first complete round in this game's existence and its central finding was that
**nobody would have seen any of it**: five phones, a hundred pushes, and every client standing on
`ScreenId.Lobby` from the first frame to the last. R1 routed the kinds that move a screen and C3
routed the tail of them, and both units end with a claim about **five phones at once** — the one
shape no single-process test can check. This is that claim checked: P3's script with the parts R1
and C3 made real taken out of it, and with the instrument P3 did not have.*

**No phone was used and none could have been.** Five iOS Simulator instances on one Mac, one of them
hosting, all five finding each other by mDNS over the Mac's own network and playing the round over
the real websocket transport. What a simulator carries honestly is the two things this unit is about
— the **wire**, which is ordinary TCP and identical to the wire between two phones, and the
**screen**, which is the real composition at real device aspect. What it cannot carry is BLE, torch,
camera and haptics; every seat's log opens with `this target has no haptic hardware — every buzz will
be a silent no-op`, and nothing here signs off any of those.

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1718, failures=0, errors=0,
skipped=0** (ui 674, model 490, core 224, platform 133, harness 115, app 65, cards 17), read out of
the test XML and never off an exit code. C3 left **1711**, so this is **7 new**, all of them in the
cheats' own tests. `vocabularyLint` clean (app 32, cards 7, and the rest), `redactionLint clean`,
`boundary: core clean`, `boundary: ui clean`, `variantSweepRelease` and `variantSweepPlaytest` green,
`verify-guards.sh` **6 passed, 0 failed**.

**And the round is visible.** Two complete five-seat rounds on two seeds, fourteen beats each, all
five screens read at every one of them, thirty frames kept. **18 of 21 screen assertions pass and
the three that fail are three of the escalations below** — they are written as assertions on purpose,
so that the day somebody fixes them the instrument says so.

### 1 · The fake list shrank, and this is the whole of it

| # | P3 | Rehearsal 2 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | the Revoke firing — `Event.ContactMade` | **still a stand-in.** The BLE handshake is unbuilt hardware and `factOf` rules it *reported, never requested* |
| 2 | the Revoked report — `MeetingTrigger.RevokeReported` | **still a stand-in**, the same physical act by the same rule |
| 3 | the Egress firing — no wire member for the finger | **gone.** Every living seat presses the tile, the frame is `RoundBody.Fire`, the house answers at `Landing.Draw` |
| 4 | the Sync Pulse — no wire member at all | **the frame is real** and the grading is real. What is left is the **tick anchor** — §4 |
| 5 | the round's five timed transitions — no tick driver | **gone.** `tickHouse` mints all five off a monotonic clock |
| 6 | a correct Subroutine entry | **still a stand-in.** A phone is shown the question and never the answer (rule 3) |

**Three remain and one is half of what it was.** Two of the three are hardware that does not exist;
the third is rule 3 working. Two more became real that P3 did not have at all: **STOP NOW** goes up
as `IntentMessage.StopSubroutine` from a phone that really had work open, and every entry in the
round goes up as `IntentMessage.HandOverEntry`. **A Static jam is fired from a phone**, which P3's
script had no act for at all.

**And one hand on the clock is new, deliberately.** P3's script set `RoundAuthority.at` to jump each
cooldown. With a driver running that is no longer possible and no longer wanted — the authority's
clock *is* the driver's, and a script that set the counter would leave the driver's own timestep
behind it, so every deadline would be evaluated against a clock the driver could never reach. So the
bench pushes **the driver's view of real time** and then waits for the house to arrive. Every one of
the five transitions is still `HouseClock`'s, off elapsed nanoseconds, and a skipped backlog is
reported out loud as abandoned steps — which a jumped counter never was.

⚠️ **The clock is never pushed while the house is on fire**, and the first run is why. Both thumbs go
onto both tiles when the meeting ends, so an Egress had already been fired during the jam's act — and
the next push moved the house a hundred and seventy seconds forward, straight through a countdown two
seats were walking across the house to answer. A skew during a crisis is a crisis nobody can contain,
which is the one thing that act exists to watch happen.

### 2 · The instrument: what each seat's screen said, at every beat

`ScreenLedger` reads the same `FlowModel` the composition draws from — `LobbyCheat`'s discipline one
wire down — and prints one line per seat whenever the screen or any field on it moves, plus a line at
every **beat** the bench announces whether or not anything moved. That second half is the one that
matters: *the couch stayed on `Ghost3` while the room voted* is a claim with no evidence behind it
unless something wrote a line at that instant.

**Every line carries a monotonic stamp**, and that is what makes a table across five processes
possible at all. `mach_absolute_time` is the host kernel's and five simulator instances are five
processes on one kernel, so the stamps really are comparable. **On five phones in a house they would
not be** — `TransportFrame.TimeProbe`/`TimeMark` exist for that day and nothing reads them yet, which
is the same missing carrier E-R2-3 is about.

It prints no one line, no reveal text and no house body: lengths and counts stand in for all three,
for the reason `RoundClient` refuses them one wire down. Names are printed, because the house sends
the roster to every phone (D-115) and a name in this log is on five screens already.

### 3 · What a player would now see that P3's player could not

P3's sentence was *every distinct screen a client stood on, whole round: LOBBY*. Here is the same
sentence for the same script, off the same kind of log, one round later:

```
seat0  9 screens   ARMED · SCANBAD · SCANCAUGHT · GHOST2 · GHOSTMEETING · GHOST3 · WININSIDERS · JOIN · LOBBY
seat1 11 screens   NOTIFY · SCANBAD · SCANCAUGHT · CALLING · NOTICE · DISCUSSION · VOTE · RESTRAINED · WININSIDERS · JOIN · LOBBY
seat2 10 screens   NOTIFY · SCANBAD · SCANCAUGHT · FOUND · NOTICE · DISCUSSION · VOTE · WININSIDERS · JOIN · LOBBY
seat3 10 screens   (as seat 2)
seat4 10 screens   (as seat 2)
```

**Ten screens where there was one.** The evening a person on seat 2 actually had: the lobby, the
opening banner, a scan that opened nothing and a scan that opened work with its own briefing on it,
their phone ringing **FOUND** because somebody reported a Revoked player, the assembly, the talk with
its own clock, the ballot, the tally, the house going noisy for ten seconds, and an ending naming one
Insider.

**Seat 1 rang CALLING and nobody else did** — E-R1-1's ruling alive on a socket: `MEETING_CALLING` is
addressed to the calling seat by `deliveries`, the wire member has no field at all, and exactly one
phone of five drew the caller's own header. C3 built it and this is the first round in which anybody
saw it.

**And the seat P3 could not have shown at all: the couch.** Seat 0 was Revoked mid-shift, walked in
when the house rang (`Ghost2`), watched the meeting from `GhostMeeting`, and stood on `Ghost3`
through the crisis **with a clean map while the three living phones had noise over theirs** — the jam
reached `10S` on all three living seats and `-` on both out seats, which is D-186's row and its two
independent denials, on real sockets.

### 4 · The three instruments, and the fourth that is this unit's

**The one-line probe, per client.** `LineWatch` on every body that arrived and every body that went
up, on raw bytes, before anything decided what they were. Five verdicts, one per seat, across a round
whose last act publishes a line on purpose — **`0 CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE` on all five.**

**The role-swap differential, on delivered streams.** Two rounds, two seeds, the same five processes
in the same five simulators, so the draw moves and nothing else does. The two kinds only an Insider
may receive **moved with the role and not with the seat**:

```
                       run1 (seed 20260822)   run2 (seed 19770506)
ABILITY FIRED                 00010                  00001        <- MOVED
IsolateTargetsOffered         00010                  00001        <- MOVED
```

⚠️ Nine narrow kinds landed on the *same* seats in both rounds — `I AM THE ONE CALLING`,
`STAND AND WALK IN`, `THE ROOM HELD ME`, `THE BALLOT`, the five `SEAT n SELECTED` rows. **That is the
bench being deterministic and not a leak**: the script always reports from the first living seat that
is not the Insider and always votes for the caller, so which seat is Revoked and which is Restrained
is fixed by seat order rather than by the draw. Worth saying out loud, because a reader of that table
would otherwise have found a leak in it.

**A mid-round gap, on one seat.** One client stopped during the shift and relaunched **without
FORGET**: it presented its stored token, resumed *that* seat, and its log reads
`SEATED ON THIS CONNECTION — ASKING FOR WHAT WAS SAID WHILE IT WAS GONE` followed by the whole outbox
from sequence 1. It rode the rest of the round and finished on the ending screen with everybody else.

**And the fourth is the beat table** — §2, and the reason there is a unit here at all.

### 5 · What went up the wire, per seat

```
BeginSubroutine     11  11  11  15  11      CheckIn              1   1   1   1   1
HandOverEntry        8   8  16  11   7      DeclareReadyToVote   1   1   1   1   1
ReturnSyncPulse      1   1   1   1   1      SelectVote           1   1   1   1   1
StopSubroutine       0   0   0   0   1      LockVote             1   1   1   1   1
FireStatic          26  26   3   2   3      THE EGRESS TILE      7   7   8   9   8
```

Every one of those is a frame on a real socket with **no seat on it**, attributed by `IntentDesk` to
the connection it arrived on. The two rows worth reading twice are the last two, and both are E-R2-1:
`FireStatic` at 26 from the two seats that were **out of the round** — they kept pressing because the
jam is denied to out classes so they never saw one land — and the Egress tile pressed by all five,
**including both out seats**, with the house making a draw for every press.

### 6 · The injections — six on the guards, one on the build

Never `git checkout` — R1 lost a body of uncommitted work to exactly that. Each file was copied
first, the marker replaced, the tests run, the failures read **out of the test XML**, and the file
restored from the copy. Baseline green before, green after, and the tree grepped for every marker.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | the reveal's insiders printed instead of counted | `ScreenLedgerTest.theRevealIsCountedAndNeitherTheNameNorTheLineIsPrinted` |
| 2 | the house's body text printed instead of its length | `ScreenLedgerTest.theHousesLastWordIsALengthAndNeverItsWords` |
| 3 | the stamp dropped off every line | `ScreenLedgerTest.everyLineCarriesAStampAndTheStampsDoNotGoBackwards` |
| 4 | a beat prints only when the screen moved | `ScreenLedgerTest.aBeatPrintsEvenWhenTheScreenHasNotMoved` **and** the stamp test |
| 5 | an absent field omitted rather than drawn as absence | `ScreenLedgerTest.aFieldTheHouseHasNotFilledReadsAsAbsence` |
| 6 | the Egress tile goes up as an ordinary request | `RoundClientTest.theEgressTileGoesUpAsItsOwnEmptyFrame` |

**A seventh, on the build itself.** `ScreenLedger.kt` was given a deliberate type error to prove it
is in the compilation at all — `touch` proves nothing against Gradle's content hashing, and a new
source file that is silently not compiled reports green forever. One `e:` line, then restored.

**And an eighth that was not planned: the bench's own bug, caught by looking at the pixels.** The
first pass at the ending had five phones going from the reveal to a wiped lobby inside two seconds,
and the beat table said no seat ever stood on an ending screen. Written up, that would have been an
escalation about `Flow.endings`. **It was mine**: a replacement that was supposed to make the reveal
wait fourteen seconds before anybody presses NEW ROUND had not matched, so `onEnding()` still fired
on arrival. The rule that caught it is the project's own — *read the evidence, not the summary* — and
the evidence was a screenshot that disagreed with a claim I was about to make.

### 7 · Two notes on method, both of which cost a run

- **P3 wrote down that rewriting a running `bash` script corrupts the running instance, and it
  happened again here.** The driver was edited mid-run and the already-started copy resumed at a
  stale byte offset inside the word `TALLY`, reporting `line 205: ALLY: command not found` from a
  line that was correct on disk. It is now run from a copy in `/tmp` with `$REPO` pointing back at
  the working tree, which makes the mistake impossible rather than remembered.
- **A beat is a snapshot of five phones and has to be taken after the push has reached them.** The
  first complete run took every beat the instant the *rules* reached the moment, which is before any
  of the four sockets has carried anything — so that run's table has no ring in it at all:
  `MEETING-RINGING` photographed five phones still on the Subroutine screens they had been on a
  second earlier. The beat settles first now, by a named constant, and the delay is deliberately
  **not** a wait for the screens to agree with the script.

### 8 · Flagged design choices, and one honest limit

- ⚠️ **The pixels are illustrative and the ledger is the instrument.** Five simulators on one Mac do
  not all keep drawing: a window the other four are covering can stop compositing, and
  `simctl io screenshot` then returns the last frame that device drew — one shot here was ten
  minutes stale. So the thirty frames are *a frame that phone drew at or before this beat*, and
  `shots5/README.md` carries the ledger's table beside them and says which one to believe.
- **Every living seat presses every tile, and that is the only thing a phone in this build can do.**
  Nothing tells a phone its role (E-R2-2), so a bench that pressed only where it would work would be
  a bench that knew something no phone knows. The house admitting one of five is the design working —
  except that it admits all five for the Egress, which is E-R2-1.
- **The bench walks in, talks and votes on named delays** — four seconds to the meeting card, eight
  before a hand goes up, twelve before READY. Not decoration: a room that checked in inside one
  second left no ring for anything to observe, and four phones locking in the same millisecond closed
  a whole ballot inside half a second. The delays are a person's pace, and they are named constants
  rather than waits for anything to become true.
- **The node walkers alternate across the seat order.** A first pass paired seats 0,1 on one node and
  2,3 on the other, and a Revoke and a Restrain put both of one node's walkers on the couch — a
  crisis with nobody at one end of it. Which seats survive to reach a node is the draw's business.
- **`BenchHome` is still a stand-in** and no phone in this run has walked a real home (P3's flag,
  unchanged).

### ⚠️ ESCALATIONS

**⚠️ E-R2-1 · NOBODY CHECKS WHO IS FIRING. A Revoked Resident on the couch fired an Egress and the
whole house lit up.**

This is the finding, and it was not reasoned to — it happened, twice, and the five logs record every
step. Seat 0 was a plain Resident, had been Revoked mid-shift and was standing on `Ghost3`. Its bench
pressed the Egress tile the way every seat did. The house drew a Tether and pushed `EgressOpened` to
all five phones.

```
[round]  PRESSED — THE EGRESS TILE          (an out seat, a plain Resident)
[house]  EGRESS DRAWN — Tether AT BENCH-J, BENCH-B
[round]  PUSH 48 — EGRESS Tether · NODES BENCH-J, BENCH-B · 2400 TICK(S)    (on all five)
```

Across one round the five seats pressed the tile 39 times between them and **the house made 39
draws**. Every press from every seat, living or out, Resident or Insider, reached `egressFor`.

**Four things are each individually right and the gap is between them.**

- `RoundBody.Fire` carries nothing but its dedup sequence — R1's ruling, and correct: a frame
  carrying the type or the nodes would let a client choose its own Egress.
- `IntentDesk` stamps the seat off the connection, so a client cannot *lie* about who it is. It does
  not have to; it just asks.
- `egressFor` (`core/Egress.kt:64`) is a **pure draw**. It reads `actor` only to put it on the event
  it returns, and returns null only when the home has fewer than two candidate markers.
- `admit` (`core/Admission.kt:65`) has a branch for meetings, one for Tier 2 and one for
  `needsEgress`, and **no branch that asks who the actor is**. All three of `phaseOf`, `isTierTwo`
  and `needsEgress` name `Event.EgressFired` explicitly as *not theirs*.
- `egressFired` (`core/Rules.kt:1508`) reads the cooldown and whether the drawn pair names two nodes.
  It never reads `state.roleOf(actor)` and never reads `state.isOut(actor)`.

**And it is not only the Egress.** `roleOf` and `isOut` appear in `Rules.kt` exactly three times —
the scan (`:421`), the entry grading (`:933`, `:937`) and the Sync Pulse (`:1605`). **No ability
handler in the game reads either one.** `SurgeFired`, `IsolateFired`, `StaticFired` and `RevokeArmed`
all arrive from any socket by their own `IntentMessage` members and are admitted from a seat of any
role in any round state.

**What stands in for the check today is a client-side one.** D-142 draws no live tile on a Resident's
page 2 — *their tier-2 tiles take no pointer input at all* — so an honest client cannot produce the
request. That is exactly the guarantee `admit` refuses to rely on one branch earlier: the
Tier-2-at-a-meeting refusal says in as many words that it exists **against a forged client**, because
*no honest client can produce the intent* is a fact about a build rather than about a boundary.

**It is not a leak, and it is worse than most leaks.** Nothing tells the firing Resident anything —
`ABILITY_FIRED` is refused to them by the allowlist, so their phone learns nothing at all. What they
can do is **act**: spend the house's Egress cooldown, or, from outside the round, end it for
everybody by firing one nobody knows to contain. **A round can be decided by somebody who is not in
it.**

**Not fixed, and deliberately.** The freeze is on for rules and the fix is a rules decision: whether
the gate is `admit`'s (a recorded refusal, silent to the player, where every other cross-cutting gate
in that file lives) or the handler's (an effect of the same shape whatever happened, rule 1's
arrangement). The two produce different recordings, and D-066's *an unrecorded refusal is an
invisible drop* points at the first.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-2 · No push tells a phone it is an Insider, and the one message only Insiders receive does
not set the role.**

Read off five ledgers, every line, both rounds: `ROLE RESIDENT`. Including the seat holding the
round's only Insider role, which the kind table names without ambiguity:

```
IsolateTargetsOffered      0   0   0   5   0     <- 1 SEAT (run 1, seed 20260822)
IsolateTargetsOffered      0   0   0   0   4     <- 1 SEAT (run 2, seed 19770506)
```

`Effect.IsolateTargetsOffered` is emitted for every seat at arming and delivered by the allowlist to
`Insider/Live` alone, so **that phone was told something no Resident is ever told** — and
`Flow.houseSaid` writes it to `PanelState.isolateTargets` and stops. `PanelState.role` is set by the
cheat picker and by nothing else in the app; no `RoundMessage` carries a role and no branch of
`houseSaid` writes one.

**The loop closes on itself.** Page 2 draws the Insider's tiles on `role`, so an Insider's phone draws
the Resident's page; the Isolate picker is reachable only from that page, so the one field the house
*did* send is drawn by nothing. **In a real evening no Insider could fire any ability at all** — the
E-P3-1 of the Insider's half of the game: everything below it works and nobody would ever see it.

Whether the carrier is a role on an existing message, a message of its own, or the client inferring it
from the arrival of the Isolate list is a design decision with a redaction consequence. Escalated,
not guessed.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-3 · The house catches fire and no screen changes — and the nodes never reach a phone.**

`ScreenId.EgressWidget` and `ScreenId.Banner` were reached by **no seat in either round**. The branch
is `Flow.kt:1816`:

```kotlin
is RoundMessage.EgressOpened -> state = state.copy(
    egressType = message.type.name.uppercase(),
    egressRunning = true,
)
```

Two fields and no navigation. The only caller of `go(ScreenId.Banner)` is `FlowModel.armEgress`,
which is **this phone's own echo of pressing the tile** (`Flow.kt:1002`) — so the alert exists for the
Insider who fired it and for nobody else. A living player working a Subroutine when the house catches
fire keeps working it; the ledger has three living seats on `ScanCaught` and `ScanBad` through a whole
crisis with `RUNNING true` sitting on a panel nobody is looking at.

⚠️ **And `message.nodes` is dropped.** `PanelState.egressNodes` stays null for the entire round —
`NODES -` on all five seats at every beat — so **the two markers a Resident has to walk to are never
put on a phone.** The design's whole containment loop is two people who may not speak walking to two
ends of a house; the house drew them, published them to all five sockets, and the client threw them
away. Containment is unreachable for a player for this reason **before** it is unreachable for
E-R2-4's.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-4 · The Sync Pulse is on the wire and the taps have no anchor.**

R1 built `IntentMessage.ReturnSyncPulse` and E-P2-5 is closed as a wire question. What this rehearsal
found is that the member is **unusable from a client**: `onTheBeat` (`core/Egress.kt:217`) grades
`taps` against `egress.firedAt` in **absolute house ticks**, and nothing on the wire carries the
house's clock. `RoundMessage.EgressOpened` carries `remaining`, which is a duration.

Both halves are in the run on purpose. Two seats scanned a node card for real, composed the best four
taps a phone can compose, and sent them up their own sockets; the house graded them, they missed, and
each got its own `SyncPulseAnswered · HELD=false` — **which is the design working**, one shape for all
five ways to fail. Then the host minted the same two seats' beats off `firedAt` so the round could go
on, and that mint is the one stand-in of P3's four that **changed shape instead of disappearing**.

What is missing is a carrier, and it is the same one `EgressHeld.remaining` wants (E-R1-4's
ticks-versus-seconds, inherited unchanged). `TransportFrame.TimeProbe`/`TimeMark` exist for the wall
clock and nothing reads them.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-5 · A Revoked player's phone shows the Subroutine they were working on, and `outBy` is
never set on that path.**

Seat 0 was Revoked mid-shift. Its screens, in order, off the ledger:

```
… SCANCAUGHT · SCANCAUGHT · SCANCAUGHT · GHOST2 · GHOSTMEETING · GHOST3 …
```

It never stood on `ScreenId.Revoked`, and every line it printed for the rest of the round read
`OUT -`. It stayed on the caught-scan screen through the Revoke, through the report and through the
ring, and the first thing that moved it was the meeting's own `StandAndWalkIn`.

**The Restrain half works**: the seat the room held printed `ON RESTRAINED · OUT RESTRAINED` within
one sample of the tally, because `RestrainedTakeover` is routed and sets `outBy`. So the two ways out
of this game are **asymmetric on the screen** — one has a takeover and a cause, the other has neither.

The lamp is not the gap: D-077 makes the lamp going dark the whole of what a Revoke looks like *in the
room*, and rule 7 is about the frame it fails in. What is missing is what the phone does afterwards,
and `ScreenId.Revoked` exists and is reached by nothing.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-6 · The couch's live ballot is routed to nothing.** `Flow.kt:1762` reads
`is RoundMessage.VoteSelectionShown -> Unit`. The pushes arrive — the kind table has
`SEAT 0 SELECTED SKIP`, `SEAT 1 SELECTED SEAT 1` and three more landing on the out seat and on no
other — and `PanelState.ballots` is null on every seat at every beat of both rounds. So `GhostMeeting`
draws its fixture, and **the one thing D-117 gives the defunct — watching selections appear live — is the
one thing they do not get.** It is E-P1-4's routeless list with one entry left on it that neither R1
nor C3 picked up.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-7 · Nothing puts a countdown on a screen.** `SECS -` on all five seats at every beat of both
rounds, including a 45-second ballot and a 120-second Egress. `PanelState.secondsLeft` is written by
nothing. The bar the screens draw is `Countdown`'s own, which R1 pointed at `Balance` — so it counts
the right *length* from whenever the screen appeared, and not from when the house started counting.
Six phones each counting on their own is what `PanelState.secondsLeft`'s own KDoc says must not
happen.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-8 · The house's last word reached one phone.** `THE HOUSE SIGNED OFF` landed on exactly one
seat of five in both rounds — and **on a different seat each time**, so it is following something
about the round rather than the seat order. It is the round's closing text, drawn by both ending
screens; four phones ended the evening without it. `HOUSE_SIGNED_OFF` is one of the two rows
`EmitSchema` spells out as a one-class set on purpose, which is exactly where a wrong class would go
unnoticed.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-9 · The living's tally rows are empty and the couch's are not.** `TALLY 0` on every living
seat at the tally beat and `TALLY 1` on the out seat. E-R1-2 with a number on it: the drawn tally
screen shows per-candidate counts to everybody and `Effect.MeetingResult` carries only `restrained`,
so on a real round the living's tally is a header with nothing under it.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-10 · NEW ROUND still returns one phone** (E-P3-5, unchanged and re-measured). The bench
presses it on all five. `THE LINES AFTER THE WIPE — 4 OF 5` on the host: this phone's line is gone and
the other four are still standing, because `LobbyLink.roundEnded()` is a call into the desk beside the
host and on a client the desk is not there.

---

**⚠️ E-R2-11 · The Kotlin/Native link leaks 141 MB of scratch per run and never cleans it.** The
evening stopped three times — twice on `unable to attach DB … database is locked` and once on a
`linkReleaseFrameworkIosSimulatorArm64` that failed with 250 MB free on the volume. The cause was not
this project's build output: `/private/var/folders/…/T` held **582 directories named `included…`, 141
MB each, twelve gigabytes**, dating back to 21 August, each one a link extracting the Compose native
archives (`libskia.a`, `libharfbuzz.a`, `libicu.a`) into scratch that is never removed. They were
deleted — only those untouched for five minutes, with no linker running — and the volume went from
250 MB free to 12 GB. **Flagged rather than automated**: a build that needs a sweeper has a defect in
it, and where the sweeper belongs is not this unit's call.

---

## R2F

*Rehearsal 2's two holes, closed. The house now checks who is firing, and every phone is finally
told what it is. Both were found by playing rather than by reading, and both had been true since
the day the abilities were built.*

**Outcome: green.** `./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`: **tests=1726, failures=0, errors=0,
skipped=0** (ui 678, model 492, core 225, platform 133, harness 116, app 65, cards 17), read out of
the test XML and never off an exit code. Rehearsal 2 left **1718**, so this is **8 new**.
`vocabularyLint` clean (model 53, ui 69, core 30, app 32, cards 7), `redactionLint clean — 53
