file(s)`, `boundary: core` and `boundary: ui` clean, `variantSweepRelease` and `variantSweepPlaytest`
green, `verify-guards.sh` **6 passed, 0 failed**. **No phone was used and none could have been.**

### 1 · E-R2-1 — the house checks who is firing

**One branch, in `admit`, third in the chain.** `firedBy(event)` is exhaustive over `Event` and
returns the actor for the five requests that *are* the Insider's kit — `RevokeArmed`, `SurgeFired`,
`StaticFired`, `IsolateFired`, `EgressFired` — and null for everything else. A seat that is not an
Insider, or is not in the round, is refused with `RefusalReason.AbilityNotTheirs`.

**Above the rules rather than inside them, which is D-066's whole argument.** Written in the handler
it would be `if (!insider) return Reduction(state, emptyList())` — rule 1's forbidden shape, in the
file rule 1 is about. At the gate the refusal **costs nothing** (no cooldown, no pool, no draw) and
is **recorded**, which an early return never is.

**`ContactMade` is deliberately not gated**, and it did not need to be: `factOf` rules it reported
rather than requested, and a contact revokes only from a seat holding an armed Revoke — which now
nobody but a living Insider can be. Gating a *report* above the rules would put an invisible drop in
the recording, which is the thing D-066 records refusals to prevent.

⚠️ **A new `RefusalReason` member that is NOT publicly observable, and the enum says that is
forbidden.** It is argued at the member rather than waved past: the entry condition was D-068's, and
**D-097 deleted the channel it was about** — a refused event is answered by re-pushing that seat's
own authorized view, and no effect and no wire kind can carry a reason down. What is left is a
reason the authority and the recording read. The second half of the argument is the
Tier-2-at-a-meeting branch's, one line below: **no honest client can produce the intent** (D-142
gives a Resident's page-2 controls no pointer input at all, and an out seat has no springboard), so
this is not a client-visible path — it is the boundary doing what it is for, against a forged frame.
Refused, recorded, surfaced to nobody.

**The rehearsal's exact reproduction is the test.** `AbilityRulesTest` gains a fourth rule about the
whole kit: each of the five requests, from a living plain Resident, from a Revoked plain Resident on
the couch, and from a Restrained Insider — refused, with **the round byte-identical afterwards** —
plus the control that a living Insider's five still land, without which the test would be green on a
build that had deleted the Insider's kit.

**⚠️ It found the shared fixture doing exactly what the rehearsal did.** `Round.kt` fired every
Revoke, all three Tier-2 abilities and the whole Egress from `Seat(1)` — the Insider **the room
restrains at that fixture's first meeting**, before any of them. Nine refusals said so. The fixture
now fires from `state.livingInsiders.last()`, read off its own walked state like its cards and its
entries. *The last* rather than the first is the differential's requirement: `first()` moves to
another seat the moment the room holds seat 1, and an actor that moves mid-round puts an
`AbilityFired` on a different phone in each of two runs.

**And a second fixture bug behind it.** The Tier-2 pass pushed the Access pool ready with
`state.withCooldown(...)` — the fixture's own walked state, which is **not an event**, so a replay
of the list never applied it. The Surge and the Static were fired into a running pool every time and
came back `cooldownStarted=false`; nobody saw it because the actor was Restrained and the allowlist
declined the delivery. It pushes the **clock** to the pool's deadline now, which is the one currency
the event list carries.

### 2 · E-R2-2 — the role rides the arming push

`Effect.RoleRevealed(seat, role)`, emitted **once per seat, for every seat**, as the fifth run of the
arming burst, carrying that seat's own role and nothing else. `ROLE_REVEALED` is permitted to both
living classes and addressed to the seat it names. On the wire it is
`RoundMessage.RoleRevealed(insider: Boolean)`.

- **Both living classes, never one.** A row of `Insider/Live` would deliver the reveal to Insiders
  and silence to everybody else, and the silence would be the answer — rule 1's forbidden shape
  written into the allowlist, where it would look like a tightening.
- **The addressing is the other half and the allowlist cannot see it**: two living Insiders are one
  class, so what stops seat 5 learning seat 3's role is `audienceOf`.
- **A Boolean on the wire, because `Role` carries no `@ClientFacing` and no `@Serializable`** and
  must never acquire one — `redactionLint`'s own KDoc records the day that guard default-permitted
  the most alignment-revealing type in the game. Rule 3's *construct a narrower type*, applied at
  the transport.
- **Silent.** No haptic field, in either `when` of `HouseBuzzer`. That is F-003 (`gdd.md:930`): the
  reveal leaking through the *number* of buzzes, at the one moment nobody has dispersed.
- **Known mode is not widened.** `gdd.md:923` gives an Insider a message from each fellow Insider
  and it rides the opening text thread, diegetically. This carries self-knowledge only, and there is
  nowhere on the member to put anything else.
- **No screen moves.** The reveal is text on a thread the player opens; the opening banner is what
  says *now*. A navigation would be one phone doing something a Resident's phone does not, in the
  light, with the party still standing together.

**The client half closes the loop.** `Flow.houseSaid` writes `PanelState.role`, which `PanelVals`
turns into `insider` — the `armable` flag on every page-2 tile (D-142) and the REVOKE/POWER,
SURGE/BUS, STATIC/THERMAL, ISOLATE/CACHE label pairs. Before this, `role` was written by the cheat
picker and by nothing else in the app, so **in a real evening no Insider could have fired anything at
all.** `RoutingTest` asserts the field, the capability and the labels, and — separately — that the
Isolate list arriving does **not** make a phone an Insider, which is the inference with the shortest
fuse in the app.

**⚠️ The differential harness needed real work, and it is sharper than before, not softer.**

- Its own KDoc said *role is inert in the rules, so this harness currently has nothing to find; its
  value starts the day a rule branches on role.* **That day is today**, twice over.
- `withRolesExchanged` now moves a fire's **actor** with the role it exchanged — the five gated
  requests **and `ContactMade`**, which is not gated anywhere and had to move anyway: the arm and
  the touch are one act by one person, and a variant that moved one and not the other is a round in
  which **nobody is ever revoked**. It read as three hundred unexplained lines before that was
  written. Targets are never rewritten: who you reached in the dark is not a property of your role.
- Nothing is rewritten unless a role actually moved, which keeps *exchanging two seats of the same
  role is a no-op* true.
- `THE_ROLE_REVEAL` is a **second named, ruled asymmetry**, subtracted in exactly one place — the
  two tests that compare the *authority's own effect stream*. The per-client diff needs nothing: the
  reveal is addressed to the seat it is about, so it lands on a swapped seat and is already
  explained. Paired with `the role reveal moves with the role and reaches one seat`, so the
  subtraction can never quietly become a filter over a leak.
- `exchanging two passive seats` is now `exchanging the acting seat` (seats 5 and 6), and the 1↔3
  exchange became the passive pair — the two intents survive with the labels swapped.

### 3 · The injections — eight, each restored from a copy

Never `git checkout`. Each file copied first, the marker applied, the tests run, the failures read
**out of the test XML**, the file restored from the copy, and the tree grepped for every marker.

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | the gate's out-seat half dropped | `AbilityRulesTest` — *arming a Revoke from a Restrained Insider reached the rules* |
| 2 | the gate's role half dropped | `AbilityRulesTest` — *arming a Revoke from a plain Resident reached the rules* |
| 3 | `firedBy` returns null for `EgressFired` | `AbilityRulesTest` — *an Egress from a plain Resident reached the rules* |
| 4 | the reveal addressed to every seat | **six instruments**: two `EmitSchemaTest`, four `DifferentialTest`, **and `FuzzPropertyTest`** |
| 5 | `Flow`'s role branch made a no-op | `RoutingTest.the house tells this phone what it is…` |
| 6 | the contact left behind on an exchange | two `DifferentialTest` — the variant's Revokes stop landing |
| 7 | every seat sent seat 0's role | `DifferentialTest.the role reveal moves with the role…` **and** `FuzzPropertyTest` |
| 8 | `ROLE_REVEALED` narrowed to `Insider/Live` | two `EmitSchemaTest`, including the alignment invariant |

Injection 4 is the one worth reading twice: **the fuzzer caught it on a seeded round nobody wrote**,
which is the leak instrument doing the job the differential's KDoc says it was built early for.

### 4 · E-R2-3 and the rest — looked at, and all still escalated

Item 3 of the unit was *implement only if the worklog's own text shows it is already ruled*. **None
of them does**, and two of them look ruled until you open the file.

- **E-R2-3 · the Egress reaches no screen and the nodes are dropped.** Two halves and neither is
  ruled. *Where a living player is pushed when the house catches fire* is a routing decision the
  worklog cites nothing for — D-118 and D-076 rule the **dim** and the **buzz**, not a navigation.
  The nodes half looks like pure wiring (`PanelState.egressNodes` exists and its KDoc says it holds
  what the house said) and is **still not taken**: the two halves are one escalation, and a phone
  holding two markers on a panel nobody is looking at closes nothing on its own.
- **E-R2-6 · the couch's live ballot.** The one that cites a ruling — D-117's *watching selections
  appear live* — and it is still blocked, one layer down. `PanelState.ballots` is
  `List<Ballot(by: String, forWhom: String?)>` and its KDoc says the seats are *resolved to names by
  the transport*, because `ui` mapping seats to names would be `ui` keeping a second copy of who is
  in the house. The wire member carries **seats**. So the open question is not the routing, it is
  **where the resolution happens**, and that is a protocol decision. Escalated.
- **E-R2-4 · the Sync Pulse anchor** and **E-R2-7 · nothing puts a countdown on a screen** are one
  missing carrier between them, and it is E-R1-4's ticks-versus-seconds, inherited unchanged.
- **E-R2-5 · `ScreenId.Revoked` is reached by nothing.** D-077 rules what a Revoke looks like *in
  the room*; what the phone does afterwards is unruled, and the asymmetry with `RestrainedTakeover`
  is the shape of the question rather than the answer.
- **E-R2-9 · the living's tally rows** is a disclosure decision (per-candidate counts to the
  living), which is E-R1-2 and belongs to whoever rules it.
- **E-R2-10 · NEW ROUND** needs `LobbyLink.roundEnded()` to travel, and **E-R2-11** is a build
  defect the rehearsal was explicit is not a unit's call.
- ⚠️ **E-R2-8 · the house's last word reaching one phone may not be a bug at all**, and the
  rehearsal's own evidence points that way. `HOUSE_SIGNED_OFF` is permitted to `Insider/Ended` and
  addressed per seat, so in a round with one Insider it reaches **exactly one phone, and a different
  one whenever the draw moves** — which is precisely what both runs recorded, and the same shape
  `ABILITY FIRED` and `IsolateTargetsOffered` showed in the role-swap table one section above. The
  check that would settle it is one line of the kept logs: whether that seat is the seat holding
  `IsolateTargetsOffered` in the same run. **Not closed here** — the logs are the rehearsal's, and a
  closure read off a summary rather than off the evidence is the mistake this project keeps a rule
  about.

## Summary — the routing day

*Written by the closing sweep, which built nothing. Everything below was re-measured rather than
copied: the counts come out of `*/build/test-results/*/*.xml` on a full `--rerun-tasks` run made
after the last commit, the guards were actually run, and the screenshots were counted on disk.
Where a unit's own claim and the re-measurement disagree, the re-measurement is what is written.
**They did not disagree** — every count, every lint file total and every guard figure the five units
reported is confirmed to the number.*

*The day has one subject and it is worth naming before the tables. **This is the day the game became
visible.** It opened on a house that could play a complete, correct round no player would see any of
— five phones standing on `ScreenId.Lobby` from the first frame to the last — and it closes with
five phones that stand on ten screens each, one of which knows it is the one who called the meeting,
all of which now know what they are.*

### The day, re-measured

**`PATH="$HOME/.local/share/mise/shims:$PATH" ./gradlew check --rerun-tasks` — BUILD SUCCESSFUL in
7m 8s, 125 actionable tasks, 125 executed.** Counted out of the test XML and never off the exit code:

```
tests=1726  failures=0  errors=0  skipped=0

ui        678   (desktopTest 441 + iosSimulatorArm64Test 237)
model     492   (iosSimulatorArm64Test 246 + jvmTest 246 — commonTest, run twice)
core      225   (iosSimulatorArm64Test)
platform  133   (iosSimulatorArm64Test)
harness   116   (iosSimulatorArm64Test)
app        65   (iosSimulatorArm64Test)
cards      17   (jvmTest)
```

`vocabularyLint clean` at 7 / 30 / 32 / 53 / 69 file(s), `redactionLint clean — 53 file(s) scanned`,
`boundary: core clean — 3 artifacts vs 2 library rule(s), 7 components vs 3 project rule(s)`,
`boundary: ui clean — 40 artifacts vs 0 library rule(s), 82 components vs 1 project rule(s)`.
**This is R2F's reported total to the test, and to the module.**

**`./verify-guards.sh` — 6 passed, 0 failed**, run against the finished tree and watched to reject
its own violation on each of the six: the mechanic synonym, the elimination framing, the unmarked wire
type, coroutines reaching `core`, `core` reaching `ui`, and a cheat surface reachable from a release
build. The tree was clean afterwards.

⚠️ **Three compiler warnings survive the run and none is new work.** `core/…/Rules.kt:1535`
*Condition is always 'false'* and `:1545` *Condition is always 'true'* are overnight #3's two,
unchanged — redundant null re-checks in `pulseReturned` the compiler can prove from a smart-cast.
The third is new and is the cheats': `app/src/cheats/…/Cheats.kt:246` *Elvis operator always returns
the left operand of non-nullable type 'RoundAuthority'*. All three are harmless. Recorded, not
touched; this unit fixed nothing.

### Every unit, with its commit and what the counts did

| unit | commit(s) | tests after | new | phone? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| *(rev 38, committed mid-R1)* | `d316584` | — | — | no |
| **R1 · routing, the tick driver, the meeting's hard stop** | `0d7589e`, `49c2879` | 1681 | +45 (from 1636) | no |
| **Static · Spoof replaced end to end** | `7713c56` | 1689 | +8 (five files added, one deleted) | no |
| **C3 · briefings, the caller's message, the routing tail** | `a0a0d0f` | 1711 | +22 | no |
| **Rehearsal 2 · five seats, two rounds, two seeds** | `4b343b8` | 1718 | +7 | **no — five simulators** |
| **R2F · the ability gate and the role reveal** | `cd8093e` | **1726** | +8 | no |

**Ninety tests added across the day**, 1636 → 1726. **Fifty-seven injections** were run and reverted
across the five units (R1 twelve plus one on method, Static seventeen, C3 fourteen, Rehearsal 2 six
plus one on the build, R2F eight) — **and five of them found holes in the tests rather than in the
build**: C3's #6 and #8, R2F's #4 (caught by the *fuzzer* on a seeded round nobody wrote, which is
the leak instrument doing the job its KDoc says it was built early for), and R1's discovery that
`IntentAdmissionTest` and `IntentWireTest`'s three hand-written completeness lists had all silently
stopped covering the three Tier-2 fires.

⚠️ **R2F's injection 1 also found the shared `Round.kt` fixture doing exactly what the rehearsal
did** — firing every ability from `Seat(1)`, the Insider the room restrains at that fixture's own
first meeting. Nine refusals said so, and a second fixture bug behind it: the Tier-2 pass pushed the
Access pool with `state.withCooldown(...)`, which is not an event, so a replay never applied it.

**One method finding cost real work and is written here because it will happen again.** R1 used
`git checkout <file>` to revert an injection on a file holding a large body of **uncommitted** work
and took all of it. Every unit after checkpointed to a commit first, copied each file before
injecting and restored from the copy. *Reverting an injection is not the same operation as reverting
a file.*

### ⚠️ Every escalation, R1 through R2F, in one place

**Twenty-five in the table: eight closed and seventeen open.** Seven were closed by a later unit of
the same day and one by this sweep reading the evidence; three of the eight (`E-P3-*`) were
inherited from the rehearsal the day before and closed by R1. **A twenty-sixth is raised below by
this sweep, so eighteen are open in all.**

| # | escalation | status |
|---|---|---|
| **E-P3-1** | a complete round nobody would see | **CLOSED** — R1 routed eleven kinds; Rehearsal 2 measured ten screens per seat |
| **E-P3-2** | the check-in gate could not close | **CLOSED** — R1; the escalation was half wrong, the control existed and had no route |
| **E-P3-3** | no tick driver | **CLOSED** — R1's `HouseClock` (pure) + `tickHouse` (the loop) |
| **E-R1-1** | the calling seat cannot be routed | **CLOSED** — owner ruled OPTION 1; C3 built it; **D-191** |
| **E-R1-4** | five kinds in the owner's table unrouted | **CLOSED** — C3 routed all five |
| **E-R2-1** | **nobody checks who is firing** | **CLOSED** — R2F's `admit` branch; **D-192** |
| **E-R2-2** | no push tells a phone it is an Insider | **CLOSED** — R2F's `RoleRevealed`; **D-193** |
| **E-R2-8** | the house's last word reached one phone | **CLOSED BY THIS SWEEP — it is not a bug.** See below |
| **E-R1-2 / E-R2-9** | the living's tally rows have no sender | **OPEN — a disclosure ruling.** `MeetingResult` carries only `restrained`; the drawn screen shows per-candidate counts to everybody |
| **E-R2-3** | the Egress reaches no screen **and the two nodes are dropped** | **OPEN — a routing decision this project has never ruled.** D-118 and D-076 rule the dim and the buzz, not a navigation |
| **E-R2-4** | the Sync Pulse taps have no anchor | **OPEN — a missing carrier.** `onTheBeat` grades against absolute house ticks; nothing on the wire carries the house's clock |
| **E-R2-5** | `ScreenId.Revoked` is reached by nothing and `outBy` is never set on that path | **OPEN.** The Restrain half works; the two ways out of the game are asymmetric on the screen |
| **E-R2-6** | the couch's live ballot is routed to nothing | **OPEN — a protocol decision, not a wiring one.** The wire carries seats, `PanelState.ballots` wants names, and where the resolution happens is the question |
| **E-R2-7** | nothing puts a countdown on a screen | **OPEN** — the same missing carrier as E-R2-4 |
| **E-R2-10** | NEW ROUND still returns one phone | **OPEN** (E-P3-5, re-measured unchanged) — `LobbyLink.roundEnded()` must travel |
| **E-R2-11** | the Kotlin/Native link leaks scratch per run and never cleans it | **OPEN — a build defect.** Re-measured below |
| **E-R1-3** | `GameState.accessPausedAt` is unused machinery | **OPEN** — D-183 and D-184 between them made it unobservable; kept and flagged, because removing state is a recording-shape decision |
| **E-P2-3** | `MeetingModel.send` / `SubroutineModel.send` have no production caller | **OPEN** — the cycle is unchanged: the models want the round transport, which wants a `FlowModel`, which wants the models |
| *Static 1* | `Balance.STATIC_COOLDOWN` is 120s and the figure is the build's | **OPEN** — D-186 ruled the direction and named no number; what is built is an ordering, Surge 150 > Static 120 > Isolate 90 |
| *Static 2* | the Timelapse's window arithmetic is unbuildable today | **OPEN — blocked on E8**, which is design-complete and build-pending |
| *Static 3* | `Transcript.staticFires` counts a metric whose question has changed | **OPEN** — `gdd.md:1347` asked whether the ability was ever used and D-186 took that risk |
| *Static 6 / T2·6* | a phone that reconnects mid-round is re-sent no Isolate list | **OPEN** — D-190's other half; its plan half dissolved with the plan |
| *C3 2* | `CurrentSubroutine.instruction` has no carrier and is always null in play | **OPEN — a copy call nobody has made** |
| *C3 4* | the seat total only reaches a phone at the first meeting | **OPEN — a carrier gap, not a routing one.** A phone put outside the system before one has a meter that does not move |
| *C3 6* | the Surge's sound has no route and nothing to route to | **OPEN** — `Speaker` is a stub and every actual of it is silent |

**Three more are decided-and-flagged rather than open**: a jam has no expiry event and the client
runs the window (argued on `FlowModel.staticLapsed`); where a surged phone lands is the build's and
not D-182's (`Home`, because that is where a rescan starts); and `EgressHeld.remaining` stays
unrouted — ticks versus seconds, E7's with the tick rate.

#### ⚠️ E-R2-8 is closed here, and it took one line of the rehearsal's own logs

R2F named the check that would settle it and correctly declined to make it off a summary. **The logs
were still on disk** (`/private/tmp/someones-home-rehearsal/run{1,2}/seat-*.log`) and the check is
one grep:

```
              seat0  seat1  seat2  seat3  seat4
run 1   THE HOUSE SIGNED OFF     ·      ·      ·      1      ·
        IsolateTargetsOffered    ·      ·      ·      6      ·
run 2   THE HOUSE SIGNED OFF     ·      ·      ·      ·      1
        IsolateTargetsOffered    ·      ·      ·      ·      5
```

**The seat that got the house's last word is the seat holding the Insider's kit, in both runs, and it
moved when the draw did.** `EmitSchema.kt:514` reads
`HOUSE_SIGNED_OFF to setOf(ClientClass(Role.Insider, RoundState.Ended))`, written as an explicit
one-class set on purpose, and its comment calls it *the narrowest row in the table* and says why it
is lawful: `INSIDERS_REVEALED` has already published who the Insiders were, to everyone, on the
screen this message appears on. **One phone of five is exactly what the row says, and the row is
argued.** Not a delivery bug and not a wrong class — the same shape `ABILITY FIRED` and
`IsolateTargetsOffered` showed in the role-swap table, arriving on a kind nobody had checked against
the draw.

#### ⚠️ A new one, found by this sweep: the recording format did not bump for `RoleRevealed`

`Recording.HEADER` is `someone-is-home/recording/15` and R2F did not move it, but
`Transcript.kt:262` renders a **new effect row** — `RoleRevealed|seat=…|role=…` — that a version 15
build cannot name. **That is the format's own stated bump condition**, and the file's version-15
comment argues it in as many words for a smaller change: *the events are the same and the effect
transcripts are not, so a differential run across the boundary would report a divergence that is
really a format gap.* Nothing pins the literal number (both tests read `Recording.HEADER`), so the
fix is one constant and a comment. **Not made here** — this unit's scope is verification, and a
version constant is a claim about a contract rather than a typo. Escalated.

#### E-R2-11, re-measured

`/private/var/folders/*/*/T/included*` held **128 directories totalling 403 MB** at the close of this
sweep's own `--rerun-tasks` run, on a volume with 11 GB free. The accumulation is exactly as
Rehearsal 2 described — nothing removes them — but **the size is not**: these average 3 MB against
the 141 MB each the rehearsal measured, so the twelve-gigabyte pile that stopped the evening three
times was a worse case than a routine run produces. The defect is the same one; the urgency is
lower than the original figure suggests.

### ⚠️ Rehearsal 2's fake list — the project's remaining build map

**Restated because it is the centrepiece, and because it is now three rather than six.** Everything
a bench stood in for, with what each is waiting on:

| # | what is faked | what it is waiting on |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | the Revoke firing (`Event.ContactMade`) | **the BLE handshake — unbuilt hardware.** The stand-in constructs the same `Event` the handshake will |
| 2 | the Revoked report (`MeetingTrigger.RevokeReported`) | **the same physical act, the same rule.** `factOf` rules it *reported, never requested* |
| 6 | a *correct* Subroutine entry | **nothing — this is rule 3 working.** A phone is shown the question and never the answer; the inverse lives in `core`'s grading, where no bench can call |

**Three fell during the day.** The Egress firing became `RoundBody.Fire` answered at `Landing.Draw`;
the five timed transitions became `tickHouse` off a monotonic clock; and the Sync Pulse's **frame and
grading are real** — what is left of it is the tick anchor, which is E-R2-4 and is a carrier rather
than a fake. **Two acts became real that P3 had no act for at all**: STOP NOW from a phone that
really had work open, and a Static jam fired from a phone.

**And one stand-in is unchanged and is not on the list**: `BenchHome`. No phone in any run has walked
a real home.

### ⚠️ Every drafted copy string awaiting the owner

**The nine briefing lines are the big one and they are drafts, not decisions.** D-188 ordered them in
Sniff's register — second person, imperative, what the phone will do and then what you do about it,
two lines each, no nouns from the fiction. They live on `Subroutine.briefing` (`ui/Subroutines.kt`)
and nowhere else, and the field is **non-null now**, so a Subroutine cannot be added without one.

| # | Subroutine | the line, as drafted |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | REPLAY | `DOTS WILL FLASH IN ORDER.` / `TAP THEM BACK IN THAT ORDER.` |
| 2 | INTERRUPT | `A SWEEP WILL RUN BACK AND FORTH.` / `TAP WHILE IT IS INSIDE THE BAND.` |
| 3 | PARITY CHECK | `A GRID WILL FILL IN A PATTERN.` / `TAP THE ONE BLOCK THAT BREAKS IT.` |
| 5 | DEALLOCATE | `THE COLUMNS WILL STAND UNEVEN.` / `TAP THEM DOWN TO THE SHORTEST.` |
| 6 | DRIFT | `A DOT WILL MOVE, THEN HIDE.` / `WHEN THE PHONE BUZZES, TAP WHERE IT IS.` |
| 7 | SHORT | `THE PHONE WILL ASK FOR FINGERS.` / `HOLD THAT MANY UNTIL IT RETURNS.` |
| 8 | SIGNAL TRACE | `TWO ENDS WILL BE MARKED.` / `TAP NODE TO NODE FROM ONE TO THE OTHER.` |
| 9 | JAM | `TWO RINGS WILL SIT APART.` / `TAP UNTIL THEY SIT ON ONE ANOTHER.` |
| 10 | HANDSHAKE | `THE PHONE WILL BUZZ A RHYTHM.` / `TAP IT BACK BY FEEL.` |

*(4 · SNIFF is the owner's existing line and is unchanged.)*

**The other three, unchanged from revision 38's list:**

- **`CHECK LOCAL NETWORK PERMISSION`** — D-180's refusal copy, the builder's words inside a ruled
  shape.
- **Sniff's briefing line** — the owner's own, and the register the nine above were written to match.
- **`HOLD TO USE THESE THREE`** — `tier2Sub`'s row, changed by the Static unit because the line it
  replaces (`HOLD TO SELECT A TARGET`) became false when two of three abilities select and one does
  not. 23 characters, exactly the length of the line it replaces, so the row's shape against the
  Resident's `WITHIN NOMINAL PARAMETERS` has not moved (D-142). **Decided rather than awaiting** —
  listed so nobody thinks it slipped in.

**And one that is a copy question rather than a copy draft:** `CurrentSubroutine.instruction` is
nullable and always null in play. *RESYNC THE KEEPALIVE* is flavour and nothing on the wire carries
it. Whether it wants a carrier or belongs on the client's roster beside the label is a call nobody
has made.

### The screenshots

**Eighty-one frames across five sets, and thirty of them are the day's.**

| set | frames | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| `shots/` | 15 | — |
| `shots2/` | 18 | overnight #2's pass |
| `shots3/` | 5 | — |
| `shots4/` | 13 | overnight #3's pass |
| **`shots5/`** | **30 + README** | **the day's, and the only new set** — Rehearsal 2, fifteen beats × living/out |

`shots5/` is the first set in this project's history taken off **five phones playing at once** rather
than off a render harness, and its README carries the ledger's beat table beside the images and says
which one to believe. ⚠️ **The pixels are illustrative and the ledger is the instrument**: five
simulators on one Mac do not all keep drawing, a covered window can stop compositing, and
`simctl io screenshot` then returns the last frame that device drew — one shot in that set was ten
minutes stale. Each frame is *a frame that phone drew at or before this beat*.

### Where the project stands

**End to end, there is now a game.** A host arms a round for five seats found by mDNS; the house
deals roles and work orders and **tells every phone which of the two it is**; players scan real
marker cards and are handed real Subroutines with a briefing on the card before the screen opens;
the meter moves on the house's count; an Insider fires from a page-2 tile that only an Insider's
phone draws, and the house now **refuses the fire from anybody else** — recorded, and surfaced to
nobody; a Revoke or a report rings every phone, one of which knows it is the caller; the discussion,
the ballot and the tally each end on a clock the house owns rather than five clocks that happen to
agree; the couch watches with a map the living cannot have; a Static jam goes over three living
screens and neither out screen; an Egress draws two markers and runs a countdown; and the round ends
with a reveal that reaches everybody and a last word that reaches the Insider. **Two complete
five-seat rounds on two seeds were played over real sockets to prove it**, with a one-line probe on
every arriving byte returning `0 CARRIED THIS PHONE'S LINE` on all five seats, and a role-swap
differential showing the two Insider-only kinds moving with the role and not with the seat.

**Three tracks remain, and they are genuinely different kinds of work.**

1. **Hardware hands.** Everything a *player's body* does still has a bench between it and the rules.
   The BLE contact handshake does not exist, so the Revoke and its report are both stand-ins; torch,
   camera and haptics have never run outside a simulator that announces at every launch that it has
   none of them; and no phone in any run has walked a real home. **This is the track that cannot be
   advanced from this machine at all** — it needs phones, in a house, in the dark.
2. **Balance playtests.** The numbers that shipped as orderings rather than values are waiting on
   people: `STATIC_COOLDOWN` at 120s is the build's figure inside D-186's ruled direction, the
   half-charge D-184 hands back at every meeting has never been felt by anybody, and D-182's five
   seconds of a dark rectangle is a length nobody has stood through. **None of these is a bug and
   none can be settled by a test.**
3. **The deferred list.** Eighteen open escalations above, and they sort into four piles rather than
   one: **three disclosure or routing rulings** the owner owes (the living's tally rows, where a
   living player is pushed when the house catches fire, where a seat resolves to a name); **one
   carrier** that closes three escalations at once (the house's clock on the wire — E-R2-4, E-R2-7
   and `EgressHeld.remaining` are one missing field between them); **one loop join** that closes two
   (E-P2-3's cycle, and E-R2-10's `roundEnded()` travelling); and **the rest**, which are individual
   gaps with individual answers.

**The shortest path to a fourth complete rehearsal being worth running is the carrier**, because it
is one field that turns three escalations into screens a player reads. **The shortest path to the
game being playable by people who are not us is the first track**, and no amount of work in this
repository shortens it.

---

## Engineering — Unit E1

*The routing day's ruled leftovers, built. Six of eight items closed, one escalated on a ruling
the charter's citation does not cover, and one item's real blocker turned out to be a field with
no writer that nothing had ever flagged.*

**Outcome: green.** `PATH="$HOME/.local/share/mise/shims:$PATH" ./gradlew check --rerun-tasks`:
**tests=1743, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (ui 690, model 494, core 226, platform 133,
harness 116, app 67, cards 17), read out of `*/build/test-results/*/*.xml` and never off an exit
code. The routing day closed at **1726**, so this is **+17**. `vocabularyLint` clean (cards 7,
core 30, app 33, model 53, ui 69), `redactionLint clean — 53 file(s)`, `boundary: core` and
`boundary: ui` clean, `./verify-guards.sh` **6 passed, 0 failed**. **No phone was used and none
could have been.** Commit `27b414f`.

### 0 · The mining, and where it disagreed with the sweep

The charter said every item was already ruled. **Two of the citations contradict R2F's own
escalation text**, so both were re-read before anything was built:

- **E-R2-3.** R2F wrote *"a routing decision this project has never ruled — D-118 and D-076 rule
  the dim and the buzz, not a navigation."* That is correct about D-118 and D-076 and **the ruling
  is in the GDD's own M7 table**, which R2F did not open (`gdd.md:370-378`): *Alert: **must be
  dismissable.** You cannot scan a marker through a modal.* / *Display: **the System Integrity
  widget becomes the Egress countdown, and names the two nodes.*** The answer to *where is a living
  player pushed* is **nowhere** — it is not a navigation, which is why no navigation ruling exists.
  Built.
- **E-R2-6.** R2F correctly identified the open question as *where the resolution happens* and
  called it a protocol decision. **D-115's second-site clause rules it** — *clients receive names
  where the interaction needs them, **the vote ballot being the obvious second site*** — and the
  resolution is house-side. Built.

The other four citations (D-134 for E-R2-5, E-P3-5's *what is missing is a push, not a button*,
the format's own stated bump condition, D-157) hold as written.

### 1 · ⚠️ The finding: `PanelState.roster` had no writer, anywhere

**Not on the charter's list, and it is the largest thing in this unit.** `FlowModel.nameOf` is a
lookup against `PanelState.roster`; C3 built it on the routing day and wrote a careful KDoc
arguing it is lawful. **Nothing in the application ever wrote that field.** It is null in every
real round, so every lookup returned null and **four screens drew the port's fixture names**:

| surface | what a real round drew | reads |
|---|---|---|
| the ring | `PRIYA` / `ELLIOT`, always | `state.caller` ← `nameOf` |
| the tally | `MARCUS WAS RESTRAINED`, always | `state.tally` ← `nameOf` |
| the couch's live ballot | the port's five, always | `state.ballots` — branch was `Unit` |
| the Surge picker | the port's five, always | `PanelVals.roster` ← `state.roster ?: ROSTER` |

These were never four gaps. **They are one missing carrier read four ways**, and E-R2-6 could not
have been closed honestly without it — wiring the ballot alone would have produced rows of nulls.

**`Effect.RosterIssued(seat, roster)`**, emitted once per seat as the fourth run of the arming
burst. **One entry per seat, in seat order, blank where a phone never said what to call its
owner** — `LobbyBody.Standing.names`' own rule, load-bearing here because the client indexes into
it *in both directions*: a seat to a name for the tally, and a tapped row back to a seat for the
picker and the ballot. A list holding only the seats that happened to be named shifts every index
after the gap and **fires an ability at somebody else**.

**It discloses nothing and that is provable rather than argued.** Every phone in the lobby was
already sent every name (D-115's own widening, `LobbyBody.Standing.names`). What this adds is the
**order**, which is the one thing a client cannot derive and must never guess — `Standing.names` is
arrival order and its own KDoc forbids pairing it positionally with anything. The list is
**byte-identical on all six phones**, so a phone still cannot read its own seat out of it.

**C3·4 rides the same field** and needed no new disclosure, exactly as the charter predicted:
`PanelState.seats` is the roster's **length**, it is a public lobby fact, and it now arrives at
arming rather than at the first meeting — so a phone put outside the system before one no longer
watches a meter that cannot move. Written in the same assignment as the roster, for
`CheckInProgressed`'s reason: one branch writes both, so they cannot drift.

**The recording holds seats and no names.** `RosterIssued|seat=N|seats=K` — the row renders the
list's *length*, so a replay rebuilds blanks of the same length and the row comes out
byte-identical. D-115's round-scoping kept in the one artefact of this game that outlives the
evening, for the third time after the lines and the arming row's names.

### 2 · ⚠️ E-R2-5, and the shape of it is the finding

D-134 rules the screen — *newly Revoked: a screen tells them they are Revoked and to sit silently
where it happened* — and `ScreenId.Revoked` was reached by nothing. The room holding you pushed
`RestrainedTakeover`; the house withdrawing your lamp pushed nothing at all, so a Revoked player
went on being shown the round they were no longer in until somebody called a meeting.

**The first version emitted `Effect.RevokeLanded` only when the contact landed, and
`AbsentEffectIsTheLeakTest` refused it on the first run.** That is the project's rule-1 guard and
it was right: the authority's effect stream then differs between a Revoke that took and one that
hit a seat already out, which is `Rules.kt`'s own header rule with a new effect written into it.

**It is constructed on every contact now**, outside every branch exactly as `AbilityFired` is, and
**the emit boundary is what narrows it**: `REVOKE_LANDED` is permitted to the two OUT classes and
to neither living one, so a contact that revoked nobody constructs one for a seat that is still
Live and the delivery is declined. `Surged`'s two independent denials, inverted. Four cases and
one shape — landed, already-out, self-contact, never armed — and **no client's stream differs
between any two of them.**

**It carries no haptic** (D-156). Constructed on every contact, a buzz would fire again every time
an Insider touched a player who was already out: a phone going off in the hand of somebody sitting
silently on a couch, reporting that an Insider is nearby and has just fired. `HouseSignedOff`
carries no haptic for this reason and this is the same one. The player is not left unaware — their
lamp is already defunct in their hand, which rule 7 puts on the device's own contact path.

### 3 · The rest of what was built

- **E-R2-3 · the Egress alert routed.** `arrived(ScreenId.Banner)` — a **dismissable alert, not a
  navigation**. It inherits D-119's two cases rather than restating them: a phone nobody picked up
  gets it under the clock, and a phone on a house-driving screen keeps that screen (a banner over
  the tally would take the room's result off the screen it is being read from). **The two nodes now
  travel as `Destination`** — card, glyph and room — so the alert and the widget name **rooms**.
  They read one pair of strings out of `PanelState`, so they cannot send two people who may not
  speak to two different places. Nothing about those two markers is narrowed by sending less:
  D-150's amendment to F-002 turns on *the two nodes are already named to every phone in the house*.
- **E-R2-6 · the couch's live ballot.** Rows resolved through `nameOf` against the pushed roster,
  rebuilt whole in **seat order** on every tap (rule 4 — two people on one couch must be reading
  the same list, and a list that grew as taps landed would be two lists), keyed on the **seat** and
  never on the name (two players may type the same name, and a phone that typed none has none). A
  Skip renders `SKIP` and never `STILL DECIDING` — D-075: *not voting counts as a Skip*, so the two
  must stay distinguishable on the screen somebody is reading them off. Cleared when a meeting
  begins, like everything else `MeetingModel` clears.
- **E-R2-10 · NEW ROUND returns every seat.** `LobbyBody.RoundEnded`, host → client, carrying
  nothing — *what is missing is a push, not a button*. Everything a second round is built from
  survives it (D-157); everything that does not is dropped on **both** sides independently rather
  than sent, so the one text promised never to come back down still never travels. It is a sibling
  of `Standing` and not a flag on it: a flag would be a moment expressed as a field, arriving on
  every publish, and the first client to read it twice would wipe a round that had already begun.
  The client resets the round's inbox before telling the panel — a phone carrying last round's
  sequence would refuse the next round's opening pushes as frames it already held.
- **E-P2-3 · the cycle untied.** `MeetingModel.send`, `SubroutineModel.send` and `FlowModel.send`
  had **no production caller**, so I AM HERE, READY TO VOTE, a row under a thumb, READY, the
  Subroutine hand-over and page 2's two fires reached nobody through two whole rehearsals — the
  gates were closed by the bench pressing intents directly. Fixed with the holder idiom `Cheats.kt`
  already uses twice (`rules`, `seam`): an **ordering fix and nothing else**, no seat named, no
  request composed. `MeetingModel.names` became house-written in the same move — it was a
  constructor fixture with no writer, so the vote screen offered the port's five names to a house
  with five other people standing in it.
- **The format bumped, 15 → 16.** Three effects a version 15 build cannot name (`RoleRevealed`,
  which was version 15's own arrival and never got its bump, plus this unit's two) and one row that
  changed shape: `EgressOpened|nodes=` held two bare card ids and holds six escaped fields now,
  which is the half that could not have been a tolerant read.

### 4 · The injections — twelve, each restored from a copy

Never `git checkout`. Each file copied first, the marker applied, the module's tests run, the
failures read **out of the test XML**, the file restored from the copy, and the tree grepped for
every marker at the end (clean).

| # | what was broken | what failed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | the Egress alert's route dropped | 2 × `RoutingTest` — the work screen and the lock screen both |
| 2 | the Egress nodes not resolved to rooms | `EgressTest` — *two phones were sent to different places* |
| 3 | the roster branch made a no-op | `RoutingTest` |
| 4 | the ballot not told the roster | **NOTHING — see below** |
| 5 | `REVOKE_LANDED` widened to the living | **3 instruments**, incl. `FuzzPropertyTest` on a seeded round |
| 6 | the Revoke landing made conditional | **4 instruments**, incl. `AbsentEffectIsTheLeakTest` |
| 7 | the couch's rows left in arrival order | `RoutingTest` |
| 8 | the couch's ballot not cleared at a meeting | `RoutingTest` |
| 9 | the client ignores the round-ended push | `RoundEndedPushTest` — **by hanging; see below** |
| 10 | the round inbox not reset, only the screen | `RoundEndedPushTest` |
| 11 | `ROSTER_ISSUED` narrowed to Insiders | **NOTHING — see below** |
| 12 | a kind removed from the every-kind fixture | `HouseBuzzTest.everyKindIsHere` |

**⚠️ Two injections found holes in this unit's own tests rather than in the build.**

- **#4** passed because the assertion compared the pushed roster against `MeetingModel.sample()`'s
  names, **which are the same five** — so *the house wrote the ballot* and *nobody wrote the ballot*
  were the same observation. The test now pushes a list no fixture can supply, including a blank
  seat, and asserts the blank keeps its slot.
- **#11** is the sharper one. Narrowing `ROSTER_ISSUED` to `Insider/Live` **broke nothing in the
  entire build.** It looks like the safest row in the table — the list is byte-identical on every
  phone, so there is nothing in it to leak — and **the leak is in what its absence would say**:
  every screen that names anybody is populated on an Insider's phone and blank on a Resident's, so
  one role sees the ring's caller, the tally and the picker and the other sees nothing. That is
  `ROLE_REVEALED`'s own argument arriving at a message with no role in it. `EmitSchemaTest` gained
  the row guard, and #11 re-run fails on it.

**⚠️ #9 originally failed by TIMING OUT rather than by asserting** — thirty seconds of nothing and
a `TimeoutCancellationException` with no words on it. That is exactly the failure mode
`StaleSeatTest`'s own KDoc warns about (*a guard that hangs is a guard that will one day be stopped
by a CI timeout and read as infrastructure*). The wait is bounded and followed by a real assertion
now, and the re-run says *the push arrived and the panel was never told*.

**⚠️ A third finding is older than this unit.** `HouseBuzzTest.everyKindIsHere` compares two
hand-written lists **in one file**, which is a pair that agrees with itself — and it did, for three
kinds: `RoleRevealed` and `MeetingCalling` were in the game and had never been run through the
haptic motor by anything in that file. `RoundWireTest` uses `EmitSchema.knownKinds()` as its anchor
and had no such gap. `HouseBuzzTest` anchors on it now, in both directions.

### 5 · ⚠️ ESCALATED — E-R2-4 / E-R2-7, the house-clock carrier

**Not built, and this is the one item of the eight that turned out to need a ruling.** The
charter's citation (`gdd.md:371` — *both phones pulse haptically in unison off a server-scheduled
timestamp*) rules the **Sync Pulse**. It does not rule how a countdown reaches a screen, and the
two cheap answers each contradict a standing decision already written into the code:

1. **Push the number.** `PanelState.secondsLeft`'s own KDoc: *the device never advances it… six
   phones each counting down on their own would be six meetings ending at six different moments.*
   Taken literally this means one push per second per seat while any window runs — and effects come
   from the rules, so it needs a **new ~1 Hz event** minted by the tick driver and admitted through
   the gate. **That is a recording-shape decision**: every recording of every round grows a
   thousand-odd rows. E-R1-3 is already open for the milder version of exactly this question.
2. **Send an anchor and let the phone count.** What `gdd.md:371` literally describes, and it
   **reverses `secondsLeft`'s KDoc**. It also needs a shared time base the build does not have: the
   Sync Pulse window is 400 ms (`Balance.SYNC_PULSE_WINDOW`), house ticks are the house's, and
   nothing on the wire relates one to the other. Deriving the beat from arrival time is wrong by
   the network delay.

**Both are rulings, so neither was taken.** What this unit did instead is close the half of E-R2-3
that does not depend on it: the alert reaches every phone and **names the two rooms**, and the
countdown on the widget is still the fixture's. `EgressHeld.remaining` is unrouted for the same
reason and is unchanged.

**It remains the shortest path to a fourth rehearsal being worth running** — the closing sweep's
own reading, and this unit confirms it from the other side: one field turns three escalations into
screens a player reads, and it is one field nobody has ruled the shape of.

### 6 · Still open, and what this unit did not touch

- **E-R2-4, E-R2-7, `EgressHeld.remaining`** — one missing carrier, escalated above.
- **The couch's ballot DENOMINATOR.** The rows are real now; the screen draws
  `votesCast OF ballots.size`, and this list holds **the seats the house has shown** rather than
  every living seat — a phone that has not touched a row has no message and therefore no line to be
  STILL DECIDING on. Seeding it wants the living seats at ballot-open, which nothing on the couch's
  side of the allowlist carries. **Flagged rather than guessed**; the rows are what E-R2-6 named.
- **The host's half of the round-ended push has no test.** `RoundEndedPushTest`'s host is
  hand-rolled, so what is proved is that a client acts on the frame. Bringing `LobbyOverTransport`'s
  house up without the radio is **E-P2-3's other, still-missing seam** — the same gap `LobbyHouse.emit`
  and `onIntent` are already recorded as having. The broadcast shares one loop with the standing's
  (`announce`), which every evening exercises. Named at the bottom of that file rather than left.
- **`Recording.HEADER`'s literal is still pinned by nothing.** Both tests read the constant, so an
  injection on the version number fails no test — the closing sweep already recorded this and it is
  unchanged. The bump is made; the guard is not this unit's to invent.
- **Nothing in E2's scope was touched.** The living's Result still carries `restrained` only and
  this unit added no rows to it; the flavour slot, the refusal's Settings control and
  `accessPausedAt` are untouched. The one adjacency is **E-R2-9** — the living's tally rows — which
  is E2's *count-only* ruling and was deliberately left alone: this unit wired the **couch's**
  ballot and made no change to `MeetingResult` or to what a living phone draws.
